# Changelog

## Unreleased

- The worker view's tokens, five-hour estimate (or dollars) and context gauge are live while a turn goes on (§ 7.5, § 10; `src/run/spending.ts`'s live count and `showSums`, `src/run/prices.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `measure` and `measureEvery`; tutti-cxgo.5): they moved only when a turn ended, as Spending read only results and the host asked getContextUsage only after a finished turn, and a worker does its whole ticket in one turn, so they stayed at 0 tokens, ≈0% and no gauge until the worker was done. Now Spending counts each assistant message's usage by model, once per API message, a subagent's too, at API prices (the model's price a result of the run gave, a table of input prices, or the session's own rate), until the turn's result replaces it; the host asks how full the context is after the session's own model calls, at most once every 10 seconds, and still after each turn; and the dashboard shows the live sums at most once a second, a result's at once. What is recorded, the turn records, the run's figures and summary, still comes from the results. `test/live-figures.test.ts` covers it.
- `--auto-answer` answers the workers' questions with their recommended option, for a run left going while you are away (§ 2, § 4.5, § 5.1, § 7.2, § 7.11, § 8.2, § 10; `src/cli/flags.ts`, `src/run/autoAnswer.ts`, `src/agent/Attention.ts`'s `autoAnswer`, `src/services/Beads.ts`'s `respond`, `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`, `src/print/summary.ts`; tutti-cmye): a ticket that asked a question waited until you came back. Now, bare, tutti answers at once; `--auto-answer=10m` answers what has waited 10 minutes with the machine awake and no answer from you, so you answer first when you are there; `--auto-answer=false` turns it off, and it is off by default. A question ticket filed during the run, with options and one recommended in its metadata (tutti-r2ac), is answered with `bd human respond <question> "<label> (the recommended option, chosen by tutti --auto-answer, not by the maintainer)"`, labelled `auto-answered`, said by an `AUTO_ANSWERED` line and an `auto_answered` record, and its ticket resumed, the worker told which option tutti chose. An `AskUserQuestion` is answered with the option marked `(Recommended)`, else the first, a `QUESTION` line `by --auto-answer` and `via: "auto_answer"`, tallied apart from yours. Left to you, the `ASKED` line saying why: a `maintainer_only` question, one without options, one open before the run; and never a permission request, nor the `--feature` interview's questions. The `START` line ends ` · auto-answer`, the start record has `auto_answer`, the title line has an ` auto-answer ` pill in its own `autoAnswer` role, and the summary lists what it answered and `bd list --all --label auto-answered`. With a time, the run doesn't end while a question it will answer waits. orchestra has no such option: a departure on purpose. `test/auto-answer-run.test.ts`, `test/auto-answer-attention.test.ts` and `test/auto-answer-shown.test.ts` cover it.
- Toasts sit above what you write (§ 7.11; `src/ui/Toasts.tsx`'s `useLinesUnderToasts` and `ToastStore.under`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `underToasts` and `ViewHint`; tutti-fknd): in the worker view and the Interview view a toast was drawn just above the hint, on the composer's row, and, opaque since tutti-fgwf, hid the end of what you typed for its 4 seconds; `<id>'s worker has finished: your message wasn't sent` covered the very message it was about. Now the first toast sits on the rule over the held line, the finished or steered line, the queued messages and the composer, and a second or third over the request panel's or the transcript's last lines, counting the lines viewLayout gave those parts, so a composer that grows moves them up with it. The dashboard's toasts stay above its hint. `useHintHeight` is `useLinesUnderToasts`, for what it now counts. `test/toasts-over-composer.test.tsx` covers it.
- Question tickets give their options and the recommended one in their metadata, as orchestra's do since 0.9.x (§ 4.11, § 11; `src/project/templates/worker-prompt.md`, the worker prompt template's version 7; `src/services/Beads.ts`'s `questionOptions` and `maintainerOnly`; tutti-r2ac): the worker prompt asked for the options and a recommendation in the description only, which workers wrote several ways or not at all. Now every question gives 2 to 4 options, each a label and a one-line trade-off, one of them recommended, in its description and in `--metadata '{"options": [...], "recommended": <index>}'`; and, tutti's own, a question that asks the maintainer to act rather than choose, or to say yes to something done in their name, adds `"maintainer_only": true`, which tutti never answers for them. `questionOptions` reads the options, the recommended one first, sanitised, or none unless there are 2 to 4 with labels and one recommended; `maintainerOnly` is true only for the boolean true. Nothing shows them yet. Projects with an older `.tutti/worker-prompt.md` are told of the change; `tutti init --update` takes it. `test/question-options.test.ts` covers the rule and the reading.
- tutti says what it is doing while it starts (§ 7.1 "Getting ready"; `src/run/gettingReady.ts`, `src/main.ts`'s run path, `src/agent/claudeBinary.ts`'s `Sources.copied` and `unpacking`; tutti-t17u): nothing showed for several seconds before the first thing a bare `tutti` drew, the start question, the dashboard, the Nothing to run box or why it can't start, so the terminal looked idle or hung. Now one spinner line on the main screen, the printer's organ line, says `Getting ready` and the step under way, `unpacking Claude Code` while a compiled build's embedded one is extracted, `checking the setup and your Claude login` during the startup checks, `reading the backlog` for the tickets left unmerged, carried, counted or the nothing check, and `starting the run` for the rest, from the flags parsed until the first thing written or drawn, then is erased with no final line, so what follows looks as it did; `--feature`'s organ lines start after it. Its first frame comes after 100 ms, so a fast start shows nothing, and it is drawn only where stdout is a terminal and `TERM` isn't dumb: `tutti --plain | …` and scripts get nothing new. Ctrl+C, a signal or a failure erases it. It is drawn in the terminal's own colours, the time faint, as the appearance is read later. `test/getting-ready.test.ts` covers it.
- tutti says what it is doing while it starts (§ 7.1 "Getting ready"; `src/run/gettingReady.ts`, `src/main.ts`'s run path, `src/agent/claudeBinary.ts`'s `Sources.copied` and `unpacking`; tutti-t17u): nothing showed for several seconds before the first thing a bare `tutti` drew, the start question, the dashboard, the Nothing to run box or why it can't start, so the terminal looked idle or hung. Now one spinner line on the main screen, the printer's organ line, says `Getting ready` and the step under way, `unpacking Claude Code` while a compiled build's embedded one is extracted, `checking the setup and your Claude login` during the startup checks, `reading the backlog` for the tickets left unmerged, carried, counted or the nothing check, and `starting the run` for the rest, from the flags parsed until the first thing written or drawn, then is erased with no final line, so what follows looks as it did; `--feature`'s organ lines start after it. Its first frame comes after 100 ms, so a fast start shows nothing, and it is drawn only where stdout is a terminal and `TERM` isn't dumb: `tutti --plain | …` and scripts get nothing new. Ctrl+C, a signal or a failure erases it. It is drawn in the terminal's own colours, the time faint, as the appearance is read later. `test/getting-ready.test.ts` covers the line, and `test/getting-ready-binary.test.ts` a bare `tutti` on a pseudo-terminal: the line erased before the renderer, the Nothing to run box, a plain run's log, why it can't start and --feature's first organ line.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-10-25

- Runs of positional strings take their arguments by name, and a commit's hash and a message's uuid are types of their own (§ 4.8, § 5 item 7; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `Sha`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `MessageUuid`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `toHandBack` and `HandedRebase`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `toFix` and `HandedCheck`; tutti-rvzn.25): `resume`, `workerGone`, `ticketLimitStop`, `neverHanded`, `runFilesOutside`, `startFailed`, `answeredText`, the held messages and `sendMessage` took three or four strings in a row, and `AgentHost.send(ticket, text, from, uuid)`, `Transcripts.record` and `Spending.message` a ticket and a session side by side, any two of which could be swapped unseen; they take objects, as log.ts's builders do. A commit git gave is a `Sha`, made only by the Git service and `Sha.parse` (state.json's marks), and one git couldn't say is undefined, where it was `""` (or `-1` for a count of commits) that `=== ""` checks in a dozen places had to catch; a hand-back takes the stop `toHandBack` or `toFix` gave, whose commits are known, so the hand-back can't run on one git couldn't say; the lines that named such a commit as `at ` with nothing after it say the branch is left where it is, and no train is started onto one. A message's uuid is a `MessageUuid`, drawn by `MessageUuid.draw`, which `send`, `resume` and `answered` give and take. The marks in state.json leave out a commit not known, and read the `""` an earlier run wrote as none. Session and request IDs stay plain strings, as no run of them with another string is left; the ticket ID's brand is tutti-rvzn.32.
- A ticket's ID is a type of its own, `TicketId` (`src/services/Beads.ts`; tutti-rvzn.32): made only where one is drawn or decoded, from bd's issues, state.json (`ticketsIn`), the CLI's `--ticket`, the model's MCP tools and `checkTicketId`, so an ID can't be passed for the worktree, branch, base or reason that sit beside it throughout `src/run`. The ledger, the Store, AgentHost, Worktrees and Beads take it. `checkTicketId` checks an ID once, in `Worktrees.prepare`, before a folder or branch is made of it: `start` no longer checks it first as well, and `Worktrees.remove`, which makes no path of an ID, no longer fails with `BadTicketId` (`cleanupWhy` is gone, `gitWhy` says why a cleanup failed). The fake bd's seeds stay plain strings, as bd's JSON is, and its issues come out branded by decoding. Session and request IDs stay plain strings: no positional run of them with another string came up.
- The attention folder's watch is a stream (§ 4.5; `src/agent/attentionFiles.ts`'s `serve`; tutti-rvzn.26): `serve` built its own `fs.watch`, a sliding queue the watcher's callback offered to, and a loop racing the queue against the poll's sleep. Now the scan runs on `Stream.merge` of the FileSystem service's watch of the folder, its errors ignored, and `Stream.tick` of the poll, in the scope: a watch that fails, or a folder removed, which on macOS leaves the watch open with nothing to say, leaves the poll going, and a burst of events wakes one scan after the one under way. What a run does with an answer file is as it was. `test/attention-watch-poll.test.ts` covers the watch, the poll and a removed folder under TestClock.
- The service methods at process and I/O boundaries are named spans (`src/services/Beads.ts`, `src/services/Store.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/organs/Organs.ts`, `src/agent/UsageProbe.ts`, `src/agent/Account.ts`, `src/agent/Transcripts.ts`; tutti-rvzn.26): every Beads method, `Store.update`, AgentHost's `start`, `resume`, `send`, `interrupt` and `close`, `UsageProbe.probe` and Transcripts' `page` and `tail` are `Effect.fn`s of their names, and `Organs.ask` and `Account.probe` spans of theirs (Effect.fn would lose ask's type parameter, and the probe is an effect, not a function), so a failure's trace says which boundary it crossed. The pure steps, fold, emit, step and record, are left unnamed. `test/boundary-spans.test.ts` covers Beads and Store.
- tutti update writes the new binary as tutti writes any file whole (§ 12; `src/update/update.ts`'s `install`, `src/services/atomicFile.ts`'s `writeSynced`; tutti-rvzn.26): it had a `writeSynced` of its own beside atomicFile's, renamed the new binary over the old without syncing the folder after, so a power loss soon after could lose the rename, and kept the file beside the binary by hand. Now the file is made beside the binary, under a name no other writer picks, by an `Effect.acquireUseRelease` that removes it on any failure or interruption, written and synced with atomicFile's `writeSynced`, renamed over the binary through the FileSystem service, and the folder synced. What tutti update says is as it was. `test/update-install-cleanup.test.ts` covers the file's cleanup.
- The organs' provider and the workers' environment are each read once, as the run starts (§ 4.13, § 6.1; `src/run/startup.ts`'s `Started.organs`, `src/organs/AnthropicProvider.ts`'s `chosenLayer`, `src/agent/env.ts`'s `WorkerEnv` and `sharedEnv`, `src/agent/ClaudeCode.ts`'s `launchEnv`; tutti-rvzn.26): the run's start parsed TUTTI_ORGAN_PROVIDER but kept only its name, and the organs' layer read the variable and the key from the environment again; the account and tutti's environment went through the live AgentHost's, the organs' and the usage probe's options so each could make the workers' environment again. Now `Started.organs` is the provider, with its key redacted, which `chosenLayer` takes; `WorkerEnv`, beside `BranchEnv`, is the workers' environment for the run's account and billing, set once in `main.ts`, and ClaudeCode's adapter lays a worker's share of the machine over it for each session. `BilledAs.Workers` carries nothing, and the organs' and the usage probe's layers take no options. What sessions are given is as it was. `test/claude-code.test.ts`, `test/anthropic-provider.test.ts` and `test/organ-billing.test.ts` cover it.
- The Agent SDK's callbacks are fibers of their session's scope (§ 3.4; `src/agent/callbacks.ts`'s `sessionFibers` and `toolHandler`, `src/agent/Attention.ts`'s `request` and `elicitation`, `src/agent/hooks.ts`'s `activityHooks`, `src/agent/requiresTools.ts`, `src/agent/interviewTools.ts`; tutti-rvzn.26): canUseTool, onElicitation, the hook callbacks and both in-process MCP servers' tool handlers ran as root fibers no scope ended, so closing a session left a request a person hadn't answered, or a tool call under way, running. Now each session has a FiberSet in its scope, the interview's tools one in the interview's, that its callbacks run in, and closing the session interrupts them, an open request withdrawn. The two servers' handlers share one rule: the interview's tools, like the requires tools, stop with the call's abort signal, and a defect in either is the call's refusal rather than a rejected promise. `test/session-callback-fibers.test.ts` covers it.
- A Working ticket's activity is the view's, not the ledger's (§ 3.5, § 5.1; `src/run/phase.ts`'s `TicketPhase.Working` and `Detail`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `activityOf`, `rowStatus`, `statusTone`, `src/run/phase.ts`'s `countsUnder`; tutti-rvzn.24): the Working phase carried an activity nothing refined, so every Working ticket in the ledger stayed `starting`, and RunView made a new Working phase for each Working row on every frame to give its row and the totals the worker's activity. Now `Working` has no details and `Detail` only `Place` and `Step`; RunView works the activity out from the worker's state and open requests (`activityOf`) and hands it to the row, the card's status and tone and the totals, which take it beside the phase. What a run shows, logs and streams is as it was. `test/activity-of.test.ts` covers `activityOf`.
- Names a reader can trust in the run engine (§ 4, § 4.8, § 5.1; `src/run/told.ts`, `src/run/phase.ts`'s `Move`, `Finished` and `HoldBack`, `src/run/quitting.ts`'s `Quitting` and `quitStop`; tutti-rvzn.21): what moves a ticket from one phase to another, the transition table's When, is `Move`, no longer a `Cause` beside Effect's, which `src/run/run.ts` imported as `Causes`; a Done ticket's outcome is `Finished`, beside run.ts's `Outcome`, and the three holds are `HoldBack` (what holds a ready ticket back), `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `LimitHold` and `src/run/holds.ts`'s `RunHolds`; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `settle`, which places a waiting ticket, is `placeWaiting`, leaving `settle` to settle.ts. The texts tutti sends its workers are in one module, `told.ts`, as `Tag` gathers the log's words: the nudge, the answered question, the three carry-on messages, each `carryOnText` before and now `goneText`, `resumedText` and `resetText`, the reset's hand-back (`resetHandBackText`, once `carryOnHandBack`) and the hand-back prompts. A quit is a tagged union, `Quit` or `Panic`, matched by its tag rather than by `"panic" in`, and its stop, INTERRUPTED or the panic's PANIC, is built once, by `quitStop`. Nothing a worker, the log or the event stream sees changes; `test/quit-stop.test.ts` covers `quitStop`.
- The run's loop is a module of its own, its end matched case by case (§ 5 item 14; `src/run/look.ts`'s `make` and `LoopParts`, `src/run/runEnd.ts`'s `limitedOnly`; tutti-rvzn.19): the look, the wait until nothing is left and the probe after it were closures in `src/run/run.ts`, which was both the run's wiring and its control loop, so only whole-run tests reached them, and the settle checked only for `Done`. Now `make` takes the run's parts, each narrowed to what the loop reads and does, and gives `untilEnd`; its wait, `untilDone`, matches decide's `Busy`, `WaitOnYou`, `WaitForReset` and `Done` exhaustively; whether nothing runs but workers at the usage limit is the pure `limitedOnly`. What a run does and says is as it was. `test/run-loop.test.ts` runs the loop over stub parts.
- A merge is a machine of steps with one table of budgets (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `Step`, `mergeTurn` and `budgets`; tutti-rvzn.16): prepare's loop, with its pass and moved counters, a taken() closure and the merge queue's second loop around it, is now the steps Enter, Locate, Rebase, Check, Confirm and FastForward, one handler each, driven by `mergeTurn`, which runs each through the queue's restore but the fast-forward. The budgets count over the ticket's whole turn: a merge prepared again after its fast-forward found its branch or the base moved on kept none of what it had spent, so a base or branch moving on during every check could have it checked about 4 x 3 times before it was blocked. Each way, the base moving on during a check, the branch moving on during one, and either moving on before the fast-forward, now checks it again 3 times at most in the turn; the base moving on during every check checks it 4 times, where it was 3. `setUp` and `checksFailed` take their trailing arguments by name. The log lines are as they were.
- Every mark tutti draws is one table, by what it means, and a status's look is worked out as RunView is projected (§ 5.1, § 7.11; `src/ui/marks.ts`'s `marks`, `Shown`, `Tone`, `lookOf`, `Motion` and `motionTones`, `src/run/phase.ts`'s `activityRows`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `statusTone`; tutti-rvzn.9): the glyphs were strings in about 15 files, the tickets table found a row's style again from its word (`rowLooks`), and a card's status had a third set of rules, `statusLook`, run on every spinner tick. Now a mark is named by its meaning (`queued`, `working`, `waitsForYou`, `toMerge`, `merging`, `reworking`, `done`, `asked`, `setAside`, `review`, `idle`, `verdict`, `organ`, `latest`, `said`, `end`, …) and each place draws its glyph from the table; the phase table's row fact gives a row's mark, word, tone and whether the rest of it is faint, and a card carries its status's tone and its mark (`spinner`, `toMerge`, `merging` or `idle`), both worked out at most 30 times a second and kept while unchanged. What is drawn is as it was. `test/marks.test.ts` checks each glyph is a cell wide and no emoji and each meaning has its own, listing today's ambiguous-width, emoji and shared glyphs as known exceptions until the worker view's curated set (tutti-cxgo D7) replaces them.
- Simpler where an abstraction didn't pay (tutti-rvzn.30): the run's stops are one list in the order raised (`src/run/holds.ts`'s `Stops`, `firstStop`), so adding, lifting, swapping and putting a stop first are one-liners and a change that changes nothing leaves the holds as they were; the machine's hold at --machine-workers has no `attach` and no Refs filled after the scheduler is made: `src/run/machineGate.ts`'s `room`, `lookAround` and `sayRunning` take the slots the scheduler counts taken, `room` saying `room`, `lifted`, `held` or `began`, and the scheduler writes the queue record when the hold begins or ends; `src/run/holdsGate.ts` changes the holds with `SubscriptionRef.modifySomeEffect` rather than a Semaphore of its own; the merges name each service where they use it, with no bundle of seven, so `notHandedBack` and the small steps declare only theirs and `GiveEnv` is the set give uses; `afterFix` is inlined in `fixCheck`; `MergeOptions`' hooks (`noMore`, `atReset`, `loaded`, `recheckSlot`, `setUps`, `machineFull`; `atReset` defaults to `src/run/handback.ts`'s `noReset`, which sends a hand-back at once) are resolved once in `src/run/mergequeue.ts`'s `make` from `GivenMergeOptions`, and the steps that read only the main checkout and the base take `MergeTarget`; in the UI, `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`'s `Answering` is keyed on the shown request, the worker panel has no cards of its own, the worker and interview views work out their hint and hand-back lines once a render, `ViewHint` taking lines, and the dashboard's load, tickets table and wind-down lines come from its memoised layout. What a run does and shows is as it was. `test/stops-list.test.ts` covers the stops.
- tutti init's form fits a short screen (§ 7.1; `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`'s `aboveField` and `Page`; tutti-v62n): on a screen too short for it, as a checklist under a few done lines at 40 columns and 12 to 20 rows, its title, init's words, the stepper and the done lines shrank and drew over each other. Now every text of the form is wrapped by tutti's wrap, each part as tall as its lines, the field asked is drawn whole, and above it the done lines give up their top lines first, then the title, init's words and the stepper. `test/init-form-short-screen.test.tsx` covers each kind of field.
- Whether the run may take work is one snapshot of plain values, the Gate, read once for each decision, with the commands taken out of the queries (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/holds.ts`'s `Gate`, `openGate`, `holds`, `takesBack`, `takesNew` and `noMoreWork`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `gate`, `beforeStart` and `claimRoom`, `src/run/machineGate.ts`'s `claimRoom`; tutti-rvzn.17): "may the run take work" was five slightly different boolean expressions, two with side effects: the run's `taking` ran the requires check first, which may start an install, the scheduler's was that and the machine's room, which writes the hold at --machine-workers, its HOLD: MACHINE line and a queue record, and `holding`, `canTake`, the scheduler's start and its carry re-derived the rest, so one look read `taking` three to five times. Now the run's gate reads the first stop, the usage limit, the run's end, the wind-down and --limit together, the scheduler is given it with `beforeStart` (the requires check, still run before each start and carry) and `claimRoom` (the machine's room, named for its writes), and pure predicates answer from it: `holds` takes a ticket set aside back, `takesBack` the carried and answered workers, `takesNew` the fill, and `noMoreWork` says why the merges hand no failed check back. What a run does and says is as it was. `test/run-gate.test.ts` covers the predicates.
- The start question fits a short screen (§ 7.1; `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `targetLines`; tutti-lfii): on a screen too short for it, as New feature showed the description at 20 or 30 columns and 24 rows, its texts shrank to fewer lines than they had and drew over each other. Now its title, descriptions, hint and error are wrapped by tutti's wrap, each part as tall as its lines, and the select is given the lines left above the description, cut from its top, keeping its options while it is answered, and left out under the description where none are left. `test/start-question-short-screen.test.tsx` covers it.
- A done ticket is retired in one step, and merge.ts is no longer the run's hub (§ 5 item 7, § 5 item 8; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `retire`, `removeMerged` and `Cleanup`, `src/run/branch.ts`, `src/services/Git.ts`'s `gitWhy`, `isHash`, `shortHash`, `givenHash` and `abandonRebase`, `src/services/Worktrees.ts`'s `cleanupWhy`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `howFailed` and `flakyWarnings`, `src/project/configFile.ts`'s `parseConfigJson`; tutti-rvzn.22): a merge, a close with no change and a ticket merged by hand each removed the worktree and branch, recorded it closed, forgot it in state.json, asked bd of it, removed its label and closed the epics above it, with two removeMerged between them. Now `retire` does it for all three, with one removeMerged; a ticket merged by hand is retired with what bd showed of it as the run asked whether it merged, so bd is asked of it once, and its worktree, looked for once, is removed where git has it. The git reads that steer a merge are in `src/run/branch.ts`, gitWhy is beside GitError as beadsWhy is beside BeadsError, and merge.ts no longer re-exports beadsWhy, so 15 modules import merge.ts where 23 did, 7 of them for its types only. abandonRebase is a Git method, and a merge's rebase is no longer aborted a second time on an interrupt, git.rebase doing it already. The full check's and the setup's copies of how a command failed, where its output goes and its FLAKY warnings are check.ts's; a commit's hash is cut by `shortHash` (7, for a line) or `givenHash` (10, for a hash handed on to git), and taken as one by `isHash`; a merge's set-aside lines are made by one `closedBut` template; the settings a merge changed are parsed as the config files are. What a run does and says is as it was, but for the line saying the full check's output can't be kept in its worktree for a symbolic link there, which now says so as a ticket's check's does. `test/retire.test.ts` and `test/git-abandon-rebase.test.ts` cover it.
- Every scenario runs the live AgentHost, over scripted Claude Code sessions (§ 3.3, § 13; `src/testing/ClaudeCode.ts`, `src/testing/AgentHost.ts`, `src/agent/ClaudeCode.ts`'s `ClaudeSession.send`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`; tutti-rvzn.31): the scripted AgentHost had a lifecycle of its own beside the live one's, its own reading of what a session sends and its own checks of a session's start, so a scenario passed on code no run runs. Now ClaudeCode's test layer plays a script as a ClaudeSession (its messages, each taken in by the host before the next step plays; its initialize and mcpServerStatus() answers; its interrupts' receipts and cancel_async_message answers; hook, canUseTool and onElicitation calls through the session's options), and the scripted AgentHost is the live one over it, in a test FileSystem, recording what it was sent as before. A session opened without a prompt has an inbox, `send`, which the adapter hands the SDK as the prompt and ends as the session closes. The live host leaves a stream that ends before the session's initialize is answered to the start, which fails saying why Claude Code didn't start: the close its reader forked could cancel the start first, which then failed `it was closed while it started`. In scenarios, a gone session's why is the live host's (`its session ended: …`), a resume of a session begun in an earlier run carries the live host's notes before its message (`withoutNotes` leaves them out), and a setup problem is what the host's checks find in a script's init or initialize answer: the `setupProblem` and `stopAtStart` steps are gone. `test/claude-code-inbox.test.ts` and `test/start-settles-early-end.test.ts` cover the inbox and the start.
- A merge's outcomes are tagged unions with constructors, and its retries are Schedule policies named once (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/again.ts`'s `again` and `retrying`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `Prepared`, `gitAgain`, `Refused` and `diagnose`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `readAgain`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `FixEnd`, `FixRefusal`, `Verified`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `Resolved`, `src/run/setup.ts`'s `SetUp` and `WorktreeSetUp`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `Checked`; tutti-rvzn.15): what prepare, a pass's check, a hand-back, a setup and a check gave were object literals built at about 45 sites, a pass's outcome was translated into prepare's by a switch, a check hand-back ended as `"verified"`, `"aside"` or a CheckStop, and `""` said hand-backs were turned off. The fast-forward's retry was a loop of its own, and bd's reads were made again four ways. Now each is a `Data.TaggedEnum` and a pass's outcome is prepare's but for Checked; a refused fast-forward is diagnosed once (InTheWay, BaseMoved, NoFastForward, Unreadable, Kept) and only Kept is tried again, by `Effect.retry`; git's and bd's reads are made again by `gitAgain` and `readAgain`, whose limits sit under the tap that says a read is made again, so the read that gives up isn't said to be. What a run does and says is as it was. `test/again.test.ts`, `test/fast-forward-refused.test.ts` and `test/fill-read-again.test.ts` cover it.
- A worker view is a screen-model hook and pieces taking plain props, and a click target is a segment's (§ 7.5, § 7.3; `src/ui/worker/useWorkerScreen.ts`, `src/ui/worker/Head.tsx`, `Story.tsx`, `Panel.tsx` and `Looks.tsx`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `Segment.target`, `Line`'s `press`, `frameEdge` and `FrameRow`; tutti-rvzn.28): `WorkerView.tsx` held the syntax styles, the transcript's items, the request panel, the head and the commands a worker view sends in one file of 1600 lines, and only attention cards had buttons (`ButtonSegment`), the Modal had its own `ChoicePiece`, and the Modal, the reason box and the whole ticket drew their frames by hand. Now `useWorkerScreen` gives a view its card, what it consumes, whether the run holds its messages, its transcript, its composer and its commands, and the pieces in `src/ui/worker/` draw from plain props; a segment may have a `target`, an id kept through `cut`, `wrap` and `padded`, that a `Line` or `Lines` given `press` presses on a click, so a line worked out once stays the same line; the attention cards', the panel's, the composer's and the Modal's buttons, `▾ whole ticket  t` and the whole ticket's `▴ less  t · Esc` are such segments, and the Modal's, the reason box's and the whole ticket's frames are `boxed`'s (the answer form's and tutti init's buttons are tutti-rbem), their edges `frameEdge`'s with a label in them, and a `FrameRow` its sides around the reason box's field and the whole ticket's scrollbox. What is drawn is as it was. `test/segment-targets.test.tsx` covers the targets and frames; the worker view's tests are as they were.
- The forms' click targets are segments too, and the answer form's frame is `boxed`'s (§ 7.3, D9; `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx`, `InitForm.tsx`, `StartQuestion.tsx`, `PlanConfirmation.tsx` and `ResourceLine.tsx`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `targeting`; tutti-rbem): the answer form's rows and buttons, init's Yes and No, its options and its scout's Esc line, the start question's options, `[y/N]`'s `y` and `N` and the resource line were raw `<text>` or boxes made clickable, and the answer form's frame an OpenTUI border. Now each is segments with a target that `Line` or `Lines` presses, init's and the start question's options wrapped by tutti's `wrap` (`src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `Targets`) as init's descriptions are, between words only, where OpenTUI's word mode broke after a `-` or `/` too, and the answer form's frame is `frameEdge`'s with a `FrameRow` around its scrollbox. What is drawn, and where a click lands, are as they were; `test/form-click-segments.test.tsx` covers a wrapped option, the start question on a narrow screen and the answer form's frame.
- What a tool call is doing and which of its input's fields it is about is one table, `tools` (§ 4.2, § 7.5; `src/agent/tools.ts`'s `tools`, `toolOf` and `aboutOf`, `src/agent/untrusted.ts`'s `asRecord` and `asString`, `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s `classify`, `src/agent/Transcripts.ts`'s `toolSummary`, `src/agent/Attention.ts`'s `summarize`, `src/organs/triage.ts`; tutti-rvzn.10): the worker view, the permission requests and DENIED lines, and triage each had their own list of the fields that say what a call is about, and they drifted: a Grep call `{pattern, path}` showed its pattern in the worker view and triage but its path in the request and the DENIED line, and triage showed an Agent call's prompt. Now each reads `aboutOf`, a known tool's field from the table (Grep's and Glob's pattern, not their path; an Agent call's description) and another tool's first string field, keeping its own cut and sanitising; `classify` reads its doing from the same table, and `summarize` takes the tool's name. The five copies of `asRecord` and `asString` are one. `test/tool-about.test.ts` covers it.
- A line is fitted to the pane by priority, and a screen's lines handed out as a list, each by one helper (§ 7.2, § 7.5; `src/ui/fit.ts`'s `firstFit`, `firstFitting` and `fitParts`, `src/ui/allot.ts`'s `allot` and `stack`; tutti-rvzn.27): the load line, the title line, the totals strip, the wind-down line, the end-of-run box and the resource line each wrote their own loop trying a line's forms until one fit, and the dashboard and the transcript views their own count of the lines left. Now a line's forms are read from a generator, built only until one fits, and the load line's and the resource line's parts each have their forms and the rank they step down at, the lowest first; `viewLayout` is a list of its parts' lines in priority order, the transcript taking the rest, and `dashboardLayout` takes its lines from `allot`. What is drawn is as it was. `test/line-fit.test.ts` and `test/screen-allot.test.ts` cover them, with properties that a fitted line is never wider than the pane and that a stack's parts add up to its height.
- Every Claude Code session tutti opens goes through one port, ClaudeCode (§ 3.3; `src/agent/ClaudeCode.ts`, `src/agent/sessionKind.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `planOf`; tutti-rvzn.13): a worker's, the environment probe's and the interview's, the login probe's, the usage probe's and an organ's sessions each said what their session is and then launched it by hand, four times over, each with its own environment, spawn, abort, close and stand-in for the SDK's `query()`. Now each gives the port its options, its prompt and how it is billed, and the adapter does the rest one way; `ClaudeCode.layerWith(query)` is the one seam a test stands in for the SDK at. A session's kind is a tagged union, `SessionKind` (`Worker`, `Probe`, `Interview`), in place of the `probe` and `interview` fields, which could say both; its MCP servers come from one rule, `serversOf`, that both of AgentHost's layers read.
- What a worker spent is the whole ticket's last section (§ 7.5; `src/ui/TicketHead.tsx`'s `TicketPanelView` and `openSpent`, `src/ui/ResourceLine.tsx`'s `spentLines`; tutti-cxgo.3): a click on the resource line opened **Spent** in a box of its own over the worker view. Now it opens the whole ticket scrolled to Spent, the panel's last section after the labels, or scrolls an open panel there, and keeps it there as the Markdown above is laid out until you scroll; `t` opens the panel at its top, and `t`, `▴ less` or `Esc` close it as before. `Box.Spent` and `SpentBox` are gone. `test/worker-view-spent.test.tsx` covers it.
- What is so of each phase is one table, `phaseFacts` (§ 5.1; `src/run/phase.ts`'s `phaseFacts`, `factsOf`, `stopsAtEnd`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `holdsWorker` and `keepsWorker`, `src/run/sessions.ts`'s `lifetime`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `rowStatus`, `src/services/Store.ts`; tutti-rvzn.7): which phases were running, past their work, at work, busy, unsettled, ready, in the queue, over, their `ticket_end` outcome, orchestra's phase, the session's lifetime and the row's mark was asked in about 15 sets and switches across the ledger, holdsGate, holds, the schedule, the sessions and RunView, some with fall-through defaults, and the phase names were listed three times. Now `phaseFacts` (which `counting` grew into) gives every fact for every phase, so a new phase doesn't compile until each is given; `stopsAtEnd` reads a set built once from the transition table, `countsUnder` and `rowStatus` give the same value each call, the ledger's phase details and the reviewer's `phaseSaid` list every phase, and state.json's phase names are the event stream's. The ledger's worker number now reads the session's lifetime from the table, as `fit` does, taking a recheck as possible. `test/phase-counting.test.ts` checks each fact against the set or switch it replaced.
- tutti init's form, the start question and the plan confirmation draw their keys as the run's screens do, from one list of commands (§ 7.3; `src/ui/keyCommands.ts`'s `useFormCommands`, `src/ui/keys.ts`'s `formKeymap`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`'s `confirmCommands`, `selectCommands`, `textCommands`, `checklistCommands` and `scoutCommands`, `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`'s `startCommands`, `src/ui/PlanConfirmation.tsx`'s `planCommands`; tutti-1und): each bound its keys in a `useKeyboard` chain and named them in a hint written apart. Now each field's hint is drawn under it from the list it binds, on the forms' own keymap, which needs none of the run's input layers; a key whose command isn't there now goes on to the focused field. `test/form-commands.test.tsx` covers it.
- ui no longer imports print (§ 7.1, § 7.11; `src/ui/initTitle.ts`, `src/ui/spinner.ts`; tutti-xvv1): init's form took its title, its about line and the scout's clock from `src/print/initSummary.ts`, and the dashboard's spinner its frames from `src/print/organLine.ts`, while print imports ui. Now init's title and the spinner live in ui and print takes them from there, never the other way. `test/ui-imports-no-print.test.ts` holds it.
- docs/analytics.md answers more questions (tutti-srst): throughput and bottlenecks (tickets merged in each hour of a run; the slots busy, and the minutes tickets queued while every slot was, which say whether more workers would have helped; where the tickets' time went by phase; the time held back by its reason, `overlap`, `solo`, `merge` or `parent`); quality (hand-backs and rechecks by ticket, `FLAKY` warnings by test, check durations by suite and run, the checks that timed out); waiting on you (the wait for an answer by request type, median and longest; questions from asked to answered across runs; the tool calls Claude Code denied by tool and reason); how workers work, from their transcripts in `.tutti/run/workers/` through a new `transcripts(path)` macro (the tool mix by ticket, the Bash calls that edit files beside Edit and Write, the minutes from dispatch to the first edit, each verifier run's verdict and report); the backlog, the events joined with bd's export `.beads/issues.jsonl` (cost, tokens and minutes to merge by issue type, priority and label; lead time from filing to merge); and trends (cost and tokens per merged ticket by run, in one project and across `~/Projects`; each run's first, highest and last usage-window figures). The `events` macro reads `concurrency`, `reason_type`, `reason`, `window` and `utilization` besides, and the section on requests is now Waiting on you. `test/analytics.test.ts` runs each query against the fixture, which gains held-back tickets, `FLAKY` warnings, denials, an elicitation, usage windows and a timed-out recheck, three transcripts and a bd export; `test/run-figures.test.ts`'s lines of the fixture's runs follow it.
- Each screen's keys are one list of commands, from which its hint, its key help and its bindings are all drawn (§ 7.3; `src/ui/keyCommands.ts`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `dashboardCommands` and `dashboardHint`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `workerViewCommands`, `src/ui/InterviewView.tsx`'s `interviewCommands`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `transcriptCommands`, `src/ui/Modal.tsx`; tutti-rvzn.8): each screen wrote its keys three times, as the hint (`RunView.hint`), the key help's rows and the bindings, and they drifted: the dashboard's key help lacked `q` and `Ctrl+Z` (tutti-jqf5), and a box named its keys apart from binding them. Now a command is its keys, what it does, whether it is there now, and its hint and help row; the quit box, the stop question, the interrupt and leave questions, the reason box and the answer form draw the list they bind. `RunView.hint` and `drawnHint` are gone: the dashboard's hint names `d` and `a` where they answer the highlighted card's request, drawn with its buttons, and `q` works wherever the hint names it, below the minimum size too. Every key help ends with `Ctrl+C`, `Ctrl+Z` and a drag. `test/key-commands.test.tsx` covers it, with a property that every key a screen binds has a help row.
- The worker view shows what the worker is doing: the ticket's description under its head, and the whole ticket a key or a click away (§ 7.5, § 7.3; `src/ui/TicketHead.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `viewLayout`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `ticketText`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `issue` and `readIssue`, `UiCommand.ReadTicket`; tutti-cxgo.1, D1 of the worker-view design): the head named the ticket by its title only. Now line 1 has the ticket's type and priority (`bug · P2`) and its title in bold; under it, the first 2 lines of its description (3 on a pane of 40 rows, none at the minimum size), wrapped at words in the normal text colour with its inline Markdown shown, cut with `…`, and `▾ whole ticket  t` at their end when there is more. `t` or a click opens the whole ticket in their place, a frame up to 60% of the pane drawn with `<markdown>` (description, design, acceptance criteria as a checklist, notes, links, labels) that PgUp/PgDn and the wheel scroll, `↓ N more lines` at its foot; `t`, `▴ less` or `Esc` closes it, Esc before it leaves the view. The run keeps the issue it read on the ticket's ledger entry, sanitised as it enters RunView, and reads it again with bd show as the panel opens, so notes added during the run show. A departure from orchestra, on purpose. `test/worker-view-ticket.test.tsx` covers it, at 80×24, 120×40 and 60×16.
- The worker view shows what the worker is doing: the ticket's description under its head, and the whole ticket a key or a click away (§ 7.5, § 7.3; `src/ui/TicketHead.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `viewLayout`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `ticketText`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `issue` and `readIssue`, `UiCommand.ReadTicket`; tutti-cxgo.1, D1 of the worker-view design): the head named the ticket by its title only. Now line 1 has the ticket's type and priority (`bug · P2`) and its title in bold; under it, the first 2 lines of its description (3 on a pane of 40 rows, none at the minimum size), wrapped at words in the normal text colour with its inline Markdown shown, cut with `…`, and `▾ whole ticket  t` at their end when there is more. `t` or a click opens the whole ticket in their place, a frame up to 60% of the pane drawn with `<markdown>` (description, design, acceptance criteria as a checklist, notes, links, labels) that PgUp/PgDn and the wheel scroll, `↓ N more lines` at its foot; `t`, `▴ less` or `Esc` closes it, Esc before it leaves the view. The run keeps the issue it read on the ticket's ledger entry, sanitised as it enters RunView, and reads it again with bd show as the panel opens, so notes added during the run show, with the tickets it blocks (`Beads.show`'s `dependents`, `--include-dependents`). A departure from orchestra, on purpose. `test/worker-view-ticket.test.tsx` covers it, at 80×24, 120×40 and 60×16, `test/read-ticket-run.test.ts` the run's side and `test/beads-dependents.test.ts` the dependents.
- The worker view shows what the worker is doing: the ticket's description under its head, and the whole ticket a key or a click away (§ 7.5, § 7.3; `src/ui/TicketHead.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `viewLayout`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `ticketText`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `issue` and `readIssue`, `UiCommand.ReadTicket`; tutti-cxgo.1, D1 of the worker-view design): the head named the ticket by its title only. Now line 1 has the ticket's type and priority (`bug · P2`) and its title in bold; under it, the first 2 lines of its description (3 on a pane of 40 rows, none at the minimum size), wrapped at words in the normal text colour with its inline Markdown shown, cut with `…`, and `▾ whole ticket  t` at their end when there is more. `t` or a click opens the whole ticket in their place, a frame up to 60% of the pane drawn with `<markdown>` (description, design, acceptance criteria as a checklist, notes, links, labels) that PgUp/PgDn and the wheel scroll, `↓ N more lines` at its foot; `t`, `▴ less` or `Esc` closes it, Esc before it leaves the view. The run keeps the issue it read on the ticket's ledger entry, sanitised as it enters RunView, and reads it again with bd show as the panel opens, so notes added during the run show, with the tickets it blocks (`Beads.show`'s `dependents`, `--include-dependents`; bd ready's `dependent_count` counts them for the control before then). A departure from orchestra, on purpose. `test/worker-view-ticket.test.tsx` covers it, at 80×24, 120×40 and 60×16, `test/read-ticket-run.test.ts` the run's side and `test/beads-dependents.test.ts` the dependents.
- The terminals tutti knows are one table, read once (§ 7.7, § 7.9; `src/services/Tty.ts`'s `terminals`, `src/services/Notifier.ts`'s `routeOf`; tutti-rvzn.11): the Notifier had its own list of the terminals that show OSC 9 or 777 and Tty one of those that draw RGB, and the Notifier read `TMUX` and sshd's variables itself. Now each row of `terminals` says how a terminal is recognised, its sequence and whether it draws RGB, the Tty gains `osc`, `tmux` and `overSsh`, and `routeOf` takes them from it. `TERM=foot-extra` (and any `foot-…`) is now truecolor, as plain `foot` was, rather than fewer than 256 colours. `test/tty.test.ts` covers the table, with each row recognised by each of its own names.
- The clipboard and the URL opener take whether tutti runs over ssh from the Tty (§ 7.3, § 4.5; `src/ui/clipboard.ts`'s `copierOf`, `src/ui/openUrl.ts`'s `openerOf`; tutti-7f4l), as the Notifier does, rather than reading sshd's variables themselves: their `Where` gains `overSsh`, and `liveClipboard` and `liveUrlOpener` are given `tty.overSsh`.
- Git's operations are one table, `gitOps`, that the live and test layers both read (§ 3.3; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `gitOps`, `gitCall` and `GitOp`, `src/testing/Git.ts`; tutti-rvzn.12): each operation's name and whether it reads or writes were written twice, once in each layer, and a test faulted an operation by a free string, so a typo in `fail("merge --ff")` or a read in one layer that was a write in the other went unnoticed. Now each operation's name, kind (a write takes GitWriter and a write's time limit) and arguments come from `gitOps`, `Repository.fail` takes a `GitOp`, so either mistake is a type error, the rebase's read of its conflicted files is in the table too, and `rebaseInProgress` asks for both `rebase-merge` and `rebase-apply` in one `git rev-parse` where it ran two, as the test layer already counted it. `test/git-memory-commands.test.ts` pins each operation's command line, and `test/git-rebase-in-progress.test.ts` covers the one rev-parse.
- A streamed token redraws the one transcript entry it grows, not the transcript (§ 7.5; `src/ui/transcriptItems.ts`'s `withLive` and `keptEntries`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `useTranscriptWindow`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `EntryList`, `src/run/same.ts`; tutti-rvzn.2): while a worker streamed with its view open, each token worked out every entry again from up to 600 items, each text sanitised anew, and every entry was a new object, so each one mounted was drawn again; each also got the selection and the expanded set, so an arrow key or a toggle drew them all. Now the items' entries are worked out as the items change and each is kept by identity while it is the same, the partial messages laid over them along their Agent call's path; each entry gets the selection only when it is in it, and the expanded set only when it nests. A token draws its entry (and the Agent call it is under), an arrow key the two whose selection changed. `bounded` counts each entry once. `test/transcript-entry-identity.test.tsx` covers it, with properties that the laid-over entries are `entriesOf`'s.
- Each SDK message of a worker's session is read once, and whether a send is answered is the worker's state's to say (§ 4.2; `src/agent/reading.ts`, `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s `step`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `emit` and `answered`, `src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `readRateLimit`, `turnLimitStep` and `report`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s nudge, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`; tutti-rvzn.3): a message was decoded with a Schema to fold it, then again to say whether it could be read; a `rate_limit_event`'s info three times; an assistant message's content walked twice; and each fold made an object of eight closures. A send's answer was read twice too, from the worker's outstanding sends and from its messages by `answers`, for which every message, each streamed token among them, was published to a per-worker PubSub that the nudge and the hand-back subscribed to before their send. Now `read` takes each message once into a `Reading`, by a table keyed on its type and subtype, with no Schema (a `stream_event` is never looked into, an assistant message's blocks walked by one loop), and the host's fold, the denials' calls, UsageLimits and Spending take that reading (a result it can't read still goes to Spending, and one with a `startup_failure_reason` is read whatever its other fields); the reducer's handlers are tables made once. `AgentHost.answered(ticket, uuid)` waits on the worker's state until the send is no longer outstanding, its session ends or the worker is ended; `AgentHost.messages`, `subscribe`, `answers`, `untilAnswered` and the PubSub are gone. A resume between the send and the wait leaves nothing outstanding, so the wait ends at once. `test/answered.test.ts` covers it, and `test/unreadable-messages.test.ts` has `read`'s properties.
- The run's status publishes only what changes, and the dashboard's view sanitises each ticket's ID and title once (§ 3.5; `src/run/runView.ts`'s `showOn` and `project`, `src/run/same.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/machineGate.ts`, `src/run/quitting.ts`, `src/run/holdsGate.ts`; tutti-rvzn.4): SubscriptionRef publishes every set, changed or not, so the usage windows, the keep-awake badge, the machine's other runs, the verdicts, the checks ahead, the probe and the load published values that hadn't changed, and each publish projected the whole view, every ticket's ID and title sanitised anew, Done ones included. Now each status writer goes through `showOn`, which leaves the status alone when its key's value is equal (the verdicts and checks ahead, Maps made anew for each change, by identity), and `project` keeps each ledger entry's sanitised texts by the entry. `same` compared objects by their own keys, so any two Maps or Sets were the same; it now compares their entries. `test/status-publish.test.ts` covers it.
- A merge reads each git value once and passes it on, and its independent reads run side by side (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `unchanged`, `setAsideDirty`, `prepare`, `closeEpics` and `gitReads`, `src/run/checkWrote.ts`'s `noteWritten`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `Checked`, `src/run/setup.ts`'s `setUp`, `src/services/Worktrees.ts`'s `remove`, `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `recheckDue`; tutti-rvzn.6): about a third of a merge's git reads repeated one made a few lines earlier. The worktree's status was read twice before the check and twice after, the branch's head three times in a row, a whole pass made again after the check passed only to find nothing moved, the worktrees pruned after every removal, the ticket shown twice to bd, and the base read once per ticket waiting for a recheck; `Effect.all` ran each pair of independent reads one after the other. Now the status the look for a change read steers the merge, the check's own read after it says what it left, the head read once goes to the setup and check, a pass after the check passed reads the base and the branch side by side and makes the whole pass again only when the base moved, `remove` takes the worktree's path and prunes only when no worktree has the branch, `closeEpics` takes the ticket bd showed, and the reads that don't depend on each other run at once. A merge on top of the base makes 19 git calls where it made 25, and one bd show where it made 2. The log's lines are as they were. `test/merge-reads.test.ts` and `test/worktrees-remove.test.ts` cover it.
- After a merge, the base and the worktree's status are read no more than they need be (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/setup.ts`'s `takeSettings` and `setUp`, `src/run/handMerged.ts`'s `mergedByHand`, `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `recheckDue`, `src/run/checkWrote.ts`'s `noteWritten`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `checkOnce`; tutti-c4we): the base was read again for the settings file and for the look for tickets merged by hand, though the fast-forward had just put it at the commit checked, and the worktree's status read for a change of its own was read once more as the setup's or check's before-status. Now the merged commit goes to `takeSettings`, `mergedByHand` and `recheckDue`, and the status read goes to the setup's `noteWritten`, and the status after the setup, or before it when none ran, to the check's. A merge on top of the base makes 16 git calls where it made 19, one rebased 21 where it made 24. `test/merge-reads.test.ts` and `test/note-written-before.test.ts` cover it.
- A RunView change draws only what reads the part that changed, and a tick only the lines with a time or a spinner (§ 3.5, § 7.2; `src/ui/useRunView.ts`'s `useRunViewPart`, `ticker`, `useNow`, `useSpinner` and `useEvent`, `src/ui/Card.tsx`'s `CardBox` and `CardHead`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `Line`; tutti-rvzn.1): every component reading the run's view was drawn anew at each change, up to 30 a second, the worker view at another worker's card or the load changing and the Answer box while closed; each card was worked out again at each change and each spinner tick; and every card ran its own two intervals, 16 cards 32 timers, their spinners out of step. Now a component selects the part of the view it reads (a worker's view its card, its panel its card's requests, the closed Answer box nothing); a card is memoised and only its head ticks; `Line` is memoised; and the shell has one ticker for the clock and one for the spinner, running while anything draws them, so every spinner turns in step. The spinner's frames are the printer's. `test/ui-redraw-parts.test.tsx` covers it.
- A permission request opened or answered no longer has the run read bd and git (§ 5; `src/run/wakes.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `fill` and `nothingLeft`, `src/run/blockedWatch.ts`; tutti-rvzn.5): one wake woke the run for everything, the ready poll, a merge, a hold, an attention change, a command, a triage verdict, a drain request, and every wake ran the whole look: bd ready, bd list twice and git rev-parse in the fill, a bd show per ticket waiting on its blockers or asked, git per running worktree. A request opened then answered cost two of them, though an attention change can't change bd. Now each wake records its reason, and a look woken by attention changes alone builds what the end's decision reads from the run's state, with no bd or git; any other reason, or none recorded, still runs the whole look. Debug logging says what each look was woken for. `test/run-wakes.test.ts` covers it, with a merge still starting the ticket waiting on it at once.
- One text model for the dashboard and what is printed outside it, and each look made once per theme (§ 7.1, § 7.11; `src/ui/theme.ts`'s `look`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `Look`, `paint` and `fit`, `src/ui/TicketsTable.tsx`'s `ticketsLines` and `rowLooks`, `src/print/summary.ts`, `src/print/initSummary.ts`, `src/ui/questions.ts`'s `planLines`, `src/cli/colours.ts`; tutti-rvzn.14): the dashboard's segments, the plan's own segments, init's painted strings and the summary's strings were four ways to write styled text, and `tutti colours` rebuilt ANSI from OpenTUI colours as a fifth; the summary drew its ticket rows again, column sums included, and ui and print imported each other. Each frame and mark asked for a new look, parsing its hex colour, a dozen times per card and per tick. Now a look is a style of the theme's, `theme.look(role, weight)` made once per theme, so the same role gives the same look and a line's runs of it are one; a line is drawn by the renderer or printed with `paint`; the summary prints the dashboard's tickets table itself, every row and no blank one, so a ticket set aside shows triage's verdict there too; and print takes `fit`, `runTime` and `rowLooks` from ui, never the other way. init's step labels are no longer bold where its output isn't a terminal. `test/text-model.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view has a resource line under its head: what the worker consumes (§ 7.5, § 7.11; `src/ui/ResourceLine.tsx`, `src/run/resources.ts`, `src/agent/contextUsage.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `measure`, `src/run/spending.ts`'s `sums`, `src/run/usageWindows.ts`'s `riseStep`, `RunView.resources`; tutti-cxgo.2). `6m53s  1.2M tokens  5h window ≈1% (estimate)  context ▰▰▰▰▰▰▱▱▱▱  126k of 200k  0.8 GB`: the worker's time on the ticket, its tokens, on an API key its dollars, and on a subscription login, where the SDK's dollars are an API-price estimate, its ticket's estimated share of the account's five-hour window in `estimate` italics with its word, made from the window's rise over the run shared by each worker's dollars at API prices; its context window as amounts against the window Claude Code compacts at, yellow from 70% and red from 90%, which the host asks each session for after each finished turn (`getContextUsage`); its compactions once there has been one; its memory; and near a usage limit, `⚠ 5h window 92% · resets 14:20`. A narrow pane drops the memory, then the reset time, then the compactions, never a gauge's figure nor the estimate's word; a short pane drops the line before the request panel's lines. A click on it opens **Spent**: tokens by model with the cache's share, model calls, dollars, how the window's share is made, the context's split and the ticket's share of the run's tokens, in a box until the whole-ticket panel (tutti-cxgo.1) takes it. A departure from orchestra's worker view, on purpose. `test/resource-line.test.tsx`, `test/resources.test.ts` and `test/agent-host-live-context.test.ts` cover it.

## [0.2.1] - 2026-10-24

- The title pill reads ` Tutti `, capitalised as orchestra's reads `Orchestra`, on the dashboard's title line, in the run's summary and on init's title (§ 7.2, § 7.11; `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`, `src/print/summary.ts`, `src/print/initSummary.ts`; tutti-y2wf). The command, `tutti --version` and the log stay lower case.
- tutti's time limits no longer count the time the machine sleeps, and the run says when it slept (§ 3.3 "Time awake", § 5 item 15, § 8.2, § 10; `src/services/Wake.ts`, `src/run/slept.ts`; tutti-dhyy): in mnemos's run of 2026-10-23, without `--keep-awake`, macOS slept 33 minutes across a merge check that had run a minute, and at the wake the check failed `did not finish within 30m`, setting its ticket aside, and bd calls failed `took longer than 30s`, sending two tickets to Waiting, as every timer counted wall time. Now a command's limit (checks, setup, bd, git), a ticket's (`--ticket-limit`, `LONG_RUNNING`), the idle grace, a hand-back's, an organ's, a new session's first turn and the environment probe count the time awake: the run's `Wake` reads the wall clock every second and takes a gap of more than 15 s for a sleep, and a limit that expires across one is put off by it. Each sleep has a log line and a `slept` record, `SLEPT: the machine slept 34m (14:03-14:37); checks and limits don't count it`, the first in a run without `--keep-awake` with `run with --keep-awake to keep the machine awake` under it, and the run's figures' Time line ends `machine asleep 34m19s`. The usage limit's resets stay wall-clock times. `test/machine-sleep.test.ts` covers it.
- tutti's time limits no longer count the time the machine sleeps, and the run says when it slept (§ 3.3 "Time awake", § 5 item 15, § 8.2, § 10; `src/services/Wake.ts`, `src/run/slept.ts`; tutti-dhyy): in mnemos's run of 2026-10-23, without `--keep-awake`, macOS slept 33 minutes across a merge check that had run a minute, and at the wake the check failed `did not finish within 30m`, setting its ticket aside, and bd calls failed `took longer than 30s`, sending two tickets to Waiting, as every timer counted wall time. Now a command's limit (checks, setup, bd, git), a ticket's (`--ticket-limit`, `LONG_RUNNING`), the idle grace, a hand-back's, an organ's, a new session's start and first turn, an interrupt's answer and the environment probe count the time awake: the run's `Wake` reads the wall clock every second and takes a gap of more than 15 s for a sleep, and a limit that expires across one is put off by it. Each sleep has a log line and a `slept` record, `SLEPT: the machine slept 34m (14:03-14:37); checks and limits don't count it`, the first in a run without `--keep-awake` with `run with --keep-awake to keep the machine awake` under it, and the run's figures' Time line ends `machine asleep 34m19s`. The usage limit's resets stay wall-clock times. `test/machine-sleep.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's totals strip gives In queue the room Set aside's triage note took from it (§ 7.2; `src/ui/Totals.tsx`'s `totalsStrip`; tutti-iolm): the first three cells lined up at the widest's, so `↷ 1 Set aside · ◆ 1 triaged`, 27 columns, made Completed and Needs you 25 wide at 120 columns and left the queue only its own 30. Now the note doesn't set the others' width: Set aside goes past them, they keep a fifth, 21, and the queue gets 38. `test/ui-totals-set-aside-width.test.tsx` covers it.
- The title line names the account's windows in Claude Desktop's words (§ 7.2; `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`'s `windowLabel`; tutti-5170): `5h ▰▰▱▱▱▱▱▱▱▱  17% · week 41%` was cryptic. Now it is `Current usage ▰▰▱▱▱▱▱▱▱▱  17% · This week 41%`, a model's weekly window `This week (Opus)`, and a narrow pane's `This week 41% · Current usage 17%`, then `This week 41%`. `test/usage-windows.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's title line shows ` keep awake `, a warm amber pill after the version, while the run's `--keep-awake` command holds the machine awake (§ 7.2, § 7.11; `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`, `src/run/keepAwake.ts`'s `holding`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `keepingAwake`, `TitleView.keepAwake`; tutti-7k79): from the run's start, gone should the command end by itself, and never where the system has none or it isn't on the PATH. A narrow pane gives it up first. Its colours are two new roles, `keepAwake` (`#B45309` / `#F59E0B`, a fill) and `keepAwakeText` (`#FFFBEB` / `#1C1917`), which a scheme may change like any other. `test/keep-awake-badge.test.ts` covers it.
- A setting for the files whose overlap holds no two tickets apart, `queue.shared_files` (§ 5 item 5, § 8.4; `src/run/repoFiles.ts`'s `shared`, `src/run/overlap.ts`, `src/init/checks.ts`'s `findFeaturesMap`; tutti-fg1e): a file nearly every ticket edits, as the features map create-check-suite writes, held tickets apart all run long though its conflicts are cheap to resolve (mnemos's run of 2026-10-23 held tickets back 519 minutes for overlap, most of it over `test/FEATURES.md`), and union merging, which footprints already leave out, garbles a file edited in place. Now the paths and globs it lists stay in the footprints but hold no tickets apart, nor link them in `tutti plan`; their `LIKELY_CONFLICT` warning stays, and a rebase conflict in one is handed back. `tutti init` lists the features map (`test/FEATURES.md`, or where the project has it) as it files the test work. `test/shared-files.test.ts` covers it.
- The run's summary has the run's figures under its totals (§ 7.1, § 5 item 14; `src/run/figures.ts`, `src/print/summary.ts`, `src/run/endOfRun.ts`; tutti-yj8x): where the tickets' time went, by phase (`63% queued · 19% held back (overlap 15%) · 14% working · 2% merging`), each suite's checks with the tickets that didn't pass and the longest one's time, the workers' tokens with the cache's share, their dollars and a ticket's median, and the account's usage windows from the run's start to its end. The EventStream adds up each record as it writes it, so the figures are those docs/analytics.md's queries give over the same run, without DuckDB. On a subscription login the dollars are the API-price estimate, `(estimate)` in the new `estimate` role's colour, sand (`#8A6D3B` light, `#D4B483` dark), in italics; on an API key they are plain and there is no Usage line. A line with nothing to say is left out, a run that took no ticket has none, and `--plain` writes the same lines. `test/run-figures.test.ts` covers it, against the analytics fixture with the machine's duckdb.
- `tutti init --update` says a project's own `.claude/agents/verifier.md` is its own (§ 8.3; `src/init/update.ts`'s `planUpdate` and `updateNext`, `src/init/templates.ts`'s `ownVerifier`; tutti-eluw): it said such a verifier `has every change to its template` and recorded the verifier template's version in `.tutti/settings.json` for it, as it only checked the file was there. Now it says the file is the project's own subagent, kept as it is, records no verifier version (dropping one the settings had), and its `Nothing to commit` line leaves the verifier out. `test/verifier-own-update.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti init` and `tutti init --update` keep `.tutti/settings.json`'s own formatting where they change a key (§ 8.3; `src/project/jsonText.ts`'s `setIn`, `src/init/init.ts`'s `writeSettings`, `src/init/update.ts`; tutti-lx3f): each rewrote the whole file with `JSON.stringify` whenever it changed a key, so a change to the concurrency, a check's time limit or the templates' versions lost the project's indentation and one-line sections. Now each edits the keys it changes in the file's text: a value replaced keeps the layout of the one it replaces, a key added goes after its object's last one, laid out as that object is, and the rest of the file, line endings included, is as it was; a new file is still written whole. `test/settings-formatting.test.ts` covers it, with a property that the text holds what the object would.
- `tutti init --prefix` reads the last run's state.json through the symlink guard (§ 9.1; `src/init/workers.ts`'s `prefixRefusal`, `src/services/Store.ts`'s `readStateText`; tutti-wv5s): it read it with `FileSystem.readFileString`, which followed a link a merged ticket can leave at `.tutti/run` or `state.json` to tell whether the last run left workers. A link there is now a reason of its own not to rename the tickets, naming the link and quoting nothing behind it.
- The Git test layer's failures name the command the live layer runs (§ 3.3; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `gitArgs` and `resetTo`, `src/testing/Git.ts`; tutti-axpo.12): it built each command's arguments by hand, without `--end-of-options` and `--no-update-refs`, so a GitFailed's command differed from live; its `resetBranch` reset to the rev without the `rev-parse` the live one runs first, so a `rev-parse` fault never reached it and an unknown rev failed as `reset --hard`; and `mergedByUnion` of no paths recorded a `check-attr` call, which a scripted fault could fail, where live runs no git. Now both layers take each command's arguments from `gitArgs` and reset through `resetTo`, and the test layer's `mergedByUnion` of no paths runs nothing. `test/git-memory-commands.test.ts` covers it, against the live layer's own failures.
- Six small test layers go through the live code (§ 3.3; `src/testing/AppConfig.ts`, `Log.ts`, `RunLock.ts`, `Transcripts.ts`, `Tty.ts`, `Worktrees.ts`; tutti-axpo.14): `appConfig({ repo })` left the prompt and worktrees under `/work/project` and overridden settings skipped `resolve`, now both go through it; the Log test layer was a copy of the live log, now `Log.layerOn` over a file that keeps nothing; the RunLock test layer kept holders as objects, now the texts `tuttiLockText` and `orchestraLockText` write, read back with `parseHolder`; the Transcripts test layer skipped the worker ID check, now in `makeTranscripts` for both (a `refused` the Storage is told of, which live warns of); `tty({ dumb: true })` kept xterm-256color and 256 colours, now `tty({ env })` goes through `fromEnvironment`; the Worktrees test layer took reused and fresh from the folder alone, now both layers share `found`, with `branchesLeft` for a branch left without its worktree. `test/shared-test-layers.test.ts` covers it.
- A merged worktree whose only changes are untracked files tutti's setup or check wrote is removed with them (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/checkWrote.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `removeMerged`; tutti-ubv4): a coverage report or build output the project doesn't git-ignore didn't set the ticket aside, but `git worktree remove` refused the worktree after the merge, `CLEANUP_FAILED`, the worktree and branch left. Now each setup and merge check notes the untracked files it added in the worktree's `.tutti/run/check-wrote`, and when they are its only changes the worktree is removed with `--force`, a line naming them; one that was there before keeps it. And a branch on top of the base with no check command no longer merges without its worktree's status read: uncommitted changes there set it aside, `DIRTY_WORKTREE`, rather than end `CLEANUP_FAILED`. `test/check-wrote.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's totals strip keeps its frame in an 80-column pane with triage verdicts in (§ 7.2; `src/ui/Totals.tsx`'s `totalsStrip`; tutti-rm9o): `↷ 1 Set aside · ◆ 1 triaged` took 27 columns, so the spec's illustrated state needed 94 and an 80- or 90-column pane fell back to the one-line totals. Now, where the strip doesn't fit, Set aside's note is cut to ` · ◆ 1`, then left out, before the totals go on one line; the Tickets table's ◆ rows still say it. `test/ui-totals-triage.test.tsx` covers it.
- The Organs and UsageProbe test layers run the live code (§ 3.3; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`'s `make`, `src/agent/UsageProbe.ts`'s `probeWith`, `src/testing/Organs.ts`, `src/testing/UsageProbe.ts`; tutti-axpo.8): each replaced the whole service, so tests never saw an organ call's or a probe's `ORGAN` cost line, a canned AiError had to carry by hand the metadata Organs reads (a QuotaExhaustedError without it read as no limit, silently), and a scripted probe had no time limit. Now the live code is split from its session, the one part that runs Claude Code, and the test layers swap only that: canned answers and scripted probe answers are played as SDK messages (`messages`, `limited`, `turnOf`), mapped to answers and errors, timed out and costed as live ones; each test layer provides the `OrganCost` its calls go to, over the test's Log. `test/organ-probe-test-layers.test.ts` covers it, with properties that the test and live layers end the same turn the same.
- A start Claude Code refuses no longer says its startup failure reason twice (§ 4.8; `src/run/start.ts`'s `notStarted`; tutti-axpo.9): the live host words the failure `Claude Code refused to start: cwd_unavailable`, and the run added the reason again, so `START_FAILED` read `… refused to start: cwd_unavailable (cwd_unavailable)`. Now the reason is added only where the failure doesn't say it. The scripted AgentHost, which said the failure as its script gave it, words it as the live host does (`src/agent/sessionChecks.ts`'s `startFailure`), and `test/start.test.ts` covers it.
- The scripted AgentHost reads a session's start and messages with the live host's code (§ 13; `src/agent/sessionChecks.ts`, `src/testing/AgentHost.ts`; tutti-axpo.9): it skipped the checks of a session's initialize answer and first system/init, took the CLI's `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` from an option, not from what the session lists, didn't end a worker on a `startup_failure_reason`, died on a message it couldn't take in and called hooks past the worker's callbacks. Now both layers share those checks, their log lines and the start's wording, a script gives the initialize answer with the new `initialize` step, and hooks go through `activityHooks`. `test/scripted-start-checks.test.ts` covers it.
- A `.tutti/run/events.jsonl` an earlier run left cut short no longer swallows this run's first record (§ 10; `src/run/eventstream.ts`; tutti-o0o9): a file ending without its newline, from a process killed mid-write, had the new run's first record appended to its last line, so neither decoded. Now the stream reads the file's last byte when it opens it and, where that isn't a newline, writes one ahead of the first record. `test/eventstream-torn-tail.test.ts` covers it.
- Biome fails a raw bidi control, C1 control or line or paragraph separator in the source (§ 13; `biome.json`, `test/lint/raw-controls.grit`; tutti-hgdi): `biome check --write` was suspected of turning a test's `\u` escapes of them into the raw characters, invisible in review and a Trojan Source hazard, but Biome keeps escapes; an agent's Write and Edit decode them in their input. Twelve test files held raw ones, now escaped. A GritQL rule flags each string, template, regex, JSX text or key that holds one, and a file with one in a comment, matching the file first so that clean files cost nothing; a test scans `src/`, `test/`, `scripts/`, `skills/` and `bin/` for the comments before a file's first code or after its last, which the rule can't see. `test/raw-controls-lint.test.ts` covers it.
- A branch, or base, that moves on before every fast-forward no longer loops a merge without end (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/mergequeue.ts`'s `merge`; tutti-5mmd): each Moved or BaseMoved from the fast-forward prepared the ticket again, and each prepare counted its passes from nothing, so commits made by hand between each check and its fast-forward ran a check each time, without a limit. Now the merge queue prepares it again up to 3 times (`maxPasses`), then blocks it, `MERGE_CONFLICT: <id> closed, but <branch or base> kept changing while its checks ran`. `test/merge-reprepares.test.ts` covers it.
- A solo ticket checked once more, or past its work without its issue, holds back the others while it rebases and merges (§ 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `withIssues`; tutti-wwkj): the scheduler set the solo ticket only from an issue a slot was taken with, and a recheck's slot, or a ticket carried over closed, takes none, so the issue the overlap check read from bd filled in its footprint but not its solo label, and the others dispatched beside it. Now a fill with a slot free and a ticket in line reads the issue of each running or merging ticket that has none, footprints on or off, and its solo label holds the others back as a dispatched solo ticket's does. `test/schedule-solo-recheck.test.ts` covers it.
- A solo ticket checked once more, or past its work without its issue, holds back the others while it rebases and merges (§ 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `withIssues` and `recheckSlot`; tutti-wwkj): the scheduler set the solo ticket only from an issue a slot was taken with, and a recheck's slot, or a ticket carried over closed, takes none, so the issue the overlap check read from bd filled in its footprint but not its solo label, and the others dispatched beside it. Now a recheck's slot is taken with the ticket's issue read from bd, and a fill with a slot free, a carried-over worker and a recheck read the issue of each running or merging ticket that has none, footprints on or off, so its solo label holds back the tickets in line, a carried worker and the next recheck, as a dispatched solo ticket's does. `test/schedule-solo-recheck.test.ts` covers it.
- The Checks test layer chooses each check's command and time limit from AppConfig as the live layer does (§ 3.3; `src/services/Checks.ts`'s `checksOf` and `resultOf`, `src/testing/Checks.ts`; tutti-axpo.7): it played its script for every check, so a project naming no merge check, or a setup given a null command, passed in tests where a run gives `NoCheckCommand`, and the merge went Checked where a run's goes Unchecked. A script may give a command's exit code and output, read as the live layer reads them; the tests' configuration names `scripts/check-fast.sh` as its merge check.
- The `AGENT_FAILED` stop is gone (§ 5 item 10; `src/run/run.ts`'s `errorStop`, `src/run/holds.ts`; tutti-0k6f): nothing raised it, as a ticket's fiber never fails with an Agent SDK error, a session that doesn't start being `START_FAILED`, which a row of holds the run as `ENVIRONMENT` (exit 7), and one that can't be resumed `WORKER_GONE`. Exit 4 now covers Beads and git, a worktree, the run's state and a panic, as the spec, `docs/running.md`, `docs/events.md` and the tutti skill say. `test/run.test.ts` and `test/exit-meanings.test.ts` cover it.
- The start question's description gets the keys that arrive in the read whose Enter chose New feature (§ 7.1; `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`; tutti-q7pb): the textarea was mounted only once React drew the description step, so `abc` pasted after Down and Enter (`\x1b[B\rabc`) was dropped. Now it is mounted, hidden, from the start and focused by the Enter that chooses, which it doesn't take as its own submit; Shift+Tab hides it again, keeping what was typed, and the keys after it in its read are the select's. `test/start-question-describe-one-read.test.tsx` covers it.
- state.json is read through the symlink guard as well (§ 9.1; `src/services/Store.ts`'s `readState`; tutti-9idc): it was read with `FileSystem.readFileString`, which followed a link a merged ticket can leave at `.tutti/run/state.json` or `.tutti/run`, so the run read another file and, failing to parse it, stopped with a reason that could quote it. Now it is opened below the main checkout with `openUnder`: a missing file is no state, and a link is `StoreUnreadable`, naming the link and nothing of what it points at. `test/state-read-links.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's totals strip puts each count beside its label on one line (§ 7.2, § 7.11; `src/ui/Totals.tsx`'s `totalsStrip`; tutti-v6bd): it took four lines, a row of faint labels over a row of counts in its frame, as orchestra's does. Now it takes three, `✓ 20 Completed`, `↷ 3 Set aside · ◆ 2 triaged`, `0 Needs you` faint whole and `0 of 9 In queue · 8 held back`, and the line goes to the tickets table and Current. The first three cells are as wide as each other, up to a fifth of the strip, and In queue takes the rest; a narrower pane gives up their width, down to each one's own, before the totals go on one line. The printed summary keeps its labels over counts. `test/ui-totals-strip.test.tsx` covers it.
- What a Waiting ticket waits for is a tagged union, no longer a string (§ 5.1; `src/run/phase.ts`'s `WaitFor`; tutti-axpo.2): its blockers' IDs joined with `, ` shared the string with `setup`, `probe` and `setup problem`, so a blocker of that name was taken for the setup or the probe, and the run report said `Waiting (back in the queue until setup merges)` for a ticket whose setup failed.
