Now it is `Blockers{ids}`, `Setup`, `Probe` or `SetupProblem`, the report says what each waits for, and the event stream's `why` is as before. `test/wait-for.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket boarding its merge train no longer races the trains starting, stopping and being left (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`; tutti-axpo.4): the board read and deleted the trains' shared state without the train's lock, which a train starting or the merges stopping held; the trains' state is one Ref now, its steps pure transitions, and the board takes its train under the lock. `src/run/run.ts` and `src/run/footprint.ts` are split into modules of their own, behaviour unchanged.
- A box's keys row breaks onto more rows in a narrow pane (§ 7.3; `src/ui/Modal.tsx`'s `choiceRows`; tutti-q55k): it was neither cut nor wrapped, so below about 54 columns the quit box's `s stop after current · q quit now · Esc cancel` ran past its frame. Now it breaks between two keys, and inside one only when it is wider than a row, as the hint does, each key still a click target on the rows it takes. `test/modal-choices.test.tsx` covers it.
- The key help fits the pane (§ 7.2, § 7.3; `src/ui/KeyHelp.tsx`; tutti-bnhb): the dashboard's was 17 lines, more than the 16-row minimum, and the worker view's 24, so its bottom, `Esc close` first, was cut off. Now a help taller than the pane leaves out its blank lines, then shows its keys a window at a time, saying which (`1–12 of 18`), with `Esc close · ↑↓ PgUp PgDn scroll` under them; `↑`/`↓`, PgUp/PgDn, `g`/`G` and the wheel scroll it. `test/key-help-fits.test.tsx` covers it.
- A filing from a finished worker's view outlives the view (§ 7.5; `src/ui/filingDesk.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`; tutti-fgr2): the view kept a Ctrl+T filing in a ref of its own, so leaving it before bd answered lost the `filed …` or `couldn't file it` toast, and with a refusal the text you typed; reopened, the view let Ctrl+T file the same text twice. Now the filings wait in a desk around the screens, which toasts how each went on whatever screen shows, and a reopened view's composer has the text again while bd files it or once bd has refused it. `test/filing-desk.test.tsx` covers it.
- The composer grows to show what you typed as it wraps, and refits when the pane is resized (§ 7.5; `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `Composer`; tutti-5bry): it counted the field's wrapped lines with `virtualLineCount`, which counts only the lines the field shows, so a long line never grew it, and it refitted only when the text changed, so a narrower pane hid the lines it wrapped onto; the field was also laid out as wide as its text, past the pane's edge. Now it counts every wrapped line, the field takes the row's width less the prompt's, and the composer refits when its width changes, up to six lines as before. `test/composer-resize.test.tsx` covers it.
- The dashboard's hint wraps every key onto as many lines as it takes, as the worker and interview views' do (§ 7.2, § 7.5; `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `dashboardLayout`; tutti-p19v): it was cut to one line, so at 60 to 75 columns with a request open, where the whole hint is 76 columns, it lost `? keys` and part of `s stop after current`. Where a request's buttons aren't drawn, the hint without `d` and `a` gives the lines it no longer needs to the blank ones above the foot. `test/dashboard-hint-wraps.test.tsx` covers it.
- `tutti init` run again files a closed test-work ticket again only where it wrote or replaced a runner (§ 8.3; `src/init/checks.ts`'s `fileTestWork`, `src/init/init.ts`; tutti-ibti): it skipped only a ticket still open, so every init run again with *use them as they are* filed a new P1 solo `Make scripts/check-fast.sh and scripts/check-full.sh pass on <base>` once the last was closed, holding the next run for runners the closed ticket had already seen to. tutti departs from orchestra here on purpose. A ticket still open is never filed again, as before. `test/init-test-work-again.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti answer --json` escapes the C1 controls, DEL, the line and paragraph separators and the bidi overrides and isolates in the requests it prints (§ 4.5; `src/answer/answer.ts`'s `showJson`; tutti-rq4j): it printed them raw, as `JSON.stringify` leaves them, so a tool input or question holding one could move the cursor or reorder the text in the terminal showing it. Now they are escaped as `\uXXXX`, as in events.jsonl, which decodes to the same text. `test/answer-json-terminal-safe.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti init` imports orchestra's ticket_limit of zero written as a Go duration as no limit (§ 8.3; `src/init/import.ts`'s `converted`; tutti-51ap): only `"0"` became tutti's null, so `"0s"` or `"0m"`, which orchestra's Go `time.ParseDuration` reads as zero and so as no limit too, was left out with `tutti can't take it: … longer than 0`. Now any zero time Go reads (`"0s"`, `"0h0m"`, `"-0s"`, …) imports as `workers.ticket_limit: null`. `test/import-ticket-limit-zero.test.ts` covers it.
- Open URL pressed again while its URL opens is ignored (§ 4.5; `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`'s `useSendAnswer`; tutti-a6ng): while `open` or `xdg-open` ran, for up to 5 s, each press opened another tab and sent another Accept. Now a press on a request whose URL is still opening does nothing, and one after the open has resolved (failed, say) opens it again. `test/open-url-once.test.tsx` covers it.
- Open URL pressed again while its URL opens is ignored (§ 4.5; `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`'s `useSendAnswer`; tutti-a6ng): while `open` or `xdg-open` ran, for up to 5 s, each press opened another tab and sent another Accept. Now a press on a request whose URL is still opening does nothing, in the view it was pressed in or another (the set is the shell's UrlOpener's), and one after the open has resolved (failed, say) opens it again. `test/open-url-once.test.tsx` covers it.
- `tutti init`'s plain summary and its lines on stderr are sanitised (§ 7.10; `src/init/steps.ts`'s `stepLine`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `stderrText`; tutti-m92r): without a terminal, a step's line printed bd's stderr (a test-work ticket not filed, a ticket prefix refused) as it came, and a refusal printed a committed `.tutti/settings.json`'s unknown key as it was named, escape sequences and controls included; only the styled summary sanitised them. Now both are, a refusal keeping its lines. `test/init-plain-sanitize.test.ts` covers it.
- The run's start time in each event's `run` is local time with its offset, as each event's `time` is (§ 10; `src/main.ts`, `src/run/localTime.ts`'s `runStarted`; tutti-jxyk): it was UTC (`…Z`), so comparing `.time` and `.run` as strings in a jq recipe, or across orchestra's streams, went wrong. The run lock's and the store's `started` are the same local time. `test/run-started-local.test.ts` covers it.
- A write to `.tutti/run/events.jsonl` cut short no longer corrupts the record after it (§ 10; `src/run/eventstream.ts`'s `write`; tutti-gd4w): a short write, on ENOSPC say, left part of a record without its newline, and the next record was appended to the same line, so neither decoded. Now the newline is written at once, or, where that fails too, ahead of the next record, so only the cut record is lost. `test/eventstream-short-write.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti init` run again keeps a check whose command is set to null (§ 8.3; `src/init/init.ts`'s `writeSettings`; tutti-cglz): null is the project's choice to merge without that check, but init filled in the runner wherever the command was null, so an init run again only to change the concurrency turned the merge check back on, and rewrote the file in its own formatting. Now it fills in the runner only where the key is absent, and `--check-fast` or `--check-full` still sets it. `test/init-null-check.test.ts` covers it.
- A defect while the run report is written or saved is said as one (§ 5 item 13; `src/run/report.ts`'s `runReport` and `notSavedWhy`; tutti-a9oz): only a Fail counted, so a defect in gathering the evidence or in the reviewer, which organs don't turn into errors, printed `Run report skipped` with no log line, as a Ctrl+C does, and a defect while saving said `report not saved: interrupted`. Now only an interruption alone skips it or reads `interrupted`; a defect is `REVIEW_FAILED: <its message>`, or `report not saved: <its message>`. `test/report-defect.test.ts` covers it.
- A project's own `.claude/agents/verifier.md` is no longer taken for a copy of tutti's verifier template (§ 8.3; `src/init/templates.ts`'s `verifierFile` and `verifierOpening`; tutti-u9in): a subagent of that name that wasn't tutti's was said at every run's start to be older than tutti's verifier template, and `tutti init --update` grafted the template's sections into it. Now a verifier without the template's opening words (`You verify a change you did not write`) or its Its checks and How to verify sections is the project's own, as a create-check-suite skill without the template's section is, and takes none of its changes. `test/verifier-own.test.ts` covers it.
- An empty or relative HOME no longer puts the user config and the update check's cache under the current folder (§ 8.5; `src/project/userConfig.ts`'s `homeFolder`, `userConfigDir`, `src/update/update.ts`'s `cacheDir`; tutti-nvsh): HOME="", as some cron and service environments set it, read the config from `./.config/tutti` and wrote the cache to `./.cache/tutti`; such a HOME is now taken as unset, and the account's home folder used.
- An empty HOME no longer has tutti look for the user's `.claude.json` under the current folder (§ 4.6; `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `claudeConfigFile`, `src/project/userConfig.ts`'s `accountHome`; tutti-0654): HOME="" read `./.claude.json` for MCP servers, Claude in Chrome and the trust fix, while Claude Code, a Bun binary, takes an empty HOME as unset and uses the account's home folder. Now tutti reads the file Claude Code uses; a relative HOME is kept, as Claude Code keeps it. `test/claude-config-empty-home.test.ts` covers it.
- An external command's non-zero exit is tagged `CommandExitedNonZero`, no longer `CommandFailed` (`src/services/exec.ts`'s `run`, `src/services/Notifier.ts`; tutti-lel4): it shared its tag with a subcommand's failure (`src/cli/commands.ts`), so `src/main.ts`'s `catchTag("CommandFailed")`, there for a subcommand that has said why on stderr, would have quietly exited 1 on an exec failure that escaped. `test/error-tags.test.ts` checks that no two tagged errors in `src/` share a tag.
- Ctrl+C while `tutti init --update` puts its files in no longer leaves them half written (§ 8.3; `src/init/staged.ts`'s `commitStaged`; tutti-ep6u): init's own commit ran inside a step that runs whole, but `--update`'s could be interrupted, leaving hidden `.<name>.tutti-<pid>-<uuid>.new` and `.old` files beside the project's, which `.tutti/.gitignore` doesn't cover, and some files renamed in and others not. Now putting the files in runs to its end uninterrupted, for init and `--update` alike. `test/init-update-interrupt.test.ts` covers it, interrupting at each write the commit makes.
- The run's end closes its sessions all at once (§ 4.8; `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-dlt5): it closed them one after another, and each session's Claude Code may take up to 10 s to exit (about 2.6 s for one mid-turn, which ignores stdin's end until SIGTERM), so a run ending with N suspended workers waited N times that. Now the end waits for the slowest. `test/end-closes-at-once.test.ts` covers it, with the scripted AgentHost's new `closeTakes`.
- A worker's partial messages no longer each cost a fold of its state (§ 4.4; `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `emit`; tutti-v2vf): every `stream_event` of the live text took the folds' semaphore and set the worker's state, waking every subscriber to it, though all it can change is ending an `api_retry`. Now each is recorded and published, and folded only while an `api_retry` holds. `test/partial-messages.test.ts` covers it.
- A nudge or hand-back an interrupt cancels no longer leaves its ticket's fiber waiting until the session ends (§ 4.2, § 4.3; `src/run/settle.ts`'s `answers` and `untilAnswered`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`; tutti-ompo): the wait ended on the send's `command_lifecycle` `completed` or a result listing it, not on `cancelled`, so a nudge withdrawn by `interrupt({ cancelQueued })` or `cancel_async_message` was never answered, and the fiber never reached the idle grace or `PAUSED`; a hand-back waited out its time limit. Now `cancelled` answers it too, and so does the worker settling, as none of its sends is outstanding then, for a send only the interrupt's receipt says was cancelled. `test/nudge-cancelled.test.ts` covers it.
- The npm package's bundle built outside a git checkout shows the bare version (§ 8.1, § 12 npm; `scripts/build-npm.sh`; tutti-t9ai): it defined `TUTTI_BUILD_COMMIT` as `none`, which `tutti --version` showed as the commit, `tutti v0.2.0 none`. Now it defines no commit there, as `scripts/build.sh` doesn't. `test/build-npm-commit.test.ts` covers it.
- A relative path in `--prompt`, `--worktrees` or `--claude` is taken from the folder tutti was started in (§ 8.2; `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`'s `resolveAll`; tutti-o6pv): `--prompt` and `--worktrees` were taken from the main checkout and `--claude` from the folder tutti started in, so `cd repo/sub && tutti --prompt ./p.md` looked for `repo/p.md`, and `--prompt ../x.md` from a linked worktree looked beside the main checkout. In `TUTTI_PROMPT`, `TUTTI_WORKTREES` and `TUTTI_CLAUDE` a relative path is taken from the main checkout, as before for the first two; `TUTTI_CLAUDE`'s was taken from the folder tutti started in. `test/flag-paths.test.ts` covers it.
- A start interrupted at any point no longer leaves its ticket unable to start again (§ 4.8; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `begin`, `reserve`, `closing` and `close`; tutti-0k95): an interrupt between reserving the ticket's session and forking its start left the ticket Starting with nothing to close it, so its next start failed `a session is already open`, and one landing after a close had moved the session to Closing but before it closed left the next start waiting for that end for ever. Now reserving, forking and closing on interrupt are uninterruptible together, as is a close from its move to Closing on; the waits for a Closing session's end stay interruptible. `test/start-interrupt.test.ts` covers it, interrupting a start at each turn of its run loop.
- The machine is kept awake with `--keep-awake` (or `TUTTI_KEEP_AWAKE`), chosen for each run, rather than with `keep_awake` in `.tutti/settings.json` (§ 8.2, § 8.4; `src/cli/flags.ts`, `src/project/settings.ts`'s `retiredSettings`, `src/run/keepAwake.ts`; tutti-v0tn): the project's settings are committed, so one person's choice to keep their machine awake for a night went to everyone running tutti on the project. tutti departs from orchestra's `keep_awake` on purpose. A `keep_awake` still in the settings isn't read: the run starts all the same, its START block warning to remove it, and the published schema no longer lists it; `tutti init` leaves orchestra's `keep_awake` out of its import, saying it is `--keep-awake`. `test/keep-awake.test.ts` covers it.
- The scout's confinement checks every brace alternative of a Glob pattern (§ 6.3; `src/organs/scout.ts`'s `braceAlternatives` and `outside`; tutti-n142): it rejected only a literal `..` segment and checked the base before the first glob character, so `{..,src}/*` or `{/etc,src}/*` had the base `.` and passed. Now each alternative's base is checked from the path, and a pattern whose braces don't close, or expand past 256 alternatives, is denied. `test/scout-glob-braces.test.ts` covers it.
- With a Claude Code older than mcp_servers' source field (an older `--claude`), a worker with MCP servers says tutti can't read where they came from (§ 4.6; `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `checkInit` and `sourcelessProblem`; tutti-t9zd): a chosen server listed without a source counted as not given, so every worker ended with `workers got MCP servers they weren't given: …`, naming the servers it was given. Now it ends with `workers' MCP servers are listed without their source in Claude Code's system/init, as a Claude Code that predates the field lists them (an older --claude), so tutti can't check they are the ones it gave: …`; a server not chosen is still one workers weren't given. `test/mcp-sourceless.test.ts` covers it.
- A finished ticket's worker is let go of (§ 4.8; `src/run/sessions.ts`'s `fit`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `forget`, `src/agent/Transcripts.ts`'s `release`; tutti-7y43): a run kept every worker it ever started, with its last 200 SDK messages (tool results included), its two open transcript files, a PubSub and a fiber counting its requests, until it ended. Now a ticket merged, closed with no change, deferred, left for review or dropped has its worker ended as its session closes, and its transcript's memory and files let go of, once its worker view, if open, closes; one asked or waiting for a merge keeps its worker for the resume. `test/worker-release.test.ts` covers it.
- A finished ticket's worker is let go of (§ 4.8; `src/run/sessions.ts`'s `fit`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `forget`, `src/agent/Transcripts.ts`'s `release`; tutti-7y43): a run kept every worker it ever started, with its last 200 SDK messages (tool results included), its two open transcript files, a PubSub and a fiber counting its requests, until it ended. Now a ticket merged (by hand too, `src/run/handMerged.ts`'s `takeAsMerged`), closed with no change, deferred, left for review or dropped has its worker ended as its session closes, and its transcript's memory and files let go of, once its worker view, if open, closes; the dashboard's latest-action poll no longer reads a worker let go of back into memory. One asked or waiting for a merge keeps its worker for the resume. `test/worker-release.test.ts` covers it.
- An organ's answer from a refusal fallback is kept (§ 6.1; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`'s `outcome` and `streamText`; tutti-ddl8): a `model_refusal_fallback` failed the call as OrganRefused, though the SDK had retracted the refused attempt and retried the turn on the fallback model, whose answer the result carries. Now only `model_refusal_no_fallback` and a result's `stop_reason` `refusal` are refusals; `streamText`, which can't take back text it streamed, fails a fallback whose refused attempt had streamed text. `test/agent-sdk-language-model.test.ts` covers it.
- A card no longer keeps showing a tool call that failed or was denied (§ 4.2; `src/agent/hooks.ts`'s `activityEvents`, `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s `onHook`; tutti-kprp): only PostToolUse put `doing` back, and a failing call (a check exiting non-zero) ends with PostToolUseFailure, a denied one with PermissionDenied, neither of which tutti hooked, so the card showed `testing` while the worker read the failure, until its next tool call. Now both are hooked and folded like PostToolUse. `test/tool-failure-activity.test.ts` covers it.
- A message sent to a worker whose session is closing fails `AgentGone` instead of being reported sent (§ 4.8; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `push`, `closingWhy`; tutti-763l): the send was recorded in the transcript and counted outstanding before the inbox was offered it, and the inbox's refusal, the session closing between finding it and the push, was ignored, so the caller got a uuid for a message that never went, shown in the transcript and outstanding for good. Now a refused message is withdrawn, nothing recorded, and the send fails `AgentGone`; a resume whose session closes as it pushes fails `AgentStartFailed` as a start closed meanwhile does. `test/push-closing-inbox.test.ts` covers it.
- A resumed worker is told the requires list a merge changed, and the check, in its resume's message (§ 4.1; `src/agent/options.ts`'s `changingNotes`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `resumedNotes`; tutti-ca7y): they went only in the standing rules' `systemPrompt.append`, which a resumed session ignores where Claude Code records the system prompt (`snapshot: true`), so a worker resumed after a merge changed the requires list never heard of it, though takeRequires said it was told. Now a resume whose notes differ from those its session was started or last resumed with in the run, or one begun in an earlier run, begins with them. `test/resume-notes.test.ts` covers it.
- A check's or setup's failure whose output couldn't be kept says it is in /dev/null (§ 9.1; `src/run/check.ts`'s `checkOnce`, `src/run/setup.ts`'s `setUp` and `setUpWorktree`; tutti-fu98): with a symbolic link at `.tutti/run/check.log` or `setup.log`, the output went to /dev/null, but CHECKS_FAILED, SETUP_FAILED, the report and the fix prompt still said the whole output was in that file, which didn't hold it and could be a link a worker planted. Now they name the file the output went to. `test/unkept-output.test.ts` covers it.
- A TypeScript check that fails in the ticket's own code no longer gets its one recheck (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `ownFailures`; tutti-0pff): it knew a failing file only by Go's `a.go:10:5: ` position, so tsc's `src/a.ts(10,5): error TS2322: …` and Biome's `src/a.ts:10:5 lint/style/useConst` or `src/a.ts format` named no folder, and a ticket set aside for its own type or lint error was checked once more. Now those name their file's folder too. `test/recheck-typescript.test.ts` covers it, from real tsc and Biome output.
- A merge check, or the setup before it, that passes but leaves tracked files changed in the ticket's worktree sets the ticket aside, `DIRTY_WORKTREE` (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `setAsideLeftDirty`, read in `setUpAndCheck` and before a pass from earlier in the run merges; tutti-jq72): an install that rewrote its lockfile, or a check that formatted files, passed on files that wouldn't merge, and after the merge `git worktree remove` refused the changed worktree, `CLEANUP_FAILED`, the worktree and branch left. Now its line says `<id> closed, but its setup or check left uncommitted changes to <files> in <worktree>, so tutti/<id> passed with changes that would not merge; worktree left for review (…)`. `test/merge-left-dirty.test.ts` covers it.
- A full check, or the setup before it, that could not be run files no ticket (§ 5 item 12; `src/run/fullcheck.ts`'s `failed`; tutti-gc2b): a command that didn't start, or whose output couldn't be kept, was `FULL_CHECK_FAILED`, a `full_check` record `failed`, and filed `Full check fails: scripts/check-full.sh` for a failure in the machine, not in a suite, overwriting `check-full.log` too. Now it is `FULL_CHECK_NOT_RUN: '<command>' could not be run (<why>) on <base> at <commit>; no ticket filed: it is not a suite's failure`, a `full_check` record `not run`, and the last failing check's output stays in `check-full.log`. `test/fullcheck-not-run.test.ts` covers it.
- A moment's bd failure no longer costs a set-aside ticket its look for a hand merge or its one recheck (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/handMerged.ts`'s `mergedByHand`, `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `recheckDue`; tutti-rzr0): the look for tickets merged by hand took the base as seen before it asked bd, so one bd failed for wasn't asked of again until the base moved, not even as the run ended; and the recheck took the ticket up before asking bd, so a failure there left it set aside for good. Now a look bd failed in doesn't count, and a ticket bd can't say is closed stays waiting for the next look. `test/recheck-bd-failure.test.ts` covers it.
- A run removes the full check's worktrees a killed run left as it starts (§ 5 item 12; `src/run/fullcheck.ts`'s `removeLeftFullChecks`, `src/services/Worktrees.ts`'s `leftDetached`; tutti-rnvc): a kill -9 or crash while the full check ran skipped its cleanup, and `git worktree prune` kept the `check-full-*` worktree, with what its setup installed, for good while its folder was there. Now each is removed with a detail line, or a `CLEANUP_FAILED` warning where it can't be; a ticket's worktree of the same name, on its branch, is kept. `test/fullcheck-left.test.ts` covers it.
- A merge's rebase moves no branch but the one it rebases (§ 5's merge steps; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `rebase`, now with `--no-update-refs`; tutti-2vtz): with `rebase.updateRefs` set in your git config, git moved every other branch pointing into the rebased commits too, a backup or orchestra's `wt/<id>`, so a merge train's early rebase of a ticket onto the unmerged branch ahead left them on that branch's commits once the ticket was put back. `test/git-rebase-update-refs.test.ts` covers it.
- A branch a merge train rebased onto the branch ahead and git couldn't put back is set aside in its turn (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `prepare`, the mark's helpers now in `src/run/ahead.ts`; tutti-b8hv): a quit, or the next run's start, that couldn't reset it only logged REBASE_FAILED and kept its `checked_ahead` mark, which nothing in its merge read, so in its turn the branch looked on top of the base, was checked and merged the commits of the branch ahead with its own, even one set aside. Now its merge sets it aside as REBASE_FAILED while it is still marked and not where its worker left it; one back there merges, its mark cleared. `test/train-mark-left.test.ts` covers it.
- A second check hand-back in a row counts as another fix on the `closed`, `ticket_end` and `warn` records' `hand_backs` (§ 10; `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `accounted`; tutti-ti9q): a hand-back counted only when the phase changed, so Fixing → Fixing left `fix` where it was; it now counts by its cause.
- The end-of-run box no longer loses a change to the run's status made while it is shown (D19; `src/run/run.ts`'s `showEndOfRun`; tutti-sm0g): it read the status and wrote it back, so the triage's verdicts, the checks ahead, the probe or the scheduler's solo and unclosed set in between were lost; it now changes the status in one step
- After a panic, the first Ctrl+C or SIGTERM says what quitting waits for, and the next exits at once (§ 4.8; `src/run/run.ts`'s `hearQuit`; tutti-k4qg): the panic had already quit the run, so the first request did nothing visible, and only the third exited.
- A ticket set aside and undeferred while the run goes is taken again in that run (§ 5.1's `Deferred` → `Queued` row; `src/run/phase.ts`'s `Undeferred` cause, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s fill; tutti-j65z): the scheduler took only tickets not in the ledger or still Queued or HeldBack, and the table had no way out of Deferred but an environment hold's, so the `bd undefer <id>` that the START_FAILED, RUN_FILES_OUTSIDE and BAD_TICKET_ID notes give brought a ticket back only for the next run. Now one a ready poll found gone from bd ready, its fiber ended, is Queued again once bd ready lists it and the run doesn't hold (an environment hold reopens its own tickets), with the log's `<id>: undeferred; back in the queue`; one bd couldn't defer, still listed, stays out. `test/schedule-undeferred.test.ts` covers it.
- Whether your message to a worker is held and its sending are one step (§ 4.3; `src/run/held.ts`'s `holdOrSend` and `entering`, which marks the ticket `src/run/mergequeue.ts`'s `offer` moves into its line as going until its phase has moved; tutti-ua78): the ticket's phase was read under the held messages' permit and the message sent outside it, so a worker whose ticket went to merge in between got your message and a turn during its merge.
- A merge that changes the requires list during a look's fill is checked before that fill starts another ticket (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/run.ts`'s `requiresChecked`, now in `taking`; tutti-372a): the look checked the list once, at its start, so a merge that changed it while the look filled the free slots (a fill can take seconds) let the same fill start tickets before the new list was checked. Now each ask whether the run takes a ticket, the scheduler's included, checks a list that is due first, one check at a time. `test/requires-recheck-fill.test.ts` covers it.
- The setup, the checks and the requires list's commands run in the workers' environment, less tutti's variables (§ 4.1 "What each command tutti starts gets"; `src/agent/env.ts`'s `branchEnv` and `BranchEnv`, exec's `baseEnv`; tutti-axpo.6): they ran in tutti's whole environment, so a branch's scripts saw the terminal pane's and a parent Claude Code session's variables, and the API key workers go without, and a check could pass or fail for tutti unlike for its worker. `test/branch-env.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket set aside for a failed check isn't checked once more while its worktree has untracked files (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `whyNot`; tutti-s504): only tracked files' changes kept it set aside, so a file added by hand in its worktree after it was set aside was seen by the check there but not merged with its branch, the gap tutti-zcqb closed for hand-backs. Now the log says `<id> is not checked again: <worktree> has untracked files: <files>; it stays set aside`; files under .tutti/run/ don't count. `test/recheck-untracked.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's title line shows the account's usage windows all the time (§ 4.12, § 7.2, § 10; `src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `windowsOf`, `withFigures` and `windows`, `src/run/usageWindows.ts`, `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`'s `windowSegments`; tutti-9k3t): tutti read only rate_limit_info's top-level utilization, which `allowed` events leave out, so it saw the account's usage only once Claude Code warned or refused. Now it reads every event's `unifiedWindows`, a field the SDK's types don't name, defensively, keeps each window at its latest figure and shows `5h ▰▰▱▱▱▱▱▱▱▱  17% · week 41%` (the tighter window first on a narrow pane), giving way to the usage banner near a limit; each figure, the first and each that differs, is a `usage_window` event. A contract test pins the field and `test/fixtures/usage-limit/allowed.jsonl` holds two real `allowed` events. `test/usage-windows.test.ts` covers it.
- A state.json the Store can't encode fails the write instead of stopping the Store (§ 3.3; `src/services/Store.ts`'s `commit`, which `src/testing/Store.ts` commits through too; tutti-axpo.3): the writer encoded the state as it wrote it, so a change leaving a value JSON can't hold (`NaN`, `undefined`, a function) in a ticket's metadata killed the writer fiber and every update after it waited forever, while the test layer neither encoded nor stamped the run and let it pass. Now both layers apply the changes, stamp the run and round-trip the state through state.json's text in one pure `commit`; one that can't be encoded is `StoreWriteFailed` and the last state stays, and a decode failure's reason says what is wrong rather than `[object Object]`. `test/store-commit.test.ts` covers it.
- One layout function per screen (§ 7.2, § 7.5, § 7.7; `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `dashboardLayout`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `viewLayout` and `panelBudget`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `Section`; tutti-axpo.5): the dashboard's line budget was spread over six helpers and the worker and Interview views had none, so nothing made the parts add up to the pane, and a part could draw over the one below (tutti-gf62, tutti-oknk, tutti-7nzv). Now each screen hands out its lines in priority order (the dashboard: the hint, the title, the totals, the foot, Current or the end-of-run box, the table, then the blank line over the load line), each section is drawn in its own lines with overflow hidden, and warnings that don't fit give way to Current's box. Beside the end-of-run box the tickets table no longer waits on room for a line each of Current, which the box replaces. The 10 Hz spinner and the clock run in each card, the compact box and the idle box, not in the whole dashboard, so a tick no longer draws the title, totals and table anew, and nothing ticks below the minimum size or behind the end-of-run box. `test/screen-layout.test.tsx` covers it.
- One layout function per screen (§ 7.2, § 7.5, § 7.7; `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `dashboardLayout`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `viewLayout` and `panelBudget`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `Section`; tutti-axpo.5): the dashboard's line budget was spread over six helpers and the worker and Interview views had none, so nothing made the parts add up to the pane, and a part could draw over the one below (tutti-gf62, tutti-oknk, tutti-7nzv). Now each screen hands out its lines in priority order (the dashboard: the hint, the title, the totals, the foot, Current or the end-of-run box, the table, then the blank line over the load line), each section is drawn in its own lines with overflow hidden, and warnings that don't fit give way to Current's box. Beside the end-of-run box the tickets table no longer waits on room for a line each of Current, which the box replaces. The 10 Hz spinner and the clock run in each card, the compact box and the idle box, not in the whole dashboard, so a tick no longer draws the title, totals and table anew, and nothing ticks below the minimum size or behind the end-of-run box. `test/screen-layout.test.tsx` and `test/dashboard-spinner.test.tsx` cover it.
- `tutti init --force` keeps a verifier in use (§ 8.3; `src/init/init.ts`'s `verifierInUse` and `verifierConsent`; tutti-zo1o): `--force` skipped reading the worker prompt, so the verifier counted as not in use and was asked about, and with no terminal its consent was "not asked" while the prompt was replaced by the template, which has no Verify section: `.claude/agents/verifier.md` stayed, workers stopped running it, and the summary said nothing had changed. Now init reads the prompt on disk under `--force` too, and where it ran the verifier asks nothing and gives the template's prompt its Verify section again; `--no-verifier` still drops it. `test/init-force-verifier.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view's request panel shows a URL elicitation's URL (§ 4.5, § 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `panelParts`; tutti-tg4e): the panel offered Open URL but never showed where it went, though the dashboard's card does. Now the URL is under the request's line, faint and wrapped whole, above the buttons. `test/worker-view-url-request.test.tsx` covers it.
- A Tab or a moved selection in the worker view no longer re-wraps a request's whole input, and the panel draws only the input lines in view (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `panelInput`, `panelParts`' `sides` and `PanelBox`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `boxSides`; tutti-x4wu): a request's parts were worked out again on every focus or selection change, input included, and the box mounted every line of it in a scrollbox, so a 3,000-line Write cost 50-70 ms a Tab and 6,001 mounted text renderables. Now the input is wrapped once a request and width, apart from the head, framed only where it shows, and the box draws its room's lines, the wheel moving them. `test/ui-panel-input-window.test.tsx` covers it.
- One helper for each rule that had drifted copies (tutti-axpo.1): how a git status is read (`src/services/gitStatus.ts`'s `trackedChanges`, `untrackedFiles`, `allChanges` and `checkoutChanges`), whether a branch is there (`Git.branchExists`, show-ref's exit 1 a missing branch whatever git says on stderr), whether a process is alive (`src/services/processTree.ts`'s `processAlive`), finding a command on the PATH tutti runs commands with (`src/services/onPath.ts`'s `whichOnPath`, now also for keep_awake's command, init's tools and `claude`), making the folders under .tutti/run (`src/native/libc.ts`'s `makeFoldersUnder`, which gives why they aren't there and counts a folder made meanwhile as made, so events.jsonl is no longer turned off for the run by a race), cutting text to one line (`src/ui/oneLine.ts`'s `oneLine`, to a width in columns and characters), whether a turn ended on the usage limit (`src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `turnLimit` and `endedOnLimit`), a record's elapsed seconds (`src/run/events.ts`'s `tenths`, never below 0, so a wall clock stepping back no longer drops a ticket_end or check record, tutti-5mlh), and a Claude Code process (`src/agent/sessionProcess.ts`'s `SessionProcess`): a worker that is Gone, or whose session or login probe didn't start, now says what Claude Code last wrote to stderr (tutti-7l57), and an organ's or the usage probe's unwanted turn is killed at once, each waiting for its process to exit before its folder is removed. `test/git-status-changes.test.ts`, `test/git-branch-exists.test.ts`, `test/process-alive.test.ts`, `test/which-on-path.test.ts`, `test/make-folders-under.test.ts`, `test/one-line.test.ts`, `test/turn-limit.test.ts`, `test/elapsed-tenths.test.ts` and `test/session-process.test.ts` cover them.
- One helper for each rule that had drifted copies (tutti-axpo.1): how a git status is read (`src/services/gitStatus.ts`'s `trackedChanges`, `untrackedFiles`, `allChanges` and `checkoutChanges`), whether a branch is there (`Git.branchExists`, show-ref's exit 1 a missing branch whatever git says on stderr), whether a process is alive (`src/services/processTree.ts`'s `processAlive`), finding a command on the PATH tutti runs commands with (`src/services/onPath.ts`'s `whichOnPath`, now also for keep_awake's command, init's tools and `claude`), making the folders under .tutti/run (`src/native/libc.ts`'s `makeFoldersUnder`, which gives why they aren't there and counts a folder made meanwhile as made, so events.jsonl is no longer turned off for the run by a race), cutting text to one line (`src/ui/oneLine.ts`'s `oneLine`, to a width in columns and characters), whether a turn ended on the usage limit (`src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `turnLimit` and `endedOnLimit`), a record's elapsed seconds (`src/run/events.ts`'s `tenths`, never below 0, so a wall clock stepping back no longer drops a ticket_end or check record, tutti-5mlh), and a Claude Code process (`src/agent/sessionProcess.ts`'s `SessionProcess`): a worker that is Gone, or whose session or login probe didn't start, now says what Claude Code last wrote to stderr (tutti-7l57), and an organ's or the usage probe's unwanted turn is killed at once, each waiting for its process to exit before its folder is removed. `test/git-status-changes.test.ts`, `test/git-branch-exists.test.ts`, `test/process-alive.test.ts`, `test/which-on-path.test.ts`, `test/make-folders-under.test.ts`, `test/one-line.test.ts`, `test/turn-limit.test.ts`, `test/elapsed-tenths.test.ts` and `test/session-process.test.ts` cover them; the fake Claude Code writes `stderrAtStart` before `exitAtStart`.
- init's test-work tickets say what the runners on disk do (§ 8.3; `src/init/checks.ts`'s `readRunners`, `runnersAsWritten` and `testWork`, `fileTestWork`; tutti-dess): "Set up the test harness" always said tutti init wrote check-fast.sh and check-full.sh checking nothing, so nothing merged was checked, though with 'create from scratch' and a check-fast.sh kept (Replace? defaults to No) check-fast.sh still checked and check-full.sh ran it, which could send the worker to rebuild a working runner; and the ticket for the suites found said init wrote both runners from them when it had kept them. Now init reads the runners once it has written the setup: each is said to check nothing (exit 0), to be written from the suites found, or to run the checks the project had, and only a check-fast.sh checking nothing says nothing merged is checked. `test/init-test-work-runners.test.ts` covers it.
- The run report's reviewer input keeps within a total budget (§ 6.2; `src/run/report.ts`'s `listCaps`, `evidenceCap`, `notesWidth`, `reviewerBudget`, `cappedList`, `cappedEvidence` and `notesTail`; tutti-0st8): only the log and the attention lines were capped, so on an overnight run with hundreds of tickets the set-aside tickets' whole notes, the closed list, the commits, the tallies and the follow-ups could overflow the reviewer's context and end in `REVIEW_FAILED` with no report. Now each list keeps its last items within its cap, after a line saying how many were cut, each section the run gathers keeps its end, and a set-aside ticket's notes keep their last 1,500 characters; the bd show calls for the tickets closed and set aside run four at a time. `test/report-budget.test.ts` covers it.
- scripts/install.sh runs the new binary's --version in the folder it installs in and reads its stdout only (§ 12 Install; tutti-3akw): it ran it in the temporary folder, so where /tmp is mounted noexec (a CIS-hardening default) the install failed with `the new binary doesn't run: Permission denied`, and it compared stdout and stderr together with `tutti <tag>`, so any warning on stderr failed it. Now it copies the binary beside the old one first, runs it there, compares stdout only, as `tutti update` does, and on a failure removes the copy and keeps the old binary; a binary that doesn't run says its exit code. `test/install-script-version.test.ts` covers it.
- A file without an extension that a ticket's text names counts toward its footprint (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/footprint.ts`'s `RepoFiles.bare`, `eachPathToken` and `takePath`; tutti-shrg): a word was taken for a path only with an extension, so with a Makefile tracked "Add a lint target to the Makefile and src/a.ts" and "Fix the Makefile install target; touch src/b.ts" ran side by side and met only as a rebase hand-back, and "Update scripts/release" named nothing. Now, when git lists the repository's files, a word without an extension is a path where it is a tracked file's path with a / (`scripts/release`, `bin/tutti`), or its base name capitalised or in backticks (`Makefile`, `` `tutti` ``); the check commands' files are found the same way. `test/footprint-extensionless.test.ts` covers it.
- A fill no longer runs a bd show for each ready ticket with no links, nor lists the repository's files on each wake (§ 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `heldFor` and `footprintsOf`; tutti-l17s): once a ticket of the run was pending, every ticket in line had its links read with `bd show`, though bd ready gives them, and each fill ran `git ls-files` and `git check-attr` over every file, so with about 100 ready tickets a fill took seconds of subprocesses with the loop blocked. Now only a ticket bd ready links to one it waits on (a blocker or a source) is shown, and the files are listed again only once the checkout's commit moves. `test/fill-reads.test.ts` covers it.
- Answered and carried-over workers take a slot only when one is free (§ 4.8, § 5's Scheduling, § 5.1; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `carry`, `src/run/carry.ts`'s `carryWaiting`, `src/run/asked.ts`'s `takeAnswers`; tutti-3ezp): the scheduler's carry occupied a slot with no check, so with `concurrent: 1` an answered ticket resumed beside the running one, six suspended workers with `concurrent: 3` all resumed at once as the run started, and `--machine-workers`, a solo ticket and the wind-down were passed over the same way. Now each is resumed only with a slot free, the run taking tickets, the machine under its cap and no solo ticket running; the rest wait, Asked or saved in state.json (`<id>, carried over from the last run, waits for a free slot to be resumed`), for a later look, and none is dispatched afresh meanwhile. `test/carry-slots.test.ts` covers it.
- Answered and carried-over workers take a slot only when one is free (§ 4.8, § 5's Scheduling, § 5.1; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `carry`, `src/run/carry.ts`'s `carryWaiting`, `src/run/asked.ts`'s `takeAnswers`; tutti-3ezp): the scheduler's carry occupied a slot with no check, so with `concurrent: 1` an answered ticket resumed beside the running one, six suspended workers with `concurrent: 3` all resumed at once as the run started, and `--machine-workers`, a solo ticket and the wind-down were passed over the same way. Now each is resumed only with a slot free, the run taking tickets, the machine under its cap and no solo ticket running; the rest wait, Asked or saved in state.json (`<id>, carried over from the last run, waits for a free slot to be resumed`), for a later look, none is dispatched afresh meanwhile, and the run doesn't end, nor show the end-of-run box, while one waits (`src/run/runEnd.ts`'s `resuming`). `test/carry-slots.test.ts` and `test/run-end-resuming.test.ts` cover it.
- Answered and carried-over workers take a slot only when one is free (§ 4.8, § 5's Scheduling, § 5.1; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `carry`, `src/run/carry.ts`'s `carryWaiting`, `src/run/asked.ts`'s `takeAnswers`; tutti-3ezp): the scheduler's carry occupied a slot with no check, so with `concurrent: 1` an answered ticket resumed beside the running one, six suspended workers with `concurrent: 3` all resumed at once as the run started, and `--machine-workers`, a solo ticket and the wind-down were passed over the same way. Now each is resumed only with a slot free, the run taking tickets, the machine under its cap and no solo ticket running; the rest wait, Asked or saved in state.json (`<id>, carried over from the last run, waits for a free slot to be resumed`), for a later look, none is dispatched afresh meanwhile, and the run doesn't end, nor show the end-of-run box, while one waits and the run would take it up (`src/run/runEnd.ts`'s `resuming`). `test/carry-slots.test.ts`, `test/carry-slots-box.test.ts` and `test/run-end-resuming.test.ts` cover it.
- The start question's keys that arrive in one read each see the ones before them (§ 7.1; `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`'s `step` and `highlighted`, now `useKeyState`; tutti-eb2i): they were React state, so Down and Enter in one read (`\x1b[B\r`) answered the current tickets and started the run while the screen showed New feature highlighted, and Shift+Tab, Up and Enter together did nothing. Now each key reads the state the keys before it left, as tutti init's form does. `test/start-question-one-read.test.tsx` covers it.
- The worker view's request panel keeps to the height the view leaves it (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `panelRooms`, `usePanelRooms` and `RequestPanel`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `usePanel` and its `PanelFit`, `src/ui/InterviewView.tsx`; tutti-7nzv): each of its two requests had a quarter of the pane for its input whatever the panel's height as a whole, so at 80x24 two Bash permission requests with 7-line commands took every row, and the transcript, the rule, the composer and the hint were pushed off screen. Now the panel has the rows the head, the rules, the lines over the composer, its first line and the hint leave, less 3 for the transcript: the inputs' rooms shrink first, to 3 lines each, then the panel draws the oldest request alone, its input in what is left, and counts the rest under it (`+1 more requests`); a request not drawn can't be selected or answered. The Interview view's panel does the same. `test/ui-request-panel-budget.test.tsx` covers it.
- The worker view's request panel keeps to the height the view leaves it (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `panelRooms`, `usePanelRooms` and `RequestPanel`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `usePanel` and its `PanelFit`, `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `Composer`, `src/ui/InterviewView.tsx`; tutti-7nzv): each of its two requests had a quarter of the pane for its input whatever the panel's height as a whole, so at 80x24 two Bash permission requests with 7-line commands took every row, and the transcript, the rule, the composer and the hint were pushed off screen. Now the panel has the rows the head (as many lines as it wraps to), the rules, the lines over the composer, the composer (as tall as what you type, `useComposer`'s `lines`) and the hint leave, less 3 for the transcript: the inputs' rooms shrink first, to 3 lines each, then the panel draws the oldest request alone, its input in what is left, and counts the rest under it (`+1 more requests`); a request not drawn can't be selected or answered. The Interview view's panel does the same. `test/ui-request-panel-budget.test.tsx` covers it.
- Clicks act on release, so a drag to copy text acts on nothing (D9, § 7.3; `src/ui/click.ts`'s `clickable`, used by `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/Modal.tsx`, `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx` and the forms; tutti-bh51): click targets acted on the press, which OpenTUI hands them as it starts a selection, so dragging across a card's title to copy it opened the worker's view mid-drag, a drag from an attention card's button pressed it, and one from a tool line toggled it. Now a target acts when the left button is released on the cell it was pressed on with no text selected. `test/click-on-release.test.tsx` covers it.
- bd's output past exec's 16 MB cap fails saying so (§ 3.3; `src/services/Beads.ts`'s `decodeWhole`, `src/services/exec.ts`'s `outputCut`, which `src/services/Git.ts` shares; tutti-bkko): bd's reads ignored `truncated`, so a `bd list --all` of about 6,000 issues, as init, scope, the nothing check and the merge read, failed as `a list of issues: not JSON` though bd exited 0, and a tail that was JSON on its own could have been read as bd's answer. Now a read or a write's issue whose stdout ran past the cap isn't decoded: bd exiting 0 fails as `bd's output ran past 16777216 bytes, and only the last were kept`, a failing bd with its exit code. `test/beads-output-cap.test.ts` covers it.
- Current no longer draws over the wind-down line and the hint when tickets waiting to merge add cards beyond the workers' slots (§ 7.2; `src/ui/Card.tsx`'s `keptLines`, `compactShown` and `compactLines`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `tableHeight`, `showsTable` and `showsGap`; tutti-gf62): Current's room came from the slots alone, so at 80×24 with 4 workers and 4 tickets to merge the compact box ran past its room, its bottom border gone and the foot garbled. Now Current is kept a line for each card where the cards outnumber the slots, the tickets table gives way first, a compact box still too short ends in `+N more`, keeping the highlighted card and those waiting for you, and the section clips what it can't hold. `test/dashboard-merging-cards.test.tsx` covers it.
- The end-of-run box keeps to its room on the dashboard (D19, § 7.2; `src/ui/EndOfRunBox.tsx`'s `endOfRunLines` and `endOfRunText`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `tableBesideBox`; tutti-oknk): the box wrapped to any length but was given what the tickets table left, with no clipping, so at 80×24 five workers waiting on permissions with long summaries needed 12 lines of 8, and its `q ends the run` line was drawn over by the hint. Now the table gives up rows for the box, down to its fewest, and is left out below them; a box still too tall names the workers that fit and counts the rest (`…, +2 more`), then leaves out the hand-back's line and its frame, and always keeps what `q` does. `test/end-of-run-fits.test.tsx` covers it.
- The run's log, state.json and reports are no longer written through symbolic links (§ 9.1; `src/run/log.ts`'s `Log.layer`, `src/native/libc.ts`'s `writeFileUnder` and `makeFoldersUnder`, `src/services/Store.ts`'s `writeStateUnder`, `src/run/report.ts`'s `runReport`; tutti-oqb2): a ticket that committed `.tutti/tutti.log` as a link had the next run append its lines, ticket titles and model text among them, to the file it pointed at, and `.tutti/run`, `.tutti/reports` or their files as links had state.json and reports written through them. Each is opened below the main checkout with openat and `O_NOFOLLOW`, as events.jsonl is, and a link is refused: the run doesn't start on a linked log, state.json's write is `StoreWriteFailed`, and the report is `report not saved`.
- The run lock no longer writes through a link at its name (§ 9.2; `src/services/RunLock.ts`'s `take`, `probeOne` and `singleLinked`; tutti-jmzk): `tutti.lock` and `orchestra.lock` in the git folder every worktree can write were opened following symbolic links, so a worker's `ln -s ~/.zshrc .git/tutti.lock` had tutti write its JSON over the target and empty it on release. Now each is opened with `openUnder` (O_NOFOLLOW, a regular file only), one with more than one link is refused too, and the run fails with LockFailed, leaving the other file as it was; the holder is read from the locked descriptor, and the probe opens the files the same way. `test/run-lock-links.test.ts` covers it.
- Git's local environment variables no longer reach the commands, checks and workers tutti starts (§ 3.3, § 4.1; `src/services/gitEnv.ts`'s `withoutGitLocal`, used by `src/services/exec.ts`'s `exec`, and `gitLocalVariable`, used by `src/agent/env.ts`'s `workerEnv`; tutti-m6hh): tutti started from a git hook, a `rebase --exec` or a shell exporting `GIT_DIR` (a dotfiles setup) passed `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and the rest on, so every `git -C <worktree>` acted on that repository instead: a worktree's branch read as main, a rebase in a worktree rebased main, and workers' commits landed on main's index. Now the variables `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` prints are left out of every command's and worker's environment; git's others (`GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`) stay. `test/git-local-env.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket checked once more holds back a ticket overlapping it while it rebases and merges (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `overlapping`; tutti-8lkx): `recheckSlot` takes its slot without its issue, and the overlap check read the issues only of tickets past their work that held no slot, so a ready ticket touching the same files was dispatched beside it. Now the issue of every running ticket, or one past its work, that the scheduler doesn't have is read from bd once and kept until it merges. `test/recheck-overlap.test.ts` covers it.
- A failed fast-forward stops the run with `MERGE_FAILED` only when the branch doesn't fast-forward (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `fastForward` and `BaseMoved`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`'s `merge`; tutti-q8ey): every `git merge --ff-only` failure but an uncommitted file in the way ended the run, exit 6, though a transient `.git/index.lock` in the main checkout (an IDE, a shell prompt's `git status`) or a commit made on the base by hand after the merge read it left a branch that would still merge. Now a refused fast-forward reads the base again: moved on, the ticket goes back to be rebased and checked; still fast-forwarding, it is tried again 3 s later, up to 3 tries, then holds the run with `GIT_FAILED`. `test/merge-fast-forward-failed.test.ts` covers it.
- Sessions that don't start hold the run for the environment (§ 4.9, § 5 item 11; `src/run/start.ts`'s `Start.SetAside` and its `session`, `src/run/work.ts`'s `work`, `src/run/environment.ts`'s `Settling`, `FastFail` and `fastFailsWhy`; tutti-13bh): a worker whose session failed to start or didn't begin within a minute (`START_FAILED`) was deferred and counted for nothing toward the hold, so on a machine where Claude Code can't start sessions (a binary that can't be launched, a broken login) every ready ticket was deferred in turn, each after up to a minute. Now such a ticket adds to the row of tickets failing at once, and a row of them holds the run, reopens them and probes the machine (`ENVIRONMENT: the sessions of the last 2 tickets (a, b) didn't start; …`). A ticket set aside as it starts for another reason still counts for nothing. `test/environment-start-failed.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker resumed once its question is answered has a ticket time limit (§ 4.8, § 5.1; `src/run/asked.ts`'s `takeAnswers`, which calls `src/run/work.ts`'s `follow`; tutti-1wvj): `takeAnswers` followed the resumed worker with its own copy of `follow` that left out the time-limit fiber, so an answered worker, in the run or at carry-over's end, got no `TICKET_LIMIT` or `LONG_RUNNING` at all. Now it is followed as a carried worker is, its limit counted afresh from its resume, the time it waited on its answer counting for nothing. `test/asked-ticket-limit.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker resumed once its question is answered has a ticket time limit (§ 4.8, § 5.1; `src/run/asked.ts`'s `takeAnswers`, which calls `src/run/work.ts`'s `follow`, and `src/run/run.ts`'s `onResumedLimit`; tutti-1wvj): `takeAnswers` followed the resumed worker with its own copy of `follow` that left out the time-limit fiber, and the run's ready poll gave it no TICKET_LIMIT callback, so an answered worker, in the run or at carry-over's end, got no `TICKET_LIMIT` or `LONG_RUNNING` at all. Now it is followed as a carried worker is, its limit counted afresh from its resume, the time it waited on its answer counting for nothing, and past it the run holds (exit 3) and suspends it as it ends. `test/asked-ticket-limit.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker with a setup problem ends before its first turn (§ 4.1; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `checkBefore`, `begin` and `setupProblem`, `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `checkStatus`, `src/run/start.ts`; tutti-8a7x): the API key and MCP server checks ran only on the first `system/init`, which comes as the first turn starts, its model request already sent, billed to the key the check exists to block; and the session was then closed with the SDK's grace, stdin ended and SIGTERM 2 s later, which Claude Code ignores mid-turn, so a quick tool call could still run. Now a start or resume checks its initialize's `account.apiKeySource` and its `mcpServerStatus()` before the session is sent anything, and a problem ends the worker there, `Gone` with `setup`, sending it nothing, so the run stops with SETUP_PROBLEM as for one `system/init` shows (`src/run/start.ts`'s `SetupProblem`); `system/init`'s check stays as the backstop for what those don't show, such as a chosen server not yet listed. Either way Claude Code's process group is killed at once. `test/setup-check-before-turn.test.ts` and `test/setup-problem-at-start.test.ts` cover it.
- The 500 ms answer guard starts again when the target request changes (§ 4.5; `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`'s `useAnswering` and its `target`, called by `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`'s `AnswerBox` and `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `useSelection` as they draw, and by the keys that answer; tutti-14o5): it ran from when each request was first drawn, so once the target was answered and the highlight moved on to another card's request, or the worker view's panel to its next request, a key 80 ms later answered that one at once, and holding `a` allowed every waiting request in turn. Moving the highlight or the selection to another request (`↓`, `Tab` past a card's last button) starts its guard too, a move and a key read together included. `test/answer-guard-target.test.tsx` covers it.
- requires_check no longer runs commands a worker wrote behind a permission request that shows only their names (§ 4.11; `src/agent/requiresTools.ts`'s `checkIn` and `requiresServerFor`; tutti-3fhp): it read the list from the worker's own worktree, which the worker can edit, and ran each entry's check and install with sh in tutti's environment, so the classifier or the person asked saw `{"names":["x"]}`, and Bash's rules and the sandbox didn't apply. Now it runs only the entries the main checkout's list has as they are there, in the main checkout, as a run's start does; an entry the branch adds or changes it gives back, its check and install, for the worker to run with Bash, where the permission mode sees them. `test/requires-check-branch.test.ts` covers it.
- A setup problem a worker's first `system/init` shows stops the run with `SETUP_PROBLEM`, exit 2 (§ 4.1; `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s `Gone{setup}`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `setupProblem`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s `Settled.Setup`, `src/run/work.ts`'s `setupProblem`, `src/run/holds.ts`'s `SETUP_PROBLEM`; tutti-gmaw): the host ended such a worker (MCP servers it wasn't given, an API key billed beside the subscription) and kept the problem in `host.setup`, which nothing read, so its ticket was deferred as left unfinished with nothing done, counted toward the environment hold, and two in a row ended the run `ENVIRONMENT`, exit 7; a carried-over ticket was resumed and then paused (`PAUSED`, exit 3). Now the host ends the worker `Gone` with `setup`, and its ticket, neither resumed nor deferred, is reopened and waits for the next run, keeping its worktree; the run holds with `SETUP_PROBLEM`, the running tickets finishing first. `host.setup` and its findings, which the `START` block never read (it gives the login probe's permission mode), are gone. `test/setup-problem-run.test.ts` covers it.
- CI leaves a release commit pushed alone to the Release workflow's check (§ 12 Releases; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `release-commit` job, `scripts/release-commit.sh`; tutti-ac2x): pushing a release, its commit to main and then its tag, checked the same commit twice, CI on Linux and macOS beside the Release workflow's check at the tag (0.2.0's CI #16 beside Release #5). A commit whose subject is `Release X.Y.Z` (or a pre-release's), which changes only `CHANGELOG.md` and `package.json`'s version, to the subject's, and is the only commit in its push, its parent checked by CI when it was pushed, now has CI's check and build jobs skipped. Anything else is checked as before. Not `[skip ci]`, which GitHub would apply to the tag's push too, so nothing would be released. `release.yml`'s Releasing says to push the commits under a release first. `test/release-commit.test.ts` covers the script.
- A crashed run's workers are carried over though the next run wrote state.json before carry-over (§ 4.8; `src/services/Store.ts`'s `previousRun`, `src/run/carry.ts`'s `toCarry`; tutti-z3el): the Store stamps the current run on state.json at every commit, so a write before carry-over, such as clearing a killed run's `checked_ahead` mark, made the file name this run, and every ticket the crash left was skipped, left in progress in bd with nothing to resume it. A crashed run's PID reused by a live process did the same. Now the Store keeps the stamp it read as it was built, and carry-over judges by that: the run lock is held, so that run has ended, whatever has its PID now. `test/carry-after-write.test.ts` covers it with the live Store.
- A click on a transcript while you write in its composer leaves the keys in the composer (§ 7.5, § 4.10; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `TranscriptPane` and `PanelBox`, `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `useComposer`; tutti-yzkg): the transcript's and the panel's scrollboxes took the focus a click gave, while the composer's hint still said you were writing, so the letters you typed next answered the open request (`a` allowed, `d` denied or declined). Now those scrollboxes, and the answer form's, take no focus, and with no box open the composer follows the renderer's focus: a focus moved elsewhere leaves it, its hint with it. `test/ui-composer-focus.test.tsx` covers it.
- A worker's fix or conflict resolution is rejected when it leaves an untracked file (§ 4.7; `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `verifyFixed`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `verifyResolved`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `untracked`; tutti-zcqb): both counted only tracked files' changes, so a fix that committed `src/a.ts` but never git-added the `src/util.ts` it imports was taken as clean, its check passed in the worktree, where the file is, and main fast-forwarded to the branch without it. Now an untracked file outside `.tutti/run/` rejects the fix or resolution, its line naming the files (`its worker left untracked files in <worktree>: src/util.ts`), as a worker's first finish already did (CLOSED_UNCOMMITTED). `test/handback-untracked.test.ts` covers it.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-10-23

- The notifier, the clipboard and the URL opener look for their commands on the PATH they run them with (§ 7.9; `src/services/Notifier.ts`'s `installedOnPath`, which `src/ui/clipboard.ts`'s and `src/ui/openUrl.ts`'s live layers use too; tutti-xj3t): `Bun.which` alone reads the PATH the process started with, not `process.env.PATH` changed since. `test/live-layers.test.ts` makes a folder of fakes the whole PATH, so on Linux CI, which has `xdg-open` but no `xclip` or `notify-send`, the clipboard fell back to OSC 52 and the notifier to nothing, and three of its tests failed; on macOS they passed by the machine's own `osascript`, `pbcopy` and `open`. A test of `installedOnPath` uses a command only the changed PATH has.
- An organ call whose SDK query() throws as it is called fails as an AiError (§ 6.1; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`'s `layer`, which takes a `query` for tests; tutti-xpzt): with no Claude Code for the platform (no optional platform package, or a musl/glibc mismatch) the call died instead of failing. Now it fails as `AiError.UnknownError` with `queryThrew`'s advice, as the login probe and workers do, and counts a call of unknown cost. `test/organ-query-throws.test.ts` covers it.
- DEFER_FAILED and a predicted footprint bd can't keep give bd's error (§ 5; `src/run/aside.ts`'s `putAside`, `src/run/predict.ts`'s predictor; tutti-pd64): they wrote the error's message, empty for BeadsFailed and BeadsTimedOut, so the line read `bd could not defer it: ; kept out of this run` or ended in `BeadsFailed`; both now give `beadsWhy`'s reason, bd's first line of stderr or its time limit, as the other bd-failure lines do.
- The UI tests highlight their transcripts with OpenTUI's `MockTreeSitterClient`, not tree-sitter (§ 13; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `HighlighterContext` and the looks' `highlighter`, `test/render.ts`'s `RenderOptions` and `settle`; tutti-cdfz): `capture()` resolves its highlighting at once rather than wait up to 4 s for tree-sitter's, off the render loop and the most load-sensitive wait in the UI tests. The Markdown is then drawn as written, so the worker view's and the Interview view's snapshots show its marks; the tests of what highlighting draws (`test/ui-markdown-blocks.test.tsx`, `test/ui-roles.test.tsx`'s worker view, `test/ui-worker-view.test.tsx`'s roles) keep the real tree-sitter with `{ treeSitter: "real" }`.
- The daily release check answers where its cache's folder is a file (§ 12; `src/update/update.ts`'s `writeCache`; tutti-8iz.9): removing its half-written cache file failed there too (ENOTDIR), and that error escaped, so the check died instead of answering and asking again next run. Now that removal's error is ignored as the write's is. `test/update-failures.test.ts` covers it.
- A session's first `system/init` is checked from decoded fields (§ 4.1; `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `checkInit` and `unlistedProblem`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `checkFirst` and `uncheckedLine`; tutti-rexi): `checkInit` read `init.mcp_servers.map` and `checkFirst` `permissionMode` and `apiKeySource` as the SDK's types have them, so a first `system/init` without `mcp_servers`, from a CLI of another version, died, was skipped, and left the session Starting, its setup never checked. Now the fields are decoded with a Schema: without readable `mcp_servers` the worker ends as a setup problem, since tutti can't tell which servers it got; without `permissionMode` or `apiKeySource` that check is skipped, the log saying so once in the run. `test/first-init-fields.test.ts` covers it.
- The report reads each log line's tag and ticket, not its text (§ 6.2; `src/run/log.ts`'s `Marked`, `Kept` and `Log.lines`, `src/run/report.ts`'s `attentionLines` and `steeredInRun`, `src/run/triage.ts`; tutti-a3o2): it found attention lines by `  PERMISSION: ` anywhere in a line and a steer by a line ending `  STEERED: <id>`, so a ticket title or a model's words saying so, a line break made a space, added to the reviewer's attention section, triage's evidence, or made a ticket steered. Now the builders of a ticket's lines (PERMISSION, QUESTION, ALLOWED, DENIED, STEERED, RELEASED, MESSAGE) mark each with its tag and ticket, `Log.lines` keeps `{stamp, tag, ticket, text}` for each line, and the report and triage filter on those. `test/log-marked.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker reads the SDK's messages defensively (§ 4.2; `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s message schemas and `unreadable`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `emit`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `read`, `src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `RateLimitInfo` and `rateLimitOf`; tutti-aba): a CLI of another version that sent a `background_tasks_changed` without `tasks`, a `rate_limit_event` without `rate_limit_info` or an error result without `errors` threw in the reducer, which ended the session's reader and left the worker Gone. Now the fields the reducer reads are decoded with a Schema: a message without one is no change, said in the log (`<id>: a <type> message it couldn't read, left out of its state: …`); a result without `errors` fails with its subtype; a `rate_limit_info` that doesn't decode is no event; and a message that still dies as it is taken in is logged and skipped. `test/unreadable-messages.test.ts` covers it.
- A log line and its event record are kept together on quit (§ 7.6, § 10; `src/run/log.ts`'s `Log.writeWith`, `src/testing/Log.ts`; tutti-jskc): `writeWith` ran its `record`, the happening's `events.jsonl` entry, interruptibly after its line, so a quit landing between the two left the line in `.tutti/tutti.log` without its record. Now the line and the record are written together or not at all; a `record` must be quick and bounded, as every `EventStream.emit` is (a synchronous append), since a quit waits for it. `test/log-record-interrupt.test.ts` covers it.
- A finished worker's view files what you wrote in its composer as a ticket (§ 7.5; `src/run/fileTicket.ts`, the `FileTicket` command, the screen's `filings`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `FinishedLine`; tutti-g0wd): once the worker had finished, the composer kept your text but could only refuse to send it. Now `Ctrl+T`, or the File as ticket button on the finished line, files it with bd create (the first line its title, discovered from the finished ticket), logs `FILED`, and empties the composer once bd has it; a failure keeps the text and toasts why. `test/worker-view-file-ticket.test.tsx`, `test/file-ticket.test.ts` and `test/file-ticket-run.test.ts` cover it.
- `Beads.questions` reads bd's `null` as no questions (`src/services/Beads.ts`'s `decodeQuestions`; tutti-8t0): bd 1.3.1's `bd human list --json` prints `null`, not `[]`, when there are none, which was `BeadsUnreadable`. `test/beads-questions.test.ts` covers it.
- A log line interrupted on quit is written whole or not at all (§ 7.6; `src/run/log.ts`'s `Log.layer`, `src/testing/Log.ts`; tutti-0uz): a fiber interrupted after its line reached `.tutti/tutti.log` but before the Log kept it left it out of the reviewer's lines and plain mode's stdout. Now a line, once it holds the log, goes to the file, the kept lines and stdout together, interrupted or not; one interrupted while waiting for another fiber's line goes nowhere. `test/log-interrupt.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker view's head and hint say the worker has finished once its card has gone (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `hintKeys` and head, `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `finishedWords`; tutti-2n7y): after the ticket merged, the head still showed the last card's status (`working  steered`), its spinner and a ticking clock, and the hint still listed Enter send, Ctrl+X interrupt and Ctrl+R release, which did nothing. Now the head says `finished` with a `•` and its time stopped, and the hint drops those three keys. `test/worker-view-finished.test.tsx` covers it.
- Ticket IDs are sanitised where they enter RunView, and tutti plan sanitises what it prints (§ 7.10; `src/run/runView.ts`'s `label` on `Row` and `Card` and `ShownRequest`/`shownRequest`, drawn by `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx`, `src/ui/StopQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/TicketsTable.tsx` and `src/print/summary.ts`; `src/plan/plan.ts`'s `linkLine` and `beadsProblem`; tutti-4zs): workers file tickets, so an ID can hold escape sequences, and the attention card, the Answer box, the answer form, Shift+D's reason box and the stop question drew it raw, as plan did its IDs, a link's reason (a ticket's files entry among them) and bd's stderr. Now RunView gives each row, card and request a sanitised `label` that every view draws, while `ticket` stays the key the run's commands and answers name. `test/sanitised-ids.test.tsx` covers it.
- `tutti init --update` beside a flag whose value fails to read says the conflict too (§ 8.3; `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `readInitFlags`; tutti-bwkm): `tutti init --update --concurrent 0` said only the `--concurrent` problem, and `--update takes no other flag` only on the next try. Now a flag given counts whether its value read or not, so every problem is said at once. `test/init-update-conflict.test.ts` covers it.
- Toasts are opaque (§ 7.3; `src/ui/Toasts.tsx`'s `Toasts`; tutti-fgwf): a toast's text was drawn with no background, so the view's text under it showed through its spaces, and Ctrl+Z's toast over the Interview view's composer read `tutti can't be suspended` as `tuttiacan'tsbe…` or so. Now each toast's line is drawn in the terminal's own background, as the boxes over the view are. `test/ui-interview-toast.test.tsx` covers it above the Interview view's hint, wrapped onto two lines and on one.
- A message to the worker of a ticket waiting to merge or merging is held until the merge is over (§ 4.3, § 7.5; `src/run/held.ts`'s `makeHeld`, `MergeQueue.held`, called by `src/run/run.ts`'s SendMessage and `src/run/mergequeue.ts` as a merge ends, `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `heldLine` and `useSends`; tutti-yk6l): a message sent then gave the worker a turn to commit in, so its branch moved under its check and was checked a second time. Now the run steers the worker and holds the message, the worker view's composer says `<id> is merging: tutti holds your messages until its merge is over` and marks it `held`, and once the merge is over it is sent if the ticket keeps its card (its conflict waiting for you), or dropped, `not sent`, with a line in the log, when its worker has finished; Ctrl+X cancels the messages held, as it cancels those queued. `test/held-messages.test.ts`, `test/held-messages-run.test.ts` and `test/composer-held.test.tsx` cover it.
- A ticket left back in the queue as the run ends gets its `ticket_end` (§ 10; `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `Ledger.endQueued`, called by `src/run/run.ts` at the run's end and on quitting at once, `src/run/events.ts`'s `queued` outcome and `queuePhases`; tutti-xpw0): a ticket Waiting for a merge, the probe or a setup, or Queued or HeldBack after it was requeued, had no RunEnded to move it, so what its workers spent and its time were on no per-ticket record, only in its turn records and the run's totals. Now the run's end writes its `ticket_end` with outcome `queued`, its phase, and what a Waiting ticket waits for as `why`; docs/analytics.md's runs query counts them. `test/ticket-end-queued.test.ts` covers it.
- A new worker session starts without reading the ticket's transcript back for Spending's seed; only a resumed one is seeded (§ 10; `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `Workers.open` takes `resumed`; tutti-4l7x)
- A conflict hand-back's rebase a run left mid-resolution is aborted as the next run carries its ticket over (§ 4.8; `src/run/carry.ts`'s `abortLeftRebase` and `resolutionAt`; tutti-tejt): a run killed, or ended, while a ticket was `Resolving` left the ticket closed with its worker's half-done rebase, and the next run carried it over to merge and set it aside `OFF_BRANCH`. Now carry-over aborts it too, as the usage-limit end does for a hand-back never sent (§ 4.12), so its merge rebases the branch again and hands the conflicts back afresh; the line names the commit the worker's resolution had got to, which git keeps. `test/carry-killed-rebase.test.ts` covers it.
- Ctrl+C during a run's start is heard at once (§ 4.8; `src/run/run.ts`'s `untilQuit`, the quit watcher now installed before the requirements check; tutti-5mp): only the requirements check listened for a quit, so one during the read of the tickets left unmerged, the branches put back or the carry-over waited in the queue while every suspended worker was resumed and told to carry on, spending turns, before the run quit and interrupted them all; pressing again, as you would when nothing happens, ended in `quit at once`. Now a quit cuts the step under way short and skips the rest: no further worker is resumed, and those not yet taken up stay in state.json as they were. `test/carry-quit.test.ts` quits during the carry-over of four workers and while bd lists the tickets left unmerged.
- A transcript's view no longer draws itself anew ten times a second while its worker works (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `ViewHead`, which now takes its head as a function of the clock and the spinner's frame, and `PanelRequest`, `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `useSelection`, `src/ui/transcriptItems.ts`'s `editOf`; tutti-k2n1): the spinner and the elapsed time tick in the view's head, a leaf, rather than in the worker view and the Interview view; an entry's click is the same function from one drawing to the next, so the entries' memo holds; an Edit's or Write's change, its line diff and counts, is worked out once for each tool call's input; a request's box in the panel is worked out again only when what it shows changes. With 200 items, 50 of them edits, each tick redid every edit's diff. `test/ui-view-redraws.test.tsx` covers it.
- The Interview view's header and hint wrap in a narrow pane, as the worker view's do (§ 4.10, § 7.5 "A narrow pane"; `src/ui/transcriptView.ts`'s `useTranscriptWindow`, `usePanel`, `useSelection`, `useComposer` and `useTranscriptKeys`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `ViewHead`, `TranscriptPane`, `RequestPanel` and `ViewHint`, `src/ui/useKeyState.ts`; tutti-sa72): the Interview view copied about 250 lines of the worker view's transcript, panel, selection, composer and keys, and the copies had drifted, so its header cut the description's first line and its hint cut its keys where the worker view's wrapped (tutti-rfs). Now both views are built on the same hooks and components, and the ref-and-state pairs the views kept for their keys (the selection, the key help, the composer, the dashboard's highlight, the answer form's focus and values, a card's moved button focus) are one `useKeyState`, as `tutti init`'s form's were; `1`–`4` in the Interview view pick an option of the question selected, as in the worker view. `test/ui-interview-narrow.test.tsx` covers it at 50 columns.
- The usage-limit hold probes the account before it lifts on the clock (§ 4.12; `src/agent/UsageProbe.ts`, its test layer `src/testing/UsageProbe.ts`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `tries` and `probed`; tutti-eap5): at the reset or the next try of an unknown one it lifted blindly and sent every waiting worker a carry-on, each a model request the limit could refuse again. Now it first runs one isolated haiku turn of a few tokens (about $0.0001, logged as `ORGAN usage-probe`): refused, the hold takes the probe's reset or tries again 15 minutes later, saying `USAGE_LIMIT: a probe is refused for the five-hour limit still; …`, and tells no worker anything; allowed, it lifts; unanswered, it lifts on the clock as before. `test/usage-probe.test.ts` covers it, and `test/contract/usage-probe.test.ts` runs the real probe with `TUTTI_LIVE=1`.
- One helper puts a ticket's branch back (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `restoreBranch` and `Restored`, used by `src/run/handback.ts`'s `undoResolution`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `undoFix`, `src/run/mergetrain.ts`'s `putBack`, `putBackLeft` and `rebaseAhead`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `closeUnchanged` and `src/run/handMerged.ts`'s `removeMerged`; tutti-adau): each put a branch back its own way and in its own order, and the merge train and the two that move a branch to the base before removing it reset whatever the worktree had checked out, with uncommitted changes or not.
