Now each aborts a rebase in progress, leaves a branch at its commit already as it is, and resets it only in a clean worktree that has it checked out, saying in its own words what came of it (`AlreadyThere`, `Reset`, `Dirty`, `OffBranch`, `Unknown`, `AbortFailed`, `ResetFailed`). A rejected fix whose branch is where its check failed already no longer adds that its worktree has changes or another checkout. `test/restore-branch.test.ts` covers each outcome.
- tutti init's import cautions that workers will run in auto where orchestra's settings have no `worker_permission_mode` (§ 8.3; `src/init/import.ts`'s `SettingsImport.permissionModeUnset` and `importSteps`; tutti-r6ow): orchestra's workers then ran in the mode the user's Claude Code settings choose, often default, while tutti's import left `workers.permission_mode` at auto, more permissive, unsaid. Now the summary says `! import  worker_permission_mode isn't imported: …` and how to choose another mode. `test/import-permission-mode-unset.test.ts` covers it.
- tutti init's import cautions that workers will run in auto where orchestra's settings have no `worker_permission_mode` (§ 8.3; `src/init/import.ts`'s `SettingsImport.permissionMode` and `importSteps`; tutti-r6ow): orchestra's workers then ran in the mode the user's Claude Code settings choose, often default, while tutti's import left `workers.permission_mode` at auto, more permissive, unsaid. Now the summary says `! import  worker_permission_mode isn't set: …` and how to choose another mode, or, for a mode tutti can't take, that workers run in auto. `test/import-permission-mode-unset.test.ts` covers it.
- Workers get tutti's own requires tools (§ 4.11; `src/agent/requiresTools.ts`'s `makeRequiresTools` and `requiresServerFor`, the live AgentHost's `ownServer`, `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `withOwn`, `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `requiresNote`; tutti-j5fd): a worker that changed the requires list was told to run `tutti requires --readme` in a shell, which needs tutti on its PATH. Now each worker's session gets an in-process MCP server, `tutti`, made for its worktree: `requires_check` checks the worktree's list and installs what is missing with each entry's own install, `requires_readme` writes that README's section, allowed without asking. The requires note names them in place of the shell command. `test/requires-tools.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti init --update` writes its files as one (§ 8.3; `src/init/update.ts`'s `updateTemplates` over `src/init/staged.ts`'s `staging` and `commitStaged`; tutti-pud8): it rewrote the worker prompt, the verifier, the skill and `.tutti/settings.json` one by one, so a write that failed partway left the prompt updated and the settings recording the old versions, or the verifier old beside a new prompt. Now it stages them as init's setup does and puts them in together, the settings last, a failure taking back what it had done and saying `cannot write <file>: <why>; the setup's files are as they were`. `test/init-update-staged-writes.test.ts` fails each write in turn.
- tutti init says what the scout cost (§ 8.3; `src/init/checks.ts`'s `scoutCostSteps`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `cancelledEnding` and `withScout`, `src/organs/OrganCost.ts`'s `Cost.subtype` and `Total.failed`; tutti-ktn): the scout's organ cost went to a log init doesn't keep and its total was never read, so up to $2 on every init was spent unsaid. Now the summary starts with `✓ scout  cost $0.4321`, `!` where the call's result wasn't a success, on every ending, a cancelled form's too. `test/init-scout-cost.test.ts` covers it.
- The reviewer's attention section is capped too (§ 6.2; `src/run/report.ts`'s `cappedAttention` and `attentionCap`; tutti-c712): the attention requests, answers and denials of a run, as the log has them, were given to the reviewer whole, so a long run with many of them could still exceed its context. Past 500 lines or 50,000 characters it gets `[N earlier attention lines cut here, still counted in each ticket's tallies; the last 500 follow]` and the last lines. `test/report-attention-cap.test.ts` covers it.
- The reviewer's log is capped and the log appends in place (§ 6.2; `src/run/report.ts`'s `cappedLog` and `logCap`, `src/run/log.ts`'s `keptLines` and `Log.startBlockEnd`; tutti-601): the Log kept the run's lines by copying every earlier line on each write, and the reviewer was given the whole log, so an overnight run of a few hundred tickets could exceed its context and end `REVIEW_FAILED` with no report. Now the lines are appended in place, and past 1,500 lines or 150,000 characters the reviewer gets the lines up to the end of the START block, `[N lines of the log cut here, after the START block; the last 1500 follow]`, and the last lines. `test/report-log-cap.test.ts` covers it.
- The merge train's steps and a merge pass's outcomes are tagged (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`'s `Step`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `CheckOutcome`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `fixFailed`; tutti-7jnu): a train kept loose `failed`, `passed`, `head`, `why` and `setUp` fields, `failed` meaning three things, and `rebaseAndCheck` and `setUpAndCheck` returned `false`, a commit, `Unchanged`, a rebase stop or a check stop, told apart by `'files' in`. Now a train is `Rebasing`, `NotRebased`, `Checking`, `Passed`, `Failed` or `Stopped`, and a pass is `Aside`, `Checked`, `Unchanged`, `Conflict` or `CheckFailed`, each matched exhaustively. One line changes: a train whose commits change nothing on top of the branch ahead, which git could not put back, says so as its turn puts it back (`but it changes nothing on top of it`), not that its check was stopped. `test/merge-train-steps.test.ts` covers it.
- The report's prompt says what every exit code means (§ 5 item 10; `src/run/holds.ts`'s `exitMeanings`, read by `src/run/report.ts`'s `exitMeaning`; tutti-x99): it had no meaning for 2, so a run that `REQUIREMENTS_MISSING` stopped partway told the reviewer `Exit code 2 (unknown)`, and 4's named only Beads, git and the Agent SDK, not `WORKTREE_FAILED`, `STATE_UNWRITABLE` or `PANIC`. Now one table beside `exitCodes`, typed to have an entry for each code, gives each code's meaning with the stop words that end a run with it. `test/exit-meanings.test.ts` checks every code and stop word is covered.
- docs/analytics.md has ready-made DuckDB queries over the event stream (tutti-bqjq): each run's tickets merged, set aside and needing you, its minutes and cost; each ticket's cost, tokens, turns, minutes from dispatch to merge, time per phase, resumes and hand-backs; the checks' failures and durations by kind and command; how long each question and permission request waited for you; the usage-limit windows and the time held; the prompt cache's share of input tokens; each week across every project under `~/Projects`; and two runs side by side. They read `.tutti/run/events.jsonl` as it is, through one `events(path)` table macro; tutti doesn't embed DuckDB. `test/analytics.test.ts` runs each against `test/fixtures/analytics/events.jsonl` where `duckdb` is installed, which the requires list in `.orchestra/settings.json` now installs on macOS. README.md and docs/events.md link to it.
- The npm package no longer advertises `tutti update` (§ 12; `src/cli/version.ts`'s `markNpmBundle` and `buildKind`, called by `src/npm.ts`, `src/update/update.ts`'s `npm` kind; tutti-u85): its bundle is built as a release, so its runs checked GitHub daily and ended with `tutti v0.9.1 is available (you have v0.9.0): tutti update`, and `tutti update --check` said `tutti update installs it`, while `tutti update` refused it as tutti run from source and pointed to git and `scripts/install.sh`. Now the npm entry marks the build as the npm package's: its runs don't check for a newer release, and `tutti update` refuses it, `--force` too, and `--check` reports on it, both naming `bunx @noesis-sol/tutti@latest` and `bun add @noesis-sol/tutti@latest`. `test/update-npm.test.ts` covers it.
- The views wrap and cut text one way, between graphemes, a tab as four spaces (§ 7.2, § 4.5; `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `wrap`, `wrapText`, `cut`, `widthOf`, `padded` and `tabWidth`, used by `src/ui/Hint.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s header, `src/ui/Modal.tsx`, `src/ui/EndOfRunBox.tsx`, `src/print/initSummary.ts` and `src/print/markdown.ts`'s `wrap`; tutti-huy1): the header's and the hint's wrap and `cut` split a word by code point, so a flag 🇺🇸 or an emoji joined by ZWJ could be broken across lines, the boxes' wrap never broke a long word, and the report's split it by code point; and Bun.stringWidth counts a tab as no columns while OpenTUI draws it as two, so a question, description or reason with a tab pushed the worker view's panel frame's right border out, and with two the text was clipped at the pane's edge. Now one wrap and one cut serve every view, splitting between graphemes, with each tab four spaces before it is measured, and a wrapped line's indent gives way to a grapheme wider than the rest of it. `test/ui-wrap-graphemes.test.tsx` has the property tests.
- The views wrap and cut text one way, between graphemes, a tab as four spaces (§ 7.2, § 4.5; `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `wrap`, `wrapText`, `cut`, `widthOf`, `padded` and `tabWidth`, used by `src/print/summary.ts`'s `fit` (the dashboard's tickets table), `src/ui/Hint.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s header, `src/ui/Modal.tsx`, `src/ui/EndOfRunBox.tsx`, `src/print/initSummary.ts` and `src/print/markdown.ts`'s `wrap`; tutti-huy1): the header's and the hint's wrap and `cut` split a word by code point, so a flag 🇺🇸 or an emoji joined by ZWJ could be broken across lines, the boxes' wrap never broke a long word, and the report's split it by code point; and Bun.stringWidth counts a tab as no columns while OpenTUI draws it as two, so a question, description or reason with a tab pushed the worker view's panel frame's right border out, and with two the text was clipped at the pane's edge. Now one wrap and one cut serve every view, splitting between graphemes, with each tab four spaces before it is measured; a word longer than a line is broken at the line's edge where the old `wrap` broke it (a request's input, a tool call, a message) and starts a line of its own where prose wrapped (`long: "line"`: the header, the hint, the boxes, the report); and a wrapped line's indent gives way to a grapheme wider than the rest of it. `test/ui-wrap-graphemes.test.tsx` has the property tests.
- A plain run says its report's outcome once (§ 7.6; `src/run/report.ts`'s `runReport` and `ReportOut.logged`, set by `src/run/run.ts`'s `finish` when not styled, `src/print/organLine.ts`'s `OrganLine.drop`; tutti-e9h): the report wrote REPORT, REVIEW_FAILED, `report not saved` and the reviewer's skipped line to the log, which plain mode prints on stdout, then said the same on the printer, so `--plain` printed `REPORT: written to .tutti/reports/x.md` and then `◆ Report saved to .tutti/reports/x.md`. Now where the log prints on the report's output, the log's line is the only one: the printer's line is left out, and a step whose outcome the log says ends with no final line. `test/report-plain.test.ts` covers each outcome.
- A rebase a killed run's merge left in progress is aborted as the next run carries its ticket over (§ 4.8; `src/run/carry.ts`'s `abortLeftRebase`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `markHandedBack` and `handedBackOf`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `handOver`; tutti-m31): only the trains' rebases were undone at startup, so after a `kill -9` or a power cut while git rebased a ticket's branch, the next run put the ticket in line and set it aside `OFF_BRANCH` (`a rebase in progress, on a detached HEAD`). Now carry-over aborts the rebase of a closed ticket whose branch is marked `rebased_unchecked`, saying so, and the ticket merges, rebased again. A conflict hand-back notes `handed_back` on the mark, so a rebase its worker was resolving is left as it is. `test/carry-killed-rebase.test.ts` covers both.
- tutti login and tutti colours no longer need a valid project setup (§ 4.13, § 7.11; `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`'s `loadLenient`, `resolveAll` and `withScheme`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `loginCommand` and `coloursCommand`; tutti-kio): both built the run's whole configuration, which fails on any problem, so a `.tutti/settings.json` key written by a newer tutti, or `TUTTI_CONCURRENT=20` in the shell, stopped the login before Claude's ran, though the login is the user's, and stopped colours' preview. Now login reads only `--claude` (`TUTTI_CLAUDE`) and the billing, a settings file or user config it can't read giving their defaults, the user config's problems said as warnings; colours previews with what stands in for a problem, lists the setup's problems before the scheme's own, and exits 1. `test/lenient-config.test.ts` covers both with a bad settings file and variables.
- The title line fits a narrow pane by priority (§ 7.2; `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`'s `titleSegments`; tutti-u2k): it kept its left and cut the banners on the right, so at 80 columns with `· stopping`, usage at 85% and a `BLOCKED` hold it ended `…resets 14:05  BLO…`, and at 60 the hold was gone. Now it drops the branch and the time first, then shortens the usage banner to `usage 85% · resets 14:05` and `usage 85%` and drops it, then cuts the states, and keeps a hold's word whole. `test/title-line-narrow.test.ts` covers it at 60 and 80 columns, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- Ctrl+C cancels `tutti init` while it works between questions, before it takes its steps (§ 8.3; `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`'s working view and `openInitForm`, `src/init/init.ts`'s `InitForm.cancels`; tutti-298): the renderer's raw mode takes Ctrl+C as a key, and the working view did nothing with it until init caught stops, so while init read the ticket prefix through bd or waited on the lock, a hung bd or lock could only be killed. Now Ctrl+C there cancels init as it does in a field, interrupting what it reads, and says `Cancelled; nothing was changed.`; once init takes its steps it still stops init after the step under way. `test/init-cancel-setting-up.test.tsx` presses Ctrl+C while bd reads the prefix.
- The log's QUESTION and MCP elicitation lines say how to answer them with what `tutti answer` takes (§ 7.6; `src/answer/answerWith.ts`'s `answerWith`, used by `src/run/log.ts`'s `permission`, `question` and `elicitation` and `src/answer/answer.ts`'s `howToAnswer`, `src/agent/Attention.ts`'s `answersOf`; tutti-8se): a question's line said `allow --choice <option>`, one choice and no deny, though tutti answer refuses an allow without one `--choice` for each question, so following it in `--plain` for a two-question AskUserQuestion got the answer refused; an elicitation's said `answer in tutti`, with no screen in plain mode; and the listing left out `--confirm` for a question that defaults to no. Now one builder makes every request's `answer with:` text from the answers its file takes: `allow --choice "<option>" --choice "<option>" | deny`, `allow | deny` for a URL login, `deny` for a form. `test/answer-with.test.ts` follows each kind's line through the run's check.
- tutti says when git is too old (§ 5 item 1; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `minimumGitVersion`, `gitTooOld` and `version`, `src/run/startup.ts`'s `checkGit`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `mainCheckout`; tutti-v1i): tutti needs git 2.38 (`merge-tree --write-tree`), but stated no minimum, and an older git, such as Ubuntu 22.04's 2.34.1, refused `worktree list -z` with exit 129, which the startup checks read as `Not inside a git repository: cd into the project first.` Now the checks, `tutti init` and the commands that find the main checkout run `git --version` first and stop with `git 2.34.1 is too old: tutti needs git 2.38 or later. Update git, then run tutti again.` `test/git-version.test.ts` covers it, through a git that prints 2.34.1's version and refuses `-z`.
- Under `NO_COLOR` a focused button is drawn bold and reversed (§ 7.11; `src/ui/theme.ts`'s `StyleSpec.focus`, the form style `focusedButton`; tutti-6hz): it differed from the others only by its colours, so with every colour the terminal's default you couldn't see whether Allow or Deny would take your Enter on an attention card, nor which button the init form and the answer forms had focused. Now a focus whose background is the terminal's default, under `NO_COLOR` or a scheme leaving `formAccent` to `default`, is bold and reversed; coloured, it is as before. `test/ui-roles.test.tsx` checks it.
- A worker's Claude Code killed outright takes the commands it started with it (§ 3.2; `src/services/processTree.ts`'s `killTree`, `src/services/reaper.ts`'s `tree` mode, `src/agent/options.ts`'s `spawnInOwnProcessGroup`; tutti-6tc): Claude Code 2.1.301's Bash shells are detached, each leading a process group of its own, and only Claude Code's own shutdown stops them, so the SDK's SIGKILL 5 s after its SIGTERM, tutti's kill at `closeLimit` and a reaper's SIGKILL after its grace left a busy worker's check or dev server running, on its share of the cores, in a worktree tutti reuses or removes. Now each of them stops Claude Code's group, kills the groups of its descendants found with `ps -A -o pid,ppid,pgid`, and then the group. `test/process-tree.test.ts` kills a Claude Code with a detached command each way.
- A worker view's composer keeps what you typed once the worker has finished (§ 7.5; `src/ui/Composer.tsx`'s `finishedWords` and `finishedLine`, the composer's `send` now saying whether it sent; tutti-ceo): the field was emptied before the view's send found the ticket's card gone, so after the ticket merged, Enter wiped a long follow-up and sent nothing, with no word why. Now the view, once the card has gone, draws `<id>'s worker has finished: tutti can't send it messages` in place of the steered line, its placeholder says `the worker has finished`, and Enter keeps the text and toasts `<id>'s worker has finished: your message wasn't sent`. `test/composer-finished.test.tsx` merges the ticket while text is in the composer.
- tutti init's import question names a setting tutti has no place for without its value, and its descriptions keep a path whole (§ 8.3; `src/init/import.ts`'s `importSettings`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`'s `Paragraph`; tutti-4ep): it said `It can't import templates ({"worker_prompt":7,"verifier":5}): …`, a value that tells the reader nothing, and OpenTUI's word wrap, which also breaks after a `.`, `-` or `/`, left ` .` at a line's end and `orchestra/ is left as it is.` on the next. Now `terminal`, `templates` and the rest are named alone with why (a value tutti can't take, or a key it doesn't know, is still shown with it), and the form's descriptions, notes and about line are wrapped between words by tutti's own wrap. `test/init-description-wrap.test.tsx` covers both at every width from 24 to 120.
- A run's end refuses answers to the workers' requests once it has stopped the tickets' fibers (§ 4.5; `Attention.refuse`, `src/run/run.ts`'s end; tutti-t7z7): the worker's session stays open until the end closes it, and an answer through `tutti answer` in that window ran the worker on with no fiber watching, committing and closing its ticket while the run suspended it as waiting on you, which ended the ticket closed but not merged. Now the answer fails NotOpen, `request <id> is no longer open: the run is ending, and its ticket has stopped`, and `tutti answer` reports it.
- A run's end stops every ticket's fiber before its other steps (§ 4.8; `src/run/run.ts`'s end; tutti-5ko): it interrupted them only for a worker past its ticket limit or waiting on you at the end's own look, after the merges' stop and the look for tickets merged by hand, so an answer through `tutti answer` after the loop's last look, which the loop's end doesn't wait for, ran the ticket's fiber on into the end: it settled and merged the ticket under a run ending BLOCKED, or, its session closed, logged a PHASE_BUG after the summary and could change the exit code. Now a plain end interrupts the ticket fibers as soon as the loop has ended, as quitting does. `test/end-of-run-answer.test.ts` answers a waiting worker in that gap.
- The predictor no longer guesses the files of a ticket with commits on its branch (§ 5 Footprints; `src/run/predict.ts`'s `Predictor.queue`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s fill; tutti-sp8): it weighed each ready ticket's footprint without its branch, so a reopened or carried-over ticket whose text names no file looked empty, though its footprint is what its branch changes, and cost a predictor call and a `predicted_files` write for nothing. Now `queue` is given the fill's branch reader and reads a ticket's branch only when its text names nothing, once a run, queueing it only where the branch changes nothing either. `test/predict-branch.test.ts` covers it.
- The run's end notice counts its tickets as the dashboard does (§ 5.1 "One table counts the phases", § 7.9; `src/run/phase.ts`'s `counting`, read by `countsUnder`, `src/run/notices.ts`'s `finished`, `src/run/report.ts`'s sections and `src/run/runView.ts`'s wind-down marks; tutti-y0z): four places each said which phases were set aside or needed you, and disagreed, so a run ending with one `MERGE_CONFLICT` showed `Set aside 0 · Needs you 1` while its notification said `1 set aside`. Now one table says each phase's total, end notice, reviewer's section and wind-down mark, a phase without an entry failing to compile, and the notice says `· 1 needs you` for a ticket blocked from merging or waiting on a question. `test/phase-counting.test.ts` covers it.
- A git error as a ticket's worker stops, its ticket open with a blocker not merged, sets the ticket aside rather than holding the run (§ 5 item 9; `src/run/settle.ts`'s `read` and `leftNothing`; tutti-dxd): whether the worker left nothing was asked of git strictly there, unlike the check beside it, so a time-out on a loaded machine or a damaged index failed the ticket's fiber, the run held with `GIT_FAILED` and the ticket stayed Working to the end. Now a worktree git can't read counts as one with work in it in both checks, git asked once: the ticket is set aside, its worktree kept, and the log says `<id>: git can't tell whether its worker left anything in <path>: <why>; read as work left in it`. `test/settle-git-unknown.test.ts` covers a failure and a time-out.
- `scripts/build.sh host` stops with its message on a machine it has no target for (§ 12 Builds; `scripts/build.sh`; tutti-mc7): the target was read in an AND-list, where `set -e` doesn't stop on a failure, so on FreeBSD, riscv64 or armv7l, where install.sh sends a user to it, `host_target` failed, the shift was skipped and the loop read `host` forever, printing the message each time. Now it exits 1 after the message, once. `test/build-script-host.test.ts` covers it under sh, dash and bash.
- A merge rebases a ticket's branch onto the base's commit it read, not onto the base's name (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `rebaseAndCheck`; tutti-lm0): a commit made on the base by hand between the read and the rebase landed the branch on the newer base while the merge went by the older one, so a hand-back's check of its worker's resolution counted the base's new commits as the branch's own and rejected a valid one, and the setup before the check missed a lockfile those commits changed. Now the rebase goes onto the commit read, and the newer one is rebased onto in the merge's next pass. `test/merge-rebase-onto.test.ts` moves the base between the read and the rebase.
- A worker that answers a send at once no longer has its answer missed (`src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `subscribe`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s nudge, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`; tutti-5zpb): the continue nudge and the hand-back took the worker's messages from before their send through a forked `host.messages` stream, which subscribed to the worker's messages only once its fiber ran, so a reply published before then, as by a worker just started, was missed: the nudge waited on its worker going or closing, and the hand-back ran out its time limit and was ended late. Now they subscribe in their own fiber before the send, with `AgentHost.subscribe`, a scoped subscription. `test/fast-reply.test.ts` covers it.
- A failed full check's whole output is read from its worktree through the symlink guard (§ 9.1; `src/run/fullcheck.ts`'s `keepOutput` and `copyTo`; tutti-1plt): it was opened with a plain `openSync`, so a symbolic link the check left at `.tutti/run/check-full.log` or `setup.log` in the full check's worktree was followed, a file outside copied into the main checkout's `check-full.log`, and a named pipe there held the open on the JavaScript thread. Now it is opened with `Worktrees.openRunFile`, which refuses both at once, and the log says the output can't be kept, the run's log standing for it. `test/fullcheck-output-guard.test.ts` covers a link and a named pipe.
- A run that can't start always says why (§ 5 item 1; `src/run/startup.ts`'s `failures` and `collect`, `src/agent/claudeBinary.ts`'s `queryThrew`, `src/agent/Account.ts`'s `startProbeWith`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `make`; tutti-kq5): a defect in the login probe, the MCP servers, the worker prompt or the configuration left a StartupFailed with no problems, so tutti exited 2 printing nothing, and the SDK's `query()`, which throws as it is called where it finds no Claude Code for the platform (an install with `--omit=optional`, or a musl/glibc mismatch), was such a defect in the probe and made a worker's start die rather than fail. Now a defect is the problem line `Internal error in <check>: <message>`, and a `query()` that throws fails the probe as AccountProbeFailed and the start as AgentStartFailed, saying to reinstall with the optional dependencies or run another Claude Code with `--claude <path>`. `test/startup-defects.test.ts` covers both.
- The start question and the plan confirmation have the run's input guards (§ 7.3; `src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `ask`, `src/ui/keys.ts`'s `guardInput` and `InputGuards`; tutti-jomh): they took the mouse with none of the guards against OpenTUI #1661, so a mouse report split between two reads came as a lone Esc, which cancelled the form, or typed `<64;87;26M` into the description. Now ask() mounts `InputGuards`, and `guardInput` puts up one set of guards a renderer, shared by every screen that asks for them, so a form beside the run's input layers doesn't hold an Esc or filter a report's rest twice, and `InputGuards` keeps one keymap a renderer rather than one a mount. `test/form-guards.test.tsx` splits reports in both forms.
- `tutti requires` and `tutti requires --readme` work in the checkout they are started in, a ticket's worktree included (§ 8.1; `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `checkoutOf` and `tuttiWith`, `src/services/Git.ts`'s `topLevel`, `src/main.ts`; tutti-9t8): they found the main checkout, as the other subcommands do, so a worker that added a requires entry and ran `tutti requires --readme` in its worktree, as its prompt asks, wrote the README section from the old list into the main checkout, left there uncommitted (a dirty tree for the next run, `README_STALE` after the merge), and plain `tutti requires` checked the main checkout's list, not the new entry. Now `requires` uses `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` of the folder it is started in. `test/requires-worktree.test.ts` runs both in a worktree with an edited list.
- tutti init's ticket to get the runners passing, and the create-check-suite skill, run check-fast three times and check-full once (§ 8.3; `src/init/checks.ts`'s `testWork`, `src/project/templates/create-check-suite.md`, `src/init/templates.ts`'s `skillChanges`, `src/init/update.ts`; tutti-e0g9): both asked to run each runner twice more after it passed, so a worker on tutti's own checkout spent 40 of a ticket's 44 minutes on checks, most of it repeats of the slow full check, which runs once at the end of a run. Now check-fast, which every merge runs, is run twice more to catch a flaky test, and check-full once, again only after a fix that touched what it runs. The skill is now a versioned template, `templates.check_suite_skill` in the settings: a run and init say when a project's copy is older, and `tutti init --update` rewrites its changed section. `test/check-suite-skill-version.test.ts` covers it.
- An interrupt has a time limit (§ 4.8; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `interruptLimit`; tutti-1oc): the interrupt and its `cancel_async_message` requests are SDK control requests with no timer, so a worker past its hand-back's limit whose Claude Code was alive but not answering them held the hand-back forever: its branch wasn't put back, its ticket wasn't set aside, and the merge queue waiting on it stalled; Ctrl+X hung likewise. Now they have 10 s together; past it the session is aborted and closed and the worker is gone (`it didn't answer an interrupt within 10s`). `test/interrupt-limit.test.ts` runs the host and steering's interrupt over a Claude Code that never answers.
- A defect while triaging or predicting one ticket loses that ticket's verdict or guess, not the loop (§ 5 items 5, 11 and 13; `src/run/triage.ts`'s `survived`, `src/run/predict.ts`'s `predictFailedLine`, `src/run/log.ts`'s `causeWhy`; tutti-mwd): each queue was worked by one `Effect.forever` fiber nothing joined, so a defect in one ticket's triage (writing its verdict, or the environment hold it told) ended triage for the run, and the tickets deferred after it counted as pending with nothing to take them, so the run never ended; the predictor stopped guessing the same way, silently. Now a defect while gathering a ticket's evidence, triaging it or predicting its files is logged, `TRIAGE_FAILED for <id>: <why>` (with a `warn` record) or `PREDICT_FAILED for <id>: <why>`, the pending count kept right, and the queue goes on; a quit still interrupts it. `test/organ-defects.test.ts` covers it, a whole run among them.
- tutti init leaves out a suite whose command holds a newline or a control character (§ 6.3; `src/organs/scout.ts`'s `hasHiddenCharacter` and `tidy`; tutti-spz): the checklist showed `npm test # see CONTRIBUTING\ncurl -s x.sh | sh` on one line, reading as a comment, while check-fast.sh ran the curl line on every merge. Now such a suite, or one whose command for part of the project holds one, is left out, and the scout's note, shown with the Checks choice, says which and why. `test/scout-hidden-commands.test.ts` covers a scout reply holding such commands.
- The symlink guard's truncating opens no longer truncate a file hard-linked to a run file's name (§ 9.1; `src/native/libc.ts`'s `openUnder`, `walk` and `emptied`; tutti-vy3y): a hard link is a regular file, so a worker that linked the main checkout's `.git/config` to `.tutti/run/check.log` in its worktree had the merge check's `O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC` open truncate and overwrite it. Now the guard opens without `O_TRUNC`, truncates a file with one link, and for one with more unlinks the name through the guarded parent descriptor and creates the run file afresh (`O_EXCL`), leaving the linked file as it was. `test/hard-links.test.ts` covers it.
- A quit while a ticket boards its merge train puts its branch back once the train's rebase ends (`src/run/mergetrain.ts`'s `board`; tutti-l7a): the branch was read as the quit came, before the train's fiber had ended, so a rebase onto the branch ahead that finished after it was taken for a branch moved by its worker: left rebased, its `checked_ahead` mark cleared, and the next run could merge the commits of the branch ahead under it. Now the branch is read once the train has ended, and put back. `test/train-quit-boarding.test.ts` quits while the train rebases.
- tutti init no longer writes `scripts/check-full.sh` as `exit 0` beside a check-fast.sh it keeps (§ 8.3; `src/init/init.ts`'s `writeRunner` and `writeRunners`, `src/init/runners.ts`'s `runsNothing`, `src/init/checks.ts`'s `choiceScripts` and `runnersDiffer`, `src/init/form.ts`; tutti-fqt): whether check-fast.sh checks nothing came from the suites chosen, not the runner on disk, so *create from scratch* with *Replace scripts/check-fast.sh?* answered No, the default, and no check-full.sh wrote one that checked nothing, and the end-of-run full check didn't even run check-fast.sh. Now check-fast.sh is written first and check-full.sh from what is there afterwards, `exit 0` only where that runs nothing but `exit 0`; the form asks whether to replace a check-full.sh that differs beside the check-fast.sh answered for. `test/init-kept-fast-check.test.ts` covers it, running both runners.
- A merge's cleanup resets the ticket's branch on a base named like a top-level file or folder (§ 3.3; `src/services/Git.ts`'s `revisions` and `resetBranch`; tutti-nn2x): `git reset --hard --quiet main`, with a `main/` folder beside the base `main` (or `docs` beside `docs/`), failed with `ambiguous argument 'main': both revision and filename`, so the reset was skipped, `branch -d` refused the unmerged branch, and the ticket closed with `cleanup failed`, `tutti/<id>` left behind. Now every revision the Git service gives git goes through one helper, after `--end-of-options`, with any pathspecs after `--`; the reset is given the hash its target names, then `--`, as git before 2.44 takes `--end-of-options` for a revision in reset. `test/git-quirks.test.ts` runs a base named like a folder, branches named like a file, signed commits under `log.showSignature=true` and names that aren't ASCII against real git and the in-memory layer.
- A usage limit's reset says its weekday and date when it isn't today, `resets Fri Oct 23 14:05`, in the banner, cards, warning line, log lines, notification and stop line (§ 4.12, § 9.1; `src/run/localTime.ts`'s `resetLabel`; tutti-su2): a seven-day reset on Friday, or a five-hour one after midnight, read `resets 14:05` as if today. The log's stamp carries its UTC offset (`2026-10-17 09:05:03 +02:00`), so the hour the clocks go back over no longer reads twice, and the report's title and the reviewer's closing line give the end's date when the run crossed midnight. tutti's local times (the log's stamp, the event stream's time, dates, the report's name and span) now come from that one module.
- The event stream records what runs spend (§ 10 "What runs spend"; `src/run/spending.ts`'s `Spending` and `endRecord`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `accounted` and `ending`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `recorded`, `src/organs/OrganCost.ts`; tutti-0xi5): cost and turns were only in each worker's transcript and organ costs only in log lines. Now each worker turn writes a `turn` record from its SDK result, with its own share of the session's running totals (cost, round-trips, time, API time, tokens by model); each check and setup a `check` record (orchestra's `suite`, `seconds`, `result`); each organ call an `organ` record with the ticket it was about; each ticket whose part in the run ends a `ticket_end` record, and its `closed`, `deferred` or aside `warn` record orchestra's `phases`, `hand_backs`, `carried` and `tokens`, with its `cost_usd`; and the `end` record the run's `tokens`, `cost_usd` and `organs`. A live contract test confirms `total_cost_usd` and `modelUsage` are running totals that a resume carries on. `test/spending.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's answer keys answer only a request drawn with its buttons (§ 4.5 "Answering the request you read"; `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `answerKey` and `drawnHint`; tutti-d0d): below 60×16, on a compact line and behind the end-of-run box, the oldest request was still the target, so `a` allowed a Bash call whose card was never drawn, its 500 ms guard long run out, and the hint offered `d deny · a allow`. Now `d`, `Shift+D`, `a` and `r` open the Answer box on the highlighted request there, the guard starts once a card's buttons are drawn, and the hint leaves out `d` and `a`. `test/dashboard-answer-drawn.test.tsx` covers each place.
- A click beside an open box no longer reaches the view under it, and a view's own box closes as the view goes (§ 7.3; `src/ui/Modal.tsx`'s `Backdrop`, `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/InterviewView.tsx`; tutti-por): boxes took the keys but not the mouse, so Ctrl+X then a click on `← Dashboard` left the interrupt question open with nothing drawing it or binding its Esc, and the dashboard took none of its keys until Ctrl+C then Esc. Now each box draws a backdrop over the whole screen that stops every mouse event, and the worker view's interrupt question and the Interview view's leave question close when their view unmounts. `test/ui-box-mouse.test.tsx` covers it.
- Every file tutti writes whole goes through one writer, `writeFileAtomic` (§ 3.3 "Files written whole"; `src/services/atomicFile.ts`, used by `src/services/Store.ts`, `src/services/Worktrees.ts`'s `ensureExcluded`, `src/run/machine.ts`, `src/run/report.ts`, `src/init/workers.ts`'s `markTrusted` and `applyUnion`, `src/init/init.ts`, `src/init/checks.ts`, `src/init/update.ts`, `src/project/requires.ts`, and its pieces by `src/init/staged.ts`; tutti-q8fy): four temporary-file writers differed in syncing, following links, temporary names and cleanup, and init's and the README's writes truncated in place. Now each write follows links, keeps the file's mode, writes a temporary file of its own name, syncs it, renames it and syncs the folder, removing the temporary file where it fails; init's staged setup provides its own writer (`AtomicWriter`). A symlinked `~/.claude.json` stays a link when init marks a checkout trusted, and a `.gitattributes` init can't read fails the step rather than be rewritten with the one line. `test/atomic-file.test.ts` covers links, modes and failures.
- A glob with many `**`s no longer freezes the run, and a fill reads only the files it compares (§ 5 Footprints; `src/run/footprint.ts`'s `globMatch`, `collapseAny`, `askedFiles`, `readMentions` and `Sources`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `Footprints.mentions`; tutti-crj): `globMatch` tried every split of a path at each `**`, so a files metadata glob of seven `**`s took 3.6 s on 200 files 13 folders deep, on the JavaScript thread, every fill; and each fill read up to 4 MB of every file of every footprint compared, though contents matter only to place the functions a footprint names. Now a run of `**`s is kept as one and the match remembers the ways that failed, so a glob costs its parts times a path's; and the contents read are those of the pairs compared where one names a function, sharing no area or function, each file read once a fill. `test/footprint-bounded.test.ts` covers it.
- A quit while a worker starts no longer saves a session Claude Code never made (§ 4.8's Session IDs; `src/run/start.ts`'s `start` and `initLimit`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `begun`, `src/run/run.ts`'s end; tutti-y3d): the session ID was saved in state.json before the session started, so Ctrl+C in a run's first seconds said `<id>'s session is saved for the next run`, and the next run resumed a session with no transcript, failed, and left the ticket for review (WORKER_GONE), not retried. Now the ID is saved once the session's first `system/init` comes (`AgentHost.begun`, the worker's `started` fact); a start that fails or is interrupted before then ends the session, under one `Effect.onExit`, and saves nothing, an earlier attempt's session included, so the next run starts the ticket afresh, in the same worktree. A session whose `system/init` doesn't come within a minute of its first message is a failed start (START_FAILED). `test/start-session-saved.test.ts` and `test/start-quit-fresh.test.ts` cover it.
- The symlink guard's opens refuse a named pipe, a socket or a device rather than wait on it (§ 9.1; `src/native/libc.ts`'s `openUnder` and `NotAFileRefused`; tutti-v50): open(2) on a named pipe waits for its other end, and through `bun:ffi` it waited on the JavaScript thread, so `mkfifo .tutti/run/check.log` in a worker's worktree, or a named pipe at an answer file's or a transcript's name, held tutti, its screen, its sessions and its signals, until `kill -9`. Now the last component is opened with `O_NONBLOCK`, and what isn't a regular file (or a folder where one is asked for) is closed and refused, `.tutti/run/check.log under <worktree> is a named pipe, not a regular file`; a regular file has `O_NONBLOCK` cleared. The merge check then runs with its output dropped and the log saying why, and an answer file so refused is removed with `it is a named pipe, not a regular file` as its reason. `test/guarded-opens.test.ts` covers it.
- tutti init's import takes orchestra's `worker_permission_mode` as `workers.permission_mode`, its `manual` as `default` (§ 8.3; `src/init/import.ts`'s `mapped` and `converted`; tutti-4xj): it was left out as `not an orchestra setting tutti knows`, so a project that chose `manual` or `acceptEdits` got tutti's `auto`, more permissive than it chose. `usage_limit_wait`, `overnight_free_disk_gb`, `spec_timeout`, `spec_effort` and `questions` are left out with why tutti has no place for them, not as unknown. `test/import-orchestra-keys.test.ts` covers each key.
- tutti init and --update keep `$` in the commands they write into the worker prompt and the verifier (§ 4.11; `src/init/checks.ts`'s `verifierText`, `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `withSuites` and `forTicket`; tutti-4yc): they filled the templates with `String.replace` and a string, which reads `$$`, `$&`, `` $` `` and `$'` as patterns, so a suite `PORT=$((3000 + $$ % 100)) bun test` came out as `PORT=$((3000 + $ % 100)) bun test`, `$'\t'` pasted the rest of the template in at that point, and each --update took another `$` off `$$`. Now a function gives each replacement, so the text goes in as written. `test/template-dollar.test.ts` puts `$` sequences in suite commands, the stack and a ticket's ID.
- A resume no longer runs a second Claude Code on a session still closing (§ 4.8 "Lifetime"; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `Lifecycle`, `closedWhy`; `WorkerEvent`'s `session`, `Workers.open`'s session; tutti-j3w): a worker ended over a setup problem mid-turn kept its Claude Code running 2 s or more after it was Gone, and settle resumed the same session ID at once, so two Claude Codes shared one session file and worktree. Now each ticket's session is one lifecycle, Starting, Open or Closing, in place of the live host's open map, its reserved set and its flags: a start or resume waits for a Closing session's Claude Code to exit, close or end while a session starts cancels the start (it fails `it was closed while it started`), close of a session gone waits for its Claude Code to exit, and every WorkerEvent names its session, a worker folding only its session's. `test/agent-host-lifecycle.test.ts` covers it.
- tutti init's full check runs each test once (§ 6.3, § 8.3; `src/organs/scout.ts`'s `covers_fast`, `ci_build` and `cost`, `src/init/runners.ts`'s `fullScript` and `coversFast`, `src/init/checks.ts`'s `ciBuild` and `Scouted`'s `fullTicked`, `src/init/form.ts`'s `fullChecklistDescription`; tutti-tr3j): init wrote `scripts/check-full.sh` as check-fast.sh, then the scout's full suites, so a full suite that already ran every test, as `TUTTI_SLOW=1 scripts/check.sh` does in tutti's own checkout, ran them all twice, and a release compile found only in CI (a 340 MB host build) was ticked in. Now the scout says which full suite covers the fast ones, and check-full.sh runs that one alone, without check-fast.sh first, saying so in its header and the suite's comment, as the verifier and init's summary do; the scout's prompt asks for full suites as only what the fast ones don't run, or marked as covering them. Build and packaging steps found only in CI are offered in check-full's checklist unticked, with what they cost. A project's own full runner is kept unless *Replace scripts/check-full.sh?*, No by default, is answered yes. `test/init-full-check-once.test.ts` covers it, tutti's own case among them.
- exec no longer waits forever on output a leftover process holds open (§ 3.3 "Time limits"; `src/services/exec.ts`'s `LeftoverOutputGrace` and `leftoverOutputNote`; tutti-b2f): a process that left the command's group (setsid, a detached spawn with inherited stdio) kept its pipes open after the leader had exited and the group had been killed, and exec waited for it to exit: `sh -c "perl -e 'use POSIX; POSIX::setsid(); sleep 20' & sleep 1; echo started"` with a 3 s limit returned after 20 s, and a check, setup command or git hook that left a server running so would stall the merge queue past any time limit. Now exec reads on for `LeftoverOutputGrace` (2 s) once the group has been killed, then stops and returns the exit code (or CommandTimedOut) with the output so far, its stderr, output file and `onOutput` saying that a process the command left running held the output open. `test/exec-leftover-output.test.ts` covers it.
- A closed stdout no longer ends tutti early (§ 7.6; `src/print/organLine.ts`'s `guardOutput` and `stdout`, `src/main.ts`'s `main`; tutti-ypm): in Bun a write to a closed pipe is an uncaught 'error' event, so with `tutti --plain | head -200` the run's report printer, the first to write after head had gone, ended tutti with exit 1, racing the report's save, the `REPORT` line, the end event and the scope's finalizers, the lock's release among them. Now tutti puts one 'error' listener on stdout and stderr as it starts, which marks the stream closed, and the printer writes nothing to it after that: the run finishes, writes its report and exits with its own code. `test/closed-stdout.test.ts` closes the pipe mid-run.
- A passing environment probe lifts only its own stop (§ 4.9; `src/run/holds.ts`'s `swapStop`, `src/run/run.ts`'s loop; tutti-h6g): it cleared every stop, so a BLOCKED, TICKET_LIMIT, PAUSED or GIT_FAILED raised while it waited, on a worker waiting on you, was lost with its banner and exit code, and the run took tickets again with the request still open. Now the probe's pass lifts its ENVIRONMENT stop and its failure takes that stop's place, the stops raised meanwhile kept. `test/environment-probe-stops.test.ts` raises BLOCKED and TICKET_LIMIT during the probe's wait and ends the run on them, exit 3.
- The usage-limit hold lifts on what the account says, never in a loop (§ 4.12; `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `Hold`, `Reset`, `resetOf`, `liftTime` and `retryEvery`, `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`'s `newTurn`; tutti-2t9): a reset already past, from a clock ahead of the server's, lifted the hold at once, and the workers carried on into the same refusal at request speed, each round a notification and a model request per worker; a hold with no reset known (an organ's 429 alone) never lifted while workers ran, so no ticket started for the rest of the run; and a worker's rejection outlived its turn, so a later turn failing on a network error read as the limit and held the run again. Now a reset at or before now is unknown and tried again 15 minutes on, an `allowed` event for the held limit a minute or more into the hold lifts it, lifts are at least 15 minutes apart, and a new turn clears the last one's rejection. `test/usage-limit-lift.test.ts` covers a past reset, an unknown reset with workers still running, and a failed turn after a lift.
- The forms' quiet text is lighter on a dark terminal, a departure from orchestra (huh's Charm theme, bubbles' help) on purpose (§ 7.11's form roles; `src/ui/schemes/orchestra.json`, `src/ui/theme.ts`'s `formFaint` and `form.faint`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`, `src/print/initSummary.ts`; tutti-qt3): on a near-black background the descriptions (`ansi:243`), the key hints (`#626262`, `#4A4A4A`, `#3C3C3C`) and the placeholders (`ansi:238`) read dim to barely there, and `tutti init`'s faint text no better. Now the dark variant has `formMuted` `ansi:248`, `formHelpKey` `#9A9A9A`, `formHelpDesc` `#7A7A7A`, `formHelpSeparator` `#5A5A5A` and `formPlaceholder` `ansi:243`, in every form, and a new role, `formFaint`, draws init's about line, repository, steps to come, scout's clock, working line and the summary's faint parts: `default`, faint as before, on light, and `ansi:245`, not faint, on dark. The light variant is unchanged. A scheme that gives every role without `extends` now gives `formFaint` too, or tutti uses `orchestra` and says why. `test/init-faint-text.test.tsx` covers it.
- tutti init asks tickets at the same time as orchestra's init does, as a choice rather than a number typed in (§ 8.3; `src/init/form.ts`'s `concurrencyOptions`, `concurrencyStart` and `readConcurrency`; tutti-e7s): `1  · one at a time (safest)`, `2  · checks must cope with running side by side`, `3  · that, and room for three workers and their checks`, `4  · that, for four: a busy machine`, and `Custom…  · type a number, up to 16`, which then asks the number. It starts on the current setting's option, or on Custom… with the number filled in above 4; the line form (TERM=dumb) offers the same choices by number, Enter keeping the number. The memory note, about 1 GiB a worker, is in the question and under the answer. `test/init-concurrency-choice.test.ts` and `test/ui-init-form.test.tsx` cover each option, Custom… and a value above 4.
- The machine's cap counts the slots the run's scheduler counts taken, not every ticket it has going (§ 8.2; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `slots`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `ownWorkers`; tutti-qye): tickets waiting to merge or merging, which take no slot up to `concurrent` of them, no longer hold the run at `HOLD: MACHINE` with a slot free, stop the merge train checking ahead, or count in the run's registration for the other runs.
- Workers are opened by their card alone, without numbers (§ 7.2, § 7.3; `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/run/commands.ts`'s `OpenWorker`, `src/run/screen.ts`'s `Screen.Worker`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s hint; tutti-zhz), a departure from orchestra's `1`–`9` on purpose: cards were click targets and `Tab` selected one, yet each card began with its worker's number and `1`–`9` opened workers 1 to 9, leaving workers 10 to 16 without one, and the hint said `1–0 open` with only the environment probe's card. Now cards show no number, in the compact layout either, each line starting with its mark and the ID straight after it; `1`–`9` open nothing; the hint says `Tab select · Enter open`; and the worker view switches workers by way of the dashboard, `Esc` then `Tab` and `Enter`, `Tab` staying the transcript's. `OpenWorker`, the worker view's screen and the dashboard's highlight name the card's ticket rather than its number, so the highlight stays on its card as others come and go, and leaves with it rather than pass to the worker that takes its place. `test/open-by-ticket.test.tsx` covers it, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- `scripts/test.sh` reruns a test that timed out even when its code failed an expect after bun had moved on (§ 13; `scripts/test.ts`'s `timeoutLeftovers`, `betweenTestErrors`, `stackFiles` and `parseJUnit`'s `timedOut`; tutti-06a): the error between tests that left made the run fail without reruns, so a timeout on a loaded machine, the most common flake, was never rerun (`bd's prefix match … show takes another ID` in a full check, 5 s, where it takes under 1 s alone). Now each error between tests whose stack has a frame in the file of a test that timed out is left to that test's rerun, `FLAKY:` when it passes; any other error between tests still fails the run without reruns. `test/timeout-rerun.test.ts` covers both.
- A failed write no longer leaves tutti init's setup half done (§ 5 item 1, § 8.3; `src/init/staged.ts`'s `staging` and `commitStaged`, `src/init/steps.ts`'s `InitFailed`, `src/run/startup.ts`'s `setUp`; tutti-5i0): a `.claude/` owned by root or a full disk failed init after it had written the worker prompt and the runners, and the next tutti, counting the project set up, merged every ticket unchecked. init now stages its setup's files in memory and writes them together, each to a temporary file then renamed over its own, the settings last, taking back what it wrote where one fails; it then lists the steps it took and names the file that failed. A project is set up once its settings are there: a worker prompt without them has tutti say to run tutti init.
- The forms take clicks, each doing what its key does (D9, § 7.3; `src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `clickable`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`, `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/PlanConfirmation.tsx`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `initMouse`; tutti-okv): `tutti init`'s Yes and No buttons, a select's options and a checklist's took keys only, and its renderer left the mouse to the terminal. Now a click on Yes or No answers, on a select's option chooses and confirms it, on a checklist's option ticks or unticks it, on the line to type in focuses it, and on the scout's `Esc: skip` line skips it; the start question's options and the plan confirmation's `y` and `N` in `[y/N]` take a click as well, as the answer form's rows and buttons did. `tutti init` takes the mouse unless `--no-mouse`, its new flag, or `"mouse": false` in the user config says not. `test/form-clicks.test.tsx` clicks each kind of target, and `docs/dashboard.md` and `docs/setup.md` say so.
- The forms take clicks, each doing what its key does (D9, § 7.3; `src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `clickable`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`, `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/PlanConfirmation.tsx`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `initMouse`; tutti-okv): `tutti init`'s Yes and No buttons, a select's options and a checklist's took keys only, and its renderer left the mouse to the terminal. Now a click on Yes or No answers, on a select's option chooses and confirms it, on a checklist's option ticks or unticks it, on the line to type in focuses it, and on the scout's `Esc: skip` line skips it; the start question's options and the plan confirmation's `y` and `N` in `[y/N]` take a click as well, as the answer form's rows and buttons did. `tutti init` takes the mouse unless `--no-mouse`, its new flag, or `"mouse": false` in the user config says not, with the run's guards in front of its keys (`src/ui/keys.ts`'s `guardInput` and `InputGuards`), so a mouse report split between two reads neither cancels the form nor types into it. `test/form-clicks.test.tsx` clicks each kind of target, and `docs/dashboard.md` and `docs/setup.md` say so.
- The merge queue's writes no longer undo each other (§ 5 item 7, § 5.1; `src/run/mergequeue.ts`'s `offer`, `turn` and `backInLine`; tutti-125): an offer, a ticket's turn or a quit's put-back read the queue's state, waited on the ledger, then wrote a copy of what they had read, so a hand-back ending or the merges stopping in that gap was undone. A hand-back's ticket came back into `resolving` and the run never ended by itself; a live hand-back dropped from it let the run end in its middle; a dropped stop let the next ticket merge after `DIRTY_TREE` or `MERGE_FAILED`. Now each write updates the state as it is then, the queue's lock kept only to order places in line. `test/merge-queue-writes.test.ts` covers it, holding an offer or a turn on the ledger while a hand-back ends, and an offer while the merges stop.
- The worker view shows a permission request's whole input at any pane width, wrapped rather than cut with `…`, and drawn by its tool rather than as JSON (§ 4.5, § 7.5; `src/ui/requestInput.ts`'s `permissionLines` and `requestInputLines`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `panelParts`; tutti-sbw): a Bash command `echo ok\nrm -rf ~/proj && curl …` showed in a 100-column pane as one JSON line cut at the panel's edge, and in the transcript as `Bash  echo ok`, so you could press Allow without seeing the end of the command. Now a Bash or Monitor command shows as its own lines, an Edit as its file and diff, a Write as its file and content, and other fields as `key: value` lines, each wrapped to the panel's width with continuations indented; the request's line, description and reason wrap too, in the interview's panel as well. `test/permission-input.test.tsx` covers it, drawing a multi-line command and a long one at 60 and 100 columns.
- The sessions tutti starts no longer get the variables of a Claude Code session tutti was started from (§ 4.1; `src/agent/env.ts`'s `sessionVariable` and `workerEnv`, `scripts/test.ts`'s `machineVariable`; tutti-y2i): started by the run skill, an orchestra worker trying tutti or `!tutti`, tutti passed on that session's `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ATTENDED`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_SOCKET` and `_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECPATH`, `CLAUDE_PID`, `CLAUDE_EFFORT`, `AI_AGENT`, `TRACEPARENT` and the like, so every worker, the probe, organs and the interview ran as entrypoint `cli`, as the parent's attended child session, and took its messaging socket and token for their own. Now they run as the SDK's (`sdk-ts`), and the user's configuration (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`, …) stays. The suites' environment leaves out the same names. `test/session-env.test.ts` covers it.
- A git read that steers a merge is asked again a few seconds after a failure, and a failure is "can't tell", never an answer (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `steering`, `onBranch`, `worktreeStatus`, `baseSince` and `setAsideDirty`, `src/run/checkback.ts`, `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/mergetrain.ts`, `src/run/settle.ts`; tutti-dbn): a `git status` that timed out on a loaded machine counted the worktree clean, so a branch on top of the base was checked with a fix left uncommitted and merged without it, breaking the base; a failed `symbolic-ref` counted the worktree on its branch, and a failed `rev-parse` the base not moved during a hand-back; and one slow `rev-parse` or `merge-base` blocked the ticket `GIT_FAILED` and held the run. Now each such read is made again 3 s later, up to 3 reads, as orchestra asks git up to 3 times, and gives a tagged result whose failure is `Unknown`: a worktree git still can't read sets its ticket aside for review, `GIT_FAILED: <id> closed, but git could not read the status of <worktree> (3 tries): …`, the other merges going on; the main repository git still can't read blocks the ticket and holds the run as before; a hand-back whose base git can't read goes back to its merge, which reads where its branch is again; and a worktree whose branch git can't read is never reset. `test/merge-git-retry.test.ts` and `test/handback-git-unknown.test.ts` cover each read failed once and always.
- A taller pane gives the dashboard's Current cards room, not the tickets table (§ 7.2; `src/ui/Card.tsx`'s `keptLines`, `src/ui/TicketsTable.tsx`'s `tableLines`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`'s `tableHeight`; tutti-et9p), a departure from orchestra on purpose: the table took about a third of the pane, so six workers' cards went on compact lines in an 80×50 pane that holds them whole. Current is now kept room for a full card for each of up to 6 of the workers the run may have, two to a row from 120 columns, and a line for each of the rest, and the table takes what is left, 3 to 10 rows. Its height still comes from the pane and the run's workers alone, so it stays put while workers come and go. `test/ui-cards.test.tsx` and `test/ui-dashboard.test.tsx` cover it, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- `scripts/install.sh` stops on an x64 CPU without AVX2, before downloading anything, and installs the darwin arm64 build in a shell under Rosetta (§ 12 Install; `scripts/install.sh`'s `cpu` and `sysctl_value`; tutti-20p): tutti embeds Claude Code, which ships only Bun's standard x64 builds, and those need AVX2, so on an older x64 CPU tutti crashed at once with no word of why. Now the script reads `avx2` among `/proc/cpuinfo`'s flags on Linux and `sysctl -n hw.optional.avx2_0` on macOS, and without it says tutti and Claude Code need a CPU with AVX2 (Intel Haswell, 2013, or AMD Excavator, 2015, or later); where it can't tell, it says so and installs. Where `sysctl -n sysctl.proc_translated` is 1, it installs darwin arm64, which runs natively, rather than darwin x64, and says so. `TUTTI_TEST_CPUINFO` replaces `/proc/cpuinfo` in the script's test mode. `test/install-script-cpu.test.ts` covers each case, its fixtures now in `test/install-script.ts`, and `docs/setup.md` says so.
- The release builds start faster (§ 12 Builds; `scripts/build.sh`; tutti-gsn): each target is now compiled with `--bytecode --format=esm`, so its JavaScript ships precompiled. On an M-series Mac `tutti --version` starts in 85 ms rather than 218 ms, and `tutti plan` in a small repo in 0.7 s rather than 1.2 s, for 17 MB more binary. `--format=esm` is needed because Bun's default CommonJS output fails on OpenTUI's top-level `await`. `test/build-script.test.ts` checks the flags.
- `scripts/install.sh` installs into `~/.local/bin` by default, making it where it doesn't exist, rather than `/usr/local/bin` where it could write, which Homebrew owns on many Macs; `/usr/local/bin` only through `TUTTI_INSTALL_DIR`, which it now makes too (§ 12 Install; `scripts/install.sh`'s `install_dir`; tutti-nu2). The release workflow's publish job attaches the tag's `scripts/install.sh` as `install.sh`, and the README, `docs/setup.md` and the tutti skill pipe `https://github.com/noesis-sol/tutti/releases/latest/download/install.sh` rather than main's, so the script matches the archives it downloads. The script's test mode drops `TUTTI_TEST_SYSTEM_DIR`. `test/install-script-dir.test.ts` covers the default, the override and the PATH note for each shell, and `test/install-script-release.test.ts` the release asset and the instructions' address.
- A ticket merges only at the commit its merge check passed on (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `setUpAndCheck`, `prepare`, `branchHead`, `notePass` and `fastForward`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/checkback.ts`, `src/run/handback.ts`; tutti-4jh): the commit noted as passed was read after the check, and the base fast-forwarded to the branch's name, so a commit made while the check ran, as a worker's you messaged in `ToMerge` or `Merging`, or one made by hand, merged unchecked. Now the commit checked is read before the setup and check, a failed check's hand-back stops there too, `prepare` is `Ready` only while the branch is still at it, and the fast-forward goes to that hash; a branch that moved on is checked again first (`tutti/<id> moved on to <commit> while its check ran on <commit>: checking it again`), and one that moves on during every check is blocked `MERGE_CONFLICT`. `test/merge-checked-commit.test.ts` covers a commit made during the check, during every check, and between the check and the fast-forward.

## [0.1.1] - 2026-10-22

- A Release's notes stay within GitHub's limit (`scripts/release-notes.sh`): GitHub refuses notes over 125,000 characters (HTTP 422), and 0.1.1's section, every change since tutti began, was 253,019, so its release failed to publish after every build had passed. A longer section is cut after the last whole entry within 120,000 characters (`RELEASE_NOTES_LIMIT`), and a last line says how many more there are and links to `CHANGELOG.md` at the version's tag. `test/release-notes.test.ts` covers it.
- A whole-run test could end in `PANIC in the run: Expected an integer` (§ 10; `src/run/runView.ts`'s `interval`, `src/agent/Attention.ts`'s `answered`): RunView's 30 Hz throttle slept `1000 / 30` ms, TestClock moves to each sleep's end, so a test's clock could stop at a fraction of a millisecond, and `attention_answered`'s `after_ms`, a count in the event schema, came out fractional. The throttle now waits 33 ms, and `after_ms` is rounded where it is taken. Live runs weren't affected (their clock is `Date.now()`). `test/run-view.test.ts` pins the interval to whole milliseconds.
- The tests run their files in parallel (§ 13; `scripts/test.ts`'s `testWorkers` and `coreThreadVariables`): `scripts/test.sh` gives `bun test --parallel=N` as many worker processes as the run's share of the cores (`TUTTI_CORES`), or half the machine's cores (a merge check runs without a share, beside the workers' own checks), and no more than the files it is given, each worker's JavaScriptCore threads kept to its part; `TUTTI_TEST_PARALLEL` sets N. A test's time limit is 15 s where its file sets none, not bun's 5 s, as the files running side by side load the machine. One after another the suites kept one of ten cores busy: on the same loaded machine six workers took 89 s where one process took 139 s, and the full check took 99 s where it had taken about 4 minutes on a quiet one. Every merge check, and each worker's own check, is faster for it. A failed test's rerun still runs in one process, so `FLAKY:` lines are unchanged. Two tests whose limits assumed an idle machine are given room (`test/git-service.test.ts`'s rebase limit, `test/check-verdict.test.ts`'s time limit). `scripts/check-full.sh` is one pass again, `TUTTI_SLOW=1 scripts/check.sh`, rather than `tutti init`'s check-fast.sh, every test again and a build.
- A form's hint draws each key, its words and the ` · ` between them in bubbles' help greys, as orchestra's huh forms do, rather than the whole line in the description colour, which in a light terminal was the terminal's own text colour, as dark as the options (§ 7.11; the new form roles `formHelpKey`, `formHelpDesc` and `formHelpSeparator`, `src/ui/Form.tsx`'s `formHintSegments` and `Hint`, `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`'s `hintOf`, `src/ui/PlanConfirmation.tsx`; tutti-4dz): keys light `#909090` / dark `#626262`, their words `#B2B2B2` / `#4A4A4A`, the separator `#DDDADA` / `#3C3C3C`. `test/form-hint.test.tsx` and `test/ui-roles.test.tsx` cover it, and `docs/colour-schemes.md` lists the roles.
- `--machine-workers` defaults to half the cores, at least 2, rather than no cap, as orchestra 0.9.3's `DefaultMachineWorkers` (§ 8.2; `src/run/machine.ts`'s `defaultMachineWorkers`, `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`, `src/run/startup.ts`; tutti-kkb.50): in the M3 demo orchestra held at its cap of 5 while tutti, beside it, ran 3 workers regardless. Workers mostly wait on the model, but their checks are CPU-heavy, and runs slowed sharply past about 4 checks at once on 10 cores. `0` is still no cap. tutti reads orchestra's `ORCHESTRA_MACHINE_WORKERS` after `TUTTI_MACHINE_WORKERS`, as the cap is the machine's. The `START` line says the cap and what gave it, `machine workers: 5 (default: half of 10 cores)`, and a run whose `concurrent` is above the cap says so under it. `test/machine-workers-default.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- The title line draws `· shared with N runs` in `notice`, as orchestra draws it in its deferred yellow, rather than faint (§ 7.11; `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`; tutti-3ls), found in the M3 demo's light pass. `test/shared-runs-title.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view's header and hint wrap onto more lines in a narrow pane rather than cut (§ 7.5; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/Hint.tsx`'s `hintLines`, `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `wrapped`; tutti-rfs): in a pane about 85 columns wide the header cut the ticket's title (`Add tools/li…`) and the hint ended in `Enter…`, hiding keys you need. Now the title goes on under the head, breaking between words, and the hint breaks between keys onto as many lines as it takes, a toast drawn above all of them (`src/ui/Toasts.tsx`'s `useHintHeight`). Neither is in the transcript's scrollbox, so their height doesn't touch OpenTUI #1514. `test/ui-worker-view-narrow.test.tsx` covers it, with layouts at 60 and 40 columns.
- The worker view wraps a message sent to the worker and a tool call's summary to the pane's width, as Claude Code's own screen does, rather than cutting each to one line with `…` (§ 7.5; `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `wrap`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`'s `toolLines`, `promptLines`, `ClickLines` and `itemLines`; tutti-7pv): a call wraps under its summary, its mark at the end of its last line or on a line of its own; a message keeps its lines, `› ` before the first. Collapsed, either takes at most 6 lines, the last cut with `…`; Enter or a click shows a message whole. tutti wraps them itself and gives each item's box the height of its lines, so that the height changes only with the pane's width, and only grows as a call's result comes: scrolling back through the transcript stays put rather than being pulled to the bottom as OpenTUI's `stickyScroll` does when the layout shrinks (#1514). `test/ui-worker-view-wrap.test.tsx` covers it.
- A merge that changes the requires list in `.tutti/settings.json` on the base has the run take the new list and check it again before the next ticket starts, as orchestra 0.9.1's `takeRequires` and `recheckRequires` (§ 8.4; `src/run/setup.ts`'s `takeSettings` and `SetUps.requires`, `src/run/requires.ts`'s `runRequires` and `sameRequires`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `takeRequires`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/agent/options.ts`; tutti-kkb.49): the run checked the list only as it started, so a ticket that added a requirement left the tickets after it to fail on it. Now the merge says `REQUIRES: <id>'s merge changed the requirements in .tutti/settings.json on main: they are checked before the next ticket starts`, the check installs what it can, and what is still missing stops the run `REQUIREMENTS_MISSING`, exit 2, the running tickets finishing first (a `HOLD` line). Workers started or resumed from then on are told the list as it is now, not the one the run started with. A list that doesn't decode is `SETTINGS_UNREAD`, which now says the requirements stay as they were. `test/requires-recheck.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/setup.md` and `docs/running.md` say so.
- A hand-back's time limit leaves out the time the machine is loaded, up to `resolve_timeout` again, as orchestra 0.9.0's does (§ 4.7; `src/run/handback.ts`'s `clock` and `give`, `src/run/load.ts`'s `pressing`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `MergeOptions.loaded`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/checkback.ts`; tutti-kkb.47): a worker's tests run several times slower on a machine short of memory or cores, so its 20 minutes could run out while it worked. Now, while the worker isn't at the usage limit, the hand-back's clock samples every 5 seconds whether the run holds at `--machine-workers` or the dashboard's last load reading has the memory pressure or the CPU red, or swapping under way, and leaves that time out. The line ending a hand-back past its limit says how much more it was given: `its worker is working after 20m (and 5m more for the machine's load), with the hand-back not done`. `test/handback-load.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/workers.md` and `docs/dashboard.md` say so.
- A ticket deleted from bd is no longer taken for another one whose ID starts with its own, as orchestra 0.8.5 fixed it (§ 3.3; `src/services/Beads.ts`'s `issueFor`, `show` and `confirmed`, `src/project/planFile.ts`, `src/init/checks.ts`; tutti-kkb.39): bd answers such an ID with that ticket (a deleted `t-x` with its follow-up `t-x.1`), and its writes act on it, so tutti read the follow-up's status, labels and parent for the deleted one, and its closes, labels and notes went to it. Now an answer by another ID is bd having no such ticket (`bd has no such ticket: bd show t-x --json gave t-x.1, another ticket`), and each write shows its ticket first, writing nothing for a deleted one; a ticket filed under a deleted parent, or linked to a deleted ticket, is not filed. The plan and `tutti init`'s test tickets, filed under IDs bd has just given, skip the shows. `test/beads-prefix-match.test.ts` covers it, with a slow test against a real bd.
- A branch a run killed outright (SIGKILL, a crash, a power cut) left rebased onto the branch ahead, to be checked ahead of its turn, is put back as its worker left it as the next run starts (§ 4.8, § 5 item 7; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`'s `aheadKey`, `rebasedAheadOf` and `putBackLeft`, `src/run/setup.ts`'s `offBaseKey` and `makeSetUps`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-yb4): quitting and the merges stopping put such a branch back, but a killed run left it carrying the commits of the branch ahead, which the next run merged with it when that ticket didn't merge. Before a train rebases a branch, state.json marks the ticket (`checked_ahead`) with the branch's commit as its worker left it, the commit it is rebased onto and, once rebased, the commit it is rebased to; boarding the train or putting the branch back clears the mark. As the next run starts, before carry-over, a branch still marked and at the commit it was rebased to is reset to where its worker left it, saying `tutti/<id> was rebased onto tutti/<ahead> to be checked ahead of its turn by a run that was killed before its turn: put back as its worker left it, at <commit>`; one that moved since is left as it is, saying so. Dependencies set up on top of the branch ahead are set up again before the ticket's next check, in the next run too: the setups' mark of a setup off the base is kept in state.json (`set_up_off_base`) until a setup runs. `test/train-killed-run.test.ts` covers it, with a saved state.json in whole runs.
- A hand-back kept for the usage limit's reset that the run's end overtakes is taken back and named in the closing line (§ 4.12, § 5.1; `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `unsent`, `handBacks`, `neverHanded` and `AtReset.waits`, `src/run/handback.ts`'s `clock`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `limitedOnly` and end; tutti-vrp): a run ending `USAGE_LIMIT`, or quit, before the reset left the ticket `Stopped` in `Resolving` mid-rebase, its worker never sent the hand-back, and the next run set it aside `OFF_BRANCH`. Now a conflict hand-back's rebase is aborted, so that the next run merges the ticket again and hands the conflicts back, and the closing line says `b's worker, at the usage limit, was never handed its conflicts to resolve: its rebase is aborted, for the next run to merge b again` (`its failed check to fix: the next run merges b again`). The run counts a worker at the limit on a hand-back as waiting for the reset only once its hand-back waits for it, not while it is handed over or what its worker left is checked, and the hand-backs under way stop as the run ends `USAGE_LIMIT`. `test/handback-unsent.test.ts` covers it, with a worker whose session ends while its hand-back waits for the reset, and the hold's keep with the hold off; `docs/running.md` says so.
- A project lists what its machine must have in `"requires"` in `.tutti/settings.json`, as orchestra 0.9.1's requires list (§ 8.4; `src/project/settings.ts`'s `Requirement`, `src/project/requires.ts`, `src/run/requires.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/startup.ts`, `src/init/requires.ts`, `src/init/init.ts`, `src/init/import.ts`, `src/cli/requires.ts`, `src/cli/commands.ts`, `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `requiresNote`, `src/agent/options.ts`, `schema/settings.json`; tutti-kkb.27): each entry a `name`, a `check` command and an `install` command (never `sudo`, refused as the settings load) or a `manual` step for a person, optionally limited to `darwin` or `linux`. Each run checks the list before its first ticket and any worker it carries over, installing what it can (the output in `.tutti/run/requires-<name>.log`) and checking again, says so under its `START` block (`REQUIRES: jq met`), and stops with one `REQUIREMENTS_MISSING` line naming what is still missing and what to do, exit 2, rather than leave every ticket to fail on it. `tutti init` checks the list after the login probe, a line of its summary each, and `tutti requires` (`--no-install` to only check) prints each one's state. The README's section on what to install is generated from the list between `<!-- tutti:requires -->` and `<!-- /tutti:requires -->`: `tutti requires --markdown` prints it, `tutti requires --readme` writes it, `tutti init` writes it into a README that has none and keeps it up to date, and a run's start warns `README_STALE` when it differs. Workers are told the list's entries, and that what a ticket needs of the machine goes in it, never installed by hand outside it: the worker prompt template's version 6, which `tutti init --update` takes. orchestra's `requires` imports as it is. `test/requires.test.ts` and `test/requires-run.test.ts` cover it, and `docs/setup.md`, `docs/running.md` and `docs/workers.md` say so.
- The dashboard says whether the workers are overloading the machine, on a line of its own under the title line, a blank line between: `▶ 4 of 4 workers · memory 6.2 GB (workers 4.8, merge check 1.4) · pressure normal · CPU 9.8/10 cores`, read every 5 seconds while it shows, and each worker's memory (`1.2 GB`, `? GB` when its processes can't be found) on its line in Current (§ 7.2, § 7.11; `src/run/load.ts`, `src/ui/LoadLine.tsx`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/Totals.tsx`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `Status.load` and `LoadView`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `processes`; tutti-kkb.25), as orchestra 0.8.5 and 0.8.6's load line: the memory of each worker's Claude Code with its process group and all below them, and of what tutti runs besides, from one `ps` listing; the kernel's memory pressure, swapping while under way and the 1-minute load against the cores, from `sysctl` and `vm_stat` on macOS and `/proc` on Linux. On macOS memory used against the total says little; the pressure level and the swap rate do. Each figure is in its own signal's colour: the CPU `notice` past 1.5 times the cores and `needsYou` past 3 times for a minute, the pressure `needsYou` at warn or worse, swapping `notice`, the memory uncoloured. The totals strip loses its Workers column to it; a pane too short for a line a worker drops the blank line first. `test/load.test.ts` and `test/load-line.test.tsx` cover it, with fake readings for each colour and platform, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- `tutti init` offers Claude in Chrome among the MCP servers once the user's `.claude.json` records it set up (`claudeInChromeDefaultEnabled`, `cachedChromeExtensionInstalled` or `hasCompletedClaudeInChromeOnboarding` true, read leniently), labelled `built into Claude Code, drives Chrome on this machine`, as orchestra's Discover does; a chosen Chrome that isn't set up is noted in the question and cautioned in the summary (§ 4.6, § 8.3; `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `definedServers`, `src/init/workers.ts`'s `serverNotes` and `mcpSteps`; tutti-ali).
- A worker already at the account's usage limit when its ticket is to be handed back is handed it once the limit lifts, rather than the ticket being set aside (§ 4.7, § 4.12; `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`, `sendAtReset`, `AtReset` and `workerAway`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `whyNotFix`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `atReset` and lift, `src/run/holds.ts`'s `usageHolds`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `MergeOptions.atReset`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-kkb.48), as orchestra 0.8.0 has it: since tutti-kkb.2 a resolution or fix finished as the limit hits is taken from git and checked while its worker is still at the limit, and a check failing on it, or a conflict at its merge, set it aside (`its worker is at the account's usage limit`, `the run holds (USAGE_LIMIT)`). Now the ticket moves to `Resolving` or `Fixing`, the log says `<id>'s worker is at the usage limit: it is handed its conflicts to resolve once the limit lifts, the wait not counting toward the 20m`, and the usage-limit hold sends the hand-back at the reset as the worker's carry-on (`<id>: the usage limit has reset; its worker is handed its conflicts to resolve`), `resolve_timeout` counting from the hand-back's turn. `test/handback-at-limit.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/workers.md` and `docs/running.md` say so.
