- A worker already at the account's usage limit when its ticket is to be handed back is handed it once the limit lifts, rather than the ticket being set aside (§ 4.7, § 4.12; `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`, `sendAtReset`, `AtReset` and `workerAway`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `whyNotFix`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `atReset` and lift, `src/run/holds.ts`'s `usageHolds`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `MergeOptions.atReset`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-kkb.48), as orchestra 0.8.0 has it: since tutti-kkb.2 a resolution or fix finished as the limit hits is taken from git and checked while its worker is still at the limit, and a check failing on it, or a conflict at its merge, set it aside (`its worker is at the account's usage limit`, `the run holds (USAGE_LIMIT)`). Now the ticket moves to `Resolving` or `Fixing`, the log says `<id>'s worker is at the usage limit: it is handed its conflicts to resolve once the limit lifts, the wait not counting toward the 20m`, and the usage-limit hold sends the hand-back at the reset as the worker's carry-on (`<id>: the usage limit has reset; its worker is handed its conflicts to resolve`), `resolve_timeout` counting from the hand-back's turn. A worker you steer while it waits is sent nothing, the hand-back set aside as one not done (`you steered its worker while it waited for the usage limit`). `test/handback-at-limit.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/workers.md` and `docs/running.md` say so.
- `"keep_awake": true` in `.tutti/settings.json` keeps the machine from sleeping while a run goes, the organs of its report included (§ 8.4; `src/run/keepAwake.ts`, `src/project/settings.ts`, `schema/settings.json`, `src/main.ts`, `src/init/import.ts`; tutti-kkb.45), as orchestra's `keep_awake`: as the run begins, tutti starts `caffeinate -i -w <its PID>` on macOS, or `systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep … tail --pid=<its PID> -f /dev/null` on Linux, through `effect/process` in the scope of the run's services, which stops it once the report is done; the log says `keeping the machine awake while the run goes: <command>`, and says so should the command end by itself. Where the system has no such command, or it isn't on the PATH, the `START` block warns once and the run goes on. Off by default; orchestra's `keep_awake` imports to it. `test/keep-awake.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/setup.md` says so.
- A hand-back's clock stops while its worker waits at the account's usage limit, and a finished result is taken from git (§ 4.7, § 4.12; `src/run/handback.ts`'s `give`, `clock`, `endLate`, `handBackTaken` and `handBackOver`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `fixCheck`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `carryOnHandBack`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `limitedOnly`; tutti-kkb.2), as orchestra 0.8.0's and orchestra-nr1v.1 and -nr1v.2 have it: a worker whose hand-back turn ended on the limit never settled, so the wait counted toward `resolve_timeout` and the ticket was set aside after 20 minutes. Now the wait doesn't count (`<id>'s worker is at the usage limit on its hand-back: it carries on once the limit lifts, the wait not counting toward the 20m`), and the reset tells it to carry on with the hand-back, not its ticket; a rebase finished or a fix committed as the limit hits is taken from git and checked without waiting for the reset; a run with nothing else to do waits for the reset, or ends `USAGE_LIMIT`, the hand-back left as it is. A hand-back past `resolve_timeout` takes a rebase finished or a fix committed as it is, the worker stopped and told so; otherwise the worker is stopped and told the hand-back is over, its branch put back and its attempt's commit kept in git, before its branch is put back. `test/handback-usage-limit.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/workers.md` and `docs/running.md` say so.
- A branch on top of the base whose worktree has uncommitted changes is set aside before its merge check, `DIRTY_WORKTREE` (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `prepare` and `setAsideDirty`; tutti-nuk), as a rebased one is: the check ran on the worktree with those changes while only the branch's commits fast-forwarded the base, so it could pass on code that never reached it. The line says `<id> closed, but <worktree> has uncommitted changes to <files>, so tutti/<id>, on top of <base>, can't be checked as it would merge; worktree left for review (commit or undo them, check, merge)`. `test/merge-on-top.test.ts` covers it.
- The log and the event stream give happenings in one order (§ 3.3; `src/run/log.ts`'s `writeWith`, `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `transition` and its `lines`, and the run's modules that wrote a line and its record; tutti-kkb.44), as orchestra 0.8.5 found its own did not: a happening's line in `.tutti/tutti.log` and its record in `events.jsonl` were written by two calls, a line then its record in one place and a record then its line in another, so a fiber could write its own between them, and events emitted at once, such as a merge's closed line and the next ticket's dispatch, came out in one order in the log and another in the stream. Now the line and the record are written together under the log's lock, a ledger transition logging the lines it is given with its records; a line whose transition the state machine doesn't have is no longer logged, as its record isn't. `test/happening-order.test.ts` covers it, with fibers writing at once to the live log and stream.
- A worktree removal that fails after a ticket set aside with nothing done writes its CLEANUP_FAILED line and its `warn` record together, line first (`src/run/aside.ts`'s `dropNothingDone`; tutti-jtp), as the rest of the run's happenings do since tutti-kkb.44: it was written after that ticket's base, as a record then its line. `test/aside-nothing-done-order.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket waiting to merge that is checked ahead of its turn says how that check goes on its card's action line, as orchestra 0.8.6's dashboard (mergeTrain.activity) (§ 5.1; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`'s `CheckAhead` and `checksAhead`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/runView.ts`'s `Status.checksAhead` and `checkAheadText`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-6c8): `rebasing onto tutti/a, to be checked ahead of its turn on top of it as it is checked`, `checked ahead of its turn, on top of tutti/a as it is checked`, `passed its check ahead of its turn, on top of tutti/a: merges with no further check if tutti/a merges as checked`, or `failed its check ahead of its turn, on top of tutti/a: checked again in its turn`, until its turn has taken its train. The run logged these steps, but the card said only `to merge (1st)`. `test/merge-train-dashboard.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- A glob in a ticket's `files` metadata holds the files other tickets add where it matches, as orchestra 0.8.0's does (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/footprint.ts`'s `Footprint.globs`, `RepoFiles.listed`, `globHolds` and `sharedFile`; tutti-kkb.40): a glob was resolved only against the files git tracks, so a ticket listing `internal/dispatch/*.go` ran beside one adding `internal/dispatch/cart.go`, and one listing `web/src/*.ts` before there was a `web/` beside one adding `web/src/cart.ts`. A glob that matches files is kept anchored at each folder it matched in (`dispatch/*.go` as `internal/dispatch/*.go`), and one that matches none as it is, from the repository's root; each holds the files and folders other tickets name, or their worktrees add, that it would match, and two globs that can match one file overlap. A name with a `[` (`pages/[id].tsx`, `src/routes/[slug]/`) is that file or folder as well as the glob. The run's scheduler and `tutti plan` read them alike, and the footprint's log line names the globs after the files. `test/footprint-globs.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- Selected text under `NO_COLOR`, and for a scheme that leaves `selection` or `selectionText` to `default`, is drawn in tutti's default scheme's selection colours, `orchestra`'s `#FAFAFA` on `#7D56F4`, read from `src/ui/schemes/orchestra.json` (§ 7.11; `src/ui/line.tsx`'s `selectionLookOf`; tutti-ck8), where it was the palette's slots 0 on 7. § 7.11 names selected text as the one exception to `NO_COLOR`, so a selection stays visible. `test/clipboard.test.ts` and `test/ui-roles.test.tsx` cover it.
- A UI test's frame waits for the text still being highlighted (§ 13; `test/render.ts`'s `settle`; tutti-8wh): `<markdown>` draws a block only once tree-sitter has highlighted it, off the render loop, and on a loaded machine that finished after the renderer went idle, so `test/sent-messages.test.tsx`'s worker view test captured a frame without the worker's text and failed about once per check, passing on its rerun. `capture()` now waits, up to 4 s, until no `<code>` renderable is highlighting.
- The dashboard's title line says `· shared with 1 run` (`· shared with 2 runs`, …) while other tutti or orchestra runs share the machine, faint after the states (§ 7.2; `src/run/runView.ts`'s `Status.shared` and `TitleView.shared`, `src/run/run.ts`'s `lookAround`, `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`; tutti-kkb.38), as orchestra 0.8.5's dashboard does: the run keeps the count of other runs its last look at the folder of runs found. `test/shared-runs-title.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- The npm package is MIT-licensed (§ 12 npm; `package.json`, `LICENSE`; tutti-hc6), as decided in tutti-kfo: `package.json` had no `license` and the repository no LICENSE file, so `@noesis-sol/tutti` would have been published with none. `package.json`'s `license` is `MIT`, and `LICENSE` at the repository's root holds the MIT License, which `bun pm pack` and npm put in the package, and `scripts/build.sh` in each release archive. `test/npm-license.test.ts` covers it.
- Ctrl+C while `tutti init` sets the project up stops it cleanly, the setup written as one group (§ 8.3; `src/init/steps.ts`'s `runSteps`, `src/init/init.ts`, `src/cli/commands.ts`'s `stoppedEnding`, `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`, `src/print/initSummary.ts`; tutti-kkb.31), as orchestra 0.9.0's does (orchestra-qa9p): once the questions are answered, SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP, or Ctrl+C in init's renderer (`Stopping after this step…`), interrupted init wherever it was, between the worker prompt and the settings say, so a run could take a setup without its merge check, or did nothing in the renderer. Now init takes its steps in turn (Beads, the ticket prefix, the setup, the test work, the login probe) and checks for a stop after each. The ticket prefix and the setup (the worker prompt, the runners, the skill, the verifier, the settings, the .gitignore and the changelog's merge) run to their end; the other steps are interrupted. init prints the steps it took, then `tutti init: stopped …` naming where, such as `once it had written the files above, before filing the test work: tutti init again files it`, with exit code 130. `test/init-stop.test.ts` and `test/ui-init-stop.test.tsx` cover it, the first with a stop at each step in turn and a SIGINT to `tutti init` on real git.
- A ticket set aside as its worker settled with nothing done has its worktree and branch removed (§ 5 item 9; `src/run/aside.ts`'s `dropNothingDone`, `src/run/work.ts`'s `leaveAside`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s `nothingDone`, `src/run/environment.ts`; tutti-kkb.42), as orchestra 0.8.1's does: a ticket its worker deferred, or that tutti deferred for a worker that left it unfinished, with no commits beyond the base and a clean worktree, has them removed once triage has its evidence, its line saying `<id> deferred by its worker; nothing done, so its worktree and branch are removed` (`<id> still open -> noted and deferred; nothing done, …`). Such tickets left their worktrees behind. One with commits or uncommitted changes keeps both, for review, and a removal git refuses is `CLEANUP_FAILED`. `test/aside-nothing-done.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/workers.md` says so.
- A worker stopped by the account's usage limit keeps its ticket in progress (§ 4.11, § 4.12; `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `usageLimitNote`, `src/agent/options.ts`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s `undeferAtLimit`; tutti-kkb.41), as orchestra 0.8.1's does: a note after the check note in every worker's rules, the project's own prompt included, says to commit what it has and stop at the limit, without deferring or closing its ticket for it, as it carries on after the reset; and a worker that defers its ticket all the same as it stops at the limit has it put back in progress, with a note saying why and the log saying `<id>: its worker deferred it as it stopped at the usage limit; back in progress, it waits for the reset`, so it waits in the hold and carries on after the reset rather than being set aside as deferred. `test/usage-limit-deferral.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- A stop (Ctrl+C, SIGTERM) during `tutti plan --apply` stops it between two links, saying on stderr which links it added and which not, exit code 130, as orchestra 0.9.0's (§ 8.1; `src/plan/plan.ts`; tutti-kkb.43): `tutti plan: stopped. Added 1 of 3 links: t-1 waits for t-2. Not added: t-5 waits for t-4, t-8 waits for t-3.` The link under way is added whole. A stop as it reads the tickets and the code says `tutti plan: stopped before proposing any link; nothing changed.` It ended partway with no word. `test/plan-stop.test.ts` covers it.
- The next ticket waiting to merge is checked while the one ahead is, as orchestra 0.8.6's merge train (orchestra-8qk2) with 0.9.0's blame (orchestra-nr1v.10) (§ 5 item 7, § 8.4; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `CheckWatch` and `setUpAndCheck`, `src/run/setup.ts`, `src/project/settings.ts`, `schema/settings.json`; tutti-kkb.18): each merge held the queue through its check, so the queue bounded how many tickets an hour merged. While a ticket is checked in its turn, the next one waiting for its first turn is rebased onto that ticket's branch as it is checked, in its own worktree, and checked there beside it (`checking tutti/<next> ahead of its turn to merge, rebased onto tutti/<id> as it is checked`); when the ticket ahead merges as it was checked, the next merges in its turn with no second check (`… which merged as it was checked: merging it with no further check`). Otherwise its branch is put back as its worker left it and rebased and checked in its turn, the line saying whether the branch ahead didn't merge as checked or its own check failed on top of it, which is neither handed back nor sets it aside. `merge.train` says how many are checked ahead (1 by default, `0` for none; orchestra's `merge_train` imports to it); none is while the run holds at the machine's cap, nor a ticket back in line after a hand-back, a recheck or a conflict you handed over. Quitting, or the merges stopping, puts back the branches rebased ahead of their turn. `test/merge-train.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/setup.md` say so.
- The next ticket waiting to merge is checked while the one ahead is, as orchestra 0.8.6's merge train (orchestra-8qk2) with 0.9.0's blame (orchestra-nr1v.10) (§ 5 item 7, § 8.4; `src/run/mergetrain.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `CheckWatch` and `setUpAndCheck`, `src/run/setup.ts`, `src/project/settings.ts`, `schema/settings.json`; tutti-kkb.18): each merge held the queue through its check, so the queue bounded how many tickets an hour merged. While a ticket is checked in its turn, the next one waiting for its first turn is rebased onto that ticket's branch as it is checked, in its own worktree, and checked there beside it (`checking tutti/<next> ahead of its turn to merge, rebased onto tutti/<id> as it is checked`); when the ticket ahead merges as it was checked, the next merges in its turn with no second check (`… which merged as it was checked: merging it with no further check`). Otherwise its branch is put back as its worker left it and rebased and checked in its turn, the line saying whether the branch ahead didn't merge as checked or its own check failed on top of it, which is neither handed back nor sets it aside; a worktree set up on top of the branch ahead is set up again before that check. `merge.train` says how many are checked ahead (1 by default, `0` for none; orchestra's `merge_train` imports to it); none is while the run holds at the machine's cap, nor a ticket back in line after a hand-back, a recheck or a conflict you handed over. Quitting, or the merges stopping, puts back the branches rebased ahead of their turn. `test/merge-train.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/setup.md` say so.
- `CLOSED_UNCOMMITTED` names the files a ticket closed with left uncommitted (§ 5.1's Working → Review; `src/run/settle.ts`'s `changeEntries` and `listChanges`, `src/run/work.ts`; tutti-kkb.19), as orchestra 0.9.0's does (orchestra-gsaa): each with its git status code, untracked files (`??`) included, the first five then `and N more`, and says to commit or remove them, in the log's warning and the ticket's note: `<id> closed (<commit>) but <worktree> has uncommitted changes: M src/a.ts, ?? out/build.bin; worktree left for review (commit or remove them)`. A stray build output, untracked, held a ticket for review with nothing to say which file it was. `test/settle-uncommitted.test.ts` and `test/settle.test.ts` cover it.
- A failed check's lines that say what failed name the tests other runners say failed, as orchestra 0.9.0's (orchestra-nr1v.5) (§ 3.3; `src/services/Checks.ts`'s `failedTest`; tutti-kkb.21): bun's `(fail)` and `✗`, jest's `✕` and `●`, vitest's `×`, node's `✖`, pytest's `FAILED path::test` and `path::test FAILED`, cargo's `test … ... FAILED`, `test result: FAILED` and a test's panic, mocha's `1) …` and `N failing`, TAP's `not ok`, and the counts of failures jest, vitest, pytest and bun end with, where only go test's, bun's, tsc's and Biome's were, so a hand-back names the failing test rather than only its assertion. A count of none, bun's ` 0 fail`, is no longer said, and Biome's closing `× Some errors were emitted` stays out. `test/checks-runners.test.ts` covers it, with each runner's output.
- A ticket set aside or blocked and merged by hand while the run goes on is found as the base moves on (§ 5 item 8, § 5.1's Review and Blocked → Done; `src/run/handMerged.ts`'s `mergedByHand` and `removeMerged`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `dropRechecks`, `src/run/drain.ts`, `src/run/carry.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-kkb.20), as orchestra 0.9.0's (orchestra-nr1v.8, -nr1v.9): the run asked git only as it started, so such a ticket stayed set aside, held the tickets it blocks and was counted set aside in the report. Each time the base moves on, before the tickets set aside for a failed check are checked once more, and once more as a run not quit ends, each ticket closed but not merged is asked of: one on the base now is Done, `<id>, set aside in this run, is on main now (<commit>): merged by hand, so no longer set aside; worktree and branch removed`, not checked again, its `unmerged` label removed, the tickets it blocks free; one an earlier run left `unmerged` loses its label. A carried-over ticket dropped as merged by hand has its worktree and branch removed too, where they stayed behind; a worktree with uncommitted changes is kept with its branch, and the line names its files. A drain, or a run that ends stopped, names each ticket it keeps from its promised check once more (`<id> is not checked again in this run, as the run winds down; it stays set aside`), which it dropped without a word, and a ticket set aside while the run winds down isn't promised one. `test/hand-merged.test.ts` and `test/hand-merged-run.test.ts` cover it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- `WAITS_FOR_MERGE`, `DEFERRED_FREE` and the Nothing to run box agree with the holds on a blocker closed by hand while its branch is off the base (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/closedBranches.ts`'s `asking`, `startBranches` and `offBase`, `src/run/settle.ts`'s `unmergedBlockers`, `src/run/work.ts`'s `requeueWaiting`, `src/run/deferredFree.ts`'s `startPending`, `src/run/nothing.ts`'s `heldAtStart`, `src/main.ts`; tutti-kkb.36), as orchestra's pendingWhy: only the scheduler asked git, so a worker that linked such a blocker and stopped with nothing done was nudged rather than put back in the queue, a Waiting ticket went back in the queue while it hadn't merged, a deferred ticket it blocked was listed as freed, and a run whose only ready tickets it held went ahead rather than show the box. The run's ClosedBranches answers them in the run; before it, git is asked once for each blocker, without `CLOSED_UNMERGED`. `test/closed-unmerged-pending.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- The dashboard's tickets table no longer repeats the tickets Current shows, Current's compact list lines up its columns, and a ticket to merge is marked with a square (§ 5.1, § 7.2, § 7.11; `src/ui/TicketsTable.tsx`'s `rowsBesideCurrent`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/ui/Card.tsx`'s `compactLines`, `src/run/ledger.ts`, `src/run/runView.ts`; tutti-kkb.30), as orchestra 0.8.5 and 0.8.6's (orchestra-60xg, orchestra-lx4d.4, orchestra-1dvh, orchestra-7dpo): beside Current the table leaves out every ticket with a card, whatever its phase, so that a ticket is shown once a frame, and lists the tickets that came to an end, in that order, a ticket's row moving to the end as it leaves Current; with the end-of-run box in Current's place, the running tickets keep their rows. The compact Current list pads its workers' numbers, IDs, statuses and times to the widest on screen, the numbers and times right-aligned, so that the titles start at one column. A ticket waiting for its turn to merge reads `◼ to merge`, and `◼` on its card, in place of the clock `◷`, which stays for a ticket waiting on another. `test/dashboard-beside-current.test.tsx` covers it, and `docs/dashboard.md` says so.
- The parity matrix (the spec's Appendix A, § 1.1 Goal 1) covers orchestra since v0.7.7 (tutti-kkb.33): a second table, *Since orchestra v0.7.7*, goes through orchestra's changes from 0.8.0 to 0.9.1 and those unreleased after it, each done or planned with its tutti ticket, or already so or not needed with the reason, so the parity audit no longer misses them. The changes no ticket covered got theirs: bd's answer for a deleted ticket's ID (tutti-kkb.39), globs holding new files in footprints (tutti-kkb.40), a worker stopping at the usage limit (tutti-kkb.41), a set-aside ticket's empty worktree (tutti-kkb.42), `tutti plan --apply` stopped (tutti-kkb.43), the log's and the event stream's order (tutti-kkb.44), `keep_awake` (tutti-kkb.45) and overnight runs (tutti-kkb.46). `docs/parity.md` says so, and `test/parity-since.test.ts` checks the table.
- The worker prompt no longer asks a worker to keep a ticket's acceptance criteria as a to-do checklist (TodoWrite or TaskCreate) (§ 4.11; `src/project/templates/worker-prompt.md`; tutti-kkb.12), as orchestra's no longer does (orchestra-b48z): bd prime, which reaches workers through the user's settings, forbids those tools, and in about 725 orchestra worker sessions none kept one. The rule on compaction says to keep the ticket's notes current. An existing project keeps its prompt: `tutti init --force` writes it afresh. `test/prompt-no-checklist.test.ts` covers it.
- Workers, the login probe and the feature interview start at effort `high` unless `workers.effort` or `--worker-effort` chooses another level (§ 8.4; `src/project/settings.ts`, `schema/settings.json`, `src/cli/flags.ts`; tutti-kkb.14), as orchestra's do (orchestra-jvi4): `workers.effort` defaulted to `null`, Claude Code's own default. `null` still leaves it to Claude Code. `test/default-effort.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/setup.md`, `docs/workers.md` and `docs/running.md` say so.
- orchestra's run of tutti is up to orchestra 0.9.1's templates (tutti-kkb.34): `orchestra init --update` rewrote the opening, Close, Verify and Working unattended in `.orchestra/worker-prompt.md` (workers check their own change, leaving the whole suite to the merge check) and the opening, Its checks, How to verify and Rules in `.claude/agents/verifier.md` (it quotes the worker's passing check rather than run it again, and builds on earlier rounds), and `.orchestra/settings.json` records the templates' versions and names `check_own`, `scripts/check-own.sh`: `scripts/check.sh`'s steps on what the branch changed since main, committed or not (tsc when a TypeScript or JSON file changed, biome on the changed files, shellcheck on the changed scripts), and the tests the change touches, picked by `scripts/check-own.ts`: those that changed, import a changed module at any depth or name a changed file by path (a script or fixture they run or read). A change to the project's setup runs `scripts/check.sh` whole. `test/check-own.test.ts` covers it.
- A run says under its `START` line when the project's worker prompt or verifier is older than tutti's templates, naming the sections changed since and what changed in them (`templates: .tutti/worker-prompt.md is older than tutti's worker prompt template, which has since changed the opening (…) and Close (…)`), and `tutti init` says it of the files it keeps (§ 8.3, § 8.4; `src/init/templates.ts`, `src/init/update.ts`, `src/run/startup.ts`; tutti-kkb.15), as orchestra 0.9.0 does (orchestra-nr1v.3). `tutti init --update` takes the changes: it rewrites only those sections from the templates, keeping the rest of each file, the project's own sections and its lines on running each suite included, records the versions, prints the diff and leaves the commit to you. `.tutti/settings.json` now records the version of each template the files came from (`"templates"`), as `init` writes them; a project set up before, or a prompt imported from orchestra, has its files read for each change's words instead. A change to a template is a new version of it, which `test/template-versions.test.ts` pins. Templates never reached a project once init had written them: orchestra's run of tutti spent about 620 verifier-minutes on repeated checks with its prompt and verifier of 16 Oct.
- A worker's share of the machine's cores counts the workers running in the machine's other tutti and orchestra runs, no more than `--machine-workers` where it is set, taken as its session starts (§ 4.1; `src/run/machine.ts`'s `OtherWorkers`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`; tutti-kkb.37), as orchestra 0.9.0's does: the run keeps how many they run each time it reads their registrations, and a worker keeps the share it started with. `test/machine-core-share.test.ts` covers it.
- Each worker gets its share of the machine's cores for its checks (§ 4.1, § 4.11, § 13; `src/agent/env.ts`'s `coreShare` and `coreVariables`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `coresNote`, `scripts/test.ts`; tutti-kkb.24), as orchestra 0.9.0's do (orchestra-nr1v.14): the cores divided among the run's `concurrent` workers, at least one, in `TUTTI_CORES` and `ORCHESTRA_CORES`, and as `-p=<share>` after the `GOFLAGS` the environment has unless those set `-p`. The rules' note on checks tells the worker to keep a check that runs things side by side to it. One whole-suite test run kept dozens of threads runnable, and several workers' checks pushed the load up until tests timed out (tutti-0ye). tutti's own `scripts/test.ts` keeps bun's garbage collector markers and FTL compiler threads to a share below the machine's cores. `test/core-share.test.ts` covers it.
- An organ call refused for the account's usage limit puts the run's usage-limit hold on (§ 4.12; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`'s `outcome`, `src/organs/Organs.ts`'s `OrganHold.refused` and `refusalOf`, `src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`'s `refused`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`'s `organHold`; tutti-kkb.26), as orchestra 0.8.1's does: tutti only skipped organ calls while a hold was already on, so an organ refused for the limit, with no worker refused yet, failed as any other error and new tickets went on starting. A turn ending on the limit (a 429, a rejected `rate_limit_event` or an assistant message's `rate_limit` error, as for a worker) now fails with effect/ai's QuotaExhaustedError, carrying the limit's name and reset from its session's events, and Organs reports it to UsageLimits as a rejection: `USAGE_LIMIT: the five-hour limit is reached; …`, no new tickets, organ calls skipped, lifted at the reset plus a minute while workers still run, as orchestra 0.9.0's (orchestra-8jw3). `test/organ-usage-limit.test.ts` covers it, with a whole run.
- A worker checks its own change and leaves the whole suite to the merge check, as orchestra 0.9.0's does (§ 4.11, § 6.3, § 8.4; `src/project/templates/worker-prompt.md`, `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `withSuites`, `src/init/checks.ts`'s `suiteLines` and `verifierSection`, `src/init/init.ts`, `src/organs/scout.ts`, `src/project/settings.ts`; tutti-kkb.8): the worker prompt had workers run `scripts/check-fast.sh` before closing, a whole suite per ticket on top of the merge check that tutti runs on every ticket, and the slowest part of a worker's time on a loaded machine. Now a worker runs the suites on what its change touches, the slower ones where they cover it, and lint and type checks on the files it changed, and closes once its checks pass; the Verify section asks for the verifier once those pass. The scout gives each suite's command for part of the project (`targeted`, such as `bun test <files>` or `go test ./<package>/...`; dropped without a placeholder), and the worker prompt `tutti init` writes from the suites found lists each with it, or whole where it has none, a suite that takes turns left to the merge check. `checks.own.command` names a worker's own check, such as `scripts/check-own.sh`: the check note gives it in place of the merge check, footprints leave its files out as the other checks', and orchestra's `check_own` imports to it. An existing project keeps its prompt: `tutti init --force` writes it afresh. `test/own-check.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/setup.md` and `docs/workers.md` say so.
- A handed-back ticket is checked once, after rebasing (§ 4.7, § 5 item 7; `src/run/handback.ts`'s `resolve` and `resolvePrompt`, `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `fixCheck`, `afterFix`, `Carried` and `fixPrompt`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `baseMoved`, `prepare` and `setUpAndCheck`; tutti-kkb.17), as orchestra 0.8.5's (orchestra-nk2x): when another ticket merged while its worker resolved its rebase or fixed its check, what the worker left was checked on the base it had been rebased onto, then rebased and checked again, two merge checks for one hand-back. Now it goes back in line unchecked (`<id>'s worker finished the rebase, but <base> moved on meanwhile: rebasing tutti/<id> onto it before checking it`, or `<id>'s worker committed 1 commit, but …`), and its merge rebases it and checks it there, once: a fix is carried to that check, a pass saying `as its worker fixed it`, a failure handed back again or setting the ticket aside with every attempt in its notes, and a conflict going back to the worker, the fix's attempt noted. The hand-back prompts ask the worker to run what failed and the tests of the code it changed, leaving the whole check to tutti, rather than to run the check until it passes. `test/handback-check-once.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/workers.md` say so.
- The suites run in an airtight environment (§ 13; `scripts/test.ts`'s `testEnvironment`, `test/env.ts`, `biome.json`, `test/lint/bun-which.grit`; tutti-kkb.32), as orchestra 0.9.0's (orchestra-lx4d.12): `scripts/test.sh` gives bun test a PATH on which each folder holding claude, bd, brew, tmux, herdr or cmux is replaced by links to all else in it, so no test finds the machine's and passes, or skips, only where they are installed, and leaves out the variables of the terminal and its pane (`HERDR_*`, `TMUX`, `TERM_PROGRAM`, …), of a Claude Code session or orchestra worker the tests run under (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*`, …), Beads' (`BD_*`, `BEADS_*`) and tutti's own settings, which reached the live tests' sessions (tutti-y0e). The machine's PATH stays in `TUTTI_TEST_MACHINE_PATH`, where the slow test against a real bd finds it (`machineWhich`). Tests read their environment through `test/env.ts`, and Biome's `noProcessEnv` and a GritQL rule against `Bun.which` fail any other read in `test/` outside the live tests in `test/contract/` and the fixtures. `test/airtight-env.test.ts` covers it.
- Workers start without the tools they never use (§ 4.1; `src/agent/unusedTools.ts`, `src/agent/options.ts`'s `sharedOptions`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`; tutti-kkb.13), as orchestra's do (orchestra-6th9, orchestra-328r): every worker's session, and the login probe's, has Artifact, ListAgents, ReportFindings, ScheduleWakeup, SendFeedback and Workflow in `disallowedTools`, and Skill with the listing of your skills unless the project has skills of its own (anything in `.claude/skills` or `.claude/commands`) or enables a plugin in its `.claude/settings.json` or `settings.local.json`, decided once from the main checkout as the run begins. Their descriptions came to about 28k tokens on every call: a worker's first call opened with 11.9k tokens rather than 39.2k. Workers keep Bash, Read, Edit, Write, Agent, ToolSearch, the task tools and AskUserQuestion. `test/unused-tools.test.ts` pins the list, and a live contract test, `test/contract/tools.test.ts`, finds in `system/init` only the tools workers keep. `docs/workers.md` says so under *Tools left out*.
- A run has no ticket limit unless `--limit` (or `TUTTI_LIMIT`) gives one (§ 8.2; `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`, `src/cli/flags.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `progress`, `src/run/events.ts`, `src/run/startup.ts`; tutti-kkb.22), as orchestra 0.8.5's (orchestra-5ls7): a run no longer stops after 40 tickets with `LIMIT_REACHED`, but ends `READY_EMPTY` or `DRAINED`. Without a limit the dispatch lines say `[n]` rather than `[n/limit]`, the dispatch records leave `limit` out and the START block says `limit: none`; a limit given still caps the run. `test/no-ticket-limit.test.ts` covers it.
- Footprints read what orchestra 0.9.0's do (§ 5 item 5, § 8.1; `src/run/footprint.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/run/predict.ts`, `src/plan/plan.ts`, `src/services/Git.ts`'s `mergedByUnion`; tutti-kkb.16): a ticket's notes name nothing of where it works (101 of 649 skips on tutti's log rested on notes alone); the files git merges by union (`CHANGELOG.md merge=union`) are left out of every footprint, a worktree's changes, predicted files and `tutti plan`, so two tickets adding changelog entries no longer wait for each other; a file two tickets share holds them apart only when one is to change it (its `files` metadata lists it, its text names it, a document only in a sentence asking to change it, or its worktree changed it), so tickets citing `CLAUDE.md` or a guide run side by side; a ticket with commits on `tutti/<id>` (reopened, carried over) is footprinted from what its branch changes, its files metadata and its area labels, `(from its branch)`, in the run and in `tutti plan`; and a path keeps its brackets and a `+` starting a name (`pages/[id].tsx`, `src/routes/[slug]/+page.svelte`), in text and in the files metadata. `test/footprint-leave-out.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- A run says when another tutti or orchestra run shares the machine, and `--machine-workers N` caps the workers across them (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/machine.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/cli/flags.ts`, `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`; tutti-kkb.23), as orchestra 0.8.5's orchestra-3vy1, -ly87 and -ur30 have it: checks slow each other down, 1.7 to 3.3 times beside 1 to 3 others. Each run registers in the folder orchestra's runs register in (`runs/` in orchestra's cache folder, or `ORCHESTRA_RUNS_DIR`), in orchestra's format, so the two see each other's runs, and says once of each other run `SHARED_MACHINE: another orchestra run is going in <repo> with <n> workers; their checks slow each other down`, and that it has ended. With `--machine-workers` (`TUTTI_MACHINE_WORKERS`, off by default) a free slot waits while the workers across the runs, this one's included, are at the cap, with one of this run's among them, saying `HOLD: MACHINE: …` once a hold, `MACHINE` on the title line meanwhile, and reading the other runs every 2 seconds to fill the slot within seconds of a worker finishing. A registration whose run is gone, its PID ended or taken by a process started since, is ignored and removed. The event stream has `shared_machine` and `machine_hold` records, and `machine_full: true` on `queue` and `dispatch` records while the hold lasts. `test/shared-machine.test.ts` and `test/machine-workers.test.ts` cover it, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/events.md` say so.
- A run says when another tutti or orchestra run shares the machine, and `--machine-workers N` caps the workers across them (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/machine.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/cli/flags.ts`, `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`; tutti-kkb.23), as orchestra 0.8.5's orchestra-3vy1, -ly87 and -ur30 have it: checks slow each other down, 1.7 to 3.3 times beside 1 to 3 others. Each run registers in the folder orchestra's runs register in (`runs/` in orchestra's cache folder, or `ORCHESTRA_RUNS_DIR`), in orchestra's format, so the two see each other's runs, and says once of each other run `SHARED_MACHINE: another orchestra run is going in <repo> with <n> workers; their checks slow each other down`, and that it has ended. With `--machine-workers` (`TUTTI_MACHINE_WORKERS`, off by default) a free slot waits while the workers across the runs, this one's included, are at the cap, with one of this run's among them, saying `HOLD: MACHINE: …` once a hold, `MACHINE` on the title line meanwhile, and reading the other runs every 2 seconds to fill the slot within seconds of a worker finishing. A registration whose run is gone, its PID ended or taken by a process started since, is ignored and removed. The event stream has `shared_machine` and `machine_hold` records, and `machine_full: true` on `queue` and `dispatch` records while the hold lasts, with a `queue` record as it begins and as it ends. `test/shared-machine.test.ts` and `test/machine-workers.test.ts` cover it, two tutti runs sharing a folder among them, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/events.md` say so.
- The verifier builds on the worker's passing check and on earlier rounds, as orchestra 0.9.0's does (§ 4.11; `src/project/templates/verifier.md`, `src/init/checks.ts`'s `verifierSection`; tutti-kkb.9): the worker gives it the commit at which its checks passed, the commands it ran there and their output file, and the verifier quotes that result when HEAD is that commit and the tree is clean, where it ran `scripts/check-fast.sh` again (176 of 182 rounds on tutti's runs, about 39% of its time); otherwise it runs the suites only on what the change touches, leaving the whole suite to tutti. From the second round on, the worker gives it the earlier rounds' findings, and a line that starts `DISAGREES WITH ROUND <n>:` has the worker ask the maintainer rather than go round again. It passes Bash no timeout where a worker's settings raise Bash's default to the merge check's limit, and elsewhere Bash's 10-minute maximum with short waits in the foreground. Projects get it from `tutti init`'s new verifier. `test/verifier-rounds.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker's checks get their time in the foreground (§ 4.1, § 4.11; `src/agent/env.ts`'s `bashTimeouts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/project/templates/worker-prompt.md`; tutti-kkb.11), as orchestra's do (orchestra-8799, orchestra-9oep): Claude Code's Bash moves a command to the background after 2 minutes unless given a longer timeout, so a worker's check was moved there and the worker looked idle. Workers now get `BASH_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS`, the merge check's limit (`checks.fast.timeout`, 30m by default), and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS`, that or 10 minutes, whichever is longer, and the worker prompt's rule on checks says to pass Bash no timeout of their own. `test/bash-timeouts.test.ts` covers it.
- A worker's standing rules have it run a check once, its whole output saved to `.tutti/run/worker-check.log` and its exit status printed, naming the project's merge check (`checks.fast.command`) where it has one, and read the file rather than run the check again to see what it said (§ 4.11; `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `checkNote`, `src/agent/options.ts`; tutti-kkb.10), as orchestra 0.9.0's: workers piped a check through `tail`, which hides its exit status, then ran it again to learn whether it passed. This repository's `scripts/check.sh` ends with a verdict line (§ 13), `scripts/check.sh: PASS` or `FAIL in <step> (exit <status>)` (tsc, biome, test or shellcheck), `TUTTI_SLOW=1` named when the slow suites run, so `scripts/check-fast.sh` and `scripts/check-full.sh` end with it too. `test/check-note.test.ts` and `test/check-verdict.test.ts` cover it.
- Sessions get Claude in Chrome only when `workers.mcp_servers` chooses `claude-in-chrome` (§ 4.6; `src/agent/mcp.ts`'s `chromeArgs`, `src/agent/options.ts`, `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`; tutti-kkb.5), as orchestra's do: workers and the feature interview get `--chrome` when it is chosen and `--no-chrome` otherwise, the login probe and organs always `--no-chrome`. Claude Code turns Chrome on by itself once it is set up on the machine, past strict MCP config, so the `system/init` check could find a server tutti didn't pass and stop every worker as a setup problem, and a project couldn't choose Chrome: the name was looked up in config files, where it isn't defined. The check accepts Chrome, chosen, with source `dynamic`, the START block names it with the servers, and `tutti init` doesn't report it as a server not defined here (`src/init/workers.ts`'s `mcpSteps`). `test/chrome.test.ts` covers it, and a live contract test, `test/contract/chrome.test.ts`, finds no Chrome server or tools in `system/init` with it off, and Chrome with it on where it is set up.
- A blocker closed where the run didn't see it close (by hand, by another tool, under an earlier tutti) no longer counts as merged while its branch has commits the base lacks (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/closedBranches.ts`, `src/run/holds.ts`'s `asksGit` and `mergeHold`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-kkb.3), as orchestra 0.8.1's CLOSED_UNMERGED: while `tutti/<id>` has commits the base doesn't have and the base doesn't hold its changes, the tickets it blocks and the follow-ups filed from it wait with `<id> closed but not merged (tutti/<id> is not on <base>)`, and one `CLOSED_UNMERGED` warning says to merge the branch or delete it (`git branch -D tutti/<id>`). Git is asked once for each such blocker and commit the base is on, and again once its branch moves or goes, so merging or deleting it frees them at the next poll; where git can't tell, they wait (`git can't tell whether tutti/<id> is on <base>: …`), asked again at the next poll. `test/closed-unmerged.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- A conflict hand-back waiting for its steered worker stays within reach (§ 4.3, § 5 item 14, § 5.1's cards; `src/run/ledger.ts`'s `handBack`, `handBackWaits`, `keepsWorker` and `showsCard`, `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/runView.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/commands.ts`, `src/run/screen.ts`, `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/EndOfRunBox.tsx`; tutti-wh3, the maintainer's answer in tutti-e5d): its ticket, `Blocked{MERGE_CONFLICT}`, keeps its worker's number and its card (`conflict waits for you`, no spinner, framed `needsYou`), so `1`–`9` or a click opens its view, where it lost both and its number could go to another ticket; and with a dashboard the run waits for it as for a worker waiting on you, the end-of-run box naming it `(a merge conflict waits for you)`, until it is handed over, released, or `q` ends the run leaving it Blocked, where the run ended at READY_EMPTY. `test/handback-reachable.test.ts` covers it.
- A check hand-back whose worker commits nothing is checked again (§ 5.1's Fixing → ToMerge and Review; `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `verifyFixed` and `fixCheck`; tutti-kkb.7), as orchestra's checkRebased does (orchestra-2jwy): a worker that finds nothing to fix, the failure a test timing out on a loaded machine, say, had its ticket set aside with `its worker committed nothing`. Now the check runs a second time: a pass merges the ticket (`'<check>' passes on tutti/<id> on a second run; its worker committed nothing`), and a second failure sets it aside with no further hand-back, its notes saying `its worker committed nothing, and '<check>' fails again (output is in <file>)` and `not handed back again: its worker found nothing to fix`. `test/checkback-nothing-to-fix.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket past its work holds back what it did while it ran until it merges, and at most `concurrent` merge without a slot (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `slotsTaken`; tutti-kkb.1), as orchestra 0.8.5's orchestra-z8lr has it: a ticket frees its slot as it goes to the merge queue, but the scheduler's overlap check and solo ticket looked only at the running tickets, so a ticket waiting to merge, merging, resolving or fixing no longer kept back a ready ticket overlapping its footprint, nor a solo ticket next, and nothing capped the tickets merging without a slot, so resolving and fixing workers could push the live sessions past `concurrent`. From ToMerge to Merged, a ticket's footprint and solo label now count as a running ticket's, and beyond `concurrent` tickets past their work each takes a slot until a merge ends. The run's look for what is left (`src/run/run.ts`) no longer ends the run when a merge ended during it: what that merge held back, read before it ended, would be left HeldBack or Waiting at READY_EMPTY. `test/merging-slots.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- A ticket past its work holds back what it did while it ran until it merges, and at most `concurrent` merge without a slot (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `slotsTaken`; tutti-kkb.1), as orchestra 0.8.5's orchestra-z8lr has it: a ticket frees its slot as it goes to the merge queue, but the scheduler's overlap check and solo ticket looked only at the running tickets, so a ticket waiting to merge, merging, resolving or fixing no longer kept back a ready ticket overlapping its footprint, nor a solo ticket next, and nothing capped the tickets merging without a slot, so resolving and fixing workers could push the live sessions past `concurrent`. From ToMerge to Merged, a ticket's footprint and solo label now count as a running ticket's, and beyond `concurrent` tickets past their work each takes a slot until a merge ends; one that reached the merge queue without a slot holding its issue (carried over closed, checked once more, handed over again) has it read with bd show, once. The run's look for what is left (`src/run/run.ts`) no longer ends the run when a merge ended during it: what that merge held back, read before it ended, would be left HeldBack or Waiting at READY_EMPTY. `test/merging-slots.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` says so.
- Every merge is checked, a branch on top of the base already too (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `prepare`, `src/run/check.ts`'s `Passes`, `src/run/checkback.ts`, `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/log.ts`'s `branchOn`, `src/run/reportEvidence.ts`; tutti-kkb.6), as orchestra 0.8.5's: a finished ticket whose branch is on top of the base, as when nothing merged while it ran, gets the merge check (and the setup first when a setup file changed since its last setup) before it fast-forwards, `tutti/<id> is on top of <base> already: checking it with '<check>' before it merges`, where it merged unchecked unless a quit had left it marked. A failure there is handed back to its worker (`FIXING: … fails on tutti/<id> on top of <base>`, the prompt saying only that the check fails before it merges) or sets it aside (`CHECKS_FAILED`), as on a rebased branch; a branch whose commits change nothing needs no check, and the run keeps the commit each check passed on, so a fix or resolution checked already isn't checked again on top. `test/merge-on-top.test.ts` covers it.
- The project's setup runs in each new worktree before its worker starts, as orchestra 0.8.2 to 0.9.0 has it (§ 5 item 6; `src/run/setup.ts`'s `setUpWorktree`, `leaveSetupFailed`, `retrySetups`, `setupToResolve` and `takeSettings`, `src/run/start.ts`, `src/run/work.ts`, `src/run/environment.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/scope.ts`, `src/ui/Totals.tsx`; tutti-kkb.4): workers started with no dependencies installed (`Cannot find module '@opentui/keymap'`), as it ran only before a finished ticket's check. It runs within the merge check's time limit (`<id>: running '<setup>' in its new worktree before its worker starts`), and again in a reused worktree whose last setup failed or was cut short (its `.tutti/run/setup.started` mark) or whose branch changed a setup file since it was set up in the run; the first message says how the dependencies are installed. A setup that fails starts no worker (`SETUP_FAILED`): the ticket is back in the queue, `Waiting{setup}`, not set aside, until a ticket merges or a probe passes, counting toward no `--limit` and toward the environment hold as a worker failing at once does (`the setup '<setup>' failed in the worktrees of the last 2 tickets (…)`); the run's end names those still waiting (`SETUP_WAITS`), queue records count them as `held_by.setup`, the dashboard's In queue as `· 1 setup failed`, and `SCOPE_OPEN` says `its setup failed`. A conflict handed back after a rebase that brings in a setup file tells its worker to run the setup once the rebase has finished, and the `RESOLVING` line says so; a merge that changes `checks.setup` in `.tutti/settings.json` has the run use it from then on (`SETUP`, or `SETTINGS_UNREAD`), the full check's setup too. `Git.fileAt` reads a file at a commit. `test/worktree-setup.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/setup.md`, `docs/running.md`, `docs/events.md` and `docs/dashboard.md` say so.
- A worker's compactions are counted and logged (§ 4.2; `src/run/compactions.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`'s `compactions`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-4sa), as orchestra's: each PreCompact hook callback adds to its ticket's `compactions` tally, so the report and the reviewer say `compacted twice`, where the live host's callback did nothing and the tally stayed 0, and logs `<id>'s worker's context was compacted (auto)` with an `info` record. `test/compactions.test.ts` covers it, through the live host's callbacks and in a whole run.
- Closing a session whose Claude Code couldn't be launched no longer waits 10 s (`src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`'s `exited`; tutti-2bb): a spawn that fails has no PID and never exits, so its close waited out the whole close limit, in the background, and closing the host at a run's end waited for it; a child with no PID now counts as gone. `test/spawn-failure.test.ts` covers it, its hosts closing within 5 s of real time under a TestClock no one moves.
- The run's START block lists each deferred ticket in its scope whose blockers have all merged (§ 5 item 4; `src/run/deferredFree.ts`'s `sayAtStart`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-psr), as orchestra's does: `DEFERRED_FREE: <id>: its blockers have merged (<blockers>); to run it: bd update <id> --status open`, with whether its worktree is safe to remove, after the tickets left unmerged and the workers carried over, before the first dispatch. Only the box of a run with nothing to run listed them. A blocker left unmerged by an earlier run, or whose carried-over worker is still at it, frees nothing. `test/start-deferred-free.test.ts` covers it, and `docs/running.md` and `docs/parity.md` say so.
- A carried-over or resumed ticket's footprint is logged as a dispatched ticket's is (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `Scheduler.carry`; tutti-8i9), as orchestra's startFootprint does for a ticket it adopts or resumes: with the queue's footprint on, a ticket carried with its issue (a suspended worker carried over, or one resumed once its question is answered) has `<id> footprint: …` logged, the predictor's files marked `(predicted)`, where only a dispatch logged it. `test/carry-footprint-line.test.ts` covers it.
- Startup's, the interface's and init's untested details have tests (tutti-178; `docs/parity.md`'s start question, Ctrl+Z, worker view and two init rows are no longer marked untested): `test/run-start-ending.test.ts`, a cancelled start question exiting 130 with its message and no start record, and the current tickets chosen with nothing to run printing the box; `test/suspend-ignored.test.ts`, each SIGTSTP logged with its line; `test/ui-worker-view-scroll.test.tsx`, PgUp/PgDn and the wheel scrolling the worker view, past the top loading older items; `test/init-beads-install.test.ts`, bd installed with the Beads install script where brew is missing, and landing off the PATH with the advice; `test/init-skill-copy.test.ts`, a create-check-suite copy that is the same kept, and one that differs kept unless replacing it is chosen. `runFrom`'s steps are in `src/run/choose.ts` (`askThenBegin`, `cancelled`), `src/run/nothing.ts` (`endWith`) and `src/run/quit.ts` (`logSuspends`), for the tests to call.
- The README and `docs/running.md` point to the tutti skill and say how to install it (Appendix A Other; tutti-p18), as orchestra's README does for its skill: a *Running tutti through an agent* section in the README and *Running through an agent* in `docs/running.md` link to `skills/tutti/SKILL.md` and say to copy `skills/tutti/` to `~/.claude/skills/tutti/` (or `.claude/skills/tutti/` in one project). `test/skill-install-docs.test.ts` checks that they do, and that they name the folders the skill's own header does.
- The wind-down line falls back as orchestra's does (§ 7.2; `src/ui/WindDown.tsx`'s `windDownSegments`; tutti-upv): with no ticket left running it says `Winding down · nothing running, ending now`, where it said `0 running, no new tickets`, and a narrow pane drops `, no new tickets`, then the count, then the words, then all but the squares that fit, where the line was cut mid-word with `…`. A wind-down begun with nothing running shows one `windingDown` `■`. `test/wind-down-line.test.tsx` covers it.
- The setup runs before the check on a worker's fix of a failed check, or on its resolution of a rebase, when the worker's own commits change a setup file, as orchestra's checkRebased does (§ 5 item 7; `src/run/setup.ts`, `src/run/checkback.ts`, `src/run/handback.ts`; tutti-ry9): only the base's commits counted, so a fix that changed `bun.lock` was checked on the dependencies from before it. What the worktree was last set up for is now a commit of its branch too, and a setup that fails leaves it, so the next fix sets up again. `Git.filesBetween` (`git diff --name-only <from> <to>`) compares the branch before a resolution with the branch after it, whose commits the rebase rewrote. `test/fix-setup.test.ts` and `test/git-files-between.test.ts` cover it.
- A failed full check's last 40 lines go to the log, the reviewer and the run report (§ 5 item 12, § 6.2; `src/run/fullcheck.ts`'s `fullCheckEvidence` and `fullCheckReport`, `src/run/report.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/services/Checks.ts`; tutti-zfj), as orchestra's: the log has them under the `FULL_CHECK_FAILED` line (`the end of its output:`), the reviewer's "The full check" section after it, and the report a `## Full check` section after the reviewer's text, whatever it wrote: how it failed, where its whole output is, and the end in a code block no backtick in it can close. They were dropped, logged only when `check-full.log` couldn't be written. A check's `lastLines` now keeps its last 40 lines; what tells of a ticket's check still takes the last 30. `test/fullcheck-end.test.ts` covers it, and the FLAKY warnings of a full check that passed.
- A ticket set aside for a failed check is checked once more as orchestra's recheckTickets has it (§ 5 item 8; `src/run/recheck.ts`'s `recheckDue`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `recheckSlot`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `MergeOptions.recheckSlot`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-t4y): not while a stop or the usage limit holds the run, nor while a solo ticket runs or is first in line, and in a slot of its own, counting toward no limit, until its merge is over, where only a drain or stopped merges kept it from one and it took no slot. The run looks for those due each time it looks for work, before new tickets start, so one left for a hold, a solo ticket or a slot is checked once these are gone. `test/recheck-hold-solo.test.ts` covers it.
- The run loop's untested parity details have tests (tutti-b3k), each in a file of its own: the worker prompt's Removals rule (`test/prompt-removals.test.ts`), the continue nudges for a worker settled Failed on an API error (`test/settle-failed-nudges.test.ts`), a background subagent keeping its worker Working until the turn that reports it (a live contract test in `test/contract/sdk.test.ts`), SCOPE_OPEN naming a follow-up `filed by <source>'s worker; <source> is closed but not merged` (`test/scope-open-followup.test.ts`), a WAITS_FOR_MERGE settle not counting toward the environment hold (`test/waits-for-merge-uncounted.test.ts`), CLEANUP_FAILED after a fast-forward (`test/cleanup-failed.test.ts`), a second environment hold after PROBE_OK ending the run ENVIRONMENT, exit 7, unprobed (`test/environment-second-hold.test.ts`), `q` during the usage-limit wait ending INTERRUPTED, 130, the worker suspended (`test/usage-limit-quit.test.ts`), a failed spawn's and a missing folder's reasons outside the live tests (`test/spawn-failure.test.ts`), and bd failing as an epic's last subticket merges (`test/epic-close-failures.test.ts`). `docs/parity.md` cites them, and no row lists tutti-b3k any more.
- A run refused while `tutti init` holds the run lock says so (§ 9.2; `src/services/RunLock.ts`'s `AlreadyRunning`; tutti-d5g), as orchestra says `(orchestra init, pid N)`: `tutti is already running in <repo> (tutti init, pid 44497, since 08:31, main)`, where it read as a run's; orchestra's lock held by its init says `orchestra init` too. `test/run-lock-init-refusal.test.ts` covers it.
- § 9.2 says the run lock's files are emptied on release (tutti-d5g, the maintainer's answer in tutti-0l6): tutti empties `tutti.lock` and `orchestra.lock` before letting go of them, so neither names a run that has ended, where orchestra leaves its file naming the last run; `lsof -t` says whether a run is going. Appendix A's run-lock row says so, and `docs/parity.md` has the row kept.
- A Claude Code that can't be launched leaves its worker Gone with `Claude Code couldn't be launched` (§ 4.8; `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`; tutti-ned), the reason its start failed with, where the worker's state stream recorded `its session ended: Claude Code native binary at … failed to launch …`, the SDK's libc wording, which the session's stream saw first. `test/spawn-failure.test.ts` covers it.
- `--ticket` and `--feature` are checked at startup as orchestra checks them (§ 5 item 1; `src/run/startup.ts`, `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`; tutti-wir): `--ticket` naming a ticket bd can't show (`Cannot read ticket <id> (--ticket): …`), a closed one (`… is closed: nothing to run. Reopen it with: bd update <id> --status open`) or a question (`… is a question for you (label human), not work. Answer it with: bd human respond <id>`) is a setup problem, listed with the others, exit 2; so is `--feature` with `--limit 0`, which would file a plan and start none of it. `test/startup-ticket-scope.test.ts` covers it.
- With the queue's footprint on, the log shows each ticket's footprint as it is dispatched (§ 5 item 5; `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `footprintLine`; tutti-d6q), as orchestra's startFootprint does: `<id> footprint: Loop.merge, src/a.ts, area:run` under its `dispatching` line, the predictor's files marked `(predicted)`, or `nothing named`. `showFootprint` had no caller, so no dispatch logged it. `test/dispatch-footprint-line.test.ts` covers it.
- A project `tutti init` set up whose worker prompt is gone, `.tutti/settings.json` there and `.tutti/worker-prompt.md` not, can't start (§ 5 item 1, § 9.1, as orchestra; `src/project/prompt.ts`'s `loadWorkerPrompt`; tutti-04w): `Worker prompt not found: .tutti/worker-prompt.md. Recreate it with: tutti init` is listed with the other startup problems, exit 2, where the run went on with the built-in template. A `--prompt` file still runs without it, and a project init hasn't set up still says to run `tutti init` first. `test/prompt-missing.test.ts` covers it.
- No failed check is handed back to its worker while the run takes on no more work (§ 4.7; `src/run/checkback.ts`'s `whyNotFix`, `src/run/holds.ts`'s `noMoreWork`, `src/run/merge.ts`'s `MergeOptions.noMore`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-8dt), as orchestra's sayNoMore has it: a stop holding the run (`PAUSED`, `BLOCKED`, `TICKET_LIMIT`, `ENVIRONMENT`, …) or the usage limit sets the ticket aside at once, `not handed back to its worker: the run holds (<stop>)`, where only a drain did and a hold still handed the failure back. `test/check-hand-back-hold.test.ts` covers it.
- Whole runs' notifications are tested (§ 7.9; tutti-i0y): `test/run-notifications.test.ts` has a PAUSED stop notify `Stopped: PAUSED on <id>` and no finish, a quit notify `Stopped: INTERRUPTED` once, a question ticket notify `<id> needs your answer · <title>`, a title over 60 characters cut with `…`, the full check failing notify `Full check failed · <suite>` (`· setup` for its setup) after `Finished the run`, and a run with nothing to run notify nothing. `docs/parity.md`'s notifications row is now kept, and the full check's row no longer lists its notification as a gap.
- The predictor runs in real runs (§ 5 item 13, § 3.4; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/predict.ts`; tutti-76k): the run made its scheduler without one, so no ticket naming nothing was ever guessed and such a ticket ran beside anything. With the queue's footprint on and more than one slot, the run starts the predictor as orchestra does, in the background and one ticket at a time, and stops it (`Predictor.interrupt`, the guess under way aborted) once its loop ends. `docs/organs.md` and `docs/parity.md` say so. `test/run-predictor.test.ts` covers it.
- `docs/parity.md`, the parity audit against Appendix A (§ 14 row 1.0, § 1.1 Goal 1; tutti-nrg.10.9): each of the matrix's 83 rows with its disposition, the modules that implement it and the tests that show it; 70 are as the spec says, and the 13 that are missing or differ each name their follow-up ticket. `test/parity-doc.test.ts` checks it against the spec and the code: a row per row of the matrix in its order, a disposition and, for a gap, a ticket on each, and every module, test file and test name it gives there.
- A steered worker whose ticket is `Blocked{MERGE_CONFLICT}`, its hand-back waiting, takes your messages, interrupts and Release from its view (§ 4.3, § 4.8; `src/run/commands.ts`, `src/run/sessions.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`, `src/run/steer.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-6hk): the run resolves them for a steered worker outside a phase that holds one, where it dropped them as `<id> has no worker`, and its session stays open while the hand-back waits, where it was closed with the merge. Release also hands the waiting conflict over, as HandBackNow does but with no waiver (`releaseAndHandOver`): `you released <id>'s worker; <branch> is back in the merge queue, …`. `test/steer.test.ts` and `test/sessions.test.ts` cover it.
- An epic closes as its last subticket merges whatever `queue.exclude_types` holds (§ 5; `src/run/merge.ts`'s `closeEpics`; tutti-afa): it closed a parent only of a type the setting named, so with `"exclude_types": []`, or one without `epic`, epics, which a run never takes, were never closed. A parent of any type the run leaves out, epics always, now closes. `test/epic-close.test.ts` covers it.
- The run sends its notifications as orchestra does (§ 7.9, § 4.5; `src/run/notices.ts`, `src/main.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-sla), where only the usage limit's hold notified: `Closed <id> · <title>` as a ticket merges, `Set aside <id> · <why>` as one is deferred or left for review, `<id> needs your answer · <question>` as one waits on a question ticket, `<id> needs you · Allow Bash(rm -rf build)?` for the first attention request in 30 seconds, `Stopped: PAUSED on <id>` as the run stops (once, though the closing line records the stop again), `Finished the run · 12 tickets closed · 2 set aside` as it finishes by itself, and `Full check failed · <suite>`. Titles and reasons are cut to 60 characters with `…`. The EventStream the run is given notifies each record that does (`notifying`), Attention's included; `docs/dashboard.md`'s Notifications section has the table. `test/notices.test.ts` covers it.
- The run sends its notifications as orchestra does (§ 7.9, § 4.5; `src/run/notices.ts`, `src/main.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`; tutti-sla), where only the usage limit's hold notified: `Closed <id> · <title>` as a ticket merges, `Set aside <id> · <why>` as one is deferred or left for review, `<id> needs your answer · <question>` as one waits on a question ticket, `<id> needs you · Allow Bash(rm -rf build)?` for the first attention request in 30 seconds, `Stopped: PAUSED on <id>` as the run stops (once, though the closing line records the stop again), `Finished the run · 12 tickets closed · 2 set aside` as it finishes by itself (those blocked from merging counted set aside, as orchestra does), and `Full check failed · <suite>`. Titles and reasons are cut to 60 characters with `…`. The EventStream the run is given notifies each record that does (`notifying`), Attention's included; `docs/dashboard.md`'s Notifications section has the table. `test/notices.test.ts` covers it.
- The screen's commands no longer act after the run ends (`src/run/run.ts`; tutti-as1): a HandBackNow, Answer, message, Interrupt, Release, Drain or EndRun taken once the run has ended is dropped (logged at debug, as a stale one is), where a HandBackNow sent after `READY_EMPTY` put a Blocked ticket back in the merge queue, which rebased, resumed, checked and merged it after the summary while the run's outcome still had it Blocked. Those commands act one at a time, and the run ends only once its last look saw each of them acted on: one acted on as the run decided to end (or during the environment probe) has it look again. Moving between the views and quitting act as before. `test/commands-after-end.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view's hand-back notice is fed by the run (§ 4.3; `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/screen.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/ui/App.tsx`): `HandBacks` gains `changes`, those waiting now and on each change, which the run follows into the new `ScreenInputs.handBacks` as their tickets, and `dashboard()` bridges into `ShellValue.handBacks`. A steered worker's conflict that waits now shows its **Hand it the conflict now** notice in its view, and stops showing it once handed over. `test/handback-notice.test.ts` covers it.
- User docs (§ 14 M8; `README.md`, `docs/setup.md`, `docs/running.md`, `docs/dashboard.md`, `docs/workers.md`, `docs/organs.md`, `docs/events.md`, `docs/colour-schemes.md`; tutti-nrg.10.7): the README says what tutti is, how to install, log in, set up and run it, and links the guides, written from the code as it is: installing and updating, login and billing, `tutti init`, the checks, the settings and the user config; running, its flags, scheduling, the merge queue, holds, the usage limit, quitting and carrying on, exit codes, orchestra beside tutti; the dashboard, its keys, the worker view, plain output, terminals (`COLORTERM=truecolor` over ssh, tmux's `set-clipboard on`); workers, attention requests and `tutti answer`, steering; the organs; the event stream with jq recipes; colour schemes with an example file. `test/user-docs.test.ts` checks them against the code: every run flag, init flag, settings key, user config key, event kind and colour role named, the example settings, user config and scheme read as tutti reads them, and every link between the docs leading somewhere.
- `skills/tutti/SKILL.md`, the tutti skill (§ 14 M8, Appendix A Other): orchestra v0.7.7's orchestra skill rewritten for tutti, for a coding agent running tutti on the user's behalf: where the project stands (the run lock, `.tutti/` and its run files), `tutti init`'s flags, launching `tutti --plain --tickets` in the background, answering attention requests with `tutti answer` (`--json` to list, `allow`, `allow-run`, `deny`, `--reason`, `--choice`, `--confirm` for held removals, the 4-minute `BLOCKED` hold), usage limits and exit code 8, quitting and resuming saved sessions, the event stream's `jq` recipes over `.tutti/run/events.jsonl`, the stop lines with their exit codes, and finishing a ticket by hand; install instructions in its header. `test/tutti-skill.test.ts` checks it against the code: every flag it gives a tutti command is in that command's `--help`, each stop line's exit code is tutti's, its PERMISSION and QUESTION lines are the log's, and it names every field `tutti answer --json` prints.
- Steering (§ 4.3, § 7.5, § 10; `src/run/steer.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`): the run takes the worker view's SendMessage, Interrupt, Release and HandBackNow. Your message goes into the worker's inbox without `client_composed`, with origin human and the composer's uuid; the log says `MESSAGE: you → <id> (212 characters)` and its `message` record its `length`, never its text, and the ledger tallies it for the reviewer. A message or Ctrl+X's interrupt (which cancels the messages still queued) makes the worker steered first (`STEERED: <id>`, `steered`), Release unsteered (`RELEASED: <id>`, `released`). HandBackNow puts a ticket whose conflict waited for its steered worker back in the merge queue, `Blocked{MERGE_CONFLICT}` → `ToMerge` by the new `HandedOver` cause (`src/run/phase.ts`), and its next rebase's conflict is handed back with the steered check waived that once (`src/run/handback.ts`'s `waive`, `src/run/merge.ts`). Settling reads a worker whose turn failed, as an interrupted one's does, as idle, where it paused at once (`src/run/settle.ts`): a steered one waits out its grace from your last message; a released one gets the nudges it has left; a steered one whose session went isn't resumed to carry on, and pauses. `test/steer.test.ts` covers it.
- A ticket back in the queue for WAITS_FOR_MERGE counts toward --limit once even when its blocker merges before its fiber ends (`src/run/schedule.ts`; tutti-f1q): it is dispatched again only once that fiber has ended and freed its slot, where a fill inside that window started it a second time, counted it twice and left the slot count one short as the first fiber ended; and one that bd ready drops inside that window no longer counts at all, as one still waiting in the queue doesn't. `test/schedule-waiting-window.test.ts` covers both.
- With the queue's footprint on, a running ticket's changes are read from the worktree it works in (§ 8.4; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`'s `footprintsOf`, its `worktree` now an Effect): the one the Store saved for it, where `Worktrees.prepare` reused it or an earlier run's `--worktrees` put it, and `<worktrees>/<id>` only when the Store has none (tutti-8rd). It read `<worktrees>/<id>` always, so a worktree elsewhere gave no changes, and overlaps and `LIKELY_CONFLICT` missed them. `test/run-footprints-worktree.test.ts` covers it.
- The tests that start processes have a per-file timeout of 30 s (`setDefaultTimeout`), 60 s for `test/agent-sdk-language-model.test.ts`, over bun's 5 s default (tutti-0ye): `test/flaky.test.ts`, `test/smoke.test.ts`, `test/usage-limit.test.ts`, `test/folded-sends.test.ts` and `test/agent-host-live.test.ts` take up to 3 s alone, and failed the merge check when other checks ran beside it.
- An MCP URL elicitation's Open URL opens its URL (§ 4.5; `src/ui/openUrl.ts`, `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`'s `useSendAnswer`, `src/ui/App.tsx`, `src/main.ts`) before it answers Accept, where it only answered: in a local session with `open` on macOS, or `xdg-open` on Linux in a Wayland or X session, run detached through `effect/process`; over ssh, or for a URL the command can't open, it copies the URL (the drag's clipboard, OSC 52 over ssh). It toasts `opened the URL in your browser` or `copied the URL: open it in your browser`; when the URL was neither opened nor copied it toasts `couldn't open or copy the URL` and sends no Accept, the request left open. Only an http or https URL is given to the command, since an MCP server sends it. `test/open-url.test.tsx` covers it.
- `test/build-script.test.ts` checks that `scripts/build.sh` leaves no `.bun-build` copy of Bun behind (§ 12 Builds; tutti-nrg.21): the fake bun leaves one in its working folder, as `bun build --compile` does (Bun's bug oven-sh/bun#14020, still open with Bun 1.4.3), and the tests find neither it nor the compile's temp folder after a build, a failed one included; the real host build finds none in the checkout or beside the binary.
- A plain run on a terminal asks it for its background (OSC 11) only when the run report prints (§ 7.6; `src/run/run.ts`'s `finish`, `src/run/report.ts`'s `runReport`, `src/main.ts`): `finish` and the report are given the report's out to make, not made, and make it only once something prints on it, so a run without organs, with `--review=false`, or quit no longer asks after it ends. `test/report-out-lazy.test.ts` covers it.
- The npm package's dashboard runs on Alpine and other musl systems (§ 12 npm; `src/npm.ts`, `src/native/detectMusl.ts`, `src/native/musl.ts`, `scripts/build-npm.sh`, `scripts/check-npm.sh`): the bundle, built from its own entry now, sets `OPENTUI_LIBC=musl` on a musl Linux before anything loads OpenTUI, which loaded its glibc library there and failed on x64. `OPENTUI_LIBC=musl` no longer needs setting by hand. `scripts/check-npm.sh` also opens OpenTUI's native library for the libc the bundle chose, so the npm workflow's Alpine check covers it. `test/npm-musl.test.ts` covers it.
- A plain run's report and `--feature`'s organ lines carry no ANSI styles when stdout isn't a terminal (§ 7.6; `src/ui/theme.ts`'s `printedTheme` and `makeTheme`'s `styled`, `src/print/markdown.ts`, `src/main.ts`): piped or redirected to a file, they are printed with an unstyled theme, no bold, faint or colours even without NO_COLOR, and the terminal isn't asked for its background; on a terminal they stay styled, and `--feature`'s plan no longer asks a dumb terminal for its background either. `test/plain-report-unstyled.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard's status is live through the whole run (§ 7.2; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/schedule.ts`, `src/run/scope.ts`): the title line says `· stopping` while a stop holds the run, and no longer once the probe lifts it; `· solo <id> next` and `· solo <id> running` as the scheduler has its solo ticket (its new `show` option); In queue says `of N`, N the tickets not closed in the run's scope that it takes, as each fill reads them (`unclosedIn`); and the environment probe's session shows as its `probe` card while it runs. These had stayed at their empty values. `test/live-status.test.ts` covers it.
- Messages sent to a worker are in its transcript (§ 4.4, § 7.5; `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`, `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, `src/testing/AgentHost.ts`): tutti's first message, nudges, hand-backs and yours from the composer are each recorded in `messages.jsonl` as the SDK user message pushed into the session's inbox, as it is pushed, where nothing recorded them since the SDK doesn't echo them. The worker view and the interview draw each as `› …` where it was sent (`src/ui/transcriptItems.ts`'s `entriesOf`): not while the composer shows it queued below, and not once a `command_lifecycle` says the CLI cancelled it. Triage reads them as `user:` lines. `test/sent-messages.test.tsx` covers it.
- The run takes the dashboard's commands (§ 3.5; `src/run/run.ts`): each is resolved against the ledger and the open requests as it is taken, and one that no longer names anything (a worker gone, a request answered) is dropped. `1`–`9`, a click or Enter open a worker's view and Esc leaves it (OpenWorker, CloseWorker), an attention card's buttons answer its request (Answer, via `screen`), the quit box's `s` and the stop question wind the run down or take tickets again (`DRAIN: …, asked from the dashboard`), and the quit box's `q` or a second Ctrl+C quits (`QUIT from the dashboard: …`), where only the end-of-run box's `q` did anything. Steering's commands are left to `src/run/steer.ts`. `test/run-commands.test.ts` covers it.
- The run report's reviewer is given what the check said of each ticket set aside after its check failed, and a scoped run's follow-ups filed outside its scope (§ 6.2, as orchestra's `failedChecksSection` and `outsideScopeSection`; `src/run/reportEvidence.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`, `src/run/recheck.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`): for each ticket still `Review` with `CHECKS_FAILED` at the run's end, how its check failed, on which base commit, where its output is, the lines that say what failed (else its last lines) and the folders its own commits change, at most 20; and for a run with `--ticket`, each ticket not closed, filed since the run started outside the ticket and its descendants, and no question for you, with its type, status, links and description, cut to 400 characters. The run's sections now come in orchestra's order: the failed checks, the full check, the follow-ups, the feature request. `test/report-evidence.test.ts` covers it.
- exec no longer waits forever on a command whose exit Bun's node:child_process lost (§ 3.3 "Time limits"; `src/services/exec.ts`): about once in a few thousand spawns, Bun 1.4.3 reaps the child but emits no `exit`, no output and no end of it, and exec waited on that exit, as did its kill and its scope's finalizer. Once the command's process group has gone, exec waits `LostExitGrace` (5 s) for the exit, then fails with CommandNotRun (CommandTimedOut if its time limit killed it), forgets the group, and closes the spawner's scope without waiting on it; the time limit's kill stops waiting too. `test/exec-lost-exit.test.ts` covers it with a spawner that loses the exit.
- bun test's preload, `test/warm-up.ts` (`bunfig.toml`'s `[test] preload`), spawns once in a `beforeAll` before the tests: Bun 1.4.3 loses the exit of a bun test process's first spawn in its tests about once in four at a load of ~30, and not the spawns after it, so `test/exec.test.ts`'s first test no longer fails on it (tutti-cje). exec's check that a group has gone takes only ESRCH: EPERM is a group it can't signal, or on macOS one holding only a zombie.
- The release key, 2026-10 (§ 12 Releases): `src/update/keys.ts` and `scripts/install.sh`'s `release_keys` hold its public half, so `tutti update` and the install script verify releases signed with it, where they refused every release. `test/install-script.test.ts` checks that the two hold the same keys.
- `scripts/sign-checksums.ts` (§ 12 Releases) refuses a key whose public half isn't among the release keys tutti builds in (`src/update/keys.ts`), as orchestra's sign-checksums does: it signs nothing and exits 1, so a release is never signed with a key `tutti update` would refuse, and the release workflow's key check fails before anything is built. `test/sign-checksums.test.ts` covers it.
