- The run's end on the main screen (§ 5 item 14 "Then", § 7.1; `src/run/run.ts`'s `finish`, `src/main.ts`): once the dashboard's renderer is gone, the styled summary (title, totals, every ticket), the closing line (`♪ Completed the Run` and the other ends, or a stop's `■ PAUSED: …`) and a scoped run's scope line are printed in the theme of the renderer's appearance, where the log's plain summary lines were; then the full check's line, the run report with its organ lines (`src/run/report.ts`, now called), and only then the end record. A plain run keeps the log's summary lines, then runs the report. A stopped scoped run's closing line carries its `SCOPE_OPEN`/`SCOPE_DONE` part too (the log's stop line is unchanged). The report is skipped once the run was quit, and Ctrl+C during it ends the run INTERRUPTED; the live run's `OrganHold` (the usage-limit hold) is now in its context for the report to see. `test/run-finish.test.ts` covers it.
- The `START` block's billing line says what organs bill (§ 4.13, § 6.1; `src/agent/Account.ts`, `src/run/startup.ts`): with `TUTTI_ORGAN_PROVIDER=anthropic`, `billing: subscription for workers, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY for organs (TUTTI_ORGAN_PROVIDER=anthropic)`, where it said the key was set but not used; under `--billing api`, `billing: API key, for workers and organs (TUTTI_ORGAN_PROVIDER=anthropic)`. The startup checks read organs' provider from their `Checking`'s `env` and give it to the run as `Started.organs`. `test/organ-billing.test.ts` covers the line and the variable's setup problems through the checks.
- The release workflow runs all six archives' `tutti --version` (§ 12 Releases; `.github/workflows/release.yml`'s run job), where it ran three: linux-arm64 and linux-arm64-musl (in Alpine, in docker) on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` and darwin-x64 on `macos-15-intel`, which GitHub's docs list as standard runners for private repositories. `test/release-run.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti update [--check] [--force]` (§ 12, § 8.1; `src/update/update.ts`, `src/update/keys.ts`): replaces the binary with the latest GitHub Release's archive for its own build's target (a musl build the musl archive), as orchestra update does: it verifies `checksums.txt.sig` with the ed25519 release keys built into tutti, any of them (key rotation as orchestra's, in `keys.ts`), before trusting a sum or downloading the archive, checks the archive's SHA-256, writes the new tutti beside the binary, checks that its `--version` names the release, and renames it over the binary; on any failure or Ctrl+C the binary is unchanged and nothing is left beside it. A build from a clone is replaced only with `--force`, tutti run from source never. `--check` changes nothing and exits 1 when a newer release is out. Exit codes 0, 1 (`--check`), 2 refused (a clone, source, a folder it can't write in), 4 failed. A release build's run checks once a day in the background (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME/tutti/latest-release.json`, else `~/.cache/tutti/`; `TUTTI_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` turns it off) and ends with `tutti v0.9.1 is available (you have v0.9.0): tutti update`, `notice` with its command `emphasis` bold. No release key is built in until the maintainer makes one, and until then it installs nothing. `test/update.test.ts` covers it against fixture releases signed with a generated key.
- The npm package, `@noesis-sol/tutti`, for development and agents (§ 12 npm; `package.json`, `bin/tutti`, `scripts/build-npm.sh`, `scripts/check-npm.sh`, `.github/workflows/npm.yml`): public, its `bin` the `#!/bin/sh` launcher `bin/tutti`, which runs `bun --no-env-file --config=/dev/null` on the bundle beside it, so a project's `.env` files and `bunfig.toml` preload don't reach tutti. `scripts/build-npm.sh [--out <dir>] [--release]` builds the bundle into `bin/bundle/` (git-ignored) with React's production build, the platform packages left to the install; `prepack` runs it, so `bun pm pack` packs a fresh one. `scripts/check-npm.sh <tarball>` runs a tarball with `bunx -p` and from `node_modules/.bin` in a project holding `.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.production` and a preload, and checks none reaches tutti; it passes on macOS, Debian and Alpine. `.github/workflows/npm.yml` packs each `v*` tag's release bundle, checks the tarball on Linux, Alpine and macOS and publishes it, under `next` for a suffixed tag, when the `NPM_TOKEN` secret is set. A short `README.md` says it isn't the recommended install. `test/npm-package.test.ts` covers it.
- An answered ticket whose session can't be resumed (§ 5.1, § 4.8; `src/run/asked.ts`) has its record in state.json forgotten, as `workerGone` does, and its WORKER_GONE line says what brings it back: `it is in bd ready, so a later run takes it up again` when its worker left it open, `reopen it to run it again (bd update <id> --status open)` when it is in progress, where it said a later run took it up either way and kept a Review record no run read. `test/asked.test.ts` covers both.
- `scripts/install.sh` (§ 12 Install): `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/noesis-sol/tutti/main/scripts/install.sh | sh` installs the latest release, or `TUTTI_VERSION`'s, into `/usr/local/bin` where it can write, else `~/.local/bin` (`TUTTI_INSTALL_DIR` names another), never with sudo. It verifies `checksums.txt.sig` with the release keys it holds where OpenSSL 3 is installed (else says it wasn't checked), before trusting a sum or downloading the archive, then the archive's SHA-256 against `checksums.txt`, and runs the new binary's `--version` before putting it in place. On Linux it picks the musl archive by `TUTTI_LIBC`, then `ldd --version`, then `/lib/ld-musl-<machine>.so.1`, says which (`linux x64 musl`), and that a musl build needs `libstdc++` and `libgcc`. It holds no release key until the maintainer makes one, and until then installs nothing. POSIX sh, run under sh, dash and bash. `test/install-script.test.ts` covers it against fixture releases served from file paths (`TUTTI_DOWNLOAD_URL`).
- An SDK bump runs each § 13 contract once: the `TUTTI_LIVE` tests over the SDK's `query()` in `test/sdk-background-task.test.ts` (removed), `test/verifier-foreground.test.ts` and `test/queued-cancel.test.ts` are dropped for their counterparts in `test/contract/sdk.test.ts`, whose background-task test now also folds each message through `WorkerState` and checks the worker stays `Working` from the first idle until the report's idle.
- A hand-back's resolution that leaves the branch changing nothing, its worker having skipped every commit the base has already, is closed at once as unchanged (§ 5.1's `Resolving` → `Done{noChange}`; `src/run/handback.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`), as orchestra does, with its line: `its worker found the changes of <branch> on <base> already and skipped them in the rebase`. It is no longer checked and put back in line to be found unchanged there. `test/handback-unchanged.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view's Markdown quotes and rules (§ 7.11 "Markdown"; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`): a quote's bar is a faint `│ ` on each of its lines and a rule a faint `─` at most 40 long (`ruleWidth`), where OpenTUI drew both as box borders with no attributes and the rule the whole width. `markdownBlocks`, the `<markdown>`'s `renderNode`, keeps OpenTUI's boxes, with no border sides, and draws them; the paragraphs around them stay coalesced. `test/ui-markdown-blocks.test.tsx` covers it, and `test/ui-roles.test.tsx`'s worker view scene checks the bar and the rule.
- Release builds (§ 12): `scripts/build.sh [--out <dir>] [--release] [--archive] <target>... | host` compiles `src/entries/<target>.ts` for the six targets with that platform's Claude Code and OpenTUI library embedded, after `bun install --frozen-lockfile --os --cpu` for the target, keeping only its platform's packages in `node_modules` (that install adds beside the host's) and putting the host's back at the end; `--archive` writes `tutti_<ver>_<os>_<arch>[_musl].tar.gz`. `.github/workflows/release.yml` releases each `v*` tag: the full check, the six builds cross-compiled on Linux, three of them run, `checksums.txt` signed with the `TUTTI_RELEASE_KEY` secret (`scripts/sign-checksums.ts`, `checksums.txt.sig`; the run fails first without it), build provenance attested, pre-releases for suffixed tags, notes from `scripts/release-notes.sh`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `scripts/check-full.sh` and a host build on Linux and macOS. `test/build-script.test.ts`, `test/sign-checksums.test.ts`, `test/release-notes.test.ts` and `test/release-workflow.test.ts` cover them.
- `tutti plan --apply` warns on stderr when a run, tutti's or orchestra's, holds the repository's lock, as orchestra v0.7.7's plan does: a ticket it has started keeps going and may merge before a ticket it now waits for. `RunLock.probe` (`src/services/RunLock.ts`) sees a held lock without taking it, through a shared flock for a moment that a run's retries get past; a lock it can't probe warns of nothing. `test/plan-lock-warning.test.ts` covers it.
- state.json forgets what a run is done with, and keeps what a crash left (§ 4.8; `src/run/carry.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`, `src/run/work.ts`, `src/run/sessions.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`): a ticket merged, closed with no change or dropped WORKER_GONE has its record removed, where it stayed run after run. A run that ends saves the phase each of its other tickets left it in (`saveLeft`), so the next run tells a record a crash left (no suspension, no phase) from one an ended run left; once the process state.json names is gone, it carries over such a ticket in progress (resumed, told `the last run ended (crashed)`, `RESUMED: t-1 was in progress as the last run crashed; …`) or closed (merged), and forgets one in any other state. `test/state-records.test.ts` covers it.
- state.json forgets what a run is done with, and keeps what a crash left (§ 4.8; `src/run/carry.ts`, `src/run/aside.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`, `src/run/work.ts`, `src/run/sessions.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`): a ticket merged, closed with no change (by its worker or as it merges), deferred or dropped WORKER_GONE has its record removed, where it stayed run after run. A ticket left for review keeps its record, its phase saved as it is left (`leaveForReview`), and a run that ends saves the phase each of its other tickets left it in (`saveLeft`), so the next run tells a record a crash left (no suspension, no phase) from one an ended run left; once the process state.json names is gone, it carries over such a ticket in progress (resumed, told `the last run ended (crashed)`, `RESUMED: t-1 was in progress as the last run crashed; …`) or closed (merged), and forgets one in any other state. `test/state-records.test.ts` covers it.
- The API-key organ provider (§ 6.1, D10; `src/organs/AnthropicProvider.ts`): with `TUTTI_ORGAN_PROVIDER=anthropic` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, organs' LanguageModel is `@effect/ai-anthropic`'s (4.0.5, pinned) over the Messages API instead of the Agent SDK's, chosen by layer in `src/main.ts`; organs don't change. Each call takes its organ's model (`claude-sonnet-5-5` unless `--organ-model` names one) and effort (above `high` sent as `high`), and sends the system prompt alone. The API reports no price, so each call is logged as of unknown cost: `ORGAN triage: cost unknown, no price reported, 4.2s` when it answered. The scout, which reads the repository with Claude Code's tools, can't run over it. The variable set to anything but `agent-sdk` or `anthropic`, or `anthropic` with no key, is a startup setup problem. `test/anthropic-provider.test.ts` covers it.
- Scheduling scenarios (§ 14 M2, § 13; `test/scenarios-scheduling.test.ts`): orchestra's scheduling scenarios from its running and workers guides as whole runs under TestClock, each asserting its log lines, event records, phases (no PHASE_BUG), leaks and exit code: slots filled and refilled, the ready poll, footprints keeping overlapping tickets apart and growing with a worktree's changes, solo, parents after their subtickets, epics closed up the tree, blockers and follow-ups waiting for a merge, a follow-up a worker files, waits for merge, `--limit`, `--ticket` with SCOPE_DONE and SCOPE_OPEN, set aside and review, recheck, unmerged at the end, a stop draining, winding down, and nothing to run.
- A ticket put back in the queue until its blocker merges (WAITS_FOR_MERGE) counts toward `--limit` once, as orchestra's (`src/run/schedule.ts`): it doesn't count while it waits, so the next ready ticket may take its place, and it starts again as `[2/2]`, not `[3/2]`, if a place is left; the done line counts it once.
- PAUSED's HOLD line is the run's, as orchestra's (`src/run/work.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`): said only while other tickets still run, ending `; no new tickets while the 1 running finish`; with nothing else running there is none, and the PAUSED line ends the run as before.
- A scoped run whose epic tutti closed as its last subticket merged ends `SCOPE_DONE: e's 2 subtickets are merged; tutti closed e` (`src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`): the epic's close reason, which names that subticket, says so.
- Nothing to run holds back a ready ticket as the run would (`heldAtStart`, `src/run/nothing.ts`; `src/main.ts`): a parent whose subtickets aren't all merged, a ticket blocked by one closed but not merged, a follow-up filed from one, so a run with only such tickets shows `○ Nothing ready to run` with their counts rather than start its loop. A worker the last run left in state.json is something to run (`carriedTickets`, `src/run/carry.ts`), where main passed none. Main ends there through `endIfNothing`, which writes the end record and gives back exit 0.
- An organ call's time runs from before its setup (§ 6.1 Cost; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`): a call of unknown cost records the time it ran with its session folder's making, and a time limit that ends the call during that setup records it too, where it recorded nothing. `test/organ-cost-unknown.test.ts`'s timed-out call now asserts a time within the call's, not the limit's, which the limit's earlier start made it fall short of under load; `test/organ-cost-slow-setup.test.ts` covers a slow setup.
- `scripts/test.sh` gives each run a temp folder of its own (`scripts/test.ts`): bun test runs with `TMPDIR` set to a `tutti-test-` folder in the system's temp folder, removed when the run ends, failed or interrupted (SIGINT, SIGTERM). The suites' temp folders no longer pile up in `$TMPDIR`, where hundreds of thousands of entries made a nested `bun test` take ~5 s to start, past bun's 5 s test timeout. `test/test-tmpdir.test.ts` covers it.
- `scripts/test.sh`'s per-run temp folder is out of macOS's per-user `$TMPDIR` (`scripts/test.ts`): bun test reads every folder above the one it starts in, so a nested `bun test` anywhere under a `$TMPDIR` holding ~550,000 entries took ~1-2 s to start, and `test/flaky.test.ts` hit bun's 5 s timeout. The folder is made in `/tmp` on macOS (`os.tmpdir()` elsewhere; `TUTTI_TEST_TMPDIR` names another), named `tutti-test-<pid>-…`, and each run first removes those whose run has ended without removing its own (SIGKILL); SIGHUP removes it too. `test/test-tmpdir.test.ts` covers it.
- Text no role colours is drawn in the terminal's own text colour (§ 7.11), not OpenTUI's exact white, which a light terminal couldn't show and which NO_COLOR didn't take away: `plainFg` in `src/ui/line.tsx`, given by `styled()` and every `<text>` of the dashboard, the forms and the worker view, whose Markdown and command output follow it. In the worker view, H3 and below are `heading3` bold, and a link's URL, bold, italic and quotes are in the text's colour. Under NO_COLOR, selected text is the palette's black on its white (slots 0 and 7), since OpenTUI's own selection now swaps two default colours.
- `test/ui-roles.test.tsx` (§ 13 "UI tests", § 14 M3): scenes of the dashboard, the cards, the title's states, wind-down, the stop question, the end-of-run box, attention cards, toasts, selected text, the start question and the worker view, drawn in the light and the dark variant, each state's cells checked against § 7.11's role in the `orchestra` scheme's colours; every colour drawn is a role's, a scheme that changes one role changes only that role's cells, and NO_COLOR keeps every glyph, word, bold and faint. Snapshots of the 80- and 120-column layouts, the smallest size, attention cards with `defaultToNo`, the end-of-run box, the usage banner, wind-down and one per phase, the same in both variants.
- `tutti init`'s form in its own renderer (§ 7.1, § 7.11, § 8.3; `src/ui/InitForm.tsx`): on a terminal that draws one, init asks its questions one field at a time under its title (the pill, `init`, the repository) and a stepper of its two steps, `✓ Workers ──── ● Checks`, the fields answered in the step kept above the one asked with their answers. Fields are drawn as huh's Charm theme draws them: Yes and No buttons, selects, checklists ticked `✓` or not `•` (the MCP servers and the suites), and lines to type in with their error under them. The scout looks under a spinner with the time it has taken, and `Esc` skips it, stopping it, the choice of checks then starting on manual; elsewhere `Esc` or Ctrl+C cancels and nothing is changed. While init works between questions a spinner says so. Once the renderer is gone the summary is printed on the main screen in init's styles (`src/print/initSummary.ts`): each step's mark (`✓` done, `•` faint, `✗` needsYou bold, `!` notice), what's left for you in the Next box framed in the pill's colour, and the sign-off. `TERM=dumb`, fewer than 256 colours or a renderer that can't be opened get the line forms, under the same title; both forms ask the same questions from `src/init/form.ts`. `test/ui-init-form.test.tsx` covers it, with snapshots of both steps.
- The Interview view (§ 4.10, § 7.1, § 5 item 3; `src/ui/InterviewView.tsx`, `src/run/interview.ts`, `src/ui/App.tsx`, `src/main.ts`): New feature at the start question now opens the interview in the same renderer, instead of ending the run. The view is built from the worker view's components: `← Back` and the interview's status over the session's transcript, the attention panel with its AskUserQuestion requests (`1`–`4` pick, Answer in view opens the answer form), and the composer, where you type your answers (`i`, Enter sends). Leaving it (`Esc` outside the composer, or a click on `← Back`) asks `End the interview? Nothing has been filed.` (`Box.Leave`), unless file_feature filed something. Once the epic is filed and the turn has ended, the plan confirmation asks `Start the run on <epic> (N tickets)? [y/N]`: y goes on to the dashboard with the run scoped to the epic; n or Esc leaves it filed and says `tutti --ticket <epic>` runs it. An interview left, quit with Ctrl+C, or whose session ended or couldn't start says what was filed, if anything, once the renderer has gone (exit 0, 130 or 4). Picked as lines (`TERM=dumb`, fewer than 256 colours), New feature says the interview needs the full screen and points to `--feature`. `test/ui-interview.test.tsx` covers it.
- Overlap holds and LIKELY_CONFLICT in a real run (§ 5 Footprints, § 8.4; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/asked.ts`): with the queue's `footprint` on (the default), the run gives the scheduler `footprintsOf`, with the fast and full checks' commands and each ticket's worktree at `<worktrees>/<id>`, so a ticket touching a running one's files is skipped while it runs, and two running worktrees changing one file give LIKELY_CONFLICT; `footprint: false` turns both off. A ticket resumed once its question is answered is carried with its issue, as a carried-over one is, so its footprint, parent and solo label count. `test/run-footprints.test.ts` covers it.
- Overlap holds and LIKELY_CONFLICT in a real run (§ 5 Footprints, § 8.4; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/asked.ts`): with the queue's `footprint` on (the default), the run gives the scheduler `footprintsOf`, with the fast and full checks' commands and each ticket's worktree at `<worktrees>/<id>`, so a ticket touching a running one's files is skipped while it runs, and two running worktrees changing one file give LIKELY_CONFLICT; `footprint: false` turns both off. A ticket resumed once its question is answered is carried with its issue, as a carried-over one is, so its footprint, parent and solo label count. A ticket that ends while a fill reads the footprints no longer holds back one touching its files: the fill looks again, and the run doesn't end with such a ticket held back and nothing running. `test/run-footprints.test.ts` and `test/schedule-overlap-ended.test.ts` cover it.
- `tutti init` imports an orchestra project's settings and worker prompt (§ 8.3, § 4.11; `src/init/import.ts`): in a project with `.orchestra/` and no `.tutti/`, init's form first offers to import them (`InitForm.import`, the line form's `Import orchestra's settings and worker prompt? [Y/n]`), naming what it would import and what it can't. orchestra 0.7.7's keys go to tutti's: `check_fast` (or the old `check`), `check_full`, their timeouts (or `check_timeout`), `setup` and `setup_files` to `checks`; `concurrent`; `ticket_limit` (`"0"` as none), `mcp_servers` and `worker_effort` to `workers`; `organ_effort`; `exclude_types` and `footprint` to `queue`; `resolve_conflicts`, `resolve_timeout` and `check_hand_backs` to `merge`; `environment_hold`. Each value is checked against tutti's settings, and one they can't take, `terminal`, and any other key are named in the summary with why; unset `mcp_servers` gets a note, since orchestra then gives every server. The Workers step starts from the imported settings and the flags still win. The imported prompt is copied as it is, and the summary lists, by line, the changes § 4.11 needs in it: tabs and terminals, `.orchestra` paths, `wt/` branches, the orchestrator, the time and `--ticket` lines, the foreground and removal reasons, a missing `TICKET_ID`, AskUserQuestion and the maintainer's messages. `.orchestra/` is only read. Without a terminal it isn't asked, and the summary says so. `test/import.test.ts` covers it, with `test/fixtures/orchestra-project/`.
- tutti init's Checks step (§ 8.3, § 6.3; `src/init/checks.ts`): the scout looks for the project's suites (the form runs it; Esc skips it), then *use them as they are* (two checklists, check-fast's and check-full's, and *also file tickets for untested areas*), *create from scratch* (runners that check nothing yet and a ticket to set the tests up with the create-check-suite skill, written to `.claude/skills/create-check-suite/`) or *manual* (one command), selected by what the scout found. It asks whether to replace a runner, or a copy of the skill, there that differs; the setup command, offered for the lockfiles at the top (`npm ci`, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, …); the ticket prefix, renaming the tickets once confirmed; and the verifier, `--verifier` (or `=false`) answering it: `.claude/agents/verifier.md`, told the stack and the runners' commands, and a Verify section in the worker prompt. It files the test work with bd, labelled `tutti-tests`, keeping tickets still open. `test/init-checks.test.ts` covers it with canned scout answers and the in-memory backlog.
- The run resumes answered tickets (§ 5.1, § 4.8; `src/run/run.ts`): each look the run takes, on the ready poll and as a ticket's fiber ends or a merge finishes, calls `takeAnswers` after `requeueWaiting` and before the fill, so an Asked ticket whose question is answered comes back in this run, its session resumed in a slot of its own; its fiber's end is handled as a dispatched one's (the environment hold, its stop such as PAUSED, a failure or a panic). While the run holds or winds down, answered tickets stay Asked for the next run. `test/run-answers.test.ts` covers it.
- The worker view's answer form (§ 4.5, § 7.3; `src/ui/answerForm.ts`, `src/ui/AnswerForm.tsx`): Answer in view in the worker view's panel, for what a card can't hold, opens a form over the view instead of doing nothing. A question request shows each question's options, `( )` to pick one or `[ ]` several, and Other with a line to type your own; Answer sends Choose, one answer a question (several joined by ", ", as `tutti answer` reads them). An elicitation shows its requestedSchema's fields: lines for text and numbers, a yes/no, and an enum's options, one or several, with the schema's defaults; Accept sends their content, the schema's own values. A field still missing a value, or out of its bounds, is named and focused; Deny or Decline refuses; Esc closes. A request withdrawn while its form is open closes it with a toast. `test/ui-answer-form.test.tsx` covers it.
- The end of a run while workers wait on you (D19, § 5 item 14, § 7.2; `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/runView.ts`, `src/ui/EndOfRunBox.tsx`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/run/commands.ts`): with a dashboard, a run whose only work left is workers with an open attention request doesn't end. It shows the end-of-run box in place of the Current cards: `Everything else is done. 2 workers wait for you: tut-9a1 (Allow Bash(rm -rf build)?), tut-7k2 (a question)`, with `q end the run` in the hint. Answering lets them carry on, and they may finish and merge. `q` (the new `UiCommand.EndRun`, which the run takes from the screen's command queue) ends the run as usual, its closing line the stops' or `READY_EMPTY`, with each such worker Stopped and suspended `waiting on you`. A worker past its ticket limit doesn't keep the run open. In plain mode, the run waits for such workers only until a stop holds it (`BLOCKED` at 4 minutes at the latest), then ends as usual and suspends them `waiting on you`. Status gains `endOfRun`, the tickets the run waits on, and RunView gains `endOfRun`, each with what its requests ask. The run also wakes as requests open and are answered. `test/end-of-run.test.ts` and `test/ui-end-of-run.test.tsx` cover it, and `test/scenarios-merge.test.ts`'s plain `BLOCKED` scenario now answers while another ticket still runs.
- The composer in the worker view (§ 7.5, § 7.3, § 4.3, § 7.11; `src/ui/Composer.tsx`, `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`): `i` or a click focuses it, `Enter` sends what you typed as a SendMessage, `Alt+Enter` or `Ctrl+J` start a new line, `Esc` leaves it before it leaves the view. `UiCommand.SendMessage` carries the uuid the composer drew, and `AgentHost.send` takes it (both layers), so a message sent while a turn is under way shows `queued` until the transcript's `command_lifecycle` says `started`, `completed` or `cancelled`, a `result` lists it, or the worker settles. `Ctrl+X` asks (`Box.Interrupt`), then sends Interrupt and drops the queued marks; `Ctrl+R`, or **Release** on the steered line, sends Release. The prompt is `formAccent`, the cursor `formSelected`, the placeholder `formPlaceholder`. A focused text field now also takes Enter, Tab and the arrows from the view's and the global layer, as the key is pressed (`keys.ts`'s `fieldTakes`); a box's layer still leaves it only the printable keys, as the answer form moves between its fields with them. `test/ui-composer.test.tsx` covers it.
- Carry-over (§ 4.8's The next run and The other carry-over cases, § 5.1, § 2; `src/run/carry.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/work.ts`, `src/run/start.ts`): before its first dispatch the run takes up the workers the last run suspended in state.json. One in progress is resumed with its saved session (`RESUMED: t-1 was suspended as the last run ended (quit); resuming its session …`), Working by `Resumed`, and told it was interrupted and to carry on, with this run's ticket limit and scope (`runFacts`). It takes a slot but counts toward no `--limit`, and its ticket limit is counted afresh (`follow`); one whose session can't be resumed is WORKER_GONE, left for review. A ticket closed between runs waits to merge, ToMerge by `Unmerged`, or is dropped when its branch is on the base already. One waiting on a question comes back Asked by `StillAsked`, and is resumed with "your question is answered" when that happened between runs. One deferred meanwhile is said to be deferred, and isn't resumed. One deleted from Beads, or whose worktree is gone, is dropped and forgotten in state.json. One bd can't show, or outside a `--ticket` scope, is kept for the next run. As a run ends, its Asked tickets are saved too, suspended `asked`, with their question (`saveAsked`). `test/carry.test.ts` covers each case.
- An organ's systemPrompt starts with a blank line (§ 6.1; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`): Claude Code puts its identity line in front of a string systemPrompt with no separator, so `toTurn` puts a blank line before each system message, and the identity line no longer runs into the organ's first sentence.
- The report's title (§ 7.10; `src/ui/sanitize.ts`) shows a base or scope that holds an email address or a bare `http://`, `https://`, `ftp://` or `www.` address as text: `escapeMarkdown` now escapes `@` and the `:` or `.` that starts such an address, which GFM links in bare text (`.@0.0` was an email link). `test/escape-markdown-links.test.ts` covers it.
- A mouse report split after its ESC no longer sends a stray Esc (§ 7.3, OpenTUI #1661; `src/ui/keys.ts`, `src/ui/Input.tsx`): when the rest of an SGR report (`[<64;87;26M`) is read after the stdin parser's 20 ms timeout, the parser gives the ESC as an Esc key and then the mouse event, and the Esc closed an open box (the quit box, the stop question) or left the composer. A lone Esc is now held for 30 ms (`escapeHold`, `holdEscape`): dropped when the next bytes read are the rest of a mouse report (`[<`, or X10's `[M`), as the parser tells, and pressed when other bytes or another key come first, in order, or when the hold ends. `test/ui-escape-hold.test.tsx` covers it.
- Merge and hold scenarios (§ 14 M5, § 13; `test/scenarios-merge.test.ts`): orchestra's merge and hold scenarios as whole runs under TestClock, each asserting its log lines, event records, phases (no PHASE_BUG), leaks and exit code: a conflict resolved and merged, a conflict rejected, a check fixed, a fix failing twice, a resolution rebased again as the base moves, the setup on a dependency change, the full check filing a ticket, the environment hold with the probe passing and failing, the usage-limit fixtures (a hold lifted at its reset, and a reset unknown ending the run), `TICKET_LIMIT`, and `BLOCKED` lifted by answering
- `tutti init`, its Workers step and the files it writes (§ 8.3, § 9.1, § 4.13; `src/init/init.ts`, `src/init/workers.ts`, `src/init/runners.ts`, `src/init/steps.ts`, `src/init/lineForm.ts`, the templates `src/project/templates/settings.json`, `tutti-gitignore`, `check-fast.sh` and `check-full.sh`): orchestra init's flags (`--check-fast`/`--check`, `--check-full`, `--check-fast-timeout`/`--check-timeout`, `--check-full-timeout`, `--setup`, `-c/--concurrent`, `--mcp`, `--changelog-union`, `--install-beads`, `--prefix`, `--force`; `--verifier` comes with the Checks step). It refuses in a linked worktree and while a run holds the lock (exit 2), asks what the flags don't answer through `InitForm` (a line at a time in a terminal for now; nothing without one), then holds the run lock with `"init": true` while it installs bd where it is missing (Homebrew or the Beads install script, with consent), runs `bd init` (not while anything is staged), gives a new Beads `--prefix` or renames an existing one's tickets with `bd rename-prefix` (refused while a ticket has a branch, a worker is carried over or a ticket is labelled `unmerged`), and writes `.tutti/settings.json` (the answers over what is there), `.tutti/worker-prompt.md` (the built-in template; `--force` replaces one), `.tutti/.gitignore` and the runners in orchestra's format (`SUITE:` lines, subshells, the `lock`/`unlock` helper for a serial suite; one that is there is kept unless its flag is given), and `CHANGELOG.md merge=union` when chosen. Its summary gives the concurrency with the memory it takes above 1, the MCP servers (the names defined here, `definedServers` in `src/agent/mcp.ts`), the login probe's account, billing and permission mode, why the colour scheme isn't used, and what's left; an untrusted main checkout is offered `projects["<checkout>"].hasTrustDialogAccepted: true` in the user's `.claude.json`, written atomically only after a yes, then probed again. `test/init.test.ts` covers it.
- Quitting and signals (§ 4.8, § 7.4, D15, D18; `src/run/quit.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/main.ts`): SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP while the run goes, or a quit request (`RunOptions.quits`, for the quit box's `q`), quit now in § 4.8's order: scheduling stops (`QUIT with Ctrl+C: no new tickets; interrupting the workers of …`) and the dashboard's title says `· Quitting… waiting for t-1's worker, t-2's merge` (Status and TitleView gain `quitting`); the tickets' fibers and the hand-backs stop, then every worker is interrupted at once and its result waited for, 10 s at most for all; the merges stop (a merge's protected step finishes, a rebase is aborted, a check or setup killed, its ticket back ToMerge), triage is aborted (`MergeQueue.stopHandBacks` and `stopMerges`, `Triage.interrupt`); every session is closed, state.json saves each worker not merged or dropped as suspended `quit`, and the run ends `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C; t-1's session is saved for the next run`, exit 130. A second request says what is under way (`t-2's merge is under way; press Ctrl+C again to abandon it`); a third saves the workers, says `INTERRUPTED: quit at once …; abandoned t-2's merge: …` and exits at once (`RunOptions.exitNow`, the renderer destroyed first). A panic in a ticket's fiber or one of the run's watchers is logged with its stack (`PANIC in t-1's fiber: …`) and quits the run the same way, PANIC, exit 4; a panic outside them is said with its stack once the renderer has gone, exit 4. tutti's `runMain` replaces BunRuntime's so that these signals reach the run while it goes and interrupt the command before and after it; SIGHUP was not caught before. SIGUSR2 winds the run down as SIGUSR1 does, both caught before the lock names tutti's PID, and SIGTSTP is ignored with a log line. `test/quit.test.ts` covers it.
- Quitting and signals (§ 4.8, § 7.4, D15, D18; `src/run/quit.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/main.ts`): SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP while the run goes, or a quit request (`RunOptions.quits`, for the quit box's `q`), quit now in § 4.8's order: scheduling stops (`QUIT with Ctrl+C: no new tickets; interrupting the workers of …`) and the dashboard's title says `· Quitting… waiting for t-1's worker, t-2's merge` (Status and TitleView gain `quitting`); the tickets' fibers and the hand-backs stop, then every worker is interrupted at once and its result waited for, 10 s at most for all, the interrupts included; the merges stop (a merge's protected step finishes, a rebase is aborted, a check or setup killed, its ticket back ToMerge), triage is aborted (`MergeQueue.stopHandBacks` and `stopMerges`, `Triage.interrupt`); every session is closed, state.json saves each worker not merged or dropped as suspended `quit`, and the run ends `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C; t-1's session is saved for the next run`, exit 130. A second request says what is under way (`t-2's merge is under way; press Ctrl+C again to abandon it`); a third saves the workers, says `INTERRUPTED: quit at once …; abandoned t-2's merge: …` and exits at once (`RunOptions.exitNow`, the renderer destroyed first). A quit while the full check runs stops it (`FULL_CHECK_SKIPPED`) and ends the run `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C during the full check`, 130. A panic in a ticket's fiber, one of the run's watchers, the merge queue's consumer or a hand-back (`make`'s `onPanic`; a merge cut short before its fast-forward is back in line) is logged with its stack (`PANIC in t-1's fiber: …`) and quits the run the same way, PANIC, exit 4; a panic outside them is said with its stack once the renderer has gone, exit 4. tutti's `runMain` replaces BunRuntime's so that these signals reach the run while it goes and interrupt the command before and after it; SIGHUP was not caught before. SIGUSR2 winds the run down as SIGUSR1 does, both caught before the lock names tutti's PID, and SIGTSTP is ignored with a log line. `test/quit.test.ts` covers it.
- Quitting and signals (§ 4.8, § 7.4, D15, D18; `src/run/quit.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/main.ts`): SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP while the run goes, or a quit request (`RunOptions.quits`, for the quit box's `q`), quit now in § 4.8's order: scheduling stops (`QUIT with Ctrl+C: no new tickets; interrupting the workers of …`) and the dashboard's title says `· Quitting… waiting for t-1's worker, t-2's merge` (Status and TitleView gain `quitting`); the tickets' fibers and the hand-backs stop, then every worker is interrupted at once and its result waited for, 10 s at most for all, the interrupts included; the merges stop (a merge's protected step finishes, a rebase is aborted, a check or setup killed, its ticket back ToMerge), triage is aborted (`MergeQueue.stopHandBacks` and `stopMerges`, `Triage.interrupt`); every session is closed, state.json saves each worker not merged or dropped as suspended `quit`, and the run ends `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C; t-1's session is saved for the next run`, exit 130. A second request says what is under way (`t-2's merge is under way; press Ctrl+C again to abandon it`); a third saves the workers, says `INTERRUPTED: quit at once …; abandoned t-2's merge: …` and exits at once (`RunOptions.exitNow`, the renderer destroyed first). A quit while the full check runs stops it (`FULL_CHECK_SKIPPED`) and ends the run `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C during the full check`, 130. A quit as the run ends, after its loop, ends it INTERRUPTED too. A panic in the run's loop, a ticket's fiber, one of the run's watchers, the merge queue's consumer or a hand-back (`make`'s `onPanic`; a merge cut short before its fast-forward is back in line) is logged with its stack (`PANIC in t-1's fiber: …`) and quits the run the same way, PANIC, exit 4; a panic elsewhere in the run's end is said with its stack once the renderer has gone, exit 4. tutti's `runMain` replaces BunRuntime's so that these signals reach the run while it goes and interrupt the command before and after it; SIGHUP was not caught before. SIGUSR2 winds the run down as SIGUSR1 does, both caught before the lock names tutti's PID, and SIGTSTP is ignored with a log line. `test/quit.test.ts` covers it.
- Quitting and signals (§ 4.8, § 7.4, D15, D18; `src/run/quit.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/main.ts`): SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP while the run goes, or a quit request (`RunOptions.quits`, for the quit box's `q`), quit now in § 4.8's order: scheduling stops (`QUIT with Ctrl+C: no new tickets; interrupting the workers of …`) and the dashboard's title says `· Quitting… waiting for t-1's worker, t-2's merge` (Status and TitleView gain `quitting`); the tickets' fibers and the hand-backs stop, then every worker is interrupted at once and its result waited for, 10 s at most for all, the interrupts included; the merges stop (a merge's protected step finishes, a rebase is aborted, a check or setup killed, its ticket back ToMerge), triage is aborted (`MergeQueue.hold`, which also keeps any ticket from taking its turn or being handed back while quitting waits, a merge that would hand back put back in line; `stopMerges`; `Triage.interrupt`); every session is closed, state.json saves each worker not merged or dropped as suspended `quit`, and the run ends `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C; t-1's session is saved for the next run`, exit 130. A second request says what is under way (`t-2's merge is under way; press Ctrl+C again to abandon it`); a third saves the workers, says `INTERRUPTED: quit at once …; abandoned t-2's merge: …` and exits at once (`RunOptions.exitNow`, the renderer destroyed first). A quit while the full check runs stops it (`FULL_CHECK_SKIPPED`) and ends the run `INTERRUPTED: quit with Ctrl+C during the full check`, 130. A quit as the run ends, after its loop, ends it INTERRUPTED too. A panic in the run's loop, a ticket's fiber, one of the run's watchers, the merge queue's consumer or a hand-back (`make`'s `onPanic`; a merge cut short before its fast-forward is back in line) is logged with its stack (`PANIC in t-1's fiber: …`) and quits the run the same way, PANIC, exit 4; a panic elsewhere in the run's end is said with its stack once the renderer has gone, exit 4. tutti's `runMain` replaces BunRuntime's so that these signals reach the run while it goes and interrupt the command before and after it; SIGHUP was not caught before. SIGUSR2 winds the run down as SIGUSR1 does, both caught before the lock names tutti's PID, and SIGTSTP is ignored with a log line. `test/quit.test.ts` covers it.
- An organ call that ends without an SDK result (§ 6.1 Cost; `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`, `src/organs/OrganCost.ts`, `src/run/report.ts`), timed out or its session failing first, is recorded as it ends, interruption included, as a call of unknown cost with the time it ran: `ORGAN triage: cost unknown, no result after 180.0s`. OrganCost gains `recordUnknown`, and its `Total` an `unknown` count of such calls, which count as calls; the report's total names them: `$0.0456 in 7 organ calls, 9 turns in all; 1 call's cost unknown`. `test/organ-cost-unknown.test.ts` covers it.
- The setup (`checks.setup`, `src/run/setup.ts`) runs before the check on a rebase its worker resolved too, as orchestra's checkRebased has it: a lockfile the base brought in is installed before the resolution is checked, and a setup that fails is handed back as a failed check (Fixing{afterResolution}). `RebaseStop` gains `fork`, where the branch left the base, which `fixResolution` passes on to its fixes' setup in place of the base it was rebased onto. `test/resolution-setup.test.ts` covers it.
- shellcheck's fetch moves to `scripts/fetch-shellcheck.sh`, which `scripts/check.sh` runs: it extracts the binary to a temporary name and renames it into place only once tar succeeds, failing the step (exit 1) when tar fails, so a disk that fills partway no longer leaves a truncated binary that every later check runs (`Malformed Mach-o file`, exit 126). A cached binary whose `--version` fails is fetched again. `test/fetch-shellcheck.test.ts` covers it.
- The dashboard shows each worker's state from its first start (§ 7.2; `src/run/screen.ts`): `workerStates` follows the workers AgentHost knows, from its new `workers` stream, rather than the tickets whose ledger phase holds a worker, which the ledger moves to Working before the worker is started; a ticket dispatched with no ledger change after its start, such as the second of two dispatched together, had its card show no worker state. A worker ended and started again is followed anew. `test/worker-states.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti colours [<name>]` (§ 7.11, § 8.1; `src/cli/colours.ts`): previews a colour scheme without a run, the one in use by default (AppConfig's, the name given standing in for `--colour-scheme`). It lists every role with the scheme its colour comes from (the file, or one it extends) and a sample of its light and dark colour; draws a mock worker card, attention card, tickets table and hint in the terminal's own variant, with the dashboard's own lines painted in the theme's ANSI styles; and names anything that keeps the scheme from being used on stderr, exiting 1, so a scheme can be checked in CI. `test/colours-command.test.ts` covers it.
- The holds that come with time (§ 4.3, § 4.5, § 5 item 10; `src/run/holds.ts`, `src/run/run.ts`, `src/run/work.ts`): a worker still going `--ticket-limit` after its dispatch (the time it waited UsageLimited not counted, as orchestra's) notes its ticket and holds the run with `TICKET_LIMIT: <id> still working after 2h (worktree …); its worker goes on, and is suspended when the run ends`, its HOLD line said even with nothing else running. It isn't interrupted and may still finish and merge, but the run doesn't wait for it: once nothing else runs or merges the run ends (exit 3), stops its fiber and suspends it (Working → Stopped, a `suspended` record with `TICKET_LIMIT`). Without a limit, `  LONG_RUNNING: <id> still working after 2h; …` is logged once, as a `warn` record. An attention request unanswered for 4 minutes holds the run with `BLOCKED >4min: <id> needs you: <what>` and the `BLOCKED` banner on the title line; once that ticket has no request open the hold is lifted (`HOLD lifted: <id>'s requests were answered`, a `hold` record with `lifted`), and the run takes tickets again when no other stop holds it. The request stays answerable for the rest of the run. `liftStops` takes stops back out; `test/holds-time.test.ts` covers them under TestClock.
- The worker view (§ 7.5, § 7.3, § 7.11, § 4.4; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/transcriptItems.ts`), in place of App's placeholder: `← Dashboard` (a click or `Esc` sends CloseWorker), the worker's card head and its title, then tutti's transcript of the worker. Assistant text is drawn as Markdown (`<markdown>`, the scheme's `heading1`–`heading3`, `code` and `link` roles); each tool call is one line, `Tool  summary`, its result at the end (`✓` `done`, an error's first line `needsYou`, `+N −M` `added`/`removed` for Edit, MultiEdit and Write); `Enter` or a click expands it: an edit's diff (`<diff>`, signed lines, its numbers counted from the replacement's first line), a command's output (`<code>`, faint, at most 400 lines), a read's range (`lines 40–42`). An Agent call's subagent work, nested by `parent_tool_use_id`, is collapsed under it and expands behind a faint `│`; a subagent's items whose call is older than the window are grouped the same way. Thinking, system messages and results are hidden; the messages sent to the worker show as `› …`. While the view is open its transcript's open flag is set, and partial text streams in. The attention panel shows the worker's open requests (two whole, `+N more requests` after) with the card's buttons and rules (`attentionKeys.ts`: Deny focused first, `defaultToNo`, the 500 ms guard, `d`, `Shift+D`, `a`, `r`, `1`–`4`), each with its description, reason and input; a hand-back waiting for a steered worker shows as a notice whose **Hand it the conflict now** sends the new `UiCommand.HandBackNow`. `Tab`, `Shift+Tab` and the arrows move between items, requests and buttons; `1`–`9` switch workers; `?` shows the view's keys. Bounded rendering: the last 200 items stick to the bottom; past the top (the wheel, PgUp, `g`) 200 older are read from the transcript file, the newest beyond 600 let go and your place kept; `G` or the bottom goes back to the latest 200. `ScreenInputs` gains `transcripts`, which `dashboard()` turns into `ShellValue.transcripts` (`transcriptFeed`); `ShellValue.handBacks` names the tickets with a hand-back waiting (not yet fed by the run); `Transcripts.toolSummary` and `Card.tsx`'s `headOf` are exported, and `KeyHelp` takes its keys. `test/ui-worker-view.test.tsx` covers it.
- The worker view (§ 7.5, § 7.3, § 7.11, § 4.4; `src/ui/WorkerView.tsx`, `src/ui/transcriptItems.ts`), in place of App's placeholder: `← Dashboard` (a click or `Esc` sends CloseWorker), the worker's card head and its title, then tutti's transcript of the worker. Assistant text is drawn as Markdown (`<markdown>`, the scheme's `heading1`–`heading3`, `code` and `link` roles); each tool call is one line, `Tool  summary`, its result at the end (`✓` `done`, an error's first line `needsYou`, `+N −M` `added`/`removed` for Edit, MultiEdit and Write); `Enter` or a click expands it: an edit's diff (`<diff>`, signed lines, its numbers counted from the replacement's first line), a command's output (`<code>`, faint, at most 400 lines), a read's range (`lines 40–42`). An Agent call's subagent work, nested by `parent_tool_use_id`, is collapsed under it and expands behind a faint `│`; a subagent's items whose call is older than the window are grouped the same way. Thinking, system messages and results are hidden; the messages sent to the worker show as `› …`. While the view is open its transcript's open flag is set, and partial text streams in. The attention panel shows the worker's two oldest open requests, `+N more requests` under them (only those drawn can be selected or answered), with the card's buttons and rules (`attentionKeys.ts`: Deny focused first, `defaultToNo`, the 500 ms guard, `d`, `Shift+D`, `a`, `r`, `1`–`4`), each with its description, reason and whole input, which scrolls under the buttons when it is long; a hand-back waiting for a steered worker shows as a notice whose **Hand it the conflict now** sends the new `UiCommand.HandBackNow`. `Tab`, `Shift+Tab` and the arrows move between items, requests and buttons; `1`–`9` switch workers; `?` shows the view's keys. Bounded rendering: the last 200 items stick to the bottom; past the top (the wheel, PgUp, `g`) 200 older are read from the transcript file, the newest beyond 600 let go and your place kept; `G` or the bottom goes back to the latest 200. `ScreenInputs` gains `transcripts`, which `dashboard()` turns into `ShellValue.transcripts` (`transcriptFeed`); `ShellValue.handBacks` names the tickets with a hand-back waiting (not yet fed by the run); `Transcripts.toolSummary` and `Card.tsx`'s `headOf` are exported, and `KeyHelp` takes its keys. `test/ui-worker-view.test.tsx` covers it.
- Spec § 12: the musl builds' archives are `tutti_<ver>_linux_<arch>_musl.tar.gz` beside the glibc builds' `tutti_<ver>_linux_<arch>.tar.gz`, `<os>` and `<arch>` as Bun names them. `scripts/install.sh` picks musl on Linux from `TUTTI_LIBC` (`musl` or `glibc`), else `ldd --version` naming musl or glibc, else `/lib/ld-musl-<machine>.so.1`; `ldd` comes first since Debian's `musl` package installs that loader on a glibc system. `tutti update` picks the archive of its own build's target and detects nothing.
- The embedded Claude Code's extracted copies (§ 12; `src/agent/claudeExtract.ts`, `src/agent/claudeBinary.ts`): a compiled build's entry names its target (`embed(claude, "<target>")`), and a run whose Claude Code is already extracted finds the copy by the SDK manifest's checksum and size, in `<tmp>/claude-<uid>/claude-agent-sdk-<hash>/`, without reading and hashing the 250-268 MB embedded file again (130-160 ms a start on macOS arm64, about 1 s under Rosetta); only a regular file of the manifest's size, the user's own, in a 0700 `claude-<uid>` folder of the user's, is used, else `extractFromBunfs` runs as before. Once extracted, off the startup path, the other `claude-agent-sdk-*` folders there that hold a `claude-*` file and are untouched for a day are removed; a run touches its own folder as it starts and every hour while it runs, so another tutti doesn't remove a version in use. `test/claude-extract.test.ts` covers it.
- Copying selected text (§ 7.3, § 7.11; `src/ui/clipboard.ts`): a drag selects text with OpenTUI's selection, drawn in the `selection` and `selectionText` roles (OpenTUI's own under NO_COLOR, `SelectionLook` in `src/ui/line.tsx`), and on release what it selected, without the spaces padded lines leave, is copied with the toast `copied`, or `couldn't copy` when that fails; a click copies nothing. In a local session (no `SSH_CONNECTION`, `SSH_CLIENT` or `SSH_TTY`) it copies with `pbcopy` on macOS, and on Linux with `wl-copy` in a Wayland session or `xclip -selection clipboard` in an X one when installed, the text on stdin; otherwise with OSC 52, which the renderer writes in order with its frames. The command runs in a process group of its own, left running once it exits 0, so what `wl-copy` and `xclip` fork to serve the clipboard stays. `ShellValue` gains `clipboard`, which `dashboard()` passes and the run makes with `liveClipboard`. `test/clipboard.test.ts` covers it.
- `--feature`'s plan confirmation is asked in the command's one renderer with the dashboard (§ 7.1; `src/run/feature.ts`, `src/main.ts`): the screen and plan organ lines stay on the main screen, then the renderer opens for `askPlan` and is kept for the dashboard; what the feature run says while it is open (filing, declined, cancelled) is said on the main screen once it has gone (`inRenderer`). A renderer that can't open says `tutti cannot open its screen` and files nothing. Plain output still asks a line at a time. `test/feature-renderer.test.ts` covers it.
- The feature interview's session and its filing tools (§ 4.10; `src/agent/interview.ts`, `src/agent/interviewTools.ts`, `src/project/templates/interview-prompt.md`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`): `startInterview` saves the description to `.tutti/run/feature-request.md`, starts an SDK session in the main checkout (AgentHost's `Session.interview`, known as `tutti-interview`) and sends it the description between `<pasted_content id="…">` tags with a fresh ID, after orchestra's lead. Its system prompt is orchestra v0.7.7's interview instructions, adapted to tutti's tools (Matt Pocock's grilling skill, MIT License, its notice in the prompt and in the new `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`), appended to the `claude_code` preset; its settings are loaded and its tools enforced (`interviewOptions`): `tools` is Read, Grep, Glob, Agent and AskUserQuestion, and its one MCP server, under `strictMcpConfig`, is tutti's in-process `tutti` (`createSdkMcpServer`), whose `tickets_list`, `ticket_show` and `file_feature` a PreToolUse hook allows. `file_feature` checks the epic and its tickets with `validatePlan`, refusing with every reason and filing nothing, files them once with `filePlan` and answers with the epic's ID; there is no `feature.json`. Once it has filed and the session is idle, `ended` ends the session and gives the epic and its tickets by ID with `Start the run on <epic> (N tickets)?`. `ChosenServer.scope` can be `sdk`; `zod` (4.6.7, the SDK's peer) is now a direct dependency. `test/interview.test.ts` covers it.
- The usage-limit hold (§ 4.12, § 2, § 5 item 10; `src/agent/UsageLimits.ts`, `src/run/usageLimit.ts`): AgentHost reports each `rate_limit_event`'s info, and each worker becoming UsageLimited or ceasing to be, to the new UsageLimits service (its test layer in `src/testing/UsageLimits.ts`). `allowed_warning` shows `usage 85% of the five-hour limit · resets 14:05` on the title line (`· overage` beside it while overage is in use) and logs `USAGE` once per limit, without changing dispatch. `rejected`, or a worker UsageLimited by a 429 alone, holds the run without a stop: no new tickets, organ calls skipped (`organHold` provides OrganHold from UsageLimits), `USAGE_LIMIT · resets 14:05` on the title line and `usage limit (five-hour) · resets 14:05 · 3 workers wait for it` on the warning line, one notification. UsageLimited workers never settle, so they aren't nudged, triaged or counted toward the environment hold. At the reset plus a minute the hold is lifted (`USAGE_LIMIT lifted`) and each UsageLimited worker not steered is sent a client_composed message to carry on. With nothing else running the run waits for the reset, the banner counting down (`· in 1h12m`) and the log saying it every 10 minutes; it ends `USAGE_LIMIT` (exit 8), suspending the waiting workers, only when the reset is unknown or later than `--usage-limit-wait`. Each change of a limit's status is a `usage_limit` record, and the hold and its lifting `hold` records. RunView's status is now live (a SubscriptionRef the run updates), `RunHold.waiting`, `Usage.limit` and `Usage.overage` are new, the run's live layers gain the Notifier, and `Scheduler.interrupt` stops the tickets' fibers. `test/usage-limit.test.ts` covers it, with the recorded fixtures.
- Attention cards (§ 4.5, § 7.2, § 7.3, § 7.11; `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`): a card whose worker waits for you grows, in its number order, to show its oldest request: the line (the title, else the display name and the description or the input's summary), the summary and the reason faint, `+N more`, and its buttons in the form roles: Deny, Allow and Allow for this run (not with `suppressAlwaysAllowRule`, nor without an `addRules` suggestion that allows) for a permission; a question's options one a line; Decline and Open URL, or Decline and Answer in view for a form with fields, for an elicitation; Answer in view for several questions or a multiple choice. With no card chosen the card of the oldest request is highlighted and its request the target. `Tab`, `Shift+Tab`, `→` and `←` move between a highlighted attention card's buttons and on to the next card, `↓` and `↑` from card to card; `Enter` presses the focused button (Deny at first, never Allow; none for a question, whose `Enter` opens the card until `1`–`4` pick an option); `d` denies (declines), `Shift+D` opens a small field for the reason, `a` allows only without `defaultToNo`, `r` allows for this run; a click presses a button. Each sends an Answer UiCommand naming the request drawn when the key was pressed; one no longer open in the RunView is dropped with the toast `<ticket>'s request is no longer open`. Input to a card is ignored for 500 ms after its request appears or changes. On a compact line `Enter` opens the Answer box, the card over the pane with the same keys. `useLatestRunView` and `useClock` are new in `useRunView.ts`, and `Box` gains `Answer` and `Reason`. `test/ui-attention.test.tsx` covers it with mockInput and mockMouse.
- Attention cards (§ 4.5, § 7.2, § 7.3, § 7.11; `src/ui/AttentionCard.tsx`, `src/ui/attentionKeys.ts`): a card whose worker waits for you grows, in its number order, to show its oldest request: the line (the title, else the display name and the description or the input's summary), the summary and the reason faint, `+N more`, and its buttons in the form roles: Deny, Allow and Allow for this run (not with `suppressAlwaysAllowRule`, nor without an `addRules` suggestion that allows) for a permission; a question's options one a line; Decline and Open URL, or Decline and Answer in view for a form with fields, for an elicitation; Answer in view for several questions or a multiple choice. With no card chosen the card of the oldest request is highlighted and its request the target. `Tab`, `Shift+Tab`, `→` and `←` move between a highlighted attention card's buttons and on to the next card, `↓` and `↑` from card to card; `Enter` presses the focused button (Deny at first, never Allow; none for a question, whose `Enter` opens the card until `1`–`4` pick an option); `d` denies (declines), `Shift+D` opens a small field for the reason, `a` allows and `r` allows for this run, each only without `defaultToNo`; a click presses a button. Each sends an Answer UiCommand naming the request drawn when the key was pressed; one no longer open in the RunView is dropped with the toast `<ticket>'s request is no longer open`. Input to a card is ignored for 500 ms after its request appears or changes. On a compact line `Enter` opens the Answer box, the card over the pane with the same keys. `useLatestRunView` and `useClock` are new in `useRunView.ts`, and `Box` gains `Answer` and `Reason`. `test/ui-attention.test.tsx` covers it with mockInput and mockMouse.
- Triage of deferred tickets (§ 5 items 11 and 13, § 6.2; `src/organs/triage.ts`, `src/organs/prompts/triage.md`, `src/run/triage.ts`): each ticket set aside as its worker settles (deferred by its worker, or by tutti for a worker that left it unfinished), unless its worker reached the usage limit, is triaged in the background, one at a time, with orchestra v0.7.7's prompt and answer schema, adapted. Its evidence is tutti's own record of the worker: the ticket as bd show gives it, the end of its transcript (the last 40 items, at most 8,000 characters of their text), its attention requests and their answers and the calls Claude Code denied it without asking (Attention's tally and the log's lines), its compactions, its worktree's changes and the error its last turn failed with. The verdict goes into the ticket's notes (`Triage (tutti): cause = …`), on its row (`◆ environment · high · …`), in the log (`  triage <id>: …`) and a `triage` record, and counts toward the environment hold; a failed triage is `TRIAGE_FAILED`. The run doesn't end while a ticket waits for its verdict. Off with `--triage=false` or `organs.triage: false`, and in a run given no `Organs`; a quit skips it.
- The environment hold and the probe (§ 5 item 11, § 4.9, § 2, § 5.1; `src/run/environment.ts`): `environment_hold.count` tickets in a row whose workers settled within `environment_hold.window` of dispatch with the ticket still open, no commits and a clean worktree, or that triage blamed on the environment with high confidence (`EnvironmentHold.blamed`, with the hold's `generation` taken as the ticket is queued for triage), hold the run with `ENVIRONMENT` as a stop does: no new tickets, the running ones finish, and the tickets that failed at once are reopened in bd and in the run (Deferred → Waiting, `reopened` records), as is one failing at once while the run holds. A worker seen UsageLimited in its attempt, a ticket waiting for a merge and one set aside as it started count for nothing. Once nothing runs, after `environment_hold.probe` (0: no probe), one session without a ticket (AgentHost's `Session.probe`: no MCP servers, no worker prompt) is started in the main checkout and asked to run `echo ok > .tutti/run/probe`; within 5 minutes it is `PROBE_OK` (a `probed` record), the reopened tickets go back to Queued and the run takes tickets again, else the run ends `ENVIRONMENT` with exit 7, saying why the probe failed. The machine is probed once a run. RunView's Status gains `probe`, shown as a card labelled `probe`; `Worktrees.readRunFile` is new. `test/environment.test.ts` covers it.
- `--feature "<request>"` (§ 5 item 13, § 6, § 7.1 Plan confirmation; `src/organs/screen.ts`, `src/organs/plan.ts`, their prompts in `src/organs/prompts/`, `src/project/planFile.ts`, `src/run/feature.ts`): orchestra v0.7.7's screen (low effort, 2 minutes) judges the request against the repository's name and README (ok, reject or unclear, with a reason), then its plan organ, adapted, with `solo` added (high effort, 10 minutes), plans it as an epic and 1 to 12 tickets from the README, the agents' instructions (CLAUDE.md, the files a CLAUDE.md of imports imports, or AGENTS.md), the repository's files with their line counts, the tickets not closed and the files the request names, none read through a link out of the checkout. The plan is shown (type, priority, files, `after:`, what checking it changed) and `File these N tickets and start the run? [y/N]` asked a line at a time before the dashboard opens; `--yes` files it without asking, and without a terminal to ask on nothing is filed. tutti then files the epic, its tickets as its children (files in the `files` metadata, solo ones labelled `solo`) and their blocks links with bd, and runs scoped to the epic (`START … · feature <epic>`). Each stop says why on stderr and as a `FEATURE` log line: exit 2 for a request rejected, unclear or not planned, 4 when bd fails (with what was filed and how to remove it or carry on), 0 when declined, 130 when cancelled. `validatePlan` checks a plan as orchestra checks its plan organ's, giving every reason a plan can't be filed and never throwing, and `filePlan` files one; the interview's `file_feature` tool is to share them. The run's live layers gain the organs over the Agent SDK. `test/feature.test.ts` covers it, with a property test that validation of arbitrary input never throws.
- Keys and the mouse (§ 7.3, § 7.4, § 7.8, D15, D18; `src/ui/keys.ts`, `src/ui/Input.tsx`, `src/ui/QuitBox.tsx`, `src/ui/StopQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/Toasts.tsx`, `src/ui/Modal.tsx`): the input layers over `@opentui/keymap` (0.6.6, pinned), highest first: Ctrl+C and Ctrl+Z, a focused text field, an open box, the view's keys, the global keys (`useLayer`). A layer below the field leaves printable keys to a focused input or textarea, and the view's and global layers are off while a box is open; a box's keys are bound before it opens, so a key pressed as it opens reaches it. Ctrl+C opens the quit box (`s` Drain, `q` Quit{now}, `Esc` cancel, each also a click target); a second Ctrl+C quits now. Ctrl+Z shows the toast `tutti can't be suspended: s stops after the current tickets, Ctrl+C quits`. `s` on the dashboard asks orchestra's stop question (`Stop after the running tickets?`, or while the run winds down `Keep taking tickets?`), `y` sending Drain or Undrain, `n` or `Esc` nothing. Toasts are faint, or `needsYou` for a failure, for 4 s. The rest of an SGR mouse report split after `ESC [` (OpenTUI #1661) is kept out of the keys, and a bracketed paste with no end for 2 s since its last bytes (#1270) is ended, so what arrived goes into the focused field in one piece. `--no-mouse` and `"mouse": false` turn the renderer's mouse capture off (`rendererConfigFor`; `open` takes `mouse`, and `make` its config). `test/ui-keys.test.tsx` covers it.
- The dashboard's worker cards, wind-down line, hint and key help (§ 7.2, § 5.1 "How the phases are shown", § 7.11; `src/ui/Card.tsx`, `src/ui/WindDown.tsx`, `src/ui/Hint.tsx`, `src/ui/KeyHelp.tsx`, `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`). Under `Current`, a card per ticket with work under way, in its worker's stable number order: the number, the mark (Bubbles' `Dot` spinner, ten frames a second from the shell's clock, while the worker works, in `Resolving` and `Fixing`; `◷` to merge, `◆` merging, `•` after a turn), the ticket, § 4.2's status in § 7.11's style with `steered` beside it, the time in its phase (`1m07s`), the title, and the action line: the phase's (a merge's step, a recheck), else the worker's latest action behind `▸`, a denied call `notice` for its 10 seconds. Cards are framed `live` while the worker works, `needsYou` when it waits for you, `frame` otherwise; two to a row from 120 columns, all as tall as the tallest; one line each in one compact box (`■ allow?` for a request) when the pane can't hold a card each; and with no card a box says `picking the next ticket…` or `stopping…`. `Tab`, `Shift+Tab` and the arrows move a highlight (the card's frame bold in the heavy border style), and `Enter`, `1`–`9` or a click send `OpenWorker` with the worker's number. The wind-down line draws a `■` per ticket coloured by its phase, then `Winding down · N running, no new tickets`; the hint moves to `Hint.tsx`; `?` opens the key help over the dashboard, and `?` or `Esc` closes it. RunView's cards gain `latest` and `steered`; its sources gain the workers' latest actions, read from Transcripts every 500 ms (`latestActions` in `src/run/screen.ts`, `Transcripts.action`), so the run needs Transcripts. `test/ui-cards.test.tsx` covers it.
- The run asks the start question (§ 5 item 3, § 7.1; `src/run/choose.ts`, `src/main.ts`): after the startup checks and the run lock, when stdin and stdout are TTYs and no flag decides (`--tickets`, `--ticket` or `TUTTI_TICKET`, `--feature`, `--plain`, a `--limit` of 0), a bare `tutti` asks `What should this run work on?` in its one renderer, before the dashboard, or as lines over stdin when the terminal can't draw one (TERM=dumb, fewer than 256 colours). The current tickets' count is bd ready's in scope, else `none, all done` or `none ready (N open)` as nothing to run would say, else no count when bd can't say. Current tickets goes on to nothing to run and the run; a cancelled question says `tutti: stopped before the run started; nothing was changed.` and exits 130, with no start event. A new feature is named in tutti.lock (`HeldLock.rewrite`, which `RunLock.acquire` now gives) and the start event, its description is printed on the main screen once the renderer has closed, and, until the interview (tutti-nrg.9.8) is built, the run ends there, filing nothing. `--feature`'s request is named in the lock too. `test/run-start-question.test.ts` covers it.
- A turn keeps a tool or subagent its hook set before its `system/init` was folded in (§ 4.2, `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`): hooks reach the host on the SDK's control channel and messages through its queue, so a turn's first PreToolUse can come first, and the new turn no longer resets it, which showed the worker working rather than testing or editing until the next hook. The last turn's tool and subagents still go with its result. `test/hook-before-init.test.ts` covers it, and the live AgentHost test sends init and the hook back to back.
- The run checks the tickets earlier runs left labelled `unmerged` before its first dispatch and labels its own as it ends (§ 4.8, `src/run/run.ts`): `loadUnmerged` runs before the scheduler first fills its slots, so one merged by hand since (on tutti/<id> or orchestra's wt/<id>) loses its label and the tickets it blocks can start; bd failing to list them stops the run, `BEADS_FAILED: could not read the tickets left unmerged: <why>`, exit 4, with nothing started. `labelAtEnd` runs after the RunEnded transitions, before the summary. The Nothing to run box counts the labelled tickets `stillUnmerged` (`src/run/unmerged.ts`) finds not merged since, read with `mergedSince` and without unlabelling, so its count agrees with the run's. `test/run-unmerged.test.ts` covers them.
- A git read whose output runs past exec's cap fails rather than lose its start (§ 3.3; `src/services/exec.ts`, `src/services/Git.ts`): exec's `Output` says which of stdout and stderr ran past `maxOutputBytes` (`truncated`), and Git's reads (ls-files, log, status, diff --name-only, worktree list, merge-tree, rev-parse, symbolic-ref) fail with GitFailed, with git's exit code and `its output ran past <n> bytes`, when stdout did; before, a very large repository's listing silently lost its first entries and had a partial first field. Writes, whose output isn't read, are unchanged. `Git.layer` takes `maxOutputBytes` for tests; `test/output-cap.test.ts` covers both.
- The lines that say what failed (§ 3.3, `src/services/Checks.ts`) are picked for bun test, tsc and Biome too, besides orchestra's patterns for Go's tools and generic errors: bun test's `(fail) <test>` lines and its ` 2 fail` count, tsc's `file.ts(10,5): error TS…`, `Found 3 errors.`, and a Biome diagnostic that is an error (its first line after it starts with `×`) as its header, `file.ts:10:5 lint/…` or `file.ts format`, without the drawn line, followed by that first line; Biome's warnings are not said. A failing check in a TypeScript project, tutti's own, now says what failed besides its last lines. `test/checks-typescript.test.ts` covers it.
- Mode 2027 (grapheme clustering) is set back as the terminal had it once the renderer has closed (§ 7.4, `src/ui/mode2027.ts`): OpenTUI 0.6.6 sets it at start, answered or not, and its `destroy()` leaves it set (docs/spikes/05-opentui-soak.md § 4). Before the renderer starts, `open()` asks the terminal with DECRQM, then DA1 so a terminal that doesn't know DECRQM ends the wait, for up to 250 ms; after `destroy()` and `renderer.closed`, on the scope's close or before a fatal error is reported, it writes `CSI ? 2027 h` when the terminal said it was on, else `CSI ? 2027 l`. `test/ui-mode-2027.test.ts` covers the query, the reply and both paths of the renderer's lifecycle.
- The dashboard's title line, totals strip and tickets table (§ 7.2, § 5.1 "How the phases are shown", § 7.11; `src/ui/Dashboard.tsx`, `src/ui/TitleLine.tsx`, `src/ui/Totals.tsx`, `src/ui/TicketsTable.tsx`, `src/ui/line.tsx`), drawn from RunView alone in the theme's styles, as text measured and cut to the pane so no line wraps. The title line: the pill, the version, `· stopping`, `· solo <id> next` or `running`, then faint the branch, the run's time and a scoped run's ticket, and on the right the usage and hold banners (`usage 85% · resets 14:05`, `BLOCKED`, `USAGE_LIMIT · resets 14:05`); a narrow pane cuts the branch and time, then drops them. The totals strip in a rounded `frame`, zeros faint, Set aside with ` ◆ n` triaged, Workers `n of N`, In queue `n of N · N held back · held`, on one line in a pane too narrow for it. The tickets table: a row per ticket in the order the run took them up, its glyph, word and ID in the state's style, a finished ticket's note faint and triage's verdict `◆ …` organ bold in place of a set-aside ticket's note; about a third of the pane high (3 to 10 rows, blank rows filling it, `+N earlier tickets` once full), left out of a pane too short for it and a line a worker. The warning line (` ! …`) shows the colour scheme's notices above the hint; below 60×16 only the totals line and the hint are drawn. The run's time is drawn anew every 3 seconds (`useNow`). RunView's Status gains the version, the run's start, the scope, `stopping`, `solo`, the unclosed count, triage's verdicts and the warnings (RunView's `title`, `warnings`, `Totals.triaged` and `unclosed`, `Row.verdict`); the run passes the version, start, scope and scheme notices. `test/ui-dashboard.test.tsx` covers it.
- The full check (§ 5 item 12, § 2, § 9.1; `src/run/fullcheck.ts`): after a run that ended by itself (READY_EMPTY, LIMIT_REACHED, DRAINED) having merged a ticket, and before the end record, `checks.full.command` runs on the base's head in a detached worktree of its own under the worktrees' folder (`check-full-*`), the setup command first when there is one, each within `checks.full.timeout`; the worktree is removed afterwards and git's worktrees pruned. It is skipped after a stop or a quit, and when nothing merged; a quit while it runs stops it (`FULL_CHECK_SKIPPED`). A failure keeps the whole output in the main checkout's `.tutti/run/check-full.log` and, as orchestra does, files a P2 bug labelled `check-full`, titled `Full check fails: <suite>` (the last `SUITE:` line, else the command), unless one is open for that suite already, which gets a note instead; a setup that fails files none. Each outcome is a `full_check` record and a `FULL_CHECK` line, which dashboard mode prints after the closing line. `Git.addDetachedWorktree`, `Worktrees.addDetached` and `removeDetached` are new, in the test layers too, and a scripted check can be a function of its run. `test/fullcheck.test.ts` covers it.
- The start question, the plan confirmation and the line forms (§ 5 item 3, § 7.1, § 7.11; `src/ui/StartQuestion.tsx`, `src/ui/PlanConfirmation.tsx`, `src/ui/Form.tsx`, `src/ui/questions.ts`, `src/ui/plain/forms.ts`): in the command's renderer, `askStart` asks `What should this run work on?`, a select of `Current tickets: N ready` (or `none, all done`, `none ready (N open)`) or `New feature`, and for a new feature a textarea for its description, where Enter submits and Alt+Enter or Ctrl+J starts a new line; `askPlan` shows the epic and its tickets (type, priority, files, `after:`, the notes) and asks the question with `[y/N]`: y yes, n or Enter no. Esc or Ctrl+C cancels either with `FormCancelled`, and the form is unmounted, leaving the renderer to the screen after it. For TERM=dumb and no TTY, `plain/forms.ts` asks the same, and a generic select, confirm and text for init, one question per line as huh's accessible mode does (numbered options, `[y/N]`, an error and the question again for an answer that doesn't do), reading stdin a line at a time; the end of input (Ctrl+D) cancels with `FormCancelled`. The forms are drawn in the form roles through the theme's new `form` styles (`formStyleSpecs`). Not yet asked by the run. `test/start-question.test.tsx` and `test/line-forms.test.ts` cover them.
- The Claude Code sessions tutti starts (workers, organs, the account probe) and the live contract tests' don't get the variables naming the terminal pane tutti runs in (`src/agent/env.ts`'s `paneVariable`: Herdr's `HERDR_*`, cmux's `CMUX_*`, tmux's `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE`). With them, a session reported itself through the terminal's Claude Code hook as the pane's agent, and Herdr dropped the name of the agent already there, which orchestra finds its workers by.
- `test/render.ts`, the UI tests' render helper (§ 13): `withRender(node, size, body)` renders with `@opentui/react/test-utils`' `testRender` at a fixed size, gives `input` (mockInput, mockMouse or a resize run inside `act()`, then a flush) and `capture` (the characters and spans once the renderer is idle), and disposes inside `act()` even when the body throws; `renderFrame` returns the first frame. A bare `dispose()`, or a key that updates state outside `act()`, makes React warn "not wrapped in act(…)", and the frame then misses the update. `test/render.test.tsx` covers it, a bare `dispose()`'s warning as the control, and pins that `wrapMode="none"` with `truncate` keeps a fixed-height box's lines on their own rows.
- Spec § 6.1, § 6.2: what an organ sees besides its `systemPrompt` (docs/spikes/04-effect-ai.md § 2): Claude Code's identity line in front of it with no separator, so the provider's string starts with a blank line, then an environment section and the account's email address, about 600 input tokens a call, which no SDK option removes. Organ prompts don't rely on any of it, since the API-key provider sends the system prompt alone; the evidence rule needs no change, as none of it was written by a worker, a ticket's author or the repository.
- The OpenTUI shell (§ 7.1, § 7.4, § 3.5, § 7.11; `src/ui/renderer.ts`, `src/ui/useRunView.ts`, `src/ui/App.tsx`, `src/run/screen.ts`): when Tty says the terminal can draw the dashboard, a bare `tutti` opens one renderer in the alternate screen with `exitOnCtrlC: false` and `exitSignals: []`, destroyed once the run's scope closes and before anything is printed: `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection` destroy it first and then report (exit 4), and the Effect runtime's fatal path closes the scope that holds it. Each resize repaints the whole screen (#1110). The appearance is the user's choice, else the terminal's theme mode waited for up to 250 ms, else dark, and follows `theme_mode` events. The run (`RunOptions.dashboard`) projects RunView from the ledger, the workers' states, the open requests and its status, bridges it and the Screen into React through a fiber and `useSyncExternalStore` (`useRunView`, `useScreen`, `useTheme`), and React writes only UiCommands (`useCommand`, `Queue.offerUnsafe`); the App routes a placeholder screen by Screen (dashboard, worker view, interview). In dashboard mode the log goes to its file alone and the run's summary and closing lines (`Outcome.ending`) are printed after the renderer is gone. `test/ui-shell.test.tsx` covers it.
- `tutti login` (§ 4.13, § 8.1, `src/cli/login.ts`): runs Claude Code's own claude.ai login, `<claude> auth login`, with the terminal's stdin, stdout and stderr, in tutti's process group so it can read the terminal; the Claude Code is `--claude`'s (`TUTTI_CLAUDE`), else the embedded one, the SDK's platform package's, resolved as the SDK resolves it. Once it exits 0, the login probe runs in the main checkout (the folder it was started in outside a repository) and tutti prints the account (`account: <email>, <organization>, <plan>`) and the billing line; a login that fails, or a probe that finds problems, says so on stderr with exit status 1. `startup.ts` exports `failures`. `test/login.test.ts` covers the command line for the embedded and the `--claude` Claude Code, and the report.
- `tutti login` runs the Claude Code `resolveClaude` resolves (§ 4.13, § 12; `src/cli/login.ts`), as a run's startup does: `--claude`'s, looked up on PATH or taken from the folder tutti was started in, with its `--version` range warning on stderr; in a compiled build, the embedded one extracted out of `$bunfs`, where the SDK's platform package can't be resolved; from source, the platform package's. Its problems (no such `--claude`, the embedded one not extracted) stop the login before it runs, and the probe after the login runs with the same Claude Code. `test/login.test.ts` covers each.
