- Published JSON Schemas (§ 8.4, § 10; `scripts/gen-schemas.ts`): `bun scripts/gen-schemas.ts` writes `schema/settings.json` from the settings Schema, `schema/events.json` from the event records and `schema/colour-scheme.json` from the colour scheme format, each with Effect's `toJsonSchemaDocument`; it replaces `scripts/colour-scheme-schema.ts`. A settings key with a default gives it (`withDefault` annotates the encoded side), a time gives its pattern, and the record times and issue types their patterns (their regular expressions gain the `u` flag, without which Effect leaves a pattern out). `test/schemas.test.ts` fails while a committed schema differs from what the script writes, and checks the defaults and patterns against the Schemas.
- Claude Code is embedded in a compiled build, and `--claude` runs another (§ 12, § 4.1; `src/agent/claudeBinary.ts`, `src/entries/<target>.ts`): `bun build --compile --target=bun-<target>` starts from `src/entries/<target>.ts`, which imports that platform package's `claude` as a file and hands it to `embed` before calling `main`; the startup checks extract it with the SDK's `extractFromBunfs` (a folder per content hash under `/tmp/claude-<uid>/`, or `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`) and give the path to workers, the probe and the organs as `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable`, while from source the SDK resolves its own. The musl entries first import `src/entries/musl.ts`, which sets `OPENTUI_LIBC=musl`. `--claude <path>` (`TUTTI_CLAUDE`; a bare name is looked up on PATH, a relative path taken from the folder tutti started in) wins over both: tutti runs its `--version` and warns, naming the version, the range and the embedded version, when it is outside the SDK's `claudeCodeVersion` to 30 patch releases above it (2.1.301 to 2.1.331), and runs it all the same; one that doesn't run, or an embedded one that couldn't be extracted, is a setup problem. `test/claude-binary.test.ts` covers the choice from source, compiled (simulated with a `$bunfs` folder) and `--claude`, and the warning.
- The predictor (§ 5 items 5 and 13, Appendix A; `src/organs/predictor.ts`, `src/organs/prompts/predictor.md`, `src/run/predict.ts`): with footprints on, the scheduler queues each ready ticket naming nothing, not solo, once a run, and the predictor organ, orchestra v0.7.7's adapted, guesses in the background from the ticket (bd show) and the repository's files (git ls-files, at most 5,000) the files it will change, keeping at most 8 the repository has. The guess is kept as `predicted_files` metadata, a JSON list, and logged (`<id> footprint predicted: a.ts, b.ts`, or `footprint not predicted: <why>`); from then on it is the ticket's footprint, a running ticket's included, so a ticket touching those files is held back. Picking never waits for it, and two running tickets that both edit a file still give `LIKELY_CONFLICT`. `test/predict.test.ts` covers it.
- `tutti --version` (§ 8.1, `src/cli/version.ts`): package.json's version, and for a build from a clone its commit's 12-character hash, plus `+dirty` when the tree had changes (`tutti v0.1.0 f1aa2f1f67c1+dirty`). A build takes the commit from the `TUTTI_BUILD_COMMIT` define (`bun build --define 'TUTTI_BUILD_COMMIT:"<hash>[+dirty]"'`, `""` for a release, which has the bare version); run from source, tutti reads it at startup with `git rev-parse` and `git status`, only when its checkout is the top of a git repository, and shows the bare version when git can't say. `test/version.test.ts` covers the formats and, with `TUTTI_SLOW`, reading a checkout.
- `tutti plan [--apply]` (§ 8.1, `src/plan/plan.ts`), as orchestra v0.7.7's plan: proposes blocks links between the open tickets (not epics, the excluded types or questions) that touch the same code, read from their footprints, the higher-priority ticket first (then the older one, then the lower ID, `e.2` before `e.10`): tickets naming the same function, or working on the same file of at most 200 lines unless both name different functions it mentions. Areas, predicted files and the files the check command names link nobody, and a pair already ordered, directly or through other tickets, gets no link. It prints `<blocked> (P2) waits for <blocker> (P1): both touch <what>`; `--apply` adds each with `bd dep add` and fails if bd refuses one. `footprint.ts` gains `readSources`, `sharedFunc` and `sharedFile`'s `keep`. `test/plan-command.test.ts` covers it.
- The scout (§ 6.3, § 5 item 13; `src/organs/scout.ts`, `src/organs/prompts/scout.md`): orchestra v0.7.7's scout, adapted, finds a project's checks for `tutti init` with the read-only tools `Read`, `Glob` and `Grep` listed explicitly, in the repository's root, at medium effort, for at most 5 minutes and $2.00 (`maxBudgetUsd`), with no transcript and `permissionPrompts: "none"`. A `PreToolUse` hook denies any call whose path argument (Read's `file_path`, Grep's `path`, Glob's `path` and its pattern's base), resolved from the root with `~` expanded and then through `realpath`, lies outside the repository, symlinks included, and any other tool or input it can't read. Its answer is orchestra's schema (suites with name, kind, command, `found_in`, tier, `parallel_safe`, needs; the stack; a note), tidied as orchestra's; a suite with no name or command fails `OrganFailed`. An organ names such tools in `Organ.reads` (`OrganReads` in `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`), which the provider gives the session in place of no tools and a temp folder. `test/scout.test.ts` covers it.
- The scout (§ 6.3, § 5 item 13; `src/organs/scout.ts`, `src/organs/prompts/scout.md`): orchestra v0.7.7's scout, adapted, finds a project's checks for `tutti init` with the read-only tools `Read`, `Glob` and `Grep` listed explicitly, in the repository's root, at medium effort, for at most 5 minutes and $2.00 (`maxBudgetUsd`), with no transcript and `permissionPrompts: "none"`. A `PreToolUse` hook denies any call whose path argument (Read's `file_path`, Grep's `path`, Glob's `path` and its pattern's base), resolved from the root with `~` expanded and then through `realpath`, lies outside the repository, symlinks included, and any other tool or input it can't read, except `StructuredOutput`, the tool `outputFormat` adds, which the scout answers with. Its answer is orchestra's schema (suites with name, kind, command, `found_in`, tier, `parallel_safe`, needs; the stack; a note), tidied as orchestra's; a suite with no name or command fails `OrganFailed`. An organ names such tools in `Organ.reads` (`OrganReads` in `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`), which the provider gives the session in place of no tools and a temp folder. `test/scout.test.ts` covers it.
- Spec § 12: the npm package's `bin` is a `#!/bin/sh` launcher that runs the bundle with `bun --no-env-file --config=/dev/null`: under bunx (Bun 1.4.3) a bundle run as the `bin` reads the project's `.env` files and runs its `bunfig.toml` preloads, and a shebang can't carry the flags on Linux, where only `env -S` splits them and BusyBox's `env` has none.
- Notifications (§ 7.9, `src/services/Notifier.ts`): `Notifier.notify` shows a notice, sanitised, titled with the repository's folder name. In a local session it is `osascript` on macOS (the notice and title passed as arguments, never put into the AppleScript) or `notify-send` on Linux when it is installed; over ssh, or where neither is there, it is OSC 9 (iTerm2, kitty) or OSC 777 (Ghostty, WezTerm, foot) written to the terminal, passed through tmux. Off with AppConfig's `notify` false. A notice is shown in the background, closing waits up to 10 s for those still showing, and a failure is logged (`NOTIFY_FAILED`), never fatal. `src/testing/Notifier.ts` captures notices; `test/notifier.test.ts` covers it.
- The reviewer's per-ticket tallies are Attention's (§ 4.5, § 6.2): the report reads `Attention.tally` for each ticket, and `talliesLine` (`src/run/report.ts`) says its attention requests by kind, their answers by verdict (allowed, allowed for this run, answered, denied by you, declined, withdrawn), the calls a rule allowed for this run allowed, and the calls Claude Code denied without asking by type (`3 calls Claude Code denied without asking (1 by the classifier, 2 by the safety check)`), beside your messages and its compactions. The ledger's `requests`, `answers` and `denials` tallies, which nothing counted, go; it keeps `messages` and `compactions`. `test/report-tallies.test.ts` covers the line.
- `test/probe-settings-contract.test.ts`, a `TUTTI_LIVE` contract test (§ 13, § 4.13): a probe session on a temp repository whose `.claude/settings.json` doesn't parse and whose `.claude/settings.local.json` is invalid answers `getSettings()` with both files under `errors`, each with `file`, `path` and `message`, and its initialization result carries `current_permission_mode`; both are untyped in `sdk.d.ts`, so an SDK bump that moves one fails it.
- A ticket an earlier run labelled `unmerged` loses the label as it merges in this run, in `fastForward` (`src/run/merge.ts`), before the epics above it are checked, rather than as the run ends (§ 4.8): an epic whose last subticket was carried over closes as it merges. bd failing to remove it is LABEL_FAILED, the epic left open, and `labelAtEnd` tries again. `test/unmerged-at-merge.test.ts` covers both.
- A merge that leaves a ticket with no change of its own to merge closes it as unchanged, as orchestra's `dropUnchanged` does (§ 5.1's new `Merging` → `Done{noChange}`; `src/run/merge.ts`, `src/run/mergequeue.ts`): when git's rebase drops every commit, the base having their changes already, or the branch's commits together change nothing, `prepare` gives `Unchanged` and the queue's protected step, `closeUnchanged`, removes the worktree and branch (a branch with commits of its own moved to the base first, so git deletes it as merged), logs `<id> closed with no change: <why>, so there is nothing to merge; worktree and branch removed`, removes an `unmerged` label and closes the epics above it; before, the base was fast-forwarded to itself and the ticket ended `Done{merged}` naming the base's head. A passing merge check is logged as `'<check>' passes on the rebased <branch>`, as orchestra does. `test/merge-unchanged.test.ts` covers it.
- An interrupt on a CLI without `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` (`--claude`, § 4.8) cancels each message its receipt lists under `still_queued` with `cancel_async_message`, at once, without waiting for the interrupted turn's result, through the local type `QueuedControls` (`queuedControls`) in `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, which also carries `interrupt({ cancelQueued })`. A message it cancels moves to the receipt's `cancelled` and is no longer outstanding (§ 4.2); one refused or answered `cancelled: false` stays under `still_queued` and runs. The scripted AgentHost does the same, records each request and its answer (`Sent.CancelAsync`), and takes `cancelAsync: "refuse" | "notQueued"`; the fake Claude Code answers `cancel_async_message` and takes `cancelAsync` and `interruptLag`. `test/queued-cancel.test.ts` covers both paths, with § 13's `TUTTI_LIVE` contract tests for `interrupt({ cancelQueued: true })` and `cancel_async_message`.
- Each external command exec runs (git, bd, checks, setup) has a reaper, as a worker has (§ 3.3 "Time limits", § 3.2): a kill -9 of tutti mid-command ends the command's whole process group, SIGTERM then SIGKILL after the command's grace, rather than leaving it to run on past its time limit. The reaper moves from `src/agent/options.ts` to `src/services/reaper.ts` (`startReaper`, `stopReaper`, `reap`), and goes once exec's scope has killed the group. `test/exec-reaper.test.ts` kills a host with SIGKILL mid-command.
- A branch rebased before a quit cut its merge check short is checked before it merges (§ 4.8, `src/run/check.ts`, `src/run/merge.ts`): before tutti rebases a finished ticket's branch it marks the ticket in state.json (`rebased_unchecked`, with where the branch left the base and its commit then), and a merge check passing on the branch, or no check command, clears the mark. A branch on top of the base that is still marked, as the quit leaves it for the next run, is set up if need be and checked (`<branch> is on top of <base>, but its check didn't finish after tutti rebased it; checking it now`) rather than fast-forwarded unchecked; a failure there is handed back or set aside as on a rebased branch. `test/unchecked-rebase.test.ts` covers it.
- Denials Claude Code makes without asking (§ 4.5): each `permission_denied` a worker's session sends, through either host (`makeWorkers` in `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`), goes to `Attention.denial`, which logs it (`DENIED: <id>'s worker: Bash(rm -rf $T/*), by the safety check: <reason>`, the call's input from the worker's last 64 tool calls, else its tool alone; the reason the worker was told when the SDK gives none; a denial with no `decision_reason_type` is `unknown`, `by Claude Code`), records a `denied` event and tallies it per ticket by type (`Tally.deniedBy`). The card's latest action (`Transcripts.latestAction`) is `Bash(rm -rf $T/*) denied by the safety check` for 10 seconds. The scripted AgentHost gains `deniedTool`; `test/permission-denied.test.ts` covers a held removal in auto mode, each type, and the live host.
- The START block says why the colour scheme asked for isn't used, and which scheme files were ignored for a built-in's name, a line per notice from `schemeNotices` (§ 7.11), before the run's warnings. `test/start-scheme-notices.test.ts` checks them.
- A `Queued` or `HeldBack` ticket that `bd ready` no longer lists (closed, deferred or blocked outside the run) leaves the run (§ 5.1's new `Queued`, `HeldBack` → out of the run, cause `Unready`): the scheduler's fill drops it from the ledger with a `queue` record and a `phase` record without `to` (§ 10), so In queue no longer counts it, and queues it anew if it is ready again. `test/unready.test.ts` covers it.
- The login probe's SDK options come from `src/agent/options.ts`'s `probeOptions` (§ 4.13): the options a worker's session and the probe's share (settings sources, the main checkout's project settings, permission mode, model, effort, strict MCP config, AskUserQuestion disallowed when the settings say so, the environment, the executable, the spawn into a process group of its own with its reaper) are built once for both, so the two can't drift; the probe adds the main checkout as its folder, no MCP servers and `persistSession: false`. `Account`'s `ProbeOptions` is now the run's configuration and the environment. `test/probe-options.test.ts` checks the probe shares every option a worker's session has, whatever the workers' settings.
- The reviewer and the run report (§ 5 item 13, § 6.2, § 9.1; `src/organs/reviewer.ts`, `src/organs/prompts/reviewer.md`, `src/run/report.ts`): at the end of a run that wasn't quit, the reviewer, orchestra v0.7.7's report organ adapted, is given the run's evidence as orchestra gathers it (the final line, the run's log, the commits merged, the tickets closed with their close reasons, set aside with their notes and still in progress, the tickets still ready, the organs' cost so far) with each ticket's tallies of attention requests, answers, denials and your messages, whether you steered it, and the requests and denials as logged, and its Needs you recommends rules for calls that kept being asked about or denied. The report, titled with the run's date, times, base and scope (escaped for Markdown) and ending with the organs' cost total, is written to `.tutti/reports/<start time>.md` (`REPORT: written to …`) and printed under an organ line. `--review=false`, quitting and Ctrl+C skip it; while the usage limit holds it is skipped (`ORGAN reviewer: skipped, …`); an empty report is `REVIEW_FAILED`.
- Asked tickets (§ 5.1, § 4.8, `src/run/asked.ts`): a ticket whose worker filed a `human` question ticket blocking it, then settled, is `Asked` (`asked`, `ASKED`), its session closed and its slot freed; `takeAnswers`, for the ready poll, reads each `Asked` ticket and once no open question blocks it moves it `Asked` → `Working` (an `answered` record and line), resumes its session by its saved ID with a `client_composed` "your question is answered" message (`RESUMED`, a `resumed` record with `why: answered`), and carries its fiber on in a slot through `carry`, counting toward no limit, until its worker settles again. A session that can't be resumed leaves the ticket for review with `WORKER_GONE`.
- Asked tickets (§ 5.1, § 4.8, `src/run/asked.ts`): a ticket whose worker filed a `human` question ticket blocking it, then settled, is `Asked` (`asked`, `ASKED`), its session closed and its slot freed; `takeAnswers`, for the ready poll, reads each `Asked` ticket and once no open question blocks it moves it `Asked` → `Working` (an `answered` record, `ANSWERED: <question> (<title>) is answered, so <id> comes back`), resumes its session by its saved ID with a `client_composed` "your question is answered" message (`RESUMED`, a `resumed` record with `why: answered`), and carries its fiber on in a slot through `carry`, counting toward no limit, until its worker settles again. A session that can't be resumed leaves the ticket for review with `WORKER_GONE`.
- Spec § 4.13: the probe reads the settings files skipped for errors from `getSettings()` (and from the initialization result's `errors`, which Claude Code 2.1.301 leaves out) and the permission mode from `current_permission_mode`; both are untyped in `sdk.d.ts`, so § 13 adds a contract test that pins them and § 15.2's risks name an SDK update that moves them.
- exec's `Command` gains `outputFd`, an open descriptor the whole output is written to in place of opening `outputFile`, and Checks' `RunOptions` passes it on (§ 9.1): the merge check and the setup write `check.log` and `setup.log` to the descriptor `Worktrees.openRunFile` opened through the symlink guard, no longer reopened as `/dev/fd/<n>`, and a failed or timed-out check's message names the file in the worktree's `.tutti/run/`. `test/check-log-guard.test.ts` covers it.
- The `Organs` service (§ 3.3, § 6, `src/organs/Organs.ts`): an organ is a name, a prompt, an answer Schema, an effort and a time limit as orchestra sets them (`low` for triage, the predictor and the screen, `medium` for the reviewer and the scout, `high` for the plan; 2 min for the predictor and the screen, 3 for triage, 5 for the reviewer and the scout, 10 for the plan), `--organ-model` and `--organ-effort` or the settings overriding every organ's. A call past its limit is interrupted, aborting its session, and fails `OrganTimedOut`; a refusal is `OrganRefused`, any other failure `OrganFailed{subtype}`. While `OrganHold` (the usage-limit hold's, never held by default) holds, a call is skipped with `ORGAN triage: skipped, the usage limit holds the run` and fails `OrganSkipped`. `src/organs/evidence.ts` tags each section of an organ's input between `<evidence id="…">` and `</evidence id="…">` with 16 hex digits drawn afresh per call, escapes any evidence tag inside the text, and ends every system prompt with the evidence rule (`src/organs/prompts/evidence.md`). `src/testing/Organs.ts` is a `LanguageModel` with canned answers per organ.
- Session lifetime (§ 4.8, `src/run/sessions.ts`): `lifetime` is § 4.8's table, and `fit` closes a ticket's session once its phase no longer needs it, as its worker's outcome is read and as its merge or hand-back is over: kept open while it runs, waits to merge, merges or is handed back, and until the run ends when set aside after a failed check that may be checked again; closed once merged, closed with no change, asked, waiting for a merge, deferred, left for review or blocked; a ticket that stopped the run is left for the run's end to suspend. A worker whose session went (`Gone`) with its ticket in progress is resumed once per ticket per run and told to carry on (`RESUMED`, a `resumed` record with `why: gone`, a note on the ticket); gone again, the run holds with `PAUSED`. A resume that fails (no saved session, the worktree or transcript gone, a spawn that fails, a `startup_failure_reason`) leaves the ticket for review with `WORKER_GONE`, noted and dropped. `resume` resumes a closed session any number of times, recording each.
- The scripted AgentHost and the fake Claude Code take sends as the CLI does (§ 4.1): the scripted host's `fold` step folds every send waiting into the running turn, and `coalesce` (and `coalescedTurn`) has queued sends of one kind share a turn, the other kind waiting for a later one; each result lists every send its turn took, with a `command_lifecycle` per send, and a turn the CLI starts by itself lists none. The fake's `wait` step folds the queued messages in, its queued turns coalesce only messages of one kind, and its `background` step's task is reported after `idle` in a turn of its own whose result lists no message. `test/folded-sends.test.ts` shows a worker stays `Working` until each of tutti's sends is listed, whichever way it ran.
- `src/organs/AgentSdkLanguageModel.ts`, the organs' effect/ai `LanguageModel` over the Agent SDK (§ 6.1), one isolated session per call (`tools: []`, `strictMcpConfig`, `settingSources: []`, `permissionPrompts: "none"`, `persistSession: false`, a fresh temp folder removed afterwards, a worker's environment), the call's organ, model and effort from `OrganCall`: system messages become the `systemPrompt` string and the one user message the session's; a JSON answer's schema goes through `AnthropicStructuredOutput` to `outputFormat` and its `structured_output` back as the text `generateObject` decodes; retries exhausted and a success without structured output fail with `StructuredOutputError`, a refusal message or `stop_reason: "refusal"` with `ContentPolicyError` even on success (`subtypeOf` gives the result's subtype); interruption aborts the session. `src/organs/OrganCost.ts` logs each call's last result as `ORGAN triage: $0.0123 in 1 turn, 4.2s` and totals the run. The fake Claude Code gains `message` and `result` steps, logs its PID, folder and chosen variables, and with `eofAfterTurn` reads stdin's end only once its turn is over, as Claude Code does.
- A background task that ends after `idle` keeps its worker `Working` until the turn the CLI starts to report it has gone `idle` (§ 4.2): the emptied set comes about 70 ms before that turn's `running`, so `WorkerState` keeps a `report` to come from then until the next turn, and a Monitor watch or other live task that isn't ambient keeps the worker `Working` however long it lives. If no turn starts within 30 s, `AgentHost` folds in `WorkerEvent.ReportLate`, stops waiting, and the log says `<id>: a background task ended and no turn came to report it within 30 seconds; not waiting for it`. The scripted AgentHost gains `taskEnds`, `selfStartedTurn`, `backgroundCommand` (with `reported: false` for a report that never comes) and `monitorWatch`; `test/sdk-background-task.test.ts` is the `TUTTI_LIVE` contract test for it.
- The printer after the dashboard (§ 7.1, § 7.11 "The summary", `src/print/`): `summary.ts` draws the run's summary in plain ANSI from RunView's totals and rows (the title with the pill, the totals strip, or its one-line form when narrow, and every ticket in its state's style, a finished row faint) and the closing line (`♪ Completed the Run` and the other ends `done` bold with their facts faint, a stop's `■ PAUSED: …` `needsYou` bold) with a scoped run's scope line (`SCOPE_DONE` `done`, `SCOPE_OPEN` `notice`); `organLine.ts` is the printer with organ lines, a spinner redrawn on one line ten times a second until the step's final line, `◆ ` `organ` bold, its text `organText` and its duration faint; `markdown.ts` renders the report with marked's lexer (marked 17.0.1, OpenTUI's own) in the theme's heading, code and link roles: headings, lists, emphasis, code, links with their URL faint, quotes faint italic behind `│ `, rules at most 40 long, and tables. All their text is sanitised, character references in the report included.
- The setup before the merge check (§ 5 item 7, `src/run/setup.ts`): when a ticket's rebase brings in a change to a file `checks.setup.files` names (globs, by name or by path from the top), relative to the base its worktree was last set up for, `checks.setup.command` runs in its worktree before the check, `Merging{setup}` (`running the setup` on its card), its whole output in `.tutti/run/setup.log`, and the log says `<id>: bun.lock changed in the rebase; running '<setup>' before the check`. A setup that fails or times out sets the ticket aside as a failing check does (`CHECKS_FAILED: … the setup '<setup>', run before the check, fails …`, `setup failed`), and the recheck sets it up again first.
- A worker is `Idle` or `Failed` only after `session_state_changed: idle` follows its last result, with none of tutti's sends outstanding and no live background task that isn't ambient (§ 4.2): `WorkerState` no longer reads `queued_turn_count`, which leaves out queued `client_composed` sends. `AgentHost` folds each send's uuid in as it pushes it (`WorkerEvent.Sent`) and an interrupt's cancelled ones (`WorkerEvent.Cancelled`); a send stops being outstanding once its `command_lifecycle` is completed or cancelled (decoded from the raw message), a result lists it in `user_message_uuids`, or a receipt cancels it. The scripted AgentHost emits each send's `command_lifecycle`, has results list the sends taken since the last one, holds back `idle` while a send waits untaken, and takes `cancelQueued`; the fake Claude Code does the same and says `idle` only after the last queued turn.
- A finished ticket's rebase that stops on conflicts is handed back to its worker (§ 4.7, § 5 item 7, `src/run/handback.ts`) when hand-backs are on, a check command is set, it has attempts left (2 a run) and its worker is idle or failed and not steered, or its session was closed by tutti, which is resumed for it: the rebase is left in progress, the ticket moves `Merging` → `Resolving{attempt}` with the log's `RESOLVING`, orchestra's instructions are sent `client_composed`, and the merge queue goes on meanwhile. Once the send is no longer outstanding and the worker has settled, within `merge.resolve_timeout`, tutti verifies what it left as orchestra does (the rebase finished, the worktree clean and on its branch, only the ticket's own commits on top of the base) and runs the check: `ToMerge` when it passes; else `Blocked` (`MERGE_CONFLICT`), the branch put back as the ticket closed it and its notes saying why. A conflict not handed back says why in its `MERGE_CONFLICT` line; for a steered worker the hand-back waits in the queue's `handBacks`. `Git.resetBranch` (`git reset --hard`) is new.
- A check that fails on a finished ticket's rebased branch, after a clean rebase or a resolution, is handed back to its worker to fix (§ 4.7, § 5 item 7, `src/run/checkback.ts`) when it can take it: `merge.check_hand_backs` (2) on and attempts left of it, the check finished, the run not winding down, and its worker idle or failed and not steered, or its session resumed. The ticket moves `Merging` (or `Resolving`) → `Fixing{attempt, afterResolution}` with the log's `FIXING`, orchestra's instructions (what failed, the lines that say so, what landed on the base) are sent `client_composed`, and the merge queue goes on meanwhile. tutti verifies the fix (new commits naming the ticket on top of where the check failed; the worktree clean and on its branch), sets it up if need be and checks it again: `ToMerge` when it passes, `Fixing` again with attempts left; else, as when a fix is rejected (the branch reset to where the check failed), `Review` with `CHECKS_FAILED: … after it was handed back to its worker to fix twice (see its notes)`, or `Blocked` (`MERGE_CONFLICT`) after a resolution, the branch put back as the ticket closed it. Each pass through `Merging` counts toward the attempts; a failure not handed back says why in its `CHECKS_FAILED` line.
- A ticket set aside as its check failed (`CHECKS_FAILED`) is checked once more after the base moves on (§ 5 item 8, `src/run/recheck.ts`): the merge queue keeps it, and after each merge puts it back in line, `Review` → `ToMerge` with the log's `RECHECK`, unless its check failed in a folder its own commits change, it has been checked again already in this run, or something touched it since (reopened, its worktree changed, its branch moved, its worker steered), which the log says. The `CHECKS_FAILED` line says which (`checked once more if main moves on in this run`, `not checked again in this run, as it failed in …`), and a second failure `fails again`. A ticket left for review (`src/run/aside.ts`'s `leaveForReview`) now has the log's line in its notes beside its `warn` record with `aside`.
- A bare `tutti` runs (§ 5, M1): `src/main.ts` runs the startup checks (exit 2), takes the run lock, builds the live layers on a `ManagedRuntime`, writes the `start` record, ends a run with nothing to run, and runs `src/run/run.ts`, every run in plain mode (log lines on stdout, with a line saying why when the terminal couldn't show a dashboard) until the dashboard exists. The run fills the free slots as it starts, as a ticket's fiber ends, as a merge finishes and on the 30 s ready poll; serves the attention files for `tutti answer`; holds on a stop (no new tickets, the running ones finish and merge) and ends with the stops' line and orchestra's exit code (`src/run/holds.ts`: 3 for PAUSED, 4 for Beads, git and the Agent SDK's `AGENT_FAILED`, 5, 6, 7, 8 for `USAGE_LIMIT`); winds down on SIGUSR1 (`src/run/drain.ts`, `DRAINED`, exit 0); and ends, once nothing is left to start or merge, with the tickets left Stopped and their sessions suspended, the summary lines, the closing line with a scoped run's `SCOPE_DONE` or `SCOPE_OPEN`, and the `end` record.
- `test/contract/sdk.test.ts` checks the Agent SDK's behaviour that tutti relies on, against the real SDK through AgentHost's live layer, when `TUTTI_LIVE=1` (§ 13): `system/init` and `running` on every turn, `idle` only after the last queued turn, each send named in exactly one result's `user_message_uuids` and tracked by `command_lifecycle`, `canUseTool` for a call that needs asking, the held removal (`defaultToNo` in default mode, a `safetyCheck` `permission_denied` without asking in auto mode), `interrupt()` and its receipt cancelling the queued sends, resume with the session's ID, and `strictMcpConfig` against the init's `mcp_servers`. `test/fixtures/usage-limit/` holds a usage-limited turn, edited from recorded messages, which `test/usage-limit-fixtures.test.ts` folds through WorkerState.
- `test/contract/sdk.test.ts` covers the rest of § 13's list (`TUTTI_LIVE=1`), through AgentHost's live layer: `idle` with a background task live, and the turn the CLI starts by itself once the set empties, its result listing no send; follow-ups pushed during a tool call folded into the running turn, named in its one result; queued `client_composed` sends sharing a later turn; `cancel_async_message` on a send queued behind a running turn (`cancelled: true`, never run); the resume of a removed worktree, whose spawn fails (`failed to launch`) with no message and no result, and AgentHost's folder check before it; a session quit as § 4.8 does, busy and at a pending permission request, resumed with a carry-on message, making the cut-off call again in one turn, the request coming back with a new `toolUseID`; the verifier in the foreground through its hook although the model asked for the background; and the account probe's stderr line on a never-trusted checkout whose settings allow one command. What AgentHost doesn't expose goes through `query()` directly with `workerOptions`.
- `src/run/nothing.ts` says why a run has nothing to run (§ 5 item 4): `check` finds `✓ All done` (with `Still open: <ids> (bd close <ids>)` for epics) or `○ Nothing ready to run` with each reason and its command, as orchestra counts them, or `○ Nothing under <id> is ready to run` with each ticket of the scope not done; `box` draws it in a rounded box through a small `Styles` parameter (`themeStyles` gives the theme's `done`, `frame`, `live` and `emphasis` roles, `noStyles` none), `plainLines` as plain lines; `nothingToRun` records the `READY_EMPTY` done line and prints it, so the run ends with exit 0 and no dashboard.
- `src/run/deferredFree.ts` lists the deferred tickets whose blockers have all merged (`DEFERRED_FREE`), as orchestra does: in the START block (`say`) and in the nothing-to-run box, each with the command that reopens it and the one that removes its empty worktree, or shows the uncommitted changes that keep it.
- The project scaffold: Bun, Effect and OpenTUI, with `scripts/check.sh` (tsc, Biome, the tests and shellcheck) and `bun src/main.ts` printing the version.
- `test/test-script.test.ts` runs `scripts/test.sh` on flaky and broken fixtures; `docs/spikes/08-bun-test-retry.md` reports why the script reruns failed tests by name rather than reading `--retry`.
- `src/services/exec.ts` runs every external command in a process group of its own, with a time limit that kills the whole group (`CommandTimedOut`), interruption that does too, bounded output with an optional output file, and `CommandFailed { command, exitCode, stderr }`; `TimeLimit.read` (30 s) and `TimeLimit.write` (2 min) are orchestra's presets.
- `src/ui/sanitize.ts` makes untrusted text safe to show (§ 7.10): `sanitize` and `sanitizeLine` drop control characters, whole ESC sequences and bidi controls, and `escapeMarkdown` escapes text for Markdown; `test/sanitize.test.ts` proves it with fast-check.
- `src/project/settings.ts` and `src/project/userConfig.ts` define `.tutti/settings.json` (§ 8.4) and the user config at `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/tutti/config.json` (§ 8.5) with Effect Schema: every key has a default (orchestra's where the spec is silent), times such as `"30m"` decode to Durations, and `readSettings` and `readUserConfig` return the value or `ConfigFileInvalid` with every problem (file, key path, message), an unknown key or a refused `permission_mode` (`bypassPermissions`, `plan`) among them.
- `src/services/Beads.ts` is every `bd` call a run makes (`ready`, `show`, `list`, `update` with `--claim`, `--status` and `--append-notes`, `close`, `defer`, `label add`/`remove`, `dep add`/`list`, `create`, `human list`, metadata get, set and unset), with orchestra's limits (30 s reads, 2 min updates) and `--json` decoded with Schema into `Issue`s; errors are `BeadsFailed`, `BeadsTimedOut` and `BeadsUnreadable`. `src/testing/Beads.ts` is an in-memory backlog with bd's ready, blocking, close-refusal, label and metadata semantics; `test/beads.test.ts` decodes recorded outputs in `test/fixtures/bd/`, fuzzes decoding with fast-check, and (slow) checks the in-memory backlog against a real bd.
- `src/services/Git.ts` is every git command a run makes (worktree add, remove, list and prune; branch create and delete; rebase and `rebase --abort`; merge-base; `merge --ff-only`; log `base..HEAD` with messages; `status --porcelain`; `diff --name-only base...HEAD`; `ls-files`; `rev-parse`, `--git-common-dir` included; the current branch), with orchestra's limits (30 s reads, 2 min writes) and `-z` output; errors are `GitFailed { op, stderr }`, `GitTimedOut` and `RebaseConflict { files }`. A rebase interrupted, timed out or refused is aborted, leaving the branch as it was; one stopped on conflicts stays stopped. `GitWriter` is a `Semaphore(1)` every write takes, re-entrant for the fiber holding it. `test/git.ts` makes real repositories with commits, branches and worktrees for git-subject tests; `test/git-service.test.ts` runs each operation against one (slow).
- `src/testing/Git.ts`, Git's test layer (§ 3.3, § 13): an in-memory repository with branches, worktrees (each with a HEAD, an index and a working tree), commits holding whole trees, rebase that replays a file at a time and stops on files both sides changed (left in progress, for `Repository.continueRebase` or `rebase --abort`), fast-forward that refuses a branch that isn't a descendant, status, diff names and ls-files; writes take the GitWriter it provides. Its `Repository` commits and writes as a worker would, detaches HEAD, deletes a worktree's folder, forces a conflict on a branch's next rebase, makes the next call of any operation fail or time out, and records every call, so scenarios reach `OFF_BRANCH`, `DIRTY_WORKTREE`, `REBASE_FAILED`, `MERGE_CONFLICT` and `MERGE_FAILED` without git. `test/git-memory.test.ts` runs one set of expectations against it and (slow) against real git.
- `spikes/opentui-soak/` soaks an OpenTUI React app (16 cards at 10 Hz over a transcript of 200, 600 or unbounded items) for 30 minutes and checks the renderer options and test-utils; `docs/spikes/05-opentui-soak.md` reports that the bounds hold OpenTUI's native memory but React's development build grows without bound under Bun.
- `spikes/compile/` compiles a small program with `bun build --compile` for six targets, each embedding its platform's Claude Code (the SDK's `extractFromBunfs` recipe) and OpenTUI's native library; `docs/spikes/07-compile.md` reports the sizes (337–379 MB), extraction, cross-compiling, `tutti login`'s command (`claude auth login`) and the range for `--claude`.
- tutti runs React's production build (§ 3.1): `bunfig.toml` defines `process.env.NODE_ENV` as `"production"` for every `bun` run and build from the checkout, so React 19.2's development build no longer records a `performance.measure()` per render that Bun keeps forever; release builds and the npm bundle pass the same `--define` (§ 12). `scripts/test.sh` defines `"test"` over it, so the suites keep the development build `@opentui/react/test-utils` needs; `test/react-build.test.ts` checks the reconciler each way loads.
- `src/cli/AppConfig.ts`, the AppConfig service (§ 3.3): the run flags of § 8.2 (`src/cli/flags.ts`, parsed with effect/cli), each with its `TUTTI_*` variable, resolved over the user config, the settings and the defaults into one value, the colour scheme's name in § 7.11's order; every problem with a flag, a variable or a config file comes back together in one `SetupProblem`. On-or-off values take 1, true, yes or on and 0, false, no or off; orchestra's unprefixed variables are not read. `src/testing/AppConfig.ts` gives a literal value.
- `src/native/libc.ts` binds libc through `bun:ffi` for darwin and Linux (§ 9.1, § 9.2): `openCloexec`, `flockExclusiveNonBlocking` and `unlock`, `createExclusive` (O_EXCL, mode 0600), and `openUnder`, which walks a path below a directory with `openat` and `O_NOFOLLOW` and refuses a symlink at any component (`SymlinkRefused`, naming it) or a path that isn't plain (`PathRefused`); other failures are `LibcError` with errno's name. Flags come from `node:fs` (they differ between Linux x86_64 and arm64), `O_CLOEXEC` from a table per OS, and darwin arm64's variadic mode is padded onto the stack.
- `src/services/Store.ts`, the Store service (§ 3.3, § 4.8): `.tutti/run/state.json` in the main checkout, defined with Effect Schema (its version, the run that wrote it, and per ticket the session ID, worktree, branch, why it was suspended, its phase on leaving, its resume count and the run's metadata). Every write goes through one fiber and is atomic (a temporary file in the same folder, fsynced, then renamed), and returns once on disk, so a session ID saved before its session starts survives a crash; an unreadable file is `StoreUnreadable` naming it, a failed write `StoreWriteFailed`. `src/testing/Store.ts` keeps the state in memory.
- `src/run/log.ts`, the Log service (§ 3.3): `.tutti/tutti.log` lines as orchestra writes them, `2026-10-17 09:05:03 <text>`, run-level lines at the margin and a ticket's indented two spaces, naming tickets by ID (§ 5). Text reaches the log only as a `Line`, from the `line` template or a builder, every argument through `sanitizeLine`; builders word the START block, HOLD, RESOLVING, FIXING, PERMISSION and QUESTION (with their `tutti answer` command), answers, ALLOWED, DENIED, STEERED, RELEASED, MESSAGE, USAGE, USAGE_LIMIT and PHASE_BUG. `sink: "stdout"` prints each line for plain mode (§ 7.6). `src/testing/Log.ts` captures lines; `test/log.test.ts` covers the format, sanitising (with fast-check) and the sink.
- `src/run/events.ts` defines every event stream record with Effect Schema (§ 10): `v` (1), `time`, `run` and `kind`, orchestra's twenty kinds with v0.7.7's fields, and tutti's `phase`, `attention`, `attention_answered`, `denied`, `usage_limit`, `message`, `steered`, `released`, `suspended` and `resumed`; `start` has `billing` and `permission_mode` and no `terminal`, `hold` has `lifted`, and `stop`'s word `AGENT_FAILED` replaces `TERMINAL_FAILED`. Untrusted text is kept as it came, JSON-encoded, with DEL, C1 controls, line separators and bidi controls escaped too. `src/run/eventstream.ts`, the EventStream service, appends each record to `.tutti/run/events.jsonl` as one line in a single write, opened with `openUnder` so a symlink in its path is refused, keeping every run; a stream that can't be opened or written warns once and the run goes on. `src/testing/EventStream.ts` captures records; `test/eventstream.test.ts` round-trips every kind, with examples and schema-derived samples.
- `src/services/Checks.ts`, the Checks service (§ 3.3): runs `checks.setup`, `checks.fast` and `checks.full` with `sh -c` in a given folder through exec, each within its own limit from AppConfig (the setup within the limit of the check it comes before), the whole output in the file the caller names (`check.log` or `setup.log`). In memory it keeps only what orchestra says of the output, bounded: the lines that say what failed (orchestra's patterns, each once, at most 30), the last lines, the `FLAKY:` lines and the `SUITE:` names. Results are `CheckPassed` (or `NoCheckCommand`), `CheckFailed`, `CheckTimedOut` and `CheckNotRun`; a time limit or interruption kills the check's process group. exec's `Command` gains `onOutput`, which sees each chunk as it comes. `src/testing/Checks.ts` plays scripted results and records the runs.
- `src/services/RunLock.ts`, the RunLock service (§ 9.2, § 11): `acquire` takes `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on `<git-common-dir>/tutti.lock` and on orchestra's `orchestra.lock`, both opened close-on-exec, for the caller's scope, writing the run's JSON (pid, start time, version, branch, scope or feature, `"init": true`) to the first and orchestra's shape (`version` as `tutti <version>`) to the second. Another run holding either is refused with `AlreadyRunning { program, repo, holder }`: `tutti is already running in <repo> (pid 44497, since 08:31, main)` or `orchestra is running in <repo> (pid …)`. `src/testing/RunLock.ts` keeps the locks in memory; `test/run-lock.test.ts` refuses a second process and an orchestra, and shows both locks released on scope close, on `kill -9`, and despite a child the holder started.
- `src/services/Worktrees.ts`, the Worktrees service (§ 3.3, § 9.1, § 11): a ticket's worktree at `<worktrees>/<id>` on `tutti/<id>`, made from the base, or reused (its worktree, or its branch alone) with what the earlier attempt left reported (commits on `<base>..HEAD` and `git status`); `/.tutti/run/` added to the repository's `info/exclude` once, atomically, keeping the other lines; files under a worktree's `.tutti/run/` opened only through `openUnder`, a symbolic link in place of `.tutti`, `.tutti/run` or a run file refused with `RunFilesOutside`; removal of the worktree and then its branch. Ticket IDs that could escape a path or can't name a git branch, or hold control or invisible characters, are `BadTicketId` (orchestra's rules, proven with fast-check). `src/testing/Worktrees.ts` makes plain temp folders; `test/worktrees.test.ts` runs the live layer against real git (slow).
- `src/project/templates/worker-prompt.md` is tutti's built-in worker prompt (§ 4.11): orchestra v0.7.7's rules, with tutti as the orchestrator, branches `tutti/<id>`, a decision that can wait as a `human` question ticket and a quick choice as `AskUserQuestion`, checks in the foreground because a background wait holds the worker's place and its merge, and a message from the maintainer followed before carrying on; the time limit and the `--ticket` line are left to the first message. `src/project/prompt.ts` loads the prompt from `--prompt`, else `.tutti/worker-prompt.md`, else the built-in, drops the paragraphs naming `AskUserQuestion` when `workers.ask_user_question` is off, and replaces `TICKET_ID`; a missing or empty `--prompt` file, an empty project file and a prompt without `TICKET_ID` are setup problems.
- `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`, a worker's state (§ 4.2) as a tagged union (`Starting`, `Working{doing}`, `Retrying{attempt, max, status}`, `NeedsYou{requests}`, `UsageLimited{resetsAt}`, `Idle{at}`, `Closed`, `Gone{why}`) and `step`, a pure reducer folding SDK messages, hook callbacks, the count of open attention requests and the session's end into it. `Idle` needs the turn's clean result with nothing queued and no live background task but ambient ones; an `is_error` result on status 429 or a rejected `rate_limit_event` is `UsageLimited`, any other is a failed turn (not settled, its error kept); `api_retry` holds until the turn's next message; `doing` is classified as orchestra classifies tool calls. `settled` is true for `Idle`, `Closed` and `Gone` only. `test/worker-state.test.ts` has a table per rule and fast-check properties.
- `src/agent/Attention.ts`, the Attention service (§ 4.5): `request(ticket)` gives a session's `canUseTool` and `elicitation(ticket)` its `onElicitation`; each call becomes an `AttentionRequest` (tutti's ID, the ticket, `toolUseID`, `agentID`, `Permission`, `Question` or `Elicitation`, the sanitised display fields, `defaultToNo`, `suppressAlwaysAllowRule`, its creation time) queued per worker, oldest first, awaiting an answer. Answers give § 4.5's SDK results exactly; Allow for this run passes only the `addRules` suggestions, their destination rewritten to `session`, and keeps their rules so a later request from any worker suggesting only kept rules is allowed without asking (`ALLOWED … by a rule you allowed for this run`). An answer to a request no longer open fails `NotOpen`, one that doesn't fit it `DoesNotFit`; a request whose signal aborts is withdrawn. Requests and answers are PERMISSION and QUESTION log lines and `attention` and `attention_answered` events, tallied per ticket; `open` and `openFor(ticket)` stream the open requests, and `overdue` gives those open past the 4-minute `BLOCKED` limit. `src/testing/Attention.ts` adds a policy that answers as requests open (`allowAll`, `denyAll`); `test/attention.test.ts` covers each answer's result, queues, run rules, stale and withdrawn requests and elicitations.
- Spec § 4.1, § 4.13, § 8.3: project allow rules through `projectConfigRoot` need a trusted main checkout. The probe reads its stderr for Claude Code's line saying it ignores `permissions.allow` because the workspace hasn't been trusted, and reports a setup problem saying how to trust it; startup never writes `.claude.json`, and `tutti init` offers to set `hasTrustDialogAccepted` only after asking. A contract test pins the line.
- Spec § 4.5, § 7.11: `canUseTool` never gives an SDK host a `title` (docs/spikes/01-sdk-session.md, item 2), so a permission's card line is `displayName` and `description` (`Bash: Create empty file made-by-bash.txt`), the input's summary standing in for a missing `description`, and `title` only if one comes; the input's summary follows faint when the line doesn't hold it, since a Bash `description` is the model's own words. The display fields list what arrives.
- Spec § 4.5: Allow for this run passes and keeps only the `addRules` suggestions that allow, dropping `setMode` and `addDirectories` (passed back, `setMode acceptEdits` switched the session to `acceptEdits`), and a request with no such suggestion has no Allow for this run; `test/attention-run-rules.test.ts` covers it.
- `src/agent/attentionFiles.ts` answers attention requests without a dashboard (§ 4.5, D20): each open request is `.tutti/run/attention/<request-id>.json` in the main checkout (the ticket, the kind, the sanitised display fields, the options, the two flags and the creation time), created exclusively with mode 0600 in a 0700 folder and removed once answered or withdrawn. The run watches the folder (`fs.watch` plus a 2-second poll) for `<request-id>.answer.json` and takes one only if it isn't a symbolic link, belongs to the run's user, answers a request still open, and fits its kind and options (allowing a `defaultToNo` request needs `confirm`); a taken answer resolves the request as one given on screen, logged and recorded `via` `answer_command`, and a refused one is an `ANSWER_REFUSED` line and `<request-id>.refused.json`. `tutti answer` (`src/answer/answer.ts`, registered in the new subcommand table `src/cli/commands.ts`, which `src/main.ts` now runs) lists the open requests (`--json` for scripts) or answers one with `allow`, `allow-run` or `deny`, `--reason`, `--choice` (once per question) and `--confirm`, checking the answer first, then waits up to 10 seconds for the run to take it and says whether it did. `test/attention-files.test.ts` covers the files, each refusal, parsing with fast-check, and the command against a running Attention service.
- `src/agent/Transcripts.ts`, the Transcripts service (§ 3.3, § 4.4): every SDK message of a worker's session but the partial `stream_event`s is appended to `.tutti/run/workers/<id>/messages.jsonl` in the main checkout as one line (`v`, tutti's sequence number, the session ID, the time and the SDK message), numbering on across resumes and runs, and its stderr to `stderr.log` beside it, both opened with `openUnder`; a torn last line is skipped, and the next item starts a line of its own. In memory each worker keeps its last 200 items, the latest action for its card (the current tool call as `Bash(scripts/check-fast.sh)`, else the last line of its text), and, only while a view is open, the live message partial messages fold into; `view` gives a scoped view its items and every update after them. `page` reads the transcript back 200 items at a time before a sequence number, and `tail` the last N items within M characters for triage. `src/testing/Transcripts.ts` keeps the files in memory; `test/transcripts.test.ts` covers both, with fast-check for the lines and the latest action.
- The login probe reports an untrusted main checkout (§ 4.13): it starts Claude Code with the workers' spawn (its own process group, a reaper), reads every stderr line until the process has exited, and a line with `permissions.allow` and `not been trusted` is `UntrustedCheckout`, naming the settings file and the main checkout and saying how to trust it, beside the probe's other problems. `test/untrusted-checkout.test.ts` covers the line matcher (with fast-check) and the probe, and, with `TUTTI_LIVE=1`, pins Claude Code's line in a never-trusted repository whose settings allow one command.
- `src/agent/env.ts`'s `workerEnv` is a worker's environment (§ 4.1, § 4.13): tutti's own, with `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_STARTUP_FAILURE_RESULTS=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=0` and `CLAUDE_AGENT_SDK_CLIENT_APP=tutti/<version>`, and without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` beside a subscription login unless billing is `api`. `src/agent/Account.ts`, the Account service (§ 3.3, § 4.13), probes the login with one session that is sent nothing, run without the key: the account, how workers are billed and the `START` block's `billing:` line, the permission mode in effect and its `permission mode:` line; no login is `NotLoggedIn` and settings files Claude Code skipped (read from the session's `getSettings()`, as Claude Code 2.1.301 leaves them out of `initializationResult()`) are `SettingsUnreadable`, both at once when both. `src/testing/Account.ts` gives a literal account.
- `src/agent/mcp.ts`, MCP servers (§ 4.6): `resolveServers` resolves each name in `workers.mcp_servers` on this machine in Claude Code's order, local (the user's `.claude.json` under the repository's project entry, matched through symbolic links), project (`.mcp.json`), then user, with `.claude.json` in `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set; `mcpServers` gives the definitions for the SDK's `mcpServers`, in memory only. A name defined nowhere, a claude.ai connector, a definition that isn't stdio, sse or http and a config file that can't be read are setup problems, all at once; no names gives no servers and a START note saying so (§ 15, Q5). `checkInit` checks a session's `system/init`: its `mcp_servers` must be exactly the chosen names, each with source `dynamic`, `sdk` or its scope (statuses aren't compared), else `workers got MCP servers they weren't given: <names>`. `test/mcp.test.ts` uses the config folders in `test/fixtures/mcp/`.
- `src/agent/options.ts`'s `workerOptions` builds a worker session's SDK options (§ 4.1) as a pure function: the worktree as `cwd`, the main checkout as `projectConfigRoot`, `settingSources` user, project and local, the permission mode always passed, the model and effort only when set, the `claude_code` preset with the standing rules appended and `snapshot: true`, `sessionId` for a new session or `resume` for a resumed one, the chosen MCP servers with `strictMcpConfig`, Attention's and Transcripts' callbacks, the hooks, subagent text and partial messages, `workerEnv()`'s environment, `--claude`'s executable when given, and `AskUserQuestion` disallowed when `workers.ask_user_question` is off. `spawnInOwnProcessGroup` starts Claude Code detached, leading a process group of its own, and passes its stderr to the transcript, which the SDK doesn't read from a custom spawn. `src/agent/hooks.ts`'s `activityHooks(ticket, recorder)` gives in-process `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop` and `PreCompact` callbacks (§ 4.2) that return `{}` at once, record activity and compactions in a fiber of their own, and log and swallow any failure. `test/worker-options.test.ts` covers both.
- `src/agent/AgentHost.ts`, the AgentHost service (§ 3.3): `start` (with a session ID the caller draws), `resume` (with the message that carries it on), `send` (`client_composed`, or `origin: { kind: "human" }`), `interrupt` (its receipt), `close` (on purpose, the ID kept) and `end` (the worker forgotten, its streams ended); per worker a `WorkerState` stream from a `SubscriptionRef` and its sessions' raw messages; errors `AgentStartFailed { reason, startupFailureReason }`, `AgentGone` and `AgentProtocol`. `makeWorkers` is what both layers share: each message recorded in Transcripts, published and folded into the worker's state with its open attention requests. `src/testing/AgentHost.ts` plays scripted sessions per ticket (SDK messages, hooks, `canUseTool` and elicitations answered through Attention, waits for a message or an interrupt, sleeps on the Clock, effects, gone sessions, failed starts) and records everything it is sent; its helpers build the common scripts: claims and closes its ticket with a commit, defers, files a question, asks permission, hits the usage limit. `test/agent-host-scripted.test.ts` runs them under TestClock.
- `src/run/phase.ts`, the ticket state machine (§ 5.1): `TicketPhase`, a tagged union of the fourteen phases (`Queued`, `HeldBack{why}`, `Working{activity}`, `ToMerge{place}`, `Merging{step}`, `Resolving{attempt}`, `Fixing{attempt, afterResolution}`, `Done{outcome}` merged with its commit or with no change, `Asked{question}`, `Waiting{for}`, `Deferred{why}`, `Review{why}`, `Blocked{why}`, `Stopped{why}`) with `TicketPhaseSchema` for the event stream; typed causes; § 5.1's transition table as data (from, cause, to, what is recorded), with the carry-over of "The next run" as entries from outside; `transition(phase, cause)`, the only way to change a phase, giving the next phase or `InvalidTransition` (its `PHASE_BUG` line); `refine` for a phase's details (a Working ticket's activity, a place in the merge queue, a merge's step); and what the UI and totals read: `hasCard`, `countsUnder`, `isFinal` (only `Done`) and `stopsAtEnd`. `test/phase.test.ts` tests every row, rejects every pair the table doesn't list, and drives random causes with fast-check: no unlisted transition is taken, and `Done{merged}` is reached only from `Merging`.
- `src/run/startup.ts`, the startup checks (§ 5 item 1, § 4.13, § 11): `check` collects every problem a run can't start with, in § 5's order (a git repository, its main checkout on a branch, a clean tree outside `.tutti/run/`, `.orchestra/run/` and `.beads/`, bd and a Beads database it can read, the login probe's login and settings files, the settings and flags, the MCP names, the worker prompt), and fails with `StartupFailed`, which `report` puts as one message for exit code 2 (`exitSetup`). A repository `tutti init` hasn't set up says so first, without the worker prompt and Beads database problems init fixes. Workers named in orchestra's `.orchestra/run/state.json` are a warning: `orchestra left workers on <ids>; finish them with orchestra first`. `startBlock` builds the START block from what the checks resolved: version, repository, branch, scope and settings, then the billing, the permission mode in effect, the MCP servers or that there are none, and the memory the workers take (about 1 GiB each). `test/startup.test.ts` covers each problem alone, several together, the not-set-up message first and orchestra's leftover workers.
- `test/harness.ts` runs whole-run scenarios (§ 13): `scenario(setup, run)` composes every test layer (AppConfig, Account, in-memory Beads and Git, scripted AgentHost with Attention answering by policy, scripted Checks, Store, captured Log and EventStream, temp Worktrees, in-memory RunLock) under TestClock, runs the scenario in the run's scope and gives back the log lines, event records, what the workers were sent, the checks run and the backlog, with a backlog builder, scripts that change the in-memory Beads and Git, `advance` for the clock and assertions on the log, events, final phases and exit code. Leaks fail a scenario with `Leaked`: `src/run/fibers.ts`'s `RunFibers` registers every FiberSet the run forks into, and `exec` keeps the process groups it started and hasn't seen killed in `ProcessGroups`; once the run's scope closes, a set still holding fibers or a group still registered is a leak, cleaned up before the scenario fails. `test/harness.test.ts` proves both.
- The scenario harness has the Tty and Notifier test layers: `Setup.tty` gives the terminal (`tty()`, an 80×24 256-colour TTY by default) and `Ran.notices` the notifications shown, sanitised. Spinners take their clock from TestClock, so a scenario's frames move only as `advance` moves it. `test/harness-terminal.test.ts` covers them.
- `src/run/ledger.ts`, the Ledger service (§ 3.4, § 5.1): each ticket the run takes up, with its phase, the time it entered it, its place in the tickets table, its worker's number (the lowest free one, held while its session is open, never shifting) and its tallies (requests, answers, denials, your messages, compactions), and the wind-down's tickets. Every phase change goes through `transition`, which writes the `phase` record and then the orchestra-era records given beside it; an invalid change logs `PHASE_BUG` once, writes nothing and keeps the phase. `src/run/runView.ts`'s `project` derives RunView (totals as § 5.1 counts them, Needs you each ticket once; rows, cards, open requests, banners, the wind-down line, the hint) from the ledger, the workers' states, the open requests and the run's status, sanitising ticket text and keeping the identity of unchanged parts; `make` writes it into a SubscriptionRef at most 30 times a second. `src/run/commands.ts` defines `UiCommand`, the unbounded queue the screen offers into with `offer`, and `resolve`, which resolves a command's worker number, ticket or request against current state or reports it `Stale`. `src/testing/Ledger.ts` gives the ledger over captured logs and events; `test/run-view.test.ts` covers them, the throttle under TestClock.
- `src/agent/AgentHostLive.ts`, AgentHost's live layer (§ 4.1, § 4.2, § 4.4, § 3.2): one Agent SDK `query()` per open worker session in streaming input mode, its inbox an Effect Queue the SDK reads as an `AsyncIterable<SDKUserMessage>`; every message goes to Transcripts and the WorkerState reducer, hook callbacks into the state, stderr to `stderr.log`. Each session's first `system/init` is checked: a permission mode other than the one asked for is logged once and kept for the START block, and an MCP server the worker wasn't given, or an API key billed beside the subscription login, ends the worker as a setup problem; `AgentHost.setup` gives both (the scripted layer takes them as an option). `interrupt` cancels queued messages where the CLI advertises `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1`; `close` waits for Claude Code to exit, killing its process group after 10 s; `resume` passes `resume` without `forkSession`; a stream that ends or fails unasked, or a `startup_failure_reason`, makes the worker Gone; each session lives in a scope inside the layer's. `test/agent-host-live.test.ts` drives it through `test/fixtures/fake-claude/claude.ts`, a fake Claude Code speaking the SDK's stream-json protocol.
- `src/run/footprint.ts`, footprints (§ 5 item 5, § 2): `ticketFootprint` reads a ticket's footprint as orchestra v0.7.7 does, the files and functions its text names (paths, base names and partial paths resolved against `git ls-files`, `Type.method`, calls and camel-case names in backticks, not keywords, builtins, standard packages' functions or a test's methods), its `area:<name>` labels and its `files` metadata with folders (kept as `src/run/`, holding the files another ticket adds there) and globs, else its `predicted_files`; the files the check commands name and the spec its `spec` metadata cites are left out unless `files` lists them. `worktreeChanges` (`git diff --name-only <base>...HEAD` plus `git status`) and `withChanges` grow a running ticket's footprint; `overlaps` and `shared` say whether two footprints share an area, a function, a file or a folder, placing functions in files through `readMentions`. `test/footprint.test.ts` has orchestra's examples and a fast-check property that parsing arbitrary ticket text never throws.
- `src/run/schedule.ts`, the scheduler (§ 5 item 5): it reads `bd ready` within the run's scope, enters each candidate in the ledger as `Queued` (with a `queue` record) and dispatches the highest-priority one to `Working` (with a `dispatch` record and the `[n/limit] <id> dispatching: <title>` line) while fewer than `concurrent` tickets run, handing it to its per-ticket fiber through `Start`; free slots are filled at once and on the ready poll every 30 s, a failed read is tried again up to 3 times, and the ledger's phases keep any ticket from being taken twice. `--limit` counts the tickets the run starts; `carry` gives a resumed or carried-over worker a slot that counts toward no limit; a ticket whose ID can't name a worktree or branch is deferred with `BAD_TICKET_ID` and no worker. `src/run/scope.ts` reads `bd ready` in scope (`--ticket <id>`: that ticket and its descendants) minus questions, `exclude_types` and epics, in priority order, and says where a scope stands at the end (`SCOPE_DONE`, `SCOPE_OPEN` naming each ticket not done and why, or `SCOPE_UNREADABLE`). `test/schedule.test.ts` covers them under TestClock with the in-memory backlog.
- `src/ui/theme.ts`, colours and colour schemes (§ 7.11): the roles as a `Schema.Literals` union; the scheme format with Effect Schema (`name`, `description`, `extends`, `colours` giving a role one colour or `{ light, dark }`, each `#RRGGBB`, `ansi:N` or `default`), published as `schema/colour-scheme.json` (`bun scripts/colour-scheme-schema.ts` writes it); and `loadScheme`, which looks a name up in `.tutti/colour-schemes/`, then `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/tutti/colour-schemes/`, then the built-ins, ignores a file with a built-in's name with a warning, resolves `extends` (a loop refused), and for every reason a scheme can't be used gives `orchestra` with one sentence (`colour scheme 'dusk' isn't used: 'colours.frame': 'ansi:300' is not a palette slot (0–255)`). `src/ui/schemes/orchestra.json` is orchestra's colours exactly, light and dark. `makeTheme` builds orchestra's styles (`picked`, `closed`, `deferred`, `stop`, `windDown`, `heldBack`, `done`, `organ`, `say`, `testing`, `toMerge`, `merging`, `key`, `tickets`, `dim`, `title`) as OpenTUI colours and ANSI strings, truecolor or 256; `NO_COLOR` draws every role in the default colour, keeping bold and faint. `outputAppearance` takes the user config's or `TUTTI_APPEARANCE`'s choice, else the renderer's detection, else asks the terminal for its background (`queryAppearance`, OSC 11 within 250 ms), dark without an answer. AppConfig loads the scheme once at startup (`scheme`) and reads `NO_COLOR` (`noColor`).
- `src/run/start.ts` starts a dispatched ticket (§ 5 item 6, § 4.1, § 4.8): its worktree on `tutti/<id>`, reused when an earlier attempt left one (`reusing worktree …` or `worktree … on …` in the log and an `info` record); its `.tutti/run/` checked with `Worktrees.checkRunFolder`; a new session ID drawn and saved in `state.json` before the session starts through AgentHost; and the first message, sent `client_composed`: the ticket's ID, a pointer to the rules in the system prompt, the spec its metadata names, the time it has (`workers.ticket_limit`), the `--ticket` scope line, and what an earlier attempt left (`git log <base>..HEAD` and `git status`, at most 30 lines each). `BAD_TICKET_ID`, `RUN_FILES_OUTSIDE` and a start that failed (`START_FAILED`, its session ID forgotten, its worktree kept) set the ticket aside, Working → Deferred, through `src/run/aside.ts`'s `putAside` (notes, `bd defer`, the `deferred` record; `DEFER_FAILED` and a `warn` with `aside` when bd can't). `test/start.test.ts` covers each, with fast-check for the message's listing.
- Each worker's Claude Code has a reaper (§ 3.2): `spawnInOwnProcessGroup` also starts a `sh` in a process group of its own, its stdin a pipe from tutti, which sends SIGTERM to Claude Code's group (SIGKILL 10 s later) once tutti ends, `kill -9` included, so a busy worker no longer runs on until its turn is over. A worker that ends normally takes its reaper with it. `test/worker-reaper.test.ts` kills a host with SIGKILL mid-"turn".
- The merge queue (§ 3.4, § 5 item 7): `src/run/mergequeue.ts` is a `Queue<FinishedTicket>` with one consumer, so tickets merge one at a time in the order they finished, each `ToMerge{place}` with its place kept current and `Merging{step}` once its turn comes. `src/run/merge.ts` sets aside a worktree off its branch (`OFF_BRANCH`) and, when the base moved on, one with uncommitted changes (`DIRTY_WORKTREE`), rebases the branch (`REBASE_FAILED` → Review, `MERGE_CONFLICT` → Blocked with the rebase aborted, until hand-backs come) and checks it with `src/run/check.ts` (check_fast in the worktree, its whole output in `.tutti/run/check.log` opened through the symlink guard, `FLAKY:` warnings, `CHECKS_FAILED` → Review), then fast-forwards the base in the main checkout (`DIRTY_TREE` and `GIT_FAILED` → Blocked and a hold, `MERGE_FAILED` → Blocked, exit 6), removes the worktree and branch, and closes the epics whose last subticket merged, up the tree. Only the step from the fast-forward on is protected from interruption; a quit during the rebase aborts it and one during the check kills it, the ticket back in line `ToMerge` (the new `Merging → ToMerge` transition, cause `MergeInterrupted`). `test/merge-queue.test.ts` covers them with the harness and the in-memory git.
- The verifier runs in the foreground (§ 4.1): `activityHooks` adds a `PreToolUse` callback with matcher `Agent` that gives the verifier's call `updatedInput` with `run_in_background: false`, and `{}` for any other call, with no permission decision; it returns at once and never fails. `test/verifier-foreground.test.ts` covers it, with a live contract test (`TUTTI_LIVE=1`): the Agent call's `tool_result` isn't `async_launched` and comes before the turn's one `result`.
- A removal Claude Code's safety check holds (§ 4.5): in auto mode the CLI denies it without asking, so the spec takes it as a `permission_denied` with `decision_reason_type` `safetyCheck`, logged `DENIED … by the safety check` and shown as a classifier denial is; in default mode it is asked without `defaultToNo`, so Attention sets `defaultToNo` on a request whose `decisionReasonType` is `safetyCheck`. The DENIED line names a `decision_reason_type` it doesn't know as given, and the worker prompt says the safety check refuses such a removal outright in auto mode. `test/attention-safety-check.test.ts` covers the flag.
- A failed turn settles (§ 4.2, spec and `src/agent/WorkerState.ts`): an `is_error` result other than the usage limit, once nothing is queued and no background task is live, makes the worker `Failed{at, error, interrupted}`, which `settled` counts, so the continue nudge, `PAUSED` and the environment hold see an interrupted turn or an API error as orchestra's `StopFailure` does. `interrupted` is set for `error_during_execution`, an interrupt's result; the card shows `stopped` for it and `failed` otherwise. Hand-backs take a worker `Idle` or `Failed`.
- `src/services/Tty.ts`, the Tty service (§ 3.3, § 7.6, § 7.7): whether stdin and stdout are TTYs, `TERM=dumb`, `NO_COLOR`, the terminal's size, and its colour depth from `$COLORTERM` and `$TERM` alone (`COLORTERM=truecolor` or `24bit` is truecolor under any TERM, tmux and screen included; a `-direct` TERM or one of kitty, Ghostty, Alacritty, WezTerm, foot or Contour is truecolor; a `256color` TERM is 256; anything else is fewer). `outputMode` decides plain output, with its reason (`--plain`, stdin or stdout not a TTY, `TERM=dumb`, fewer than 256 colours), or the dashboard; `plainReasonLine` says why. `src/testing/Tty.ts` gives a fixed value; `test/tty.test.ts` covers the colour depths and each reason.
- Settling and the outcome (§ 5 items 6 and 9, § 4.2 Settled, § 4.3): `src/run/work.ts` is the per-ticket fiber, its worker started, settled and its ticket moved as it left it. `src/run/settle.ts` waits until the worker is `Idle`, `Closed` or `Gone` (never `NeedsYou`, `Retrying` or `UsageLimited`), then reads the ticket in Beads and its worktree: closed with a commit naming it (as a whole ID), clean and on its branch → `ToMerge`, in the merge queue; closed with no commits of its own and a clean worktree, or commits that together change nothing → `Done{noChange}`, the worktree and branch removed; closed without a commit naming it while it has changes (`CLOSED_WITHOUT_COMMIT`), with uncommitted changes (`CLOSED_UNCOMMITTED`) or off its branch (`OFF_BRANCH`) → `Review`, `warn` with `aside`; a question it filed that blocks it → `Asked` (`ASKED`); deferred → `Deferred`; stopped with nothing done, having linked a blocker that hasn't merged → `Waiting` (`WAITS_FOR_MERGE`, reopened, its worktree removed), and `requeueWaiting` puts it back `Queued` once the blocker merges; left in another status → deferred for review. A ticket still in progress gets a `client_composed` continue nudge, at most twice, each waited for until no longer outstanding and settled again; then, after a 10-minute idle grace, `HOLD: PAUSED: …` and `Stopped{PAUSED}`, with exit code 3. A steered worker (the ledger's new `steered` flag, set by `steer` and cleared by `release`) gets no nudges, and its grace counts from your last message; one gone with its ticket in progress pauses at once. `test/settle.test.ts` covers each outcome, the nudges and PAUSED under TestClock, steering and a NeedsYou worker, with fast-check for `namesTicket`.
- The `unmerged` label (§ 4.8, § 11, `src/run/unmerged.ts`): as a run ends, `labelAtEnd` labels each ticket it took up that is closed in Beads and not merged, noting its branch's fork from the base in `unmerged_fork`; a ticket still in progress isn't labelled, and one merged in the run loses an earlier run's label (`LABEL_FAILED` when bd refuses). As a run starts, `loadUnmerged` checks each closed ticket labelled `unmerged`, by tutti on `tutti/<id>` or by orchestra on `wt/<id>`, as orchestra does: merged when its branch is on the base with a commit of its own naming the ticket, when the base holds the branch's changes (squash-merged) and a commit on it names the ticket, or, the branch gone, when a commit since the fork names it; the label is then removed, and the rest are handed back for carry-over. `Git.holds` is `git merge-tree --write-tree`, in the live and in-memory layers. `test/unmerged.test.ts` covers both prefixes with the harness and, slow, with real git.
- Spec § 4.8, § 13: the next run always resumes a suspended worker with tutti's own `client_composed` carry-on message, sent as the session starts, and never sets `CLAUDE_CODE_RESUME_INTERRUPTED_TURN`: tutti's quit interrupts first, so the SDK's re-run never follows it (docs/spikes/02-sdk-lifecycle.md, § 6 and § 7). A permission request asked again is a new request; a contract test resumes a quit session, busy and at a pending request, with the message.
- Spec § 4.2, § 4.8, § 7.5, § 13: closing a session doesn't drop the messages an interrupt leaves queued, since they start a turn within milliseconds (docs/spikes/02-sdk-lifecycle.md, § 1). Every interrupt is `interrupt({ cancelQueued: true })` where the CLI advertises `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1`, through a local type, as the SDK's types declare `interrupt()` bare; on a CLI without it, tutti cancels each `still_queued` uuid with `cancel_async_message` and accepts what runs all the same. A send cancelled either way is no longer outstanding; contract tests cover both cancels.
- Held back (§ 5 item 5, § 2 Solo, § 5.1 `HeldBack`): the scheduler holds a ready ticket back for what orchestra holds it for, and puts it back in `Queued` once that is gone. A parent waits until its subtickets are closed and merged (`parent`); a ticket waits for a blocker, and a follow-up linked `discovered-from` for its source, until that one merges (`merge`); with a slot free, a ticket whose footprint overlaps a running ticket's, its worktree's changes included, is skipped for the next (`overlap`); a ticket labelled `solo` runs alone, dispatched as `dispatching solo`, and first in line waits for the running tickets while holding back those behind it (`solo`). The log says why once per reason (`<id> waits: …`, `skipping <id>: touches …, like running …`, the solo lines), two running worktrees changing one file give `LIKELY_CONFLICT` once, and `queue` and `dispatch` records carry `held`, `held_by` and `solo`. `src/run/holds.ts` holds the reasons; `footprintsOf` reads footprints from the checkout. `test/held-back.test.ts` covers each reason under TestClock, with a fast-check property that overlapping tickets never run side by side.
- Hand-backs resume sessions as `src/run/sessions.ts` does (§ 4.7, § 4.8): a session tutti closed, resumed for a conflict or check hand-back, gets the `RESUMED` line (`a's conflicts with main are handed back to its worker; resuming its session …`), a `resumed` record with `why: hand_back` and one more resume in state.json. A worker whose session went (`Gone`) takes a hand-back while its ticket's one Gone resume of the run is left (`Ledger.goneResumed`), resumed with `why: gone`; once that is used the hand-back isn't given, and a resume that fails is `WORKER_GONE`, the hand-back set aside as one not done. `test/handback-sessions.test.ts` covers these and the session closed once a hand-back ends `Blocked` or `Review`.
